words) are "one quantity in two units" was a source read of our own emitter — both are
derived from `iw` because every capture has a tight plane. Read off the part instead, by a
1x1 convolution whose two live weights copy a 16-bit position code out of the feature buffer,
so the output at `(y, x)` NAMES the atom the DMA fetched:

| register | what it is |
|---|---|
| `0x1044` low half | the DDR row ADVANCE, in 64-byte granules |
| `0x103C` high half | the FETCH LENGTH, also in granules — and it must stay at the PLANE's count |
| `0x1094` | the channel-group jump, already a caller-supplied quantity |
| `0x1078` low half | the DMA's row BUDGET, spent at the ADVANCE and not at the fetch length |

The CBUF is filled contiguously with the fetch length, and the window walks it at `iw` atoms
a row — which is why moving `0x103C` to the pitch as well puts the read back at the tight
stride and computes a full, wrong surface. The budget is the one that is not obvious: a
pitched walk consumes `fetch_rows * entries_plane` granules of ADDRESS at the pitch's rate, so
it runs out part way down the plane and every row past that reads whatever the DMA had left.
The map departs at exactly the atom where that total is spent, at two geometries, and
inflating that one field to `ceil(rows * entries_pitch / entries_plane)` makes it affine to
the last row. **`0x1090` is not the row stride whatever its RK3588 name says** — it advances
the address by its own value once every FOUR rows while the rows between step by the fetched
width, so no value of it expresses a pitch.

The window grid does NOT follow the budget: the output extent and both derived pads come from
the real geometry, so a pitched convolution still synthesizes the trailing pad that a widened
PLANE would read real bytes for. That is the whole difference between this and the cheap route
— program the pitch AS the plane and let the output extent limit the windows — which is exact
wherever the window grid stays inside the caller's plane and wrong on the TRAILING columns
wherever a trailing pad is derived (measured, scored by region: 807 elements in column 27 of
28 at 28x28 k3 SAME, 659 in columns 19-20 of 21 at k5, and nothing at all at k1 or at the
stride-2 shape whose grid lands exactly).

Bit-exact against a tight-plane reference with the surplus poisoned over **nine geometries at
four gaps** (+1/+4/+16/+64 elements; kernels 1/3/5, both strides, square and non-square planes,
2 and 4 channel groups, one row task and up to ten), plus **three column offsets** of 1/3/4
elements — the feature base being a plain address at atom granularity, as the PPU's is — and
each geometry against a CONTROL that leaves the row stride derived and DIFFERS
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_conv_pitch.c`]. `conv_params_t.in_pitch_w` is the pitch
and 0 takes `iw`, so every program that does not set it is byte-identical.

Two geometry bounds ride with it and both are SILENT — past either, the surface is written in
full and wrong. **Both the pitch and the plane must fill whole 64-byte DDR granules**
(`iw * ic * elem % 64 == 0`): an odd plane at ic=32 is 10.5 granules a row, carries a rounding
the budget arithmetic cannot undo, and computes wrong at every pitch. And **a pitched task's
ROW WINDOW is the unpitched allowance scaled down by the granule ratio TWICE** — a 112-wide
plane inside 128-wide rows has an allowance of 48 rows, and 40, 41, 42 and 47 all compute
wrong while 16, 32 and 36 are bit-exact. That is a BOUND rather than the law: the residue
between 36 and 40 is unpinned, and the missing margin has the size of the retained rows a
chained window carries, which the arithmetic does not model. It is safe in one direction only,
like the unpitched allowance it scales — too small is a submit, too large is a silently wrong
surface.

**TRAP: THE ARMS OF A REGISTER PROBE ARE NOT INDEPENDENT MEASUREMENTS ON THIS PART.** Two arms
of that probe leave the part writing nothing for every submit that follows, in this process
and the next. Worse, a preceding arm can change a following one's MAP even when the two emit
byte-identical streams: run in sequence, the pitched walk appeared to revert to the tight
stride after four rows, and run one-arm-per-process it was affine to the last row. The
sequence's story — "the row stride and the group jump do not compose" — was wrong, complete
and mechanically plausible. Run one arm per process before believing anything a sequence says.

#### On a graph the encoding's program saving now lands

ResNet-18 at `bench 100`, three repeats each, everything else equal:

| arm | wall | submits | tasks |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| materialising packed stem, joined to the pool | **7.4** | 1 | 33 |
| direct stem (the control) | 9.2-9.3 | 1 | 38 |

**1.8-1.9 ms, 20%**, at the same 27 joins of 30 adjacent pairs either way, every compute layer
bit-exact against the CPU model and TFLite's own top-1. The packed stem HEADS the run and the
pool sits inside it, so the graph stays one hardware kick, and the graph's task count falls
from 38 to 33 — the packed stem is one row task where the direct lowering needs six (measured, both arms). The other three graphs do not move (V1 5.0, V2 6.7, IV1 8.7): both MobileNets have
TFLite's `pad_left = 0`, which no extension reaches, and Inception V1's stem has `in_zp = 0`
and so materialises nothing. **The governor is not what carries this** — the two arms differ in
host work, so pinning `performance` is the control the trap asks for, and it gives 6.0 against
7.7 ms over three repeats each, the same direction at 1.7 ms. The corpus numbers above stay on
`ondemand`, which is what every other wall here is measured on.

Per op, resident and governor-pinned, at that stem geometry: the materialising packed call is
**1.41 ms against the direct lowering's 3.13**, and against **1.26 ms** for the same packed
program at `in_zp` 0 [HW sweep, `tests/rk3576_stem_cost` group E, `ROCKET_SC_ZP=-128`].

What the join removes is one tensor transposed twice — warm over 20 inferences, the stem's
readback was **1.10 ms** of its 2.10 (0.10 the DPU drain) and the max pool's scatter **0.85 ms**
of its 1.45, the same 784 KiB out of a cube and back into one.

**The alternative route is a host COMPACTION and it is not needed.** Copying the 128-wide
surface into a 112-wide cube is 4 channel groups x 112 rows of 1792 contiguous bytes: **0.178
ms** over two BOs (4.52 GB/s), 0.163 in place, against a flat memcpy of the same bytes at
0.107 (7.47 GB/s), plus a **0.154 ms** PREP/FINI bracket over 784 KiB. So it would have cost
~0.33 ms and — being host work between two layers — would still have broken the run
[`tests/rk3576_row_pitch cost`]. Reading the surface in place costs nothing.

**The frontend still chooses.** `rk3576_net_gate` takes the materialising form where the next
layer is a pool, and `ROCKET_RK3576_NET_ARGB=0` is the A/B. A stem whose consumer is a
CONVOLUTION is now a program the part will run — the pitch encoding above is bit-exact and
the generator emits it — but the library's `rocket_conv2d_int8_cube_in_rk3576()` does not yet
carry a pitch through to the handle, so no graph reaches it. **No graph in the corpus does
either**: ResNet-18's is the only materialising stem and its consumer is the max pool, which
is why the pool was enough. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-02]

### A TFLite stem is reachable, by moving the input rather than the pad

"Can the program express a zero left pad?" is settled at the GEOMETRY, not at
`CNA_PAD_CON0`: a non-zero left pad and an output extent of `ow*stride == iw` are jointly
inconsistent with a zero lead at k3 s2, where a zero lead gives `ow = iw/2 - 1` and never
`iw/2`. The entry refuses before any register is written, and no reading of the pad
register would change that.

**But the stem is reachable anyway, and the parity argument was incomplete.** "A stride-2
sample grid's parity cannot be moved by padding" is true and does not finish the sentence:
the grid also moves when the INPUT moves, and an input shifted one pixel against a pad of
one is a shift of TWO — a whole stride. Extend the image by TFLite's trailing row and
column, feed the program that buffer starting at row 1 column 1, leave both register pads
at 1, and output `(y,x)` taps image rows `2y..2y+2` and columns `2x..2x+2`. That is
TFLite's grid exactly.

Measured at 224x224 k3 s2 oc=32: **394272 of 394272 interior outputs bit-exact** against a
reference that is TFLite's own SAME convolution over the original image. What is left is
the output's own top ROW and left COLUMN — 1.8% of the surface, whose outermost taps read
the hardware's pad constant instead of image row 0 and column 0 — and that is a host
fix-up, not a barrier. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_argb_pad.c`]

**Wired into a real graph it is a LOSS, and that inverts the reading above.** The
lowering is in `rk3576_net_gate` behind `ROCKET_RK3576_NET_TFLITE_STEM=1` and is byte-for-
byte identical to the widened direct path over all 401408 elements of the stem's output —
an A/B the gate runs whatever the default is, since the border is computed by the same
reference everything else is asserted against and a comparison against that reference
alone would say nothing about it. But interleaved at `bench 100` on a resident graph it
costs **5.5-5.8 ms against the widened path's 3.5-3.7**, taking the inference from ~30 ms
to ~31.5, even though it drops the layer from five submits to one.

The "worth about 4 ms" reading compared it to the widened path **transient** (10.6-12.2
ms), and resident weights had already collected that. Two measured costs stand between it
and a win:

- **The border fix-up is 1.7-1.9 ms for 1.8% of the surface.** It is memory-bound and not
  MAC-bound — 7136 outputs, nine scattered input cache lines each — so hoisting the loop's
  bounds into registers moved it by under 0.2 ms. This is the shift lowering's own cost and
  nothing since has touched it.

The other cost that stood here — the packed path having no resident handle, so that the call
paid a per-call BO teardown of 1.0-1.8 ms — is CLOSED: `rocket_conv2d_int8_pack_rk3576()`
packs the packed-image cube too, and the host interleave is vectorised. What remains against
the TFLite-stem shift is the border fix-up alone. Kept, gated and exact; off by default,
because the MobileNets it targets carry `pad_left = 0` and their whole stem execution is
0.30 ms of `exec + drain` against that 1.7-1.9 ms.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_net_gate.c`, 2026-07-29]

**What broke it open was submitting a vendor capture verbatim.** Our emitter reproduced
twelve int8 ARGB captures register for register and still wrote nothing at every geometry
tried, and a leave-one-out over the nineteen-register delta against the working fp16
program revived none of it — which read as a dead datapath. Taking the captured op stream
out of the golden table, patching only the five address registers and submitting it
(`tests/rk3576_fc_vendor.c`) showed the vendor's own program writing its whole surface.
The mode worked; the extrapolation away from the captured geometry did not, and walking
back from that geometry one axis at a time is where all four bounds came from.

**The CNA converter's offset is inert.** The ARGB register table lists `0x1054`-`0x105C`
as the uint8 zero point the CVT subtracts, which is what the datapath's description says
— but every ARGB capture is zero point 0, so those registers were transcribed and never
exercised. Driven on the part they do nothing: an image written at `raw = s + (zp+0x80)`
comes back with the raw byte read as a signed int8 and no subtraction at all, `0x80`
reading -128 and `0xFF` reading -1. So the packed image is plain two's complement and the
input zero point folds into the coefficient group's `A` term exactly as the direct path
folds it. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**The fp16 first conv poisons its own next submit**, and that is most of its wall time.
A second submit of the same program comes back with the surface never written; the write
rate against the inter-submit gap is 1/20 at 0 and 20 ms, 8/20 at 60 ms and 20/20 from
100 ms. It is not a property of the first-conv sub-encoding — it is the one hazard the
whole part carries on any wide output, and the section below is where that is settled.
**The int8 first conv has no such hazard**: 20 of 20 submits write at a zero gap.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

## The poisoning is one hazard, and its cost is a system setting

**Any program whose DPU output element is wider than one byte leaves the NEXT submit —
of any kind, across processes — completing normally and writing nothing.** One byte never
carries it. An A-poisons-B matrix over seven program kinds, each pair measured with a
canary that is known to write, splits them exactly: the fp16 first conv, the fp16 direct
conv, and both int32 writers poison every one of the seven, while the int8 first conv,
the int8 direct conv and the int8 depthwise poison none of them. Every A wrote its own
surface in every cell, so no cell is a dead program reading as a clean one.
`tests/rk3576_poison_probe.c` is the instrument, and its design is the point: asking
whether the program under test wrote cannot tell a DEAD program from a POISONED one, so
it scores the canary instead and lets the program under test compute nonsense.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**A sacrificial submit does not absorb it.** After one int32 job, six consecutive int8
jobs at a zero gap all wrote nothing. The state persists until the power domain cycles,
so the guard has to be an idle rather than a cheap dummy job.

**The two paths poison through DIFFERENT register conditions.** Both were found by
delta debugging (`rk3576_poison_probe heal`) over the register delta between a poisoning
program and its clean counterpart at the same geometry: apply the whole delta, confirm it
stops the poisoning, then bisect. A leave-one-out asks whether any ONE register is
sufficient, and every negative it returns is equally consistent with a condition of two —
which is why the earlier one-at-a-time sweeps found nothing on the fp16 path.

- **The int32 writers: one register.** `gen_conv2d_int8_rk3576_i32out()` differs from the
  int8 direct conv in four registers, and DPU `0x4010` (`out<<29 | in<<26 | proc`) alone
  carries it — restoring it stops the poisoning with the program still writing. Forced
  onto the int8 direct conv, `out` values 1-3 are clean and 4-5 poison.
- **The fp16 path: exactly two bits, and neither alone.** The fp16 direct conv writes the
  same 96 registers as the int8 one — no register is exclusive to either — and 11 values
  differ. Minimised, the condition is DPU **`0x4038` and `0x4050` together**: each
  restored alone leaves the canary at 0/8, both restored take it to 8/8 with the program
  still writing. An exhaustive truth table over the six differing bits of those two
  registers narrows it further to **`0x4038[4]` and `0x4050[17]`**: every combination that
  clears both is clean, every combination that leaves either set poisons, and no other
  bit matters. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**Those two bits are the float arithmetic itself, so there is no fp16 encoding that does
not poison.** `0x4038[4]` is part of the low half that must read `0x92` rather than the
direct path's `0x80`, and `0x4050[17]` is the high-half field that reads 2 at float and 1
at int8. With both cleared the fp16 direct conv still writes its surface and stops
poisoning — and every one of the five fp16 direct-conv shapes that reproduces a CPU model
exactly now computes wrong. The hazard cannot be encoded away on this path; it can only
be paid for.

**The state does not live in the DPU register file.** The nine DPU registers neither
program writes (`0x4000`, `0x4008`, `0x4040`, `0x4054`, `0x4064`, `0x4068`, `0x4098`,
`0x40a0`, `0x40c4`) written explicitly by the next job leave it poisoned; the canary
already writes `0x4038` and `0x4050` at their int8 values and is poisoned anyway. Whatever
carries it is internal and is reset by the power domain collapsing, not by a register
write.

**Nothing absorbs it, on any path.** One poisoning job followed by six submits, all four
poisoning program kinds, every position 0 of 6. The guard has to be an idle.

**A DEAD program can poison.** The four truth-table rows that drive `0x4038`'s low half to
`0x80` while the rest of the program stays float write nothing themselves and still leave
the canary at 0/6. "It wrote nothing" is not a proxy for "it was harmless", which is the
trap in any register sweep here that scores the program under test.

**What IS settled is the price.** The idle that clears it tracks
`power/autosuspend_delay_ms`, and the law is TWICE the delay: over a 5x7 sweep of delay
against gap, the gap at which a chain of wide-output submits first reaches 20 of 20 is
~100 ms at a 50 ms delay, ~40 at 20, ~20 at 10, ~10 at 5 and ~5 at 1.

**Lowering that delay system-wide is not free**, though a back-to-back chain cannot see
why: a resume costs about 230 us, and lowering the delay makes every job whose inter-job
gap falls between the new delay and the old one pay it. At a 16.7 ms gap — one 60 Hz frame,
which is the pipeline mainline's 50 ms was chosen for — a submit goes from 1124 us to 1372.
At a zero gap the two are identical, because the timer never fires.

**So the library drives the power cycle instead of waiting for it.**
`rocket_rk3576_power_idle()` writes a zero delay, polls `runtime_status` until it reads
`suspended`, and puts the caller's delay back; the guard costs one real cycle rather than
a worst-case idle, and the steady-state policy every other consumer of the NPU sees is
unchanged. It needs write access to that sysfs file and falls back to the plain idle
without it (`ROCKET_RK3576_PM_KICK=0` turns it off). At the **stock 50 ms delay** and with
every gate still bit-exact, the fp16 first conv's 224x224 k3 s2 runs in **13.75 ms against
226**, its whole gate group in 2.3-15.6 ms against 107-334, and the int32 matmul gate in
9.9 s against 16.2 — better than lowering the global delay to 1 ms, which reached only
21.6 ms and taxed every gapped workload to get there. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

### The eighth victim kind writes nothing, like the other seven

The A-poisons-B matrix covers seven convolution and matmul kinds and a poisoned B in all
seven writes NOTHING, which is what the library's whole-surface write guard is built on.
A **LUT job is an eighth kind and it behaves the same way**: behind an int32-output job,
`tests/rk3576_lut_probe.c`'s table-load-plus-consumer pair comes back with its surface
UNTOUCHED — every failing span reports `surface untouched 8192/8192` and every passing one
`0/8192`. **Rate 4 of 20** behind one wide-output predecessor, interleaved.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**A STAMP READ BACK IS NOT A MEASUREMENT, AND THIS ONE COST A SESSION.** The same
observation was first recorded as the surface being written *in full, correctly sized, and
CONSTANT* — every one of 8192 sampled inputs reading `-86` against a ramp, `max |diff| 213`
— and read as the consumer running against a table it never loaded. `SENTINEL` is `0xAA`
and `(int8)0xAA` is `-86`: the probe memsets it into the output BO before every submit, so
a job that wrote nothing hands the model back the probe's own fill, and a scorer that only
knows "expected vs got" reads that as a wrong constant. **Only the FIRST span of a run
could ever show it** — `mode_gate` sleeps 150 ms between spans, over the ~100 ms idle that
clears the hazard — which is why three spans always passed alongside it. The probe now
reports how much of the surface is still at the stamp, so the two readings are separated at
the instrument rather than by argument. **A probe that stamps a buffer must report how much
of the stamp came back, and a sentinel that is a plausible output value makes the question
invisible until it is asked.**

**The library's whole-surface write guard is intact**, and nothing in the corpus needs a
different one on this account.

**AND THE LIBRARY'S ACTIVATION ENTRY IS A VICTIM AT THE SAME RATE — its `0 of 24` was its
own guard, not an immunity.** In one interleaved run behind the same predecessor, at N=20:
the raw probe fails **4 of 20**, `rk3576_act_gate` fails **0 of 20** with its guard firing
in **3 of 20**, and with no predecessor at all it fails 0 of 20 with the guard firing in
**0 of 20**. The guard's own line names the poisoned job — `rocket_act_int8_rk3576: of 1
row task(s) in this submit the first that wrote nothing is 0, on attempt 1; cycling the
power domain and redoing it` — on the first call of the process, after which the gate
passes its 180 and 28 cases. 4 of 20 against 3 of 20 is one rate. The competing reading,
that `r76_conv_int8_run()`'s own setup submits stand between the predecessor and the LUT
job, is refuted twice: a sacrificial submit was already measured not to absorb this hazard
(six after one, every position 0 of 6), and the guard fires on the LUT job itself.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**AND THE ELEMENTWISE PROBE IS A THIRD VICTIM, WHICH LOSES ITS WHOLE RUN RATHER THAN ITS
FIRST CASE.** `rk3576_add_probe gate` is a DPU-only program like the LUT pair. Behind
`rk3576_pool_probe avg` — the only pooling mode that submits an int32-output matmul — it
reports NOTHING WRITTEN at **all ten** of its shapes, not at the first one: the ten submits
sit inside one active window, and a sacrificial submit does not absorb this hazard, so once
it starts nothing writes until the power domain collapses. **2 of 6 runs** behind that
predecessor against **0 of 6** behind an idle and **0 of 6** behind `pool_probe gate`
(pooling with no int32 job), which separates the trigger from the predecessor's kind.
Guarded the same way, it is **6 of 6 green in the poisoned position with 4 of them repaired
by a single power cycle** — so one cycle clears the state for the rest of the run, which is
what "all ten fail" and "one redo is enough" say together. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-04]

**So the probe now runs the same guard the library does** — a WHOLLY untouched surface is
redone after `rocket_rk3576_power_idle()`, up to the library's eight attempts, with the
redo count printed beside the span. A partial write is deliberately NOT retried, or the
loop would paper over an encoding defect. That turns `rk3576_lut_probe` from a gate that
was intermittently red at its list position into a deterministic one without hiding the
hazard: **0 of 20 failures behind the predecessor that put the unguarded probe at 4 of 20,
with the redo column non-zero in 7 of 20 and every one of them repaired by a SINGLE power
cycle** (7 redos over 7 runs). [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**It is not the first TASK of the next submit dying.** The pair is one submit of two
tasks, and a dead task 0 with a live task 1 would produce exactly this signature — so the
table load was duplicated (`ROCKET_LUT_DOUBLELOAD=1`, job load/load/consumer), which that
reading predicts takes the rate to zero. Interleaved against its own control, 40 each:
**10 of 40 against 14 of 40.** The prediction was zero; the hazard is per submit.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**Read a predecessor arm for what the whole PROCESS did, not for the program it is named
after.** This was first recorded as "a padded average pool poisons the next process's
first LUT submits", because `rk3576_pool_probe avg` was the only one of six `pool_probe`
modes that reached it. That mode also submits an int32-output matmul before half of its
pools, and no other mode submits one at all — so the wide-output writer and the
`AVG_NOPAD` divisor mode were confounded across every arm. Separated
(`ROCKET_POOLA_PRE`, `ROCKET_POOLA_EXCL`, `ROCKET_POOLA_PRE_ONLY`): **0 of 48 with no
int32 job in the predecessor** — including the padded average pool alone, both divisor
modes — against **24 of 84 with one**, across three different binaries, and the int32 job
with no pool at all is 4 of 12. The pooling mode does not move it (7/12 `AVG_NOPAD`
against 5/12 count-include-pad). [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**An arm that swaps in another PROCESS is an idle arm.** Putting a pooling or an
activation process between the two takes the LUT failures to 0 of 24, which reads as "a
submit absorbs it" and is not evidence for that: a process boundary carries an idle, and
the price above is already settled at twice `autosuspend_delay_ms` — about 100 ms at the
stock 50. What submits do is settled separately and the answer is that they do nothing
(six after one poisoning job, every position 0 of 6).

### `rk3576_lut_probe load` reports a false negative

`load` mode submits the table-load program alone and calls the scratch BO moving off its
sentinel the positive control for "the program executed". **That control reads zero** —
0 of the scratch's whole 4096 bytes, three consecutive runs — in a state where `gate` is
exact in all four spans, so the table demonstrably loaded. The program runs and its dummy
cube does not write that BO. Only `gate` is in the gate list, which is how the mode rotted:
**a probe mode nothing asserts on can be reporting a negative about a thing that works.**
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

### An externally reported hazard on the w4a16 RDMA path

The `charsiu` project reports a second next-submit hazard with a different signature
(gahingwoo/charsiu README; ROCK 4D, their v7-lineage kernel, 2026-08-15; not reproduced
here — see SOURCES.md): a job carrying the vendor's w4a16 values in DPU-RDMA `0x5034`
(`0x4000004c`) or `0x5044` (`0x000280a1`) — either register alone — leaves the NEXT job
timing out with an `rk_iommu` reset error beside it, and the driver recovers without a
power-domain cycle. Their bisect has the shape ours does: one w4a16 job per register
mask, judged by an int8 job in a separate process that is itself long byte-exact. The
two registers differ from their int8-path values in bit 30 in opposite directions, and
setting all four RDMA registers together, the vendor's own combination, also carries it.

Read against the hazard above, it differs on both observable axes: the victim times out
with an IOMMU fault rather than completing and writing nothing, and the state clears on
driver recovery rather than on the power domain collapsing. Treat it as a separate
mechanism until measured on this stack. No generator here carries a w4a16 stage, so
nothing shipped can emit those values; the trap is for a future int4/w4a16 port taken
from vendor streams — which is exactly where those two values come from.

## What the library entries cost

`tests/rk3576_cost_table.c`, three timed repeats after a discarded first, **one process
per row**, on the driver's default 50 ms autosuspend delay. The
NPU column is the WHOLE public entry — host packing into the native cubes, the submits,
the de-scatter back to row-major — because that is what a frontend pays; it is not a
kernel time. The CPU column is a single-threaded scalar direct loop over the same
tensors, auto-vectorised at the library's own optimisation level, with no im2col, no
packing and no threading: a reference, not a tuned kernel. **Read the ratio as an upper
bound against that baseline** — a good NEON GEMM across the four A72s would close much of
every one of them. [HW, H96 MAX M9, kernel 7.1.7 / `rocket` 1.6.0 in-tree, `dpu_grace_us`
0, `ondemand`, 2026-08-10]

| Shape | NPU ms | CPU ref ms | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| stem 3x224x224 k3 s2 oc32, int8 | 3.21 | 41.7 | 13.0x |
| stem 3x224x224 k3 s2 oc64, int8 | 4.62 | 83.1 | 18.0x |
| stem 3x224x224 k7 s2 oc32, int8 | 3.12 | 188.8 | 60.6x |
| stem 3x224x224 k3 s2 oc32, **fp16** | 13.98 | — | — |
| conv 32->64 k1 56x56 | 2.41 | 12.0 | 5.0x |
| conv 64->64 k3 56x56 | 2.68 | 242.8 | 90.6x |
| conv 128->128 k3 28x28 | 2.52 | 186.9 | 74.3x |
| conv 256->256 k3 14x14 | 4.44 | 185.0 | 41.7x |
| conv 256->512 k1 14x14 | 1.87 | 52.3 | 28.0x |
| depthwise 64 k3 56x56 | 2.36 | 7.5 | 3.2x |
| depthwise 128 k3 28x28 | 1.42 | 4.0 | 2.9x |
| depthwise 256 k3 14x14 | 1.07 | 2.2 | 2.1x |
| matmul 32x1024x1024 | 4.32 | 13.3 | 3.1x |
| matmul 32x1024x2560 | 9.78 | 31.1 | 3.2x |
| matmul 128x1024x1024 | 5.20 | 52.8 | 10.2x |
| matmul 256x512x2048 | 7.12 | 106.5 | 15.0x |

**Read no single row's move against the 1.5.0 table as a result, and read the CPU column
to see why.** These are the same sixteen shapes the 1.5.0 table carried and twelve of the
sixteen NPU rows come back lower — the four matmul rows by 9-22% and the three int8 stems
by 12-16% — but the CPU reference, which no module change can touch, moves by up to **25%**
between the two runs (`conv 256->512 k1` 42.0 -> 52.3, `conv 32->64 k1` 11.4 -> 12.0).
That is the table's own run-to-run spread measured on a column with no NPU in it, and it
is wider than most of the NPU deltas, so the honest reading is one table replacing another
rather than a change with a direction. The one thing the deltas do NOT support is the
drain deadline as their cause: a job retiring EARLY is a job whose wall is short, so
curing it should raise these numbers rather than lower them.

**These rows move much less than a GRAPH does under the same change**, and the reason is
in what they measure. Vectorizing the CHW <-> NC1HWC2 transpose took a resident MobileNet
inference 30.1 -> ~21 ms (1.41x) and moved these by 0-30% — because every row here is a
TRANSIENT call, which also pays the BO allocation and teardown and packs its weight cube
from scratch, so the transpose is a much smaller share of it. The rows that move are the
ones with least other work: `depthwise 64 k3 56x56` 3.11 -> 2.11, `conv 256->512 k1`
2.02 -> 1.70, the int8 stem 3.40 -> 3.09. Individual shapes vary 20-40% run to run, so
read a single row's change with that in mind.

Four things the table says, at this operating point rather than as properties of the
silicon:

- **The submit is a floor of about 0.44 ms, and the submit-bound rows track it.** The
  rows that moved most when the completion poll went from 500 us on `PC_DONE` to 50 us on
  the DPU bit are the ones with least work per submit — `conv 32->64 k1 56x56` 3.12 ->
  2.01, `depthwise 256 k3 14x14` 1.42 -> 0.96 — while the rows dominated by host packing
  or by a power cycle barely move.
- **Depthwise wins by 2.1-3.7x**, and against a threaded CPU kernel it would be close.
  That much is arithmetic rather than encoding: a depthwise conv has `ic` times fewer
  MACs than the direct conv at the same shape, so the same submit floor buys `ic` times
  less. Batching several depthwise layers into one submit is the lever the encoding
  would have to supply.
- **The matmul's axis is N, and the planner already spends it.** At M=32 the planner
  puts K=N=1024 in a SINGLE submit — the largest N tile the part will take — so the
  earlier reading that the matmul was dispatch-bound at low M was wrong. What it was
  bound by was the host cube scatter, below.
- **The quantized stem is still the stem to run**, but the gap is 3.2 ms against 13.7
  rather than against 226. What closed it was driving the power cycle the fp16 path's
  guard waits for, not arithmetic — the residue is one real cycle per call, and the fp16
  path pays the `dpu_grace_us` blind settle on top because it raises no DPU completion.

**The host cube scatter was most of the wall, not the submit.** The entries pack
row-major tensors into this part's cubes and de-scatter the results back, and doing that
an element at a time through the index functions costs a divide, a modulo and a call per
byte — which at K=N=1024 is a million of them, and was twelve of that matmul's thirteen
milliseconds. Three of those scatters are not scatters at all once the index is written
out: at `kh=kw=1`, `weight_conv_int8()` puts thirty-two consecutive contraction elements
at thirty-two consecutive bytes on both sides, and `feature_data()` at C2=16 does the
same for sixteen, so the matmul's feature pack, weight pack and output de-scatter are
blocked memcpys. The convolution entries stay a real transpose — CHW against NC1HWC2 —
and keep a strided store, but the per-element division hoists out to one pair per
channel. Everything above is measured after that change; before it the matmul tied the
CPU reference at M=32 (1.0x), depthwise ran 1.4-1.6x, and the 1x1 convolutions 2.2x.
[HW, H96 MAX M9]

**And the convolution entries' weight side was still per-element after that.** Running a
whole graph, not a shape, is what showed it — `ROCKET_RK3576_INT8_PROF=1` splits a call
into its operand packs, its submits and its de-scatter, and on the layers that cost most
the submit was 1-3% of the wall while two host loops were 90%. Both are functions of the
WEIGHTS alone, and both were walking a million elements through index arithmetic: the
per-output-channel filter sums, which are a contiguous scan written as a triple-nested
index expression, and the weight cube scatter, whose `taps == 1` case is thirty-two
contiguous source bytes landing on thirty-two contiguous cube bytes and so a run of block
moves rather than a run of byte stores. Straightening both took MobileNetV1's graph from
146 ms to 115 ms with every layer still bit-exact, and the 1x1 convolution rows of the
table above from 5.6x/15.7x to 5.3x/21.7x. [HW, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_net_gate.c`]

**A locality rewrite of the FEATURE scatter is a measured negative.** Packing pixel-outer
and channel-inner fills each C2-byte atom in one go and touches every destination line
once instead of once per channel, which is the better trade on paper and was not on the
part: it needs C2 concurrent read streams, and on the graph it cost the depthwise layers
more than it bought the 1x1 ones (layer 3's feature pack 3.2 -> 5.3 ms). The scatter stays
channel-outer — one sequential read stream and a strided store.

**A matmul computes on this part, at int8.** `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` is the entry;
`tests/rk3576_matmul_gate.c` holds it against a CPU model over 33 shapes, including M
with no even divisor, K and N off the round grid, several N tiles, a row-windowed M, the
two alignment refusals, and eleven that gate the int32 output exactly. Nothing about the register program was new — a matmul is a 1x1
convolution and this part already computed those bit-exactly — so what the work was is
the userspace layer around one: the operand scatter into this part's cubes, the output
de-scatter, the tiling planner, and the per-chip dispatch point.

Three of its properties are the RK3588's answers inverted, and the last one is the reason
the entry is separate rather than a routing behind `rocket_matmul_int8()`:

- **int8 is the matmul precision here.** One int8 task takes `ic*kh*kw` up to 4608, so a
  K of 4608 lands in a **single submit**; one fp16 task contracts exactly sixteen input
  channels, so the same K costs 288. Run on one shape at both precisions, fp16 is 30x
  slower at K=512 and 300x slower at K=4608. On the RK3588 fp16 wins and int8 buys only
  RAM.
- **The M axis carries no constraint.** M = 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 17 and 31 are each bit-exact,
  and so is every factorization of M into a plane — a single row of pixels, a single
  column, and everything between. On the RK3588 rows are the conv's spatial height,
  height < 4 mis-computes, `M%4` is the real bound, and software pads `M==1` to 4.
- **The output is int8, through the DPU's requant**, where the RK3588's int8 matmul
  writes raw int32 and sums K partials on the host. Inside one task's contraction that is
  the fast path and the requant is the DPU's own. Past it, K is split through the
  int32-output entry below and the requant moves to the host — the boundary is invisible
  in the result and expensive in the cost.

**Throughput is MACs per submit and nothing else.** A submit costs about 0.44 ms whatever
it carries, and two of the three axes are already capped — `M*K` by the feature budget at
262144 int8 elements, K by the resident weight slice at 4608 — which leaves N as the only
axis worth spending. Measured at the feature-budget cap it rises almost linearly with N:
12 GOP/s at N=32, 47 at 128, 367 at 1024, and about 1.0 TOP/s at N=2560. Two bounds stop
it, both of which lose the trailing output channels silently, with a full correctly sized
surface and a clean prefix: **2944 output channels** computes and 3072 is intermittent,
and independently a **6 MiB weight cube** computes where 6.75 MiB does not. Both are now
refusals in the CBUF planner rather than hopes, and the default tile of 2048 sits far
enough inside that they are guards. No convolution shape reaches either — they bite only
at the output widths a matmul drives.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-27]

### The int8 GEMM against a real CPU GEMM, at LLM prefill shapes

The cost table above prices the entry against a scalar reference. Against a *tuned* CPU
GEMM at the shapes an LLM prefill actually issues — `M` at the ubatch, never 1 — the
entry is **3.3-6.8x**, median **5.4x** over the six shapes that compute correctly.
`ggml-rocket/test-rk3576-sgemm-ab.cpp` is the harness: the NPU column is the whole public
entry (host pack of A and B into the cubes, the submits, the de-scatter), the CPU column
is the **fastest** of ggml's own F32 `MUL_MAT`, ggml's Q8_0 `MUL_MAT` and OpenBLAS
`cblas_sgemm`, all four threads on the A72s, arms interleaved.

| M / K / N | NPU ms | best CPU ms | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 512 / 2048 / 512 | 10.29 | 34.39 | 3.34x |
| 512 / 2048 / 2048 | 28.20 | 133.22 | 4.72x |
| 1024 / 2048 / 2048 | 48.50 | 267.79 | 5.52x |
| 2048 / 2048 / 2048 | 78.70 | 532.24 | 6.76x |
| 512 / 2048 / 8192 | 91.07 | 533.27 | 5.86x |
| 512 / 4096 / 4096 | 100.13 | 531.80 | 5.31x |

ggml's Q8_0 kernel is the fastest CPU arm at every shape — 3.2x its own F32 and a few
percent under OpenBLAS — so the denominator is not a strawman. It is also not helped by
the ISA: this part is **ARMv8.0-A** (`/proc/cpuinfo` has no `asimddp` and no `asimdhp`),
so there is no SDOT and no fp16 arithmetic, and a part with dot-product would close some
of this. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-08-07]

**The ratio RISES with M, and what rises is amortization of a fixed cost.** At K=N=2048
over four values of M the entry fits `T(M) = 10.9 + 0.0337*M` ms. The intercept is
M-independent work — B's cube pack plus the per-call BO allocation and teardown — and it
is **39% of the M=512 wall and 14% of the M=2048 one**. This part has no resident-weight
matmul path, so the entry re-packs B on every call; building one is worth up to that
intercept, which at M=512 would take 28.2 ms to ~17.3 and the ratio from 4.7x to ~7.7x.

**A median over a shape set is not the model-weighted answer.** Weighting these shapes by
a Llama-3.2-1B-shaped layer's own MAC shares at ubatch 512 — attention QKVO 17%, gate/up
55%, down 28% — and leaving the down-projection on the CPU because it refuses (below),
one layer is **2.39x**, not 5.4x.

### The entry at M = 1, and what decode would cost

The transient entry is not a decode engine, and at M = 1 the reason is arithmetic: the
call is its M-independent intercept. Decode-shaped rows of `rk3576_cost_table`, ten timed
repeats after a discarded first, one invocation:

| shape | NPU ms | CPU ms | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| matmul 1x1024x1024 | 3.88 | 0.43 | 0.11x |
| matmul 1x2048x1024 | 8.72 | 0.78 | 0.09x |
| matmul 1x1536x1536 | 8.25 | 0.94 | 0.11x |
| matmul 32x2048x1024 | 7.85 | 24.63 | 3.14x |

M = 1 tracks M = 32 at the same K and N within 3-11% (two runs), which is the intercept
measured a second way. `ROCKET_MM_PROFILE` on single-row cells splits the whole call —
the profiler buckets the entry's setup, its BO teardown and its own entry-spanning wall,
and wall minus accounted reads 0 over 11 calls. At 1x2048x1024 (ms/call): `packB` 3.27,
`alloc` 2.36, `bofree` 1.18, submit plus fence wait ~0.27, everything else ~0 — so ~94%
of an M = 1 call is machinery a resident-weight path would not run: the pack, and the
five-BO create/mmap/munmap/GEM_CLOSE round trip (the packB cache and the BO pool named
at the end of the section above). The teardown term is M-independent (1.18 ms/call at
M = 32 too, 0.91 at 1x1024x1024) and was the ~1.3 ms an earlier read left unattributed;
the write guard's read and every PREP/FINI pair were already inside buckets. [HW sweep,
H96 MAX M9, 7.1.7 / `rocket` 1.6.0 in-tree, `ondemand`, 2026-08-18/19]

**The device-side residue sits on a weight-fetch line.** The fence-wait marginal between
the 1.05 MB and 2.10 MB rows is 86 us/MB — against the 84.3 us/MB charsiu fits on a ROCK
4D through its own kernel (`github.com/gahingwoo/charsiu`, chained tasks, 2026-08-15).
The agreement is closer than the instrument: the profiler dumps integer milliseconds over
eleven calls, so our marginal carries about +-60 us/MB. Read it as the same regime, not
the same number.

**What decode would cost with the transient machinery gone [inference, end-to-end
untested]:** a resident, chained M = 1 path pays the bytes term — an int8 model's weights
once per token at ~86 us/MB — which is the same DRAM wall a CPU GEMV pays on a part where
one A72 thread saturates memory. So int8 NPU decode projects to parity with the CPU
rather than a win, and halving the bytes (int4) is the lever that would move it. charsiu
reaches the same reading from the vendor side: the vendor runtime ships M = 1 NPU decode
at ~13 tokens/s on Llama-3.2-1B (their measurement, their board, their kernel).

### The same port on a real model: a TIE with the best CPU arm, and the denominator is half the headline

The W8A8 route running under stock llama.cpp on **Qwen2.5-1.5B**, `pp512` at ubatch 512,
four threads pinned to the A72s, governor `performance`, one job in flight. The GGUF is an
F16 conversion of the same HF snapshot the accuracy corpus is scored on; `ggml-rocket` is
the DL backend and the entry is `ROCKET_INT8=1`. Two CPU denominators run in the same
boot, interleaved, two processes each:

| arm | GGUF | pp512 t/s (two processes) | ratio to the NPU arm |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| CPU only | F16 | 5.92, 5.77 | **2.01x** |
| CPU only | Q8_0 | 11.58, 11.51 | **1.02x** |
| NPU, W8A8 | F16 | 11.78 +- 1.80, 11.78 +- 1.84 | — |

**The port ties the strongest CPU arm end to end and doubles the weakest one, and the two
are the same silicon on the same model.** The 2.01x is the number a drop-in user reads,
because the port consumes an F16 GGUF and ggml's F16 `MUL_MAT` is what that GGUF runs on
the CPU; the 1.02x is the number against ggml's Q8_0 kernel, which is what the port's
**2.37x model-weighted cap was measured against**. Quoting the first without the second
credits the port for its own input format.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, 7.1.7 / `rocket` 1.6.0, llama.cpp b10356, measured 2026-08-11]

**So the 2.37x cap overstates the end-to-end port by 2.3x**, and it was a cap over
PROJECTION MACs on shapes this model does not have. What the cap did not carry: every
offloaded call here is `K`=1536 and none of the six shapes behind the cap is; the
down-projection's 29.4% of projection MACs is on the CPU by the library's own `K >= 6176`
refusal (that part the cap did charge); and the wall also holds attention, the norms, rope,
softmax and the scheduler's copies. **Six of the seven projections per layer offload — 168
calls per ubatch over 28 layers** — with the down-projection and the `lm_head` (computed at
`M`=1 for the last token only) left behind.

**Where the tie comes from is priced by a THIRD `ROCKET_MM_PROFILE` accumulator**, added
because the first two do not fit this route: `g_prof` and `g_prof_i8` in `rocket_matmul.c`
split an RK3588 job into pack/gen/sync/submit/wait/read, and three of this route's real
terms have no bucket there — the O(K*N) `sum_abs_w` pass, the per-tile per-column ramp
plan, and the poisoning stamp-and-check. `rocket_matmul_rk3576.c` carries its own
`sumabs / packA / packB / coeff / gen / alloc / stamp / submit / wait / read`, over calls,
tiles and submits; `ggml-rocket.cpp` carries the terms outside the entry
(`valloc / quant+rot / calibrate / entry / dequant`) on the same knob. Both sum to the
OFFLOADED path, not to the prefill wall — attention, the norms, rope and the scheduler are
in neither.

**Two operating notes the arm produced.** The governor is worth **16%** on this arm — the
NPU column reads 9.87 and 9.91 t/s on `ondemand` against 11.78 pinned — and the NPU arm's
run-to-run spread is **+-1.8 t/s (15%)** against +-0.01 on both CPU arms, so a single
process is not a measurement of it.

### What refuses past a 512-token prompt is FLASH ATTENTION, and the axis is context, not ubatch

The NPU arm returns `failed to decode prompt batch, res = -3` and llama.cpp stops whenever
the prompt is longer than 512 tokens. **The refusing op is `GGML_OP_FLASH_ATTN_EXT`, the
trigger is `n_kv >= 1024`, and the micro-batch does not move it.** The cells, one process
at a time on an otherwise idle board:

| arm | `-b`/`-ub` | prompt | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| NPU, FA on | 512 / 512 | 512 | **rc=0**, 10.51 t/s |
| NPU, FA on | 512 / 512 | 2048 | `res = -3` after 79 s |
| NPU, FA on | 1024 / 1024 | 2048 | `res = -3` after 2 s |
| NPU, FA on | 2048 / 2048 | 2048 | `res = -3` after 4 s |
| NPU, FA on | 1024 / 1024 | 1024 | `res = -3` after 5 s |
| NPU, **FA off** | 512 / 512 | 2048 | **rc=0**, 12.12 t/s |
| NPU, **FA off** | 1024 / 1024 | 2048 | **rc=0**, 12.10 t/s |

**A ubatch of 512 fails at a 2048-token prompt**, which is the cell that decides it: the
micro-batch is a shape the route computes 168 times per ubatch in the passing control.
What separates the passing row from the failing ones is the CONTEXT, and the elapsed times
locate the failure inside the run — 79 s at ubatch 512 is the second micro-batch, where the
KV length first crosses 1024, against 2-5 s when the first micro-batch is already that
long.

**The chain, read in source before the arm ran.** `llama_decode`'s `-3` is
`GGML_STATUS_FAILED`; an allocation failure is a distinct `-2` (`ALLOC_FAILED`), so the
board's RAM is not what this is. `ggml-rocket`'s `supports_op` claims `FLASH_ATTN_EXT`
subject only to shape, type, `n_tokens >= 16` and `n_kv >= rocket_flash_attn_min_kv()`
(**default 1024**) — **with no `rocket_rk3576_selected()` branch**, unlike its `MUL_MAT`
case, whose comment states this exact failure mode. But the handler's only compute call is
`rocket_flash_attn_fp16*`, and every path in `rocket_attn.c` goes through
`rocket_matmul_fp16`, which begins with `mm_wrong_encoding()` and returns
`ROCKET_E_UNSUPPORTED` on any profile that is not `rk3588`. **So the FA gate was ported to
this part by not being touched**: at a prompt of 512 the `n_kv` floor kept it out of reach,
which is why the pillar's whole corpus ran.

**The refusal names its clause and the HARNESS eats it, which is not the same thing.**
`mm_wrong_encoding()`'s `ROCKET_LOGE` and `graph_compute`'s `GGML_LOG_ERROR` both fire;
`ggml-rocket` forwards the rocket channel into ggml's, and **`llama-bench` installs a no-op
ggml log callback that swallows everything sent through it**. `ROCKET_DEBUG=1` raises the
LEVEL and does not change the SINK, so it cannot help — **`ROCKET_LOG_STDERR=1` is the knob**,
and it is the one the convert / int8 / MoE profiler dumps already use for exactly this
reason. Read `res = -3` under a bench harness as "a message you are not being shown".

**This is a coverage question, not a bound.** The op is claimed and then declined, and the
scheduler has nowhere to put it — so the fix is at the gate (decline `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` on
this part and the CPU takes it), not in the handler. Whether the gate should decline or the
handler should be ported is the maintainer's call; declining is what makes the port usable
past a 512-token prompt today, and `ROCKET_FLASH_ATTN=0` is the knob that already does it.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, Qwen2.5-1.5B F16, llama.cpp b10356, measured 2026-08-11]

**The `-ub 2048` lever is NOT closed on this part** — the earlier reading that it was came
from runs in which the ubatch axis was the only one varied and the context moved with it.
With FA declined the port reads **12.12 t/s at ubatch 512 and 12.10 at ubatch 1024**, so
doubling `M` per offloaded call moved 0.2%. **That is a hint and not a measurement** — one
process per cell against this arm's own +-15% run-to-run spread. The profile buckets below
settle what it is worth; two wall-clock points do not, and the wall cannot resolve it at all.

### Where the offloaded W8A8 path's time goes, and what the wall it is a share of is

`ROCKET_MM_PROFILE=1 ROCKET_LOG_STDERR=1`, Qwen2.5-1.5B F16 on llama.cpp b10356,
`ROCKET_FLASH_ATTN=0`, `-p 2048` with `-ub` in {512, 1024, 2048} at a FIXED prompt — the
same total tokens and the same total MACs with the per-call count quartered — one process
per cell, governor pinned to `performance`, `ondemand` restored after.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, measured 2026-08-11]

**The model offloads 166 matmuls per micro-batch**, `K` = 1536 or 8960, and the entry's
buckets are constant at **2.301 tiles per call** across the whole axis while submits per
call double with `M` (9.20 / 18.41 / 36.82).

Per LIBRARY CALL, in ms:

| bucket | M=512 | M=1024 | M=2048 | scaling |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `sumabs` | 3.62 | 3.61 | 3.61 | **M-independent** |
| `packB` | 7.51 | 7.58 | 7.60 | **M-independent** |
| `coeff` | 0.219 | 0.218 | 0.215 | **M-independent** |
| `gen` | 0.044 | 0.068 | 0.116 | ~M-independent |
| `packA` | 1.87 | 5.35 | 11.32 | M |
| `alloc` | 10.63 | 13.33 | 18.74 | mixed |
| `stamp` | 2.13 | 5.29 | 14.51 | M |
| `submit` | 0.148 | 0.246 | 0.449 | M |
| `wait` | 4.75 | 10.50 | 24.88 | M |
| `read` | 3.65 | 6.96 | 15.60 | M |
| **accounted** | **34.57** | **53.16** | **97.05** | |

**THE ENTRY IS HOST-BOUND: `wait` — the part actually running programs — is 13.7% of it at
`M`=512**, 19.7% at 1024 and 25.6% at 2048. The two largest buckets are `alloc` (30.7% at
`M`=512: the weight, coefficient and output BOs are allocated and freed PER TILE PER CALL)
and `packB` (21.7%). Attribution order held: the host is the cost, and the part is a quarter
of it at best.

**THE M-INDEPENDENT INTERCEPT WAS 11.4 ms PER CALL WHEN THE ENTRY RECOMPUTED PER CALL,
MEASURED, NOT FITTED** — `sumabs+packB+coeff+gen` read **11.39 / 11.48 / 11.54 ms**
across a 4x sweep of `M`, constant to 1.3%: three passes over `K*N` every call, **31.3%**
of the converged per-call cost at `M`=512. The shipped frontend path no longer pays
them — the caller hands the sums (`_sa`) and the weight resident in a device cube
(`_wbo`, below) — so this intercept now prices the calibration forwards and any caller
still on the per-call path.

**THE INTERCEPT IS `sumabs` PLUS `packB` AND ESSENTIALLY NOTHING ELSE**: `coeff` is 0.216
ms a call and `gen` 0.045, **0.8% of the 11.4 between them**, so the two together are
**11.136 ms = 32.35%** of the accounted total. A cap over a weight cache does not need to
discount the intercept for the other `M`-independent buckets — there is nothing in them.
`alloc` is large (10.640 ms, 30.91%) and is a different lever's; it is not inside this
intercept.

**THE `M`=512 COLUMN REPRODUCES ACROSS SESSIONS ON AN UNCHANGED BINARY, WHICH IS TIGHTER
THAN THIS ROUTE'S USUAL BUCKET DRIFT.** Re-measured a session later over three processes
at `-ub 512`, 2988 library calls each: `sumabs` 3.629 (3.62), `packB` 7.500 (7.51), `coeff`
0.216 (0.219), `gen` 0.0454 (0.044), `alloc` 10.640 (10.63), `stamp` 2.121 (2.13), `submit`
0.147 (0.148), `wait` 4.745 (4.75), `read` 3.601 (3.65), `packA` 1.771 (1.87), accounted
**34.42** (34.57) — every bucket inside 5.3% and all but `packA` and `read` inside 1.5%.
Process-to-process spread within the session is **0.04-0.6% on every bucket except `packA`
(9.8%) and `read` (3.4%)**, and `pp2048` reads 12.33 / 12.37 / 12.35 t/s. That is the
resolution any A/B on these buckets has to clear; it does NOT license quoting a level from
another session, only the observation that these particular levels held.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-13]

**`sumabs` NO LONGER RUNS ON THE SHIPPED FRONTEND PATH.** The per-column sum of |B| that
caps the C ramp is a quantity `ggml-rocket` already computes once per weight (it is where
`cbound` comes from), so the entry takes it instead of recomputing it:
`rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_perc_sa()`, with `ROCKET_RK3576_WSA=0` restoring the recomputing
form for pricing. Bit-identical by construction and measured so — `llama-perplexity
--chunks 4` returns **PPL 8.7433 +/- 0.76052** with the knob either way, to every printed
digit, on the same binary and the same text, and `tests/rk3576_mm_requant`'s supplied-vs-
computed arm reads **0 of 344064 elements** over six shapes at two column-scale spreads.
**`packB` NO LONGER RUNS ON THE SHIPPED FRONTEND PATH EITHER — THE WEIGHT IS RESIDENT IN A
DEVICE CUBE — AND A BO POOL TAKES THE TRANSIENT ALLOCATIONS.**
`rocket_rk3576_wbo_create()` packs a weight once into a device BO; the whole-`N` cube is
byte-for-byte the concatenation of the per-tile cubes (`nt` is a multiple of 32), so one
BO serves every `nt` and every `M` and a tile's program offsets `weights_dma` by
`(n0/32)*nK1*1024`. `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_perc_wbo()` is `_sa` with the object in
place of `B` — `sum_abs_w` required, K/N mismatch refused, a no-plan `K` refused outright.
The frontend creates the object at a weight's first CONVERGED call and DROPS its host
`qB` (`ROCKET_RK3576_WDEV`, default on, `=0` restores per-call packing), so device memory
goes up by what host memory comes down; calibration still reads `qB` and still takes the
per-call path. `ROCKET_RK3576_BO_POOL=1` (default OFF; read per allocation) keeps the
path's transient BOs on a per-fd free list capped by `ROCKET_RK3576_BO_POOL_MB`.

**Scored over 3x3 interleaved processes at `-ub 512`, one binary, per-call ms** (2988
calls; control / resident / resident+pool): `packB` **7.49 / 1.66 / 0.95** (the residue
is the 332 calibration forwards, still on the host path), `alloc` **10.60 / 5.18 /
0.015**, `bofree` 1.32 / 0.84 / 0.004, `packA` 1.84 / 1.59 / 0.98, accounted total
**32.13 / 19.89 / 13.06 (-59%)**. A fresh CREATE_BO's demand-zero page faults were
smeared across the first-touch buckets, so the pool removes more than its own `alloc` —
including **`calibrate` 21.2 -> 11.4 s a process** (the bootstrap's create faults lived
there). `wait` rises 14.1 -> 15.9 s with the host stalls gone — the NPU becomes the
pacing element [queuing, hypothesis]. Bit-identity: `--chunks 4` PPL **8.7433** to every
digit in both WDEV modes; the requant gate's resident, pooled(x2) and forced-16-tile
arms read 0 differing bytes; gate list 60/60. The interleaved wall read: **`pp2048`
12.30 -> 13.41 (resident) -> 13.53 t/s (both, +10.0%)**, n=3 pairs resolving ~3.3%.
What a green does not show: the IOMMU-churn exposure on kernels without the BO-lifetime
fixes, behavior under real memory pressure, and decode at M=1 (unmeasured; the cache
removes that call's 77% packB+alloc share in principle).
[HW, H96 MAX M9, chain `armD`, cells `w{0,1}p{0,1}_p{1,2,3}`, 2026-08-19]

**The pool's marginal wall gain, measured against a pinned layout, is +1.86% — about twice
the +0.9% the 3x3 read above gave it.** That earlier figure came from n=3 pairs against a
~3.3% instrument resolution; collapsing the per-process layout draw with `setarch -R` on
both arms brings the resolution to **0.358%** (re-derived from the arm's own cells) and makes
the level directly measurable. Four interleaved pairs, one invocation, one binary, treatment
`ROCKET_RK3576_BO_POOL` alone: per-pair **+2.115 / +2.112 / +1.385 / +1.820**, mean
**+1.8579%**, pool 13.9800 against 13.7250 t/s. **The mechanism is on the counter and it is
DRAM traffic, not host CPU**: the pooled arm's `l2d_cache_refill` level is **-1.50%** below
the unpooled arm's (16166.3M vs 16412.6M), the same order as the wall gain and in the same
direction as the L2-refill term that carries this part's per-process wall constant. **Scope,
and it is the whole caveat**: `setarch -R` pins ONE virtual layout, so +1.86% is the effect
at the *modal* layout rather than the ASLR-on expected value a shipped default would see.
The pool stays default OFF. One of the four pooled cells ran 0.501% slow with its refill
count at its arm's mean, which is not the page-colour draw and is so far unexplained; it
biases the figure downward, since dropping it widens the gap to +2.028%.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 8 cells, chain `poolarm`, 2026-08-20]

**THE PASS'S WALL VALUE IS NOT MEASURABLE ON THIS INSTRUMENT.** The first campaign read the
knob as **+6.70%** of `pp2048` and the rebuild as **-6.96%**; re-run with the order reversed
(arm, iso, control — one process each), the knob flip read **+1.7%** and the binary flip
**+2.0% the other way**, while every bucket reproduced its attribution. **The wall moves by
up to ~5% between PROCESSES with every bucket flat and no reproducible structure.**
Fourteen processes over three campaigns: the first campaign's five same-cell sequences were
flat inside their blocks (spread <= 0.4% — ctl 12.33/12.37/12.35, arm 12.28/12.23/12.27,
iso 11.48/11.50) with monotone steps between blocks; the reversed campaign declined
monotonically by position (12.24 -> 12.03 -> 11.79); and a same-day A/A — the arm cell
three times inside ONE invocation — read **11.77 / 12.26 / 11.69**, breaking both patterns
while its buckets held (`quant+rot` within 3.4%, `packB` 1.1%, `wait` 0.2%, `sumabs` 0).
The same cell spans 11.69-12.28 within one day and +-4.5% across days. Reps inside a
process agree to +-0.25%, so the term is a **per-process constant**, fixed at process
birth, living in the unbucketed ~78% of prefill (attention, norms, rope, the CPU-placed
matmuls); mechanism **[unknown]** — it defeated every ordering story tried on it (cell
identity, invocation position, thermal soak, idle reset). **A `pp2048` difference under ~5%
between two processes means nothing here regardless of order; a wall A/B needs interleaved
arms and enough processes to beat a ~2% per-process sd read from its own controls (rule
105), and a lever's durable price is its per-call bucket** — the buckets are stable to
2-3.5% across sessions where the effects of interest are 10-43%.

**THE PER-PROCESS TERM IS NOW NAMED AT THE COUNTER LEVEL: IT IS AN L2-REFILL (DRAM)
TERM, AND THE GAP INSTRUMENT WATCHES IT MOVE.** An A/A triple under `perf stat`
(2026-08-19, one invocation, governor pinned, equality-gated epoch): walls 12.57 /
12.56 / 12.11 t/s, the inter-call `gap` 545.5 / 545.2 / 569.2 s, buckets 136.6 / 137.1
/ 135.0 s — the slow process carries its whole excess in `gap` with the smallest bucket
sum of the three, so the term lives between calls inside the span, not in the bench
scaffolding outside it. Its counters: instructions +0.05%, `l1d_cache_refill` +0.0%,
`l2d_cache_refill` **+23.7%** (+4.0e9), cycles +4.4% tracking the wall — so frequency
is excluded and the constant is DRAM traffic from L2 misses. That much holds.
**The mechanism one level down was "the L2 is physically indexed, so the physical pages a
process is handed at birth set its conflict rate for life", and it is REFUTED for the
per-process component**: pinning the VIRTUAL layout with `setarch -R` collapses the
`l2d_cache_refill` CV from 9.699% to 0.099% (~98x) and the `pp2048` wall spread from
4.966% to 0.292% (17x) over 16 interleaved cells. The draw is virtual, not page luck.
Note also that the 512 KiB / 512-set L2 the physical story was argued from is the **A53**
cluster's; every cell here runs `taskset 4-7` on the **A72**, whose L2 is 1024 KiB /
16-way / 1024 sets — 16 page colours, not 8. The same triple shows the q8/L1d dial moving
the OTHER way in the slow process: the two locality terms are independent draws, and both
are virtual.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, chain `armC`, cells `aap_p{1,2,3}`; refuted by arm (i), cells
`walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

**THE TERM IS NOT AN UNCONDITIONAL PER-PROCESS CONSTANT: TEN CONSECUTIVE FRESH
PROCESSES CAN COME BACK FLAT ON EVERY COUNTER.** A ten-cell campaign (2026-08-19, one
binary, interleaved 5+5, governor pinned, 4 threads on the A72 cluster) reads
`l2d_cache_refill` max/min **1.0061** across its five plain processes — against the
+23.7% the A/A triple showed between two of three — with cycles 1.0013, instructions
1.0002, `l1d_cache_refill` 1.0014 and a **wall span of 0.15%** (13.68-13.70 t/s).
Whatever draws the term, it did not draw on this campaign, so a wall A/B run under
these conditions resolves far better than the ~2% per-process sd implies — the
sd is a property of the campaign, not a constant of the board, and it must be read
from each campaign's own controls rather than inherited. The campaign held a 1024-page
hugetlb pool and ran `compact_memory` before it, which defragments and shrinks the
physical pool every process then draws from; that is the leading suspect for the
flatness [hypothesis], and the protocol and epoch rivals to it have since been
eliminated (below).
[HW, H96 MAX M9, chain `rk3576-hugetlb.sh`, cells `htlb_{n,a}_p{1..5}`]

**THE TERM IS A LOTTERY, NOT A SPREAD, AND IT IS ALIVE ON EVERY EPOCH MEASURED.** Read
across 30 archived processes from seven campaigns, **26 sit within 0.30% of their group
median and 4 sit 2.8-3.6% out, with an empty gap between the two populations** — and one
of the four is FAST, so the modal cluster is not the best case. A five-cell no-pool
control on the current binary (2026-08-19, governor pinned, `taskset -c 4-7`) read walls
13.73 / 13.72 / 13.71 / 13.71 / **13.26**, and that fifth process reproduces the A/A
triple's signature exactly: **`l2d_cache_refill` +15.32%** against the mean of the other
four, on **instructions +0.05%** and **`l1d_cache_refill` +0.01%**, elapsed +3.11%, with
**every timed bucket flat to +-0.2%**. So the term did not die with the binary epoch. The
consequence for measurement is sharper than a spread would be: **a CV or a span at n=3-5
measures whether a draw landed in the sample, not the term's size**, which is why the
CVs of these campaigns range 0.05% to 2.15% with no dial explaining them. Price a wall
A/B at the bucket, or interleave and count draws — do not quote a CV.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, chain `rk3576-nopool.sh`, cells `nopool_p{1..5}`]

**A SECOND PER-PROCESS LOTTERY RUNS IN THE WEIGHT-CONVERT LOOPS, AND IT IS A DRAW IN A
DIFFERENT ADDRESS SPACE FROM THE WALL'S.** `wt_rotate` is sharply two-state across processes —
**10753 ms or 11927 ms, a 1.109x separation**, with the empty band between the modes
**7.5x** the next-widest gap in the sample — on work that is byte-identical every run
(166 convert calls, 2988 library calls, 6876 tiles, 27504 submits), so it is a timing
draw and not a workload difference. On the epochs where `wt_quant` is still a scalar loop
it is two-state with it (**5800 / 6558 ms**, its own gap 739 ms against a next-widest of
**8 ms**) and agrees on the mode **12 of 12** — one state the process is handed, across
two independent loops, which places it in the allocation-layout family rather than in
code alignment. It does **not** move the `pp2048` wall (-0.02% and -0.09% within the two
flat campaigns) because it runs in weight conversion, outside the timed reps. Its value
is as a probe: binary per process, a **~50% base rate**, cleanly separated, and printed
in every profiler log ever written here — so it is readable retroactively at no cost,
where the wall's own draw is rare enough (4 of 30) that n=5 sees nothing 24-53% of the
time. **Do not read a cross-epoch `wt_rotate` or `wt_quant` difference without matching
the modes**: a 7.3% difference between two n=3 samples was the mode mix, not the epoch.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, re-read of 30 processes across seven campaigns]

**THE CONVERT-LOOP MODE IS A VIRTUAL LAYOUT DRAW, AND `setarch -R` PINS IT.** Running the
same binary with ASLR disabled collapses the draw: **0 LOW of 16 at a full spread of
0.134%**, against **4 LOW of 16 at 11.756%** in an interleaved ASLR-on control — an 88x
variance collapse, one-sided Fisher **p=0.0506** on the mode mix alone. So the state a
process is handed is set by the layout ASLR randomizes, not by which physical frames it
draws. **The convert-loop mode cannot be used to screen for a wall roll**: within the same
ASLR-on control, mode-LOW cells are bench-wall outliers 2 of 4 against 4 of 12 for
mode-HIGH, one-sided Fisher **0.489**, so the two draws are independent [measured].
**`setarch -R` is worth having on any A/B that touches the convert loops** — it removes
an 11.8% per-process nuisance draw — but it does not buy the fast state: the fixed level
(11907-11923 ms) does not overlap the ASLR-on modal cluster (11874-11895) and is 0.15%
slower than it, and 10.8% slower than LOW. **Treat HIGH as a band, not a state**: the
ASLR-on HIGH draws separate into that modal cluster (9 of 12), an 11930 and a pair near
12015, while **LOW is genuinely discrete** — 10752 / 10753 / 10753 / 10754 over four
independent processes, a spread of 0.019%. A mode reading costs one `pp512 -r 1` process,
**99-103 s**, against 630 s for a wall cell. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 32 interleaved cells
on one binary, governor pinned, dial armed by address readback]

**THE BENCH WALL'S PER-PROCESS DRAW IS VIRTUAL TOO, AND THE TERM DECOMPOSES INTO A DRAW
THE LAYOUT FIXES PLUS A SLOWER ONE THAT SURVIVES THE PIN.** The same 32 cells each ran a
`pp512` bench. Scored **within an invocation** — the campaign was two runs of the harness
47 s apart, and pooling across that boundary turns the level step below into apparent
per-process scatter and inverts the answer — the ASLR-on control shows **6 of 16 wall
outliers** (>= 1.0% off its own median; a discrete band at -2.4% to -4.1%, 3 of 8 in both
blocks, full spreads 4.366% / 4.188%) against **0 of 16 with ASLR off** (0.592% / 0.329%),
one-sided Fisher **p=0.0088**. **So the layout the process is handed sets its bench wall as
well as its convert-loop mode, and the two are independent draws in that one address
space.** The magnitude and the empty-gap signature match the `pp2048` wall lottery's
(2.8-3.6% off median, 4 of 30) closely enough to suspect one phenomenon, but this is
`pp512 -r 1` and **that identification is [hypothesis]**. What does *not* collapse: under
ASLR off the bench wall is flat inside an invocation and its **level moved +2.508% between
the two**, while the interleaved ASLR-on arm did not move (+0.031% overall, HIGH band
-0.230%, LOW band +0.484% — so a system-wide drift is excluded) and the convert loop's
ASLR-off level did not move either (-0.030%, 0.134% across all 16). **`setarch -R` bounds
the per-process draw and cannot bound the between-invocation component**; the candidate for
that component is the physical/system state the allocator hands out [hypothesis].
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, same 32 cells, no additional board time]

**AND IT IS CONFIRMED AT `pp2048`, THE DEPTH THE WALL LOTTERY WAS ACTUALLY FITTED ON — SO
THE PER-PROCESS WALL DRAW IS VIRTUAL, NOT PAGE LUCK.** 16 interleaved `pp2048 -r 3` cells
in ONE invocation, both arms otherwise identical. The ASLR-on control **reproduced the
lottery**: 3 of 8 outliers at a 4.966% full spread, **2 of them inside the 2.8-3.6% band
the corpus recorded**, the third at -4.487%. The ASLR-off arm is **0 of 8 at a 0.292%
spread** (13.69-13.73 t/s). **Spread ratio 16.99x**, against ~10x at `pp512`. The
mechanism reads on the counter: `l2d_cache_refill` CV **9.699% on -> 0.099% off**. The
mix alone is underpowered at this n by construction (Fisher 3/8 vs 0/8 = 0.1000); the
magnitude is what carries it. ("Full spread" is range/min throughout, so that 16.99x is
like-for-like against the `pp512` figures; by the median the same cells give 16.25x.)
**The pin selects the MODAL layout rather than creating a new operating point**: the two
arms' medians are 13.705 and 13.720, **0.109%** apart, and the ASLR-on non-outlier band's
median is 13.72 exactly — the arms' *means* differ by 1.33% only because three slow draws
drag the control's. `setarch -R` removes the slow tail; it does not move the level. **Scope: `setarch -R` pins the virtual layout, not physical
frames, so this bounds the PER-PROCESS draw only** and cannot separate "the draw is
virtual" from "frame assignment goes near-deterministic once the layout is fixed"; the
+2.508% between-invocation step above is untouched by it. Practical consequence for
anyone benchmarking on this part: **`setarch -R` plus one invocation takes the
process-to-process wall sd to 0.087%**, which is what makes sub-1% host levers
measurable here at all.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

**THE PHYSICAL FRAMES A BENCH IS HANDED ARE A FRESH SET EVERY PROCESS, SO FRAME IDENTITY IS
NOT WHAT CARRIES THE WALL'S LEVEL.** `/proc/<pid>/pagemap` read concurrently with a live
`pp512` bench, at fixed virtual addresses under `setarch -R`, gives the (virtual address ->
physical frame) map directly; comparing two cells by **positional agreement** — the fraction
of commonly sampled addresses whose PFN is identical — is a magnitude and is indifferent to
physical contiguity. Over 8 cells in 4 invocations the comparison is total (one VMA layout,
`common = union = 12655` on every anon pair) and **anon agreement is 0.00000 on 26 of 28
pairs and <= 0.00063 on the other two**, against a run-count standard error of 0.0094. It is
0.00000 **within** an invocation as well as between, where the wall is flat — so no timescale
exists on which the anon frames repeat. The GGUF's page cache is the opposite: **file
agreement is 1.00000 on all 28 pairs**, i.e. byte-identical frames across every invocation.
**Scope: this answers frame IDENTITY, not whether physical memory matters** — a statistical
property of the frame set could still track the wall while every identity differs.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}`]

**ANON ALLOCATION IS CONTIGUOUS IN BUDDY BLOCKS: mean run 69.6 pages (278 KiB), median 32
pages, 96.8% of run lengths powers of two over orders 4-9**, on a kernel with no THP. Read
the granularity with it — the sample is every 16th page, so 39.7% of runs sit at that floor
and shorter ones are unresolved. This is why a **16-bin L2 page-colour histogram carries no
information on this workload**: inside a run the colour walk is deterministic and long runs
cover the 16 colours near-uniformly, so the histogram is flat by construction. A stride-1
read measured chi2 **0.12** on 15 df at n=202,459, sub-multinomial by 10.9x. **A colour
histogram is only informative if allocation is fragmented, and here it is not** — and a
sampling stride that is a multiple of 16 makes it worse, advancing the colour by zero inside
a run and producing a large chi2 that is pure aliasing.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}` and `armjprice`]

**THE BETWEEN-INVOCATION STEP IS NOT A PROPERTY OF STARTING A NEW INVOCATION.** Four
invocations of two ASLR-off `pp512` cells, 60 s apart on an otherwise idle board, one binary,
`performance`: the boundaries move **-0.082 / +0.123 / +0.082%**. Decomposed, within-invocation
sd is **0.1085%** (4 df) and the sd of the four invocation means **0.0854%** against 0.0767%
expected from within-noise alone, giving a between component of **0.0374%**, F = 1.238 on 3,4
df, **95% upper bound 0.237%**. That is an order of magnitude under the +2.508% step recorded
above, whose two invocations were separated by a session's worth of other work. **Read that
bound only under a continuous-nuisance model.** The wall term on this part behaves as a
DISCRETE draw — 37.5% outlier rates at the process level — and against a draw, 0 fires in 3
boundaries bounds the per-boundary rate only at 0.63, which excludes nothing; at a rate of
0.375 it would take seven boundaries to say anything. **An sd decomposition is not a bound on a
draw, and these four invocations being flat is a description of them, not a bound on the
step.**
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}`]

**2 MB PAGES UNDER THE MULTI-MB ANON BUFFERS COST 0.64% OF THE PREFILL WALL AND DO NOT
MOVE THE L2 REFILL COUNT.** The kernel carries no THP (the sysfs node is absent), so
the route is hugetlbfs through glibc 2.42's `glibc.malloc.hugetlb=2`; it reaches
601-606 2 MB pages (~1.2 GB of anon) at the bench's peak against 0 without it. Over
five interleaved pairs the hugetlb arm is **13.600 t/s against 13.688** — 0.64%
slower, sign-consistent in 5 of 5 pairs at ~4x the 0.15% within-arm spread — with
elapsed +0.53%, cycles +0.20%, and `l2d_cache_refill` **unchanged at -0.2%**. The host
buckets move with the wall (mm_wall +1.8%, entry +1.5%, alloc +1.4%, packB +1.3%);
`read` -9.4% and `wt_rotate` -4.1% sit inside their own 7.3% and 12% process spreads
and carry nothing. Measured on a campaign in which no process rolled unlucky, so this
prices the tunable at the flat operating point and is not a test of whether 2 MB pages
would blunt the term when it does fire.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, cells `htlb_{n,a}_p{1..5}`]

**A REBUILD CAN MOVE AN UNTOUCHED HOT LOOP 2.2x THROUGH L1d SET CONGRUENCE, AND THE
COUNTERS PLUS THE DISASSEMBLY SEPARATE THAT FROM CODEGEN IN ONE PAIR.** The frontend
commit that handed the entry its weight sums (a diff whose only code is in the
weight-convert path) moved `quant+rot` 32.6-32.8 -> 45.6-47.2 s a process, cell-locked
across days. Phase stamps applied to BOTH frontends (equality-gated: the old binary with
and without stamps prints PPL 8.7433 to every digit) put the whole move in `q8`, the
quantize loop — 9942 -> 21870 ms over the same 2656 calls, 96% of the delta, while
copy/rot/max held within 3.1%. The two binaries' q8 loops are instruction-identical
(same 11 instructions, same registers, one `lrintf@plt` call per element), and the
whole-process a72 counter pair reads instructions **-0.18%**, `l1d_cache_refill`
**+8.42%**, `l2d_cache_refill` +0.11%: zero added instructions, extra L1d misses that
hit in L2 — an L1d CONFLICT cost, not a footprint one. The set-level story is a
hypothesis, but it fits every observation: this core's L1d is 32 KiB 2-WAY, the loop
interleaves a float read stream and a byte write stream with a per-element libm stack
frame, and the three-way congruence is fixed by the binary's frame sizes and startup
allocation sequence — which is why page-padding one allocation moved the level partially
(42.4 s) and could not restore it. **The commit itself is de-blamed by a third roll**:
the same diff rebuilt against the newer library (a different link state) reads `q8` at
9.9-10.0 s — the good level — in all three of that epoch's cells, so the congruence is
an EPOCH property that any translation-unit or link change re-rolls, and the diff was
one way to roll the dice. Five rolls on record: old+old-lib good, new+old-lib BAD,
old+new-lib good, new+new-lib good, and the shipped resident-cube epoch (`427aee98`)
good again (`quant+rot` 31-33 s across its nine cells). Two standing consequences: a cross-binary bucket A/B
on this core must carry rule 121's same-binary control even when the touched code is
nowhere near the bucket — a rebuild can land a 2.2x swing on an untouched loop; and the
durable fix for a loop in this position is structural — a NEON `fcvtns` quantize
(bit-exact `lrintf`, ties to even) removes the per-element call the congruence rides on.
[HW + objdump, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chains `armAB2` and `armC`]

**THE QUANT LOOPS ARE NEON LANES; THE CONGRUENCE CLASS HAS NOTHING LEFT TO FORM ON
THERE.** `rk76_quant_act` (both branches) and `rocket_quant_wt_int8_had` quantize by
`fmul`, float pre-clamp to +/-127, `fcvtns` and saturating narrows, with amax by
`vabs`/`vmax` (`ggml-rocket` 6e212db). `fcvtns` rounds to nearest ties-to-even exactly
as `lrintf` does under the default rounding mode, and the pre-clamp makes the narrows
match the scalar's integer clamp on every value including the +/-127.5 ties, so the
lane form is bit-identical by construction; the `--chunks 4` equality cell prints PPL
8.7433 to every digit, per chunk identical to the prior epoch. Three w1p0-shape cells:
`quant+rot` 30.9/30.8/32.5 -> **19.56/19.73/19.63 s** a process, `wt_quant`
5.80/5.80/6.56 -> **0.976 s** in all three, the other frontend buckets within +-1%,
wall +1.9% (recorded, not a between-process measurement). A loop with no per-element
call gives the three-way congruence nothing to form on [expected over future epochs —
design intent, not yet a measurement]. The class persists for the loops the change did
not convert: `wt_rotate` moved +7.3% across this same epoch with untouched
instructions [hypothesis: the same class's small end].
[HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chain `rk3576-neonq8.sh`]

**WHAT THE REVERSED CAMPAIGN ESTABLISHED: THE BUCKETS ARE CELL-LOCKED AND POSITION-FREE.**
`sumabs` is 10.83-10.85 s a process with the knob on and 0 with it off, at every position on
both days; the knob moves `accounted`, `entry` and `calibrate` by its own size and nothing
else. **The `quant+rot` binary regression is real**: the same diff moved that bucket
**32.6-32.8 s -> 45.6-47.2 s a process (+43%)**, seven new-binary readings against three
old-binary ones across two days and three campaigns, on a
function it does not touch — `rk76_quant_act` reads the activation and no weight — so it
belongs to the binary: codegen, or the allocation layout moved by one added per-chunk vector,
on a part whose host loops are L1d-set sensitive. **The page-pad arm refuted the moved
allocation as the mechanism**: `padarm.patch` (page-pad the diff's one new `sumabs` vector,
sole change against the shipped source) read `quant+rot` **42.4 s** — 29% above the old
level — so the regression is not that allocation's alignment; the 7-10% it sits below the
new-binary band is 2-3x the bucket reproduction spread, so a minority layout slice is
plausible and unsettled. **The interior is now mapped** (`quantsplit.patch`, its own binary
epoch, equality-gated at `--chunks 4` PPL 8.7433): per process at ubatch 512, `q8` (the
quantize loop) **48%**, `rot` (Hadamard) **36%**, `max` 12%, `copy` 4%, the four summing to
`quant` within 0.14% — the stamps' own overhead, self-reported as the difference. The next
discriminators are the same stamps in the old binary, differenced per phase, and the
`perf stat` cache-counter pair. [HW, one cell each, 2026-08-13] **The same diff also DOUBLED
`wt_quant`** — the once-per-weight cache-insert quantization on the convert line, **5.8-6.6 s
-> 10.9-12.5 s a process across all fourteen logs** — outside the timed reps (first-touch),
so it costs process startup rather than `pp2048`. The convert line had printed that in every
log of the first campaign; it was read only when the reversal made it worth reading
(rule 119).
[HW, H96 MAX M9, fourteen profiled processes over three campaigns, 2026-08-13]

**SO STEP 4's CAP TRANSFERS — 39% predicted against 31.3% measured — AND ITS DENOMINATOR
DOES NOT.** That 39% is 39% of the ENTRY's own wall, measured at `K=N=2048` with no model
around it. Inside a model the entry is a fifth of the prefill wall, so the same term is
**4.5% of prefill**, not 39%. Read from the timed pass at ubatch 512:

| term | per converged call, M=512 | per converged call, M=2048 |
|---|---:|---:|
| `valloc` | 0.41 | 7.47 |
| `quant+rot` | 16.99 | 67.07 |
| `entry` | 36.40 | 99.00 |
| `dequant` | 2.01 | 8.01 |
| **offloaded path** | **55.81** | **181.55** |

At ubatch 512 the timed pass runs 664 converged calls, so the offloaded path is
**37.1 s of a 169.7 s prefill wall = 21.8%**. **The other 78% is outside the handler
entirely** — CPU attention with FA declined, the norms, rope, the scheduler and every
CPU-placed matmul — and the per-call buckets cannot see any of it, so that 78% is a
RESIDUAL and names no term. **[expected] for every candidate in that list.** The TOTAL is
no longer a residual: the inter-call gap accumulator (a diagnostic driver patch, dumped
as `gap=` on the frontend profile line) sums the exit->entry gaps between handler calls —
its first cell read **gap 554.2 s beside 149.5 s of buckets over 2656 calls (78.7%)**,
