closing the span — so the per-process wall term of the campaigns is now directly
observable per process, still without a name. [HW, one cell, 2026-08-13]

**WHAT THE `-ub 2048` LEVER IS ACTUALLY WORTH: 4.1% of prefill.** Per weight per 2048
tokens the offloaded path costs 4 x 55.81 = 223.2 ms at ubatch 512 against 1 x 181.55 at
ubatch 2048, a 18.7% cut; over 166 weights that is 37.05 s against 30.14 s on a 169.7 s
wall. The entry-side cut alone is 32.0% (145.6 -> 99.0 ms). **A two-point wall comparison
cannot resolve 4% against this arm's +-15% spread**, which is the whole reason the 0.2%
reading meant nothing either way.

**`valloc` IS NOT A TERM.** The three value-initialized scratch vectors were expected to be
non-trivial; they are **0.59% of the offloaded path** at `M`=512 (2.7% at `M`=2048, where
the cost is superlinear in `M` and consistent with fresh pages being faulted rather than
with the memset itself). Refuted.

#### The 78% residual is CPU matmul, and it is one declined op

The matmul profiler accounts the offloaded path and nothing else, so the rest of the wall was
a residual naming no term. `perf` attributes all of it. **[HW sweep]**, Qwen2.5-1.5B F16 on
llama.cpp b10356, `-p 2048 -ub 512 -r 3`, `ROCKET_INT8=1`, `ROCKET_FLASH_ATTN=0`,
`taskset -c 4-7 -t 4`, governor pinned, 803.0 s, 509,684 samples
(`perf record`, grouped by event group):

| term | share of the prefill wall |
|---|---:|
| CPU matmul — one tinyBLAS f16 kernel | **62.6%** |
| CPU flash attention — `ggml_compute_forward_flash_attn_ext_tiled` | **19.2%** |
| our host work, `libggml-rocket` user space | 8.0% |
| kernel — the rocket `alloc` / `submit` / `wait` ioctls | 5.3% |
| `lrintf32` and `memcpy` our quant path calls | 2.5% |
| ggml's f16 <-> f32 conversion | 1.1% |
| norms + elementwise + copies + **every thread barrier** | **0.7%** |
| page faults and model load | 0.34% |

**THE SHARES ABOVE ARE WALL SHARES, WHICH IS NOT WHAT `perf` REPORTS BY DEFAULT.** Read as
cycles, our host work is 3.56% and ggml's GEMM 91.67%; weighted by 1/parallelism it is 7.97%
and 82.52%. The 2.2x gap on our side is exactly the parallelism gap — ggml's GEMM runs on
four threads and the quant, the packs and the scans are single-threaded. `perf record
--latency` is required at RECORD time for the wall view; see rule 99.

**THE FRAMEWORK IS NOT THE RESIDUAL.** Norms, elementwise, copies and every thread barrier
together are **0.7%**, and the scheduler and graph allocation do not appear at all. Attention
is not it either at 19.2% — and it is a *fused* CPU kernel, so declining `FLASH_ATTN_EXT`
buys the CPU's tiled implementation rather than a naive path.

**IT IS ONE OP THE ENTRY DECLINES.** The entry refuses `K >= 6176` and the model's FFN
down-projection has `K`=8960, so all 28 of them run on the CPU: 28 of 196 projections, and
**29.4% of the model's projection MACs** (`down` is 13.76M of 46.8M MAC a layer a token,
tied with `gate` and `up`). **The CONTROL says that op is essentially the whole 62.6%** —
with `ROCKET_INT8=0`, every projection on the CPU, the same kernel is **91.4%** of a 736.9 s
wall over 8 forwards, covering 3.399 MAC-units a layer where the offloaded arm's CPU side
covers 1.0. At the control's own per-unit rate the 28 declined GEMMs are 24.8 s of the
offloaded arm's 50.2 s a forward; measured in that arm they are 31.4 s. **The term is
49-63% of the wall** — the 13-point spread is either cache interference from our
interleaved host work or the two other matmuls the entry does not take, and this instrument
cannot separate them because both are the same symbol.

| per MAC-unit (1 unit = one `gate`/`up`/`down` a layer = 1.973e11 MAC a 512-token forward) | s |
|---|---:|
| four A72 cores, ggml tinyBLAS f16 | **24.8** |
| the NPU offloaded path, converged | **3.86** |
| ratio | **6.4x** |

That 6.4x sits beside the sgemm A/B's **5.4x median** and must not be confused with its
**2.39x model-weighted** figure, which is an end-to-end cap over a whole GEMM mix and was
never a per-MAC rate (rule 100).

**THE PORT'S CONVERGED `pp2048` SPEEDUP IS 1.96x** — 10.91 t/s against the control's
5.57 t/s, same build, same threads, same governor, the W8A8 route the only difference. This
is the first end-to-end number on this route taken outside the calibration regime; the
`pp512` 1.02x / 2.01x pair is a **100% calibration** measurement (`pp512 -r 1` reports 332
of 332 calls as calibration forwards, confirmed directly).

### The denominator that was missing: 1.31x against ggml Q8_0 at `pp2048`

The 1.96x above is against an **F16** control, and the `pp512` **1.02x / 2.01x** pair is a
**100%-calibration** measurement — so until 2026-08-23 the route had never been priced at a
converged prefill length against the CPU arm it actually competes with. It now has been.
Qwen2.5-1.5B, `pp2048`, `-ub` 512, four threads on the A72s, governor `performance`, one job in
flight, three processes an arm interleaved in one boot:

| arm | GGUF | `pp2048` t/s (three processes) | mean | ratio |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|
| NPU, W8A8 | F16 | 13.37 / 13.34 / 13.38 | 13.36 | — |
| CPU only | Q8_0 | 10.31 / 9.95 / 10.27 | 10.18 | **1.31x** |
| CPU only | F16 | 5.64 | 5.64 | 2.37x |

**So the route is a real but modest win over the strongest CPU arm**, not the tie the `pp512`
figure implied and not the 2x the F16 control suggests. Per-pair ratios 1.30 / 1.34 / 1.30.
Quote the F16 ratio and this one together or neither: the first is what a drop-in user reads
because the port consumes an F16 GGUF, the second is what a user who already knows to quantize
gets.

**Run-to-run spread at `pp2048` is small** — +-0.02 t/s within a process and 3.5% across the
three Q8_0 processes — so the **+-15% spread recorded for this arm elsewhere in this file is a
`pp512` calibration-regime property, not a property of the route.** One process is a measurement
here; at `pp512` it is not.

**What it settles for the roadmap: the main prefill levers are spent.** The host K-split lifted
the `K >= 6176` refusal that was 62.6% of the wall, and resident weights landed +10%; a further
lever inside the entry is bounded by a path already winning 1.31x. [HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0,
llama.cpp b10558, `ggml-rocket` build of 2026-08-19, measured 2026-08-23]

**SO THE LARGEST PREFILL LEVER ON THIS PART IS THE `K >= 6176` REFUSAL, NOT ANYTHING INSIDE
THE ENTRY.** Every lever inside the entry is bounded by the offloaded path's 21.8% and the
largest of them — a BO pool for the per-tile allocations — caps at 4.2%. Routing `ffn_down`
onto the NPU at the offloaded path's own per-unit rate takes the wall from 50.2 s a forward
to 22.7 s: **2.21x on prefill**, and the port to about **4.3x** against pure CPU.
**That figure counts only what the change REMOVES, and with what it ADDS enumerated the
lever is 1.05-1.27x** — the measurement is the next section but one. What it prices
correctly is the SIZE of the term left on the CPU; what it omits is the whole cost of
putting it anywhere else.

The pure planner's map over (`M`, `K`, `N`) — `rocket_matmul_plan_int8_rk3576` costs no
device time (a pure-planner map, no device) — says the refusal boundary at `N`=1536 is
**exactly `K`=6176 and is independent of `M`** across {58, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048}, and
that the output tile narrows on the way there: `Nt` is 1536 up to `K`=4096, **768 at
`K`=4480-4608**, and **32 at `K`=5120-6144**, with `Mt` falling 128 -> 64 -> 32.

**AND THE ONE DEVICE PROBE OF A CHUNK SHAPE MEASURED THE PROBE, NOT THE ENTRY.**
`rk3576_matmul_probe ncap` at `M`=58 `K`=4480 `N`=1536 refuses with "no row window fits —
the weight slice alone exceeds the CBUF pool at ic=4480 k=1x1", which reads as a refutation
of the chunk route and is not one: `mm_int8_ex` builds a `conv_params_t` and drives the raw
conv path with a hand-picked plane, so what refused is the **conv row-window planner**, and
the matmul entry — which tiles — is a different planner that accepts the same cell. A probe's
refusal names its own clause.

#### What a host K-split of `ffn_down` costs, measured

The cap above is **refuted at its magnitude**: the lever is worth **1.05-1.27x on prefill**,
not 2.21x, and takes the port to **2.06-2.49x**, not 4.3x. Three measurements say so, and
each also corrects a piece of the framing the cap was written in.

**THE PREVIEW RECONCILES WITH THE PROFILER, AND WITH BOTH ITS COUNTERS.**
`rocket_matmul_plan_int8_rk3576`'s RETURN VALUE is `ceil(N/Nt) * ceil(ih/rows)` — N tiles
TIMES row tasks — while `r76_mm_int8`'s profiler increments `tiles` once per N TILE and
`submits` once per ROW TASK. Model-weighted over Qwen2.5-1.5B's six offloaded projections at
`M`=512 the preview gives **2.333 tiles and 9.333 submits a call** against the measured
**2.301 and 9.20** — the same 0.986 ratio on both, which is what a few zero-tile calls
(refusals, folded on every exit) give. So the preview's `Mt`/`Nt` ARE the entry's tiling; the
earlier "does not reconcile" was the return value read against the wrong counter.

**THE ENTRY'S COST IS LINEAR IN `K` AT FIXED (`M`, `N`), WITH A SMALL PER-CALL INTERCEPT.**
Warm repetitions of `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_perc` at `M`=512 `N`=1536, first rep
discarded, governor pinned to `performance`:

| `K` | 1536 | 1792 | 2048 | 2176 | 2304 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| ms a call | 17.0 | 19.0 | 20.9 | 23.5 | 24.6 |

**1.94 + 0.00983·`K` ms**, residuals under 0.6 ms over twelve cells on a 64 step. The
`K`-proportional term is **SPLIT-INVARIANT** — `sumabs`, the weight cube's `memset`, `packB`
and `packA` are all O(`N*K`) or O(`M*K`) per call and sum to the same total however `Ktot`
is cut, and the weight BO's `alloc` scales with it too — so a whole `K`=8960 `ffn_down` in
`n` chunks costs **1.94`n` + 88.1 ms** and the chunk COUNT is nearly free: **95.9 ms at
`n`=4**, corroborated at 92 ms of wall and 82 ms of accounted buckets by a four-chunk run.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 2026-08-11]

**SUMMING INDEPENDENTLY REQUANTIZED int8 PARTIALS IS FREE.** Four chunks (3 x 2304 + 2048),
each carrying its own two-pass bootstrap and its own frozen per-column scale, each
dequantized by that scale and summed in f32, score a composed **RMS relative error of
2.2385%** against the exact int32 product over the whole `Ktot`=8960 — against the
**single-chunk control's 2.2448%** on chunk 0 alone, a ratio of **0.997**, and against the
shipped route probe's 2.234% at `K`=1536 `N`=1536. The composition costs nothing measurable.
Pseudorandom Gaussian operands and no rotation, so this says nothing about a real activation
distribution and nothing about the rotation block size a real split would also change.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, one seed, 2026-08-11]

**SO THE CAP, WITH WHAT THE CHANGE ADDS ENUMERATED.** Per `down` call at `M`=512: entry
**96 ms** [measured], the frontend's rotate-and-quantize of a `K`=8960 activation **96-99 ms**
([expected] — 21.6 ns an element measured at `K`=1536, scaled by `K` and by the 8/9 butterfly
ratio of a 256-block rotation), and the dequantize plus f32 sum **~3 ms** [expected]. That is
**185-200 ms added** against the **221-280 ms** the CPU GEMM costs, so 112 such calls a
forward save 2.4-10.6 s of 50.2 — **1.05-1.27x**. **The lever is therefore 2-6x the best
in-entry lever (1E-4d's 4.2%), not 10-50x it**, and the two are now in the same class.
The largest single added term is the entry's own O(`N*K`) work, which a weight cache would
remove: the entry has none, so `sumabs`, the cube `memset` and `packB` are three passes over
`N*K` on every call, ~27 ms of the 96 at `Ktot`=8960.

**AND THE PROPOSED 2 x 4480 CUT IS THE WORST ONE ON THE PLANNER'S OWN MAP.** At `M`=512
`N`=1536 the plan reads 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 / **32** / 768 submits at `K` = 640 / 896-1280 /
1536-2560 / 2880-4096 / **4480-4608** / 5120-6144: `K`=4480 is the first cell where `Nt`
collapses 1536 -> 768, so two 4480 chunks plan **64 submits** where four chunks of ~2300
plan **16** at the same four N tiles. Any cut into the 2880-4096 band costs 8 submits a
chunk; the 1536-2560 band costs 4. [pure planner, no device]

#### The host K-split runs, and it costs the model 1.60x perplexity

The split is built and it works: `ggml-rocket.cpp` holds each RK3576 W8A8 weight as a list of
K-chunks, one whole W8A8 GEMM per chunk — its own rotation, its own per-output-channel weight
scale, its own two-pass bootstrap and its own frozen per-column output scale — with the
dequantized f32 partials summed. `K`=8960 cuts to 3 x 2304 + 2048 by the shipped preference.
It is **off by default**; `ROCKET_RK3576_KSPLIT=1` turns it on.

**IT IS 2.3x ON PREFILL, WHICH IS 1.9x WHAT THE CAP ABOVE PREDICTED.** `pp2048 -b 2048
-ub 2048 -r 3`, governor pinned to `performance`, split against the SAME build's
`ROCKET_RK3576_KSPLIT=0`, two process pairs interleaved:

| pass | nosplit t/s | split t/s | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 11.71 +- 1.59 | 26.42 +- 8.63 | **2.26** |
| 2 | 11.39 +- 1.51 | 27.01 +- 9.22 | **2.37** |

**Those means UNDERSTATE the converged lever, and the standard deviations say why.** At
`-ub 2048` a forward is one call per projection, so `NCAL`=2 spends the warmup and the FIRST
timed rep on calibration — and the split's calibration is four chunks x two bootstrap passes
where the control's is one x two. Reading the three reps back out of mean and stddev under
the one-slow-two-fast structure gives a converged **32.3 against 12.3 t/s, 2.64x**; that is
an inference from two summary statistics, not a per-rep reading, and the 2.26-2.37x means are
what is measured. **Do NOT multiply either into the port's recorded 1.96x** — that
denominator is a pure-CPU F16 arm which was not re-run at these settings, and it costs ~26
minutes a cell (rule 100).

Per-call, from the `ROCKET_MM_PROFILE` buckets differenced between the two profile cells over
their 27 extra claimed matmuls at `M`=2048, **0 redos over 39200 submits**:

| term | split - nosplit, per `ffn_down` call at `M`=2048 | quartered to `M`=512 | predicted at `M`=512 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `entry` | 285 ms | 71 | 96 +- 8 |
| `quant+rot` | 289 ms | 72 | 96-99 |
| `calibrate`, per calibration forward | 841 ms | 210 | ~197 |

The quartering assumes both terms are `M`-proportional, which the entry's is only in part
(`packA` is O(`M*K`), `sumabs` and `packB` are not) — so read the `M`=2048 column as the
measurement and the rest as arithmetic. Both compute terms came in **below** their bands, and
in the direction that makes the lever larger. `calibrate` is the term the cost cap contained
nothing about and it is the largest of the three: **122 s even without the split**, over two
forwards, on a run whose converged forward is 77 s.

It is off by default because of what it does to the output. Qwen2.5-1.5B, wikitext-2, 8 windows of 512,
one build, one boot, `llama-perplexity` (
`rk3576-ksplit-attrib.sh`):

| arm | what runs on the part | PPL | x CPU | x nosplit |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `cpu` | nothing (F16 CPU) | 9.7872 | 1.000 | |
| `nosplit` | q/k/v/o/gate/up at `K`=1536; `ffn_down` on the CPU | 9.8969 | 1.011 | 1.000 |
| `machinery` | the same set, but `K`=1536 forced to **512+512+512** | 9.8211 | 1.003 | **0.992** |
| `split` | + `ffn_down` as **3 x 2304 + 2048** | 15.8395 | 1.618 | **1.600** |
| `chunk1024` | + `ffn_down` as **8 x 1024 + 512 + 256** | 18.2188 | 1.861 | **1.841** |

`nosplit` reproduces the route's separately recorded 1.011x, which is the harness control.

**THE SPLIT MACHINERY IS EXACT, AND THAT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The `machinery`
arm leaves `ffn_down` on the CPU — so its offloaded SET is identical to `nosplit` — and cuts
only the `K`=1536 projections. At `K`=1536 the block-diagonal fallback is `H_512` over three
aligned blocks, which is bit-identical to what three 512-chunks each take, so the rotation is
held fixed and the only things that move are the slicing, the per-chunk activation scale and
the summed partials. It comes back **0.992x** — slightly better than unsplit, because each
chunk's per-tensor activation scale now covers a narrower slice. So the 1.600x is not a
defect in the cut; it is what happens when `ffn_down` reaches the route.

**AND IT IS NOT THE FROZEN OUTPUT SCALE, IN EITHER DIRECTION.** The entry's own saturation
report does not fire on either split arm — the rocket channel is confirmed reaching stderr in
the same logs — so the per-column scale is not being outrun. That excludes only the
UNDER-estimation direction, and the report's threshold is **0.1% of the surface**, not zero,
so "does not fire" means "under a tenth of a percent". The other direction was then measured:
cutting `CALSAFE` 3.0 -> 1.2, so a partial spends 106 int8 codes instead of 42, makes the
model **WORSE** (15.84 -> 17.99) and starts the report firing at 0.105-0.225% on four weights.
The 3x headroom is not wasted resolution; buying resolution back costs more than it returns.

#### The chunking is the accuracy knob, and the rotation's block width is why

The chunk depths are a bigger lever on this model's output than the whole 1.60x they were
supposed to be a detail of. Same `K`=8960, same offloaded set, same build, same boot, same
eight windows — only `ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK` moves:

| arm | `ffn_down` cut as | chunks | weighted `H` block width | PPL | x nosplit | windows worse than `split` |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `k2048` | 4x2048 + 512 + 256 | 6 | 1909 | **14.4401** | 1.459 | 3 of 8 |
| `split` (shipped) | 3x2304 + 2048 | 4 | 666 | 15.8395 | 1.600 | — |
| `k6144` | 6144 + 2304 + 512 | 3 | 1499 | 17.9495 | 1.814 | 6 of 8 |
| `chunk1024` | 8x1024 + 512 + 256 | 10 | 973 | 18.2188 | 1.841 | — |
| `k1792` | 5x1792 | 5 | 256 | 21.8054 | 2.203 | **8 of 8** |
| `k4096` | 4096 + 4096 + 512 + 256 | 4 | 3781 | 22.0116 | 2.224 | **8 of 8** |
| `k1280` | 7x1280 | 7 | 256 | 24.3497 | 2.460 | **8 of 8** |

The rotation is block-diagonal `H_b` with `b` the largest power of two dividing the chunk's
`K` (`rk_hadamard_rotate_blocks`), so a chunk depth chooses how far an outlier is smeared —
`K`=2048 gets a full 2048-wide FWHT and `K`=1792 gets `H_256` seven times over.

**"MORE CHUNKS IS WORSE, MONOTONE AND STEEP" IS REFUTED AS STATED, AND IT WAS A CONFOUND.**
`k2048` cuts `ffn_down` into **six** chunks and scores **better** than the shipped four;
`k1792` cuts it into five and scores decisively worse. What the earlier `chunk1024` cell
varied was not the count — cutting a `K` like 8960 into more pieces at non-power-of-two
depths also narrows every piece's rotation, and the two moved together. Held at a fixed
`b`=256 the count term is real and much smaller: 5 chunks 21.81 against 7 chunks 24.35.

**AND THE ROTATION WIDTH DOES NOT ORDER THE SET EITHER.** `k4096` has the widest rotation
reachable — 91% of `K` under a full `H_4096`, every chunk a power of two, the same chunk count
as the shipped arm — and it is **22.01**, 8 of 8 windows worse and level with the narrowest
arm measured. `k6144` has the FEWEST chunks and twice the shipped arm's weighted width and is
2.11 PPL worse. Any model built on width and count alone fails on both rows.

**WHAT IS CLEAN IS AN INTERIOR OPTIMUM IN THE CHUNK DEPTH.** Three of the arms are pure
powers of two, so every chunk takes a full-width FWHT and the rotation construction is held
constant across them; the only thing that moves is the depth:

| pure-pow2 depth | cut | chunks | PPL |
|---:|---|---:|---:|
| 1024 | 8x1024 + 512 + 256 | 10 | 18.2188 |
| **2048** | 4x2048 + 512 + 256 | 6 | **14.4401** |
| 4096 | 2x4096 + 512 + 256 | 4 | 22.0116 |

**Both directions off 2048 are worse**, and **a one-sided search on this axis would have
found a monotone story in whichever direction it started.** Every cell in this table is a
per-TENSOR activation scale, which is the whole of the effect: re-run with a per-ROW scale the
same three chunkings land within 0.04 PPL of each other and of `nosplit` (below), so the
optimum is a property of the scale's granularity and not of the depth.

**READ THE PAIRED PER-WINDOW EVIDENCE, NOT ONLY THE HEADLINE PPL.** These arms are
deterministic — both controls reproduce across sessions and boots to four decimals, so the
reported `+-0.6` to `+-1.04` is the spread of one arm's own windows and NOT run-to-run noise.
The sample is therefore the eight windows, and a paired-window reader backs the
per-window mean NLL out of the running estimate to compare them pairwise. Only the two narrow
arms separate cleanly: `k1792` +0.3197 +- 0.0813 and `k1280` +0.4300 +- 0.1057 of mean NLL,
both 8 of 8 windows worse. `k4096` is +0.3291 +- 0.0804 at 8 of 8. The +-1.4 PPL moves —
`k2048` -0.0925 +- 0.0566 at 3 of 8 windows, `k6144` +0.1251 +- 0.0604 at 6 of 8, `CALSAFE`=1.2
+0.1270 +- 0.0793 at 5 of 8 — are 1.6 to 2.1 standard errors and are **not** established at
eight windows. **A headline PPL that moves 1.4 on this harness can rest on three windows**, so
`k2048`'s 1.459x is the best cell measured and not yet a better configuration.

#### Per-ROW activation scales close it

**The term is the per-tensor INPUT scale after all, and giving each ROW its own scale takes
`ffn_down` on the split route to `nosplit` quality.** `ROCKET_RK3576_AROW=1` scores **9.9071**
against the same binary's shipped-path **15.9894** — **0 of 8 windows worse, a paired -0.4787
+- 0.1045 of mean NLL** — which is **1.0010x** of the `nosplit` control's 9.8969 and 1.012x of
the CPU F16 arm. It reproduces to four decimals across builds and boots (9.9348 twice, 9.9071
on the other build). It is not a fallback: the arm runs at **67.78 s per pass**, the split
arm's own speed, against `nosplit`'s 80.29, so `ffn_down` is on the part throughout.

It is free at this interface and needs no library change. The entry takes A **already
quantized** and its `scale` parameter is the OUTPUT's, so a per-row activation scale never
crosses the ioctl — the rows are each normalized to +-127 before the submit and the row factor
is applied in the host dequantize. **This is a rival to the recorded per-axis-input-scales
result** (better on every per-GEMM norm, 38x worse end to end on one model of two): that one
was measured on `K`=1536 projections and this is `ffn_down`, and rule 95's own conclusion was
that a single-model reading is model-specific. **Neither result bounds the other, and neither
bounds a second model.**

**A gratuitous fp32 re-association in the dequantize moved the shipped arm 15.8395 ->
15.9894.** Folding `a_scale` into the per-column factor once, as the path shipped, is not the
same float as multiplying it per element. At 0.4 of a paired standard error that is not an
accuracy change, but it costs the shipped path its bit-for-bit reproducibility across builds,
which is the property every arm on this route is read against — so the per-tensor case keeps
the original association and the per-row case is a separate branch.

It does not say the split is the wrong mechanism — the mechanism is fast and exact, and the
op it unlocks needed an activation quantization the route was not giving it.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### The chunk depth is not an accuracy knob once the scale is per row

**Re-running the depth ladder with `ROCKET_RK3576_AROW=1` collapses a 9.9 PPL spread to
0.04.** The three cells hold the chunking fixed against the per-tensor table above and change
only the activation scale's granularity:

| cut | chunks | per-TENSOR scale | per-ROW scale | paired dNLL vs `nosplit` | windows worse |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| 3x2304 + 2048 (shipped) | 4 | 15.8395 | **9.9348** | +0.0038 +- 0.0048 | 5 of 8 |
| 4x2048 + 512 + 256 | 6 | 14.4401 | **9.8944** | -0.0003 +- 0.0045 | 6 of 8 |
| 7x1280 | 7 | 24.3497 | **9.9012** | +0.0004 +- 0.0045 | 5 of 8 |

Control `nosplit` 9.8969. All three per-row cells sit inside **one standard error** of it and
none is worse on more than 6 of 8 windows; the same three chunkings per-tensor are +0.4703,
+0.3778 and +0.9003 at **8 of 8**. Pairwise against each other the three per-row arms are
within 1.1 se (4 of 8 worse each way), so **they are one cell, not a ladder.**

**This identifies the term the depth was trading.** Two candidates were on the table and
neither had been separated: the per-tensor scale's cross-ROW reach — a chunk's divisor is a
`max` over `M*K` rotated values, so one outlier row sets the resolution for every other row of
that chunk — and the number of independently requantized PARTIALS, which is an output-side
count no input scale touches. The first accounts for essentially all of it, and **the second
is bounded at <= 0.04 PPL over 4 to 7 chunks**, which is to say it is not a term. The interior
optimum at 2048 is a property of the per-tensor arm and does not survive the fix.

Consequence for the route: **`ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK` is a COST knob and not an accuracy one**,
so a chunk size is chosen on the entry's cost law and the stall map alone. What this does not
bound is a chunking far outside 4-7 pieces, and it says nothing about `K` other than 8960.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### A second model: the split costs 6.77x there and the per-row scale still closes it

SmolLM2-1.7B is the corpus's other ~1.5B member and the one that has already disagreed with
Qwen2.5-1.5B on a term's size by 9x and by 38x. Its `ffn_down` is `K`=8192, which the greedy
cuts **3x2304 + 1024 + 256** — five chunks and a different tail from Qwen's four:

| arm | PPL | x `nosplit` | paired dNLL | windows worse |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `nosplit` | 10.3436 | — | — | — |
| `split` | **70.0420** | **6.77** | +1.9127 +- 0.6832 | 8 of 8 |
| `split` + `AROW` | **10.3354** | **0.9992** | -0.0008 +- 0.0043 | **3 of 8** |

**The per-tensor split hurts this model 4.2x harder than it hurts Qwen** — 6.77x against
1.600x — and the saturation report fires at **15.417%** of the surface on `blk.7.ffn_down`,
where Qwen's worst arm reached 0.225%. **The per-row scale takes it back to indistinguishable
from `nosplit`, on the wrong side of zero and worse on only 3 of 8 windows.** So the fix is
not Qwen-specific, and rule 95's coin flip — per-axis input scales being 38x worse end to end
on one model of two — does not reach this interface: that result was measured on `K`=1536
projections under a different quantization of the output, and the two do not bound each other.

Both models therefore say the same thing about the mechanism: **what the K-split was costing
was the per-TENSOR activation scale, and nothing else measured here.**
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### A third model separates the disagreement from the geometry, and it is the weights

The two models above differ in both their pretraining and their contraction geometry, so
nothing in that pair says which the 4.2x came from. **Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct carries
SmolLM2-1.7B's K set exactly** — `n_embd` 2048, `n_ff` 8192, read off the GGUF headers
against Qwen2.5-1.5B's 1536/8960 — from a third pretraining family, with 16 blocks against
24 and GQA against MHA. Holding the geometry and therefore the K-split's own cut fixed
against one recorded model makes it a discriminator rather than a third data point.

Four cells at `--chunks 64` on `wiki-test-full.txt`, `-ub 512`, one process each:

| arm | what runs on the part | PPL, 64 windows | x `nosplit` |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| `nosplit` | q/k/v/o/gate/up at `K`=2048; `ffn_down` on the CPU | 15.3461 | 1.000 |
| `ctl` = `split` + `AROW` | + `ffn_down` cut, per-ROW activation scale | 15.4192 | 1.0048 |
| `ncal1` | `ctl` at `NCAL`=1 | 15.5343 | 1.0123 |
| `split` | + `ffn_down` cut, per-TENSOR scale | **37.2952** | **2.4303** |

**The per-tensor split's damage is a property of the weights, not of the cut.** Depth-matched
at the 8 windows the other two ratios were measured at, this model reads **2.0100x** — 1.26x
from Qwen's 1.600x and **3.37x from SmolLM2's 6.772x** — while running SmolLM2's identical
`K`=8192 cut. The whole-offload cost says the same thing from an independent contrast:
`ctl` against `nosplit` is **+0.476%** (paired **+0.0048 +/- 0.0021** of mean NLL), which sits
with Qwen's +0.567% and not with SmolLM2's -0.079%.

**The split's damage grows with depth**, 2.0100x / 2.3434x / 2.4303x over 8 / 29 / 64
windows, so the two recorded 8-window ratios are lower bounds on their own depth-64 values
and the disagreement's magnitude at depth is unmeasured [the direction is what the geometry
refutation needs, and it survives depth-matching]. The whole-offload cost wanders the same
way — +0.863% / +0.123% / +0.476% at the same three depths — which is one more reading of
the 8-window instrument being inadequate on this route.

**`NCAL`=1's converged-window cost is small, positive and real, and the two-model
indistinguishability was a power artefact.** Over windows 3-64 this model puts `ncal1` at
**+0.0074 +/- 0.0025 se** above `ctl` — **3.0 se from zero**, 41 of 62 windows worse, sign
test two-sided **p = 0.0151**. Qwen separated the same term at 0.56 se and SmolLM2 at 0.55 se,
which is what "does not reach either model" was reporting. Depth-matched the three agree —
0.69 se from Qwen at `d`=8, 1.45 se from SmolLM2 at `d`=29 — and pooled by inverse variance at
each model's deepest cell they are homogeneous (**Q = 1.360 on 2 df**) at
**+0.00564 +/- 0.00199 nats = +0.566% of perplexity, 2.83 se from zero**.

Scope: one corpus, one `-ub`, a first prompt only, no decode, and the `K` set {2048, 8192}
alone. This model is instruction-tuned where SmolLM2 is a base checkpoint, and these cells
cannot separate that from family.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, 2026-08-20]

#### The perplexity instrument: the text is the sample, and its resolution is computable

Every cell on this route is deterministic to four decimals — a re-run on a rebuilt `.so`
reproduces its predecessor's cumulative sequence byte-for-byte — so a headline's uncertainty is
entirely **which windows the text contains**, never the measurement's (rule 104). The sample is
the windows, and the instrument's resolution follows from two measured numbers.

**The text.** `wiki-test-full.txt` is `grep -v "^ *$"` over wikitext-2-raw's `wiki.test.raw`:
1287656 bytes, 2891 lines, md5 `3ce70e93`, **298938 tokens under Qwen2.5's tokenizer -> 583
windows** at `n_ctx`=512. `wiki-test-small.txt` (md5 `e454e460`) is exactly its first **60190
bytes**, which caps it at **29** windows. Because the small file is a byte-prefix, the deeper
text is a strict **extension**: window *i* covers tokens `[i*512, (i+1)*512)` taken from the
start, the seam at byte 60190 falls at token ~15000 against window 29 ending at 14848, and
`--chunks` is therefore the depth dial with every recorded cell preserved as a prefix. Measured,
not assumed: a `--chunks 64` control cell on the full text reproduces all 29 recorded cumulative
values of the 29-window control byte-for-byte, ending [29]**8.5745**.

**The spread.** The paired per-window difference on this route has sd **0.01734 nats**, now
measured over 64 windows rather than 29 (the first 29 give 0.01853, the 35 beyond them 0.01655 —
homogeneous, so no phase correction applies past the `NCAL` calibration windows). Resolution is
`2*sd/sqrt(n)`:

| windows | 2-se floor (nats) | 2-se floor (PPL) | cost, Qwen, split on |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 29 | 0.0064 | 0.65% | 11 min |
| **64** | **0.0043** | **0.43%** | **21 min** |
| 128 | 0.0031 | 0.31% | 42 min |
| 256 | 0.0022 | 0.22% | 84 min |
| 583 (the whole text) | 0.0014 | 0.14% | ~3.2 h |

**So the corpus has a floor, and two live questions sit under it.** Establishing an effect of
size *d* at 2 se needs `(2*sd/d)^2` windows: additivity's 0.0057 needs 38 and the regime-transfer
DiD's 0.0051 needs 47, both reachable — but the **measured** `AROW`-scoping recovery of 0.00127
needs **746**, against the 583 the whole of wikitext-2 test affords. That effect cannot be
established against zero on this corpus at any depth; deciding its sign needs a different
corpus, not a longer run. State which of the two a question is before spending board time on it.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-13]

#### `AROW` is a GLOBAL dial, and off the split route it is a net loss of 0.415%

`ROCKET_RK3576_AROW` is read once per context and handed to `rk76_quant_act` and
`rk76_dequant` on **every** offloaded GEMM. The K-split does not scope it — the split only
decides which GEMMs are offloaded at all. So a cell that turns the split off still carries the
dial unless the dial is turned off too, and no arm before this one separated them.

The site map, read off the `[rk3576-cache]` line the driver already prints (`ROCKET_DEBUG=1`
plus `llama-perplexity -v`; the line goes through `GGML_LOG_DEBUG` and lands on **stdout**):
at `-ub` 512 Qwen2.5-1.5B offloads **197** GEMM sites under `KSPLIT`=1 — **169** taken whole
at K=1536 in one chunk, and **28** cut at K=8960 into `2304+2304+2304+2048`, one per layer.
Under `KSPLIT`=0 those same 28 are refused by the entry's `K >= 6176` bound and run on the
CPU, so the two `nosplit` cells below differ on exactly the 169.

Three arms on Qwen2.5-1.5B, one build, one boot, paired window by window, at both depths (a
level is only comparable within a depth; the paired difference is the quantity):

| arm | `KSPLIT` | `AROW` | PPL @29 | PPL @64 | against the row above, @64 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `nosplit_a0` | 0 | 0 | 8.5236 | 9.3653 | — |
| `nosplit` | 0 | 1 | 8.5723 | 9.4042 | **+0.415%**, paired +0.00415 +/- 0.00208 (1.99 se) |
| `ctl` | 1 | 1 | 8.5745 | 9.4184 | **+0.151%**, paired +0.00151 +/- 0.00197 (0.77 se) |

**Only the aggregate is established, and the decomposition is not.** The whole offload feature
— `ctl` against `nosplit_a0`, both dial and split — costs **+0.567%, paired +0.00565 +/-
0.00204 (2.77 se)**, and that figure is stable across depths (+0.597% at 29). Neither of the
two terms it splits into reaches 2 se on its own. **Do not quote a ratio between them**: at 29
windows they read 0.571% against 0.026% and the ratio was carried as "the dial costs 22x the
split"; at 64 they read 0.415% against 0.151% and the same ratio is **2.7x**. The denominator
was never distinguishable from zero, so the ratio was never a measurement: a ratio whose
denominator has not been separated from zero carries no information about its size.

What does survive is the dial's **regime-dependent sign**, which is a large effect and not
statistical: with the split ON a per-tensor activation scale is catastrophic (Qwen 15.8395
against 9.9348), so `AROW` is mandatory there; with the split OFF it is a loss on every site
that never needed it. That is the same direction rule 95 records for per-axis input scales end
to end, at a different interface.

**The consequence for the recorded 8-window numbers**: the pair quoted as the K-split
feature's accuracy cost, `nosplit` 9.8969 against `arow` 9.9348 (**+0.38%**), is the dial plus
the split rather than the split, which is why it is 15x the split's own 8-window figure.

The build term on this path is exactly zero, measured three times: `nosplit_a0` reproduces the
earlier session's `nosplit` cell byte-for-byte on all eight of its cumulative values, and on
the deeper text both `ctl_L` and `nosplit_a0_L` reproduce their recorded 29-window predecessors
byte-for-byte on all 29. The 0.28% separating the two recorded `arow` levels is therefore
confined to code only the AROW/split route executes.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-13]

#### Scoping the dial to the split's own weights recovers at most 0.43%, not the 0.571% it costs off the route

`ROCKET_RK3576_AROW=2` keeps the per-row activation scale only where the K-split cut the
weight (`w.ch.size() > 1`). It needs no second activation quantization: the decision is a
property of the WEIGHT — every chunk in one call belongs to one weight — and the chunk loop
already quantizes each chunk's slab separately with its own scale, so it is one boolean at
three call sites. The calibration forwards take the same flag, so the frozen scales match the
mode, and the chunk map is unchanged (169 x 1 + 28 x 4).

| arm | windows | `KSPLIT` | `AROW` | PPL | against `ctl` |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `ctl2` | 29 | 1 | 1 (all 197) | **8.5745** | — (reproduces `ctl` byte-for-byte on all 29) |
| `arow2` | 29 | 1 | 2 (28 only) | **8.5690** | **-0.064%**, paired -0.0006 +/- 0.0034 (0.19 se) |
| `ctl_L` | **64** | 1 | 1 (all 197) | **9.4184** | — (reproduces `ctl` byte-for-byte on its first 29) |
| `arow2_L` | **64** | 1 | 2 (28 only) | **9.4064** | **-0.127%**, paired **-0.00127 +/- 0.00217** (0.59 se) |

A 64-window level is not comparable to a 29-window one — only the paired difference is. The
deeper cells split as 1-29 at -0.00064 +/- 0.00344 (reproducing the shallow pair to the digit)
and 30-64 at -0.00180 +/- 0.00280, so the effect is homogeneous across the text and the deeper
windows do not carry it.

**At 64 windows additivity is refuted and the null is not.** The 2-se interval on the recovery is
**[-0.0056, +0.0031]**: it excludes the -0.0057 an additive decomposition predicts (**2.04 se**,
just past the floor) and comfortably contains zero (0.59 se). So the honest statement is that the
scoping is worth **somewhere between nothing and about a fifth of what the dial costs off the
route** — the +0.571% is ruled out, and no depth reachable on this corpus can separate 0.127%
from 0 (746 windows needed, 583 available).

**The dial's +0.571% does not transfer, and the reason is structural rather than statistical.**
Isolating the dial required turning the split OFF, which moves the 28 `ffn_down` to the CPU in
f32 — so the +0.571% is the dial's cost in a regime the scoping does not ship in. The
reachability argument was exact (the same 169 sites, by readout) and the magnitude still moved
**8.9x**. The `(KSPLIT, AROW)` corners do not compose: the fourth one, `KSPLIT`=1 `AROW`=0, is
catastrophic at 15.84 because it disarms the split sites too, so the scoped mode is a fifth
cell and cannot be interpolated: two dials that each look additive alone need their fourth
corner measured before either is described as composing with the other.

**What this arm cannot decide.** Whether the scoping is worth anything at all: 0.59 se from
zero, and out of reach of the corpus. One model, one `-ub`, a first prompt only, nothing about
decode. Shipping the mode is a maintainer decision, and the accuracy case for it is now bounded
above by 0.43% rather than argued from 0.571%.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-13]

#### `AROW` costs 8.5% of the dequantize and nothing else, which is ~0.5% of a forward

The lever is priced at the frontend profiler's per-call buckets, not at the wall. Six
interleaved processes — `split` / `arow` / `split` / `arow` / `split` / `arow` after a discarded
warm-up process, governor pinned to `performance`, `taskset -c 4-7`, Qwen2.5-1.5B `pp2048
-ub 2048`, 772 calls a process of which 386 are calibration forwards, 7 of 7 rc=0, **0 redos**,
backstop delta **0**, zero `WARNING`s. Per-call ms, bucket total over `calls`:

| bucket | `split` mean | `arow` mean | delta | 9 pairings | control-to-control spread |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `valloc` | 2.23 | 2.23 | -0.02% | -0.41 .. +0.41 | 0.35% |
| `quant+rot` | 82.62 | 83.45 | +1.00% | -0.70 .. +3.15 | **0.82%** |
| `calibrate` | 220.33 | 224.12 | +1.72% | -0.73 .. +5.11 | 0.63% |
| `entry` | 61.68 | 61.45 | -0.37% | -9.54 .. +11.33 | **9.38%** |
| `dequant` | 9.06 | 9.83 | **+8.50%** | +8.10 .. +8.92 | **0.57%** |
| `quant+rot` + `dequant` | 91.69 | 93.29 | **+1.75%** | +0.18 .. +3.72 | 0.80% |

**The whole price is in `dequant` and it is +8.50%**, nine pairings inside +8.10..+8.92 — about
15x that bucket's own control spread, and the tightest cell on this route. It is the per-row
branch's one extra f32 multiply per output element per chunk, which the per-tensor branch hoists
into `f[n]`. Net: **+1.60 ms a call, 193 calls a forward = +0.31 s against a converged forward of
62-67 s on the same processes, ~0.5%.**

**The per-tensor branch's second flat pass over the rotated activation costs nothing measurable,
and that refutes the obvious mechanism.** `rk76_quant_act`'s per-tensor branch writes `rot` in
the row loop and then makes a SECOND flat pass over `rot[M*K]` to quantize — 73.4 MB re-read per
tensor per call at `M`=2048 `K`=8960, **2.05 GB a forward** — which the per-row branch never
makes, quantizing each row while its 9.2 KB is still in a 32 KiB L1d. Removing it moves
`quant+rot` by **+1.00%**, i.e. under 0.6 ms of an 82.6 ms/call bucket, with pairings spanning
zero. So that loop was never bandwidth-bound: a byte count is not a cost, and 2 GB a forward of
streaming re-read is invisible next to whatever `quant+rot` actually spends its time on
(`rk76_rotate` is the candidate and is unmeasured) **[hypothesis]**.

**The buckets are a 5x tighter instrument than the wall on the terms this lever touches, and the
buckets that are NOT tight say where this route's process-to-process variance lives.** Over the
three identical `split` processes the host arithmetic buckets spread **0.35-0.82%**, against the
wall's 4.1% on the same processes. `entry` spreads **9.38%** (12.52% on the `arow` arm), and the
matmul profile decomposes that: over all six processes `packA` runs **13295-24188 ms (+82%)** and
`read` **19338-24581 ms (+27%)**, while `wait` holds **35626-35883 ms (0.7%)** and `submit`,
`packB`, `sumabs`, `coeff`, `gen`, `alloc` and `stamp` all hold inside 1.5%, at an identical
2940 tiles / 47040 submits. **The device is not the variance — the host's activation pack and
output readback are.** Neither is arm-correlated (both arms span the same `packA` range), and the
last two processes of the session carry the two highest values, so it drifts within a session:
**the interleave is what protects a measurement here**, and a blocked three-then-three design
would have taken up to 50% of `packA`'s range as signal.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### A `pp2048` wall cannot resolve a term of this size, and its spread is not a constant

A `llama-bench` `pp2048 -ub 2048` pair cannot price this lever, because the control arm's own
process-to-process spread is larger than anything the lever could do. Five processes, governor
pinned to `performance`, `taskset -c 4-7`, thermals 47-51 C, backstop delta 0:

| process | arm | converged s/forward | t/s |
|---:|---|---:|---:|
| 1 | `split` (discarded warm-up process) | 61.87 | 33.10 |
| 2 | `split` | 84.32 | 24.29 |
| 3 | `split` + `AROW` | 76.34 | 26.97 |
| 4 | `split` | 74.31 | 27.56 |
| 5 | `split` + `AROW` | 76.64 | 26.72 |

Reps 2-4 *within* each process agree to under 1% — 61.88/61.87/62.53, 84.56/84.32/84.85 — and
rep 1 is always ~2x the rest, which is `NCAL`=2 landing on the warm-up plus the first timed
rep exactly as recorded. Between processes the identical `split` arm reads **33.10, 24.29 and
27.56 t/s, a 36% spread**, and the two adjacent pairs disagree in SIGN: +11.0% for `AROW` on
one, -3.1% on the other. **The state is latched at process start**, since nothing drifts
inside a process; a 3.1 GB mmap and a second 3.4 GB model file competing for a 7.9 GB box's
page cache is the leading candidate and is untested.

**The 36% is not a constant of the instrument.** Six further processes on the same board, same
build, same pinning read converged `split` at 32.75/32.80, 32.31/32.54 and 31.51/31.46 t/s — a
**4.1%** spread — and `arow` at 30.64/30.60, 31.81/31.86 and 32.47/32.46. Those two arms differ
by **-1.8%**, the same SIGN the buckets give and 3.7x the magnitude, on an instrument whose
control spread is 2.2x the effect: the wall agrees and still cannot resolve it. What separates
the 36% session from the 4.1% one is that the former ran within the hour a 3.4 GB second model
was written to the box's 7.9 GB of RAM, which keeps the page-cache candidate alive and leaves it
**[hypothesis]** — one 36% observation is not a rate.

The operational rule survives the smaller spread: **no `pp2048` wall number on this route means
anything from fewer than three processes per arm**, and that reaches back to the K-split's own
recorded 2.26-2.37x, which was taken over two process pairs. To price a term inside the W8A8
handler, difference the frontend profiler's `quant+rot` and `dequant` buckets instead — they are
per call and hold to under 1% where the wall does not.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### An int8 matmul job can raise no completion, and the shape decides

Specific `K` make an otherwise ordinary int8-output matmul job **never report completion**:
`rocket` retires it at its 125 ms backstop (`job of 1 task(s) did not report completion in
125000 us; retiring it`), the output surface is untouched, the entry's sentinel guard sees a
task that wrote nothing, and the redo — after `rocket_rk3576_power_idle()` — succeeds. The
result stays CORRECT and the call runs **40-480x** slower: `K`=2240 costs **1028 ms** against
`K`=2304's 24.6 ms at the same plan, same `Mt`, same `Nt`, and `K`=4480 costs **8120 ms**.
Deterministic per shape, and every first attempt fails — 8 redos over 8 submits at `K`=2240,
64 over 64 at `K`=4480.

**It is not the unnamed-completion-class population.** `rocket_submit_matmul` passes
`job_flags = 0`, which is the CORRECT hint for an int8-output DPU convolution: the DPU does
raise a completion on this path, and `NO_DPU_DONE` / `PPU_DONE` describe other programs. So
the job is asking for the completion it should ask for and not getting it.

The map at `M`=512 `N`=1536, redos per submit, one process a cell:

| `K` | stalls |
|---|---|
| 1536, 1600, 1664, 1728, 1792, 1856, 1920, 1984, 2048, 2112, 2176 | none |
| **2208, 2240, 2272** | **every submit** |
| 2304, 2560, 2688, 2816, 2944, 3072, 3200, 3328, 3456, 3584, 3712, 3840, 3968, 4096 | none |
| **2432** | **every submit** |
| **4416, 4448, 4480, 4512, 4544** | **every submit** |

**Read this as a MAP with a step, not as bands with edges.** The walk is 128-wide from 2304
up and 32-wide only across 2176-2304 and 4416-4544, so a narrow stall region between the
clean samples would not have been seen, and the clean cells bound nothing between them. The
operational rule for any K-split is therefore that **a chunk `K` is validated by running it,
not by reading a band**: 2304 and 2048 are measured clean over 48 submits with 0 redos.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 2026-08-11]

Five further chunk depths were screened the same way before being used as arms, at `M`=512
`N`=1536, four warm reps each, first discarded: **768 (12-14 ms, plan 2), 1280 (16.7-16.9,
plan 2), 1792 (22.5-23.1, plan 4), 2048 (25.4-27.1, plan 4) and 2304 (27.9-29.7, plan 4)**
are all clean and all sit on the entry's linear cost law, with the backstop count unmoved
across the whole screen.

**`K`=6144 also computes — and it costs 166 ms, 2.7x what the linear law predicts.** The
single-task planner collapses the output tile there and plans **768 row tasks**, so the call
is submit-bound rather than contraction-bound; this is the same per-submit-cost-is-not-a-floor
effect the 32x-in-submits / 2.7x-in-wall result records, met from the other end. It is inside
the entry's `K < 6176` refusal by 32 and it does not wedge the part — the wedge that costs a
reboot is the int32 K-split ROUTE, not a single call at a large `K`. A three-chunk cut of
`K`=8960 (6144 + 2304 + 512) therefore costs about **201 ms a call against the shipped
four-chunk cut's ~109**, which makes it an accuracy probe and not a candidate default.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 2026-08-11]

#### Calibration is 58.6% of the frontend's accounted time, and 45% of it was one loop nest

`calibrate` is the largest bucket the RK3576 W8A8 frontend accounts for — **171.3 s a
process against 47.5 s of `entry`** on Qwen2.5-1.5B `pp2048 -ub 2048`, 772 calls of which
386 are calibration forwards. Its two halves separate cleanly, and the profiler now prints
the split directly: `calscan` is a **subset** of `calibrate` carrying the bootstrap's
per-column maximum scan alone.

| half | per process | per call | control-to-control spread over 3 processes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `calscan` — the per-column max scan | 78.9 s | 102.25 ms | **10.37%** |
| the rest — two device GEMMs a chunk plus the `N`-wide loops | 119.7 s | 119.71 ms | **0.96%** |

The device half is exactly the two extra passes and nothing else: 119.71 ms a calibrating
call against 59.55 ms of `entry` a converged one is **2.01x**, and the arithmetic over the
matmul profile's own call count agrees — 1644 device passes = 548 converged + 1096
bootstrap, and the `accounted` total's 33.3% converged share is 45.0 s against the
frontend's 47.5 s `entry`, a 5.5% per-call overhead gap. **A bootstrap pass costs what a
converged pass costs**; the calibration regime is two of them.

**The two spreads are the finding to carry forward.** The device-and-loops half holds to
**0.96%** across processes while the scan half spreads **10.37%** — so the noisy part of
this route's largest bucket is a host memory-access term, not the device. After the loop
swap below, `calibrate`'s own spread falls **5.20% -> 0.90%**: removing the term removed
the variance with it. [HW sweep, 2026-08-11]

#### The scan's cost was entirely its loop form: two bit-identical rewrites take it to 1.0% of calibration

`rk76_bootstrap_pass` takes each column's maximum. Written column-outermost —
`for n { for m { C8[m*N + n] } }` — it reads a row-major `M`x`N` int8 surface at a stride of
`N` bytes. Written `m`-outermost against an `N`-wide running max it reads the same surface
once, sequentially. The arithmetic is identical: a column saturates exactly when its largest
|code| reaches 127, so the flag falls out of the maximum, and `llama-perplexity --chunks 4`
returns **PPL 12.8615 +/- 1.17731 to every printed digit both ways** — which matters here
because a wrong colmax on this route computes a full, correctly sized, plausible surface.

Measured over three interleaved processes an arm, governor pinned, 7 of 7 rc=0, 0 redos,
backstop delta 0:

| bucket, per call | column-outer | row-major | delta | 9 pairings | own control spread |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `calscan` | 102.25 | 10.72 | **-89.52%** | -90.07 .. -89.02 | 10.37% |
| `calibrate` | 221.95 | 128.73 | **-42.00%** | -43.86 .. -40.41 | 5.20% |
| `entry` | 59.55 | 58.39 | -1.95% | -4.26 .. +1.38 | 2.54% |
| `quant+rot` | 78.99 | 79.04 | +0.06% | -3.95 .. +2.61 | 2.75% |
| `dequant` | 9.09 | 9.08 | -0.06% | -0.27 .. +0.16 | 0.14% |
| `valloc` | 2.22 | 2.21 | -0.10% | -0.29 .. +0.12 | 0.18% |

**At the wall it is 1.42x on the forward a user actually meets.** `llama-bench`'s per-rep
`samples_ns` separates the regimes by construction — at `NCAL`=2 the warm-up process's
forward and timed rep 1 are the calibrating ones — and rep 1 goes **140.02 -> 98.59 s**
(-41.43 s) while reps 2 and 3 move -2.9% and -2.8%, which is this instrument's own
process-to-process drift and not the lever. **A single-prompt prefill is 100% calibration
regime**, so 1.42x is what a first prompt gets; a converged prefill is unchanged.

**What was LEFT was the scan's scalar rate, and it is now spent too.** The row-major form
with an `int` accumulator measures **1.30 ns an element** on the device — a scalar rate,
because `if (v > mx[n]) mx[n] = v;` is a conditional store and does not vectorize. Written
unconditionally it vectorizes; held in a `uint8_t` instead of an `int` it vectorizes 16
elements at a time instead of 4. A standalone on the board
(a standalone micro, `M`=2048, best of 3) prices all four forms:

| ns/element, -O2 / -O3 | `N`=256 | `N`=1536 | `N`=8960 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| column-outer | 5.69 / 5.34 | **17.74 / 16.25** | 12.33 / 12.44 |
| row-major, conditional store, `int` | 1.76 / 1.33 | 1.73 / 1.38 | 1.74 / 1.32 |
| row-major, unconditional store, `int` | 0.41 / 0.35 | 0.41 / 0.52 | 0.43 / 0.35 |
| row-major, unconditional store, `uint8_t` (shipped) | **0.11 / 0.11** | 0.13 / 0.20 | 0.13 / 0.14 |

**The byte accumulator's win is LANE COUNT, not the accumulator's cache residency**, and the
`N` sweep is what says so: it is **flat at 2.4-3.7x over `rowmax_uncond` at every `N`**,
largest at `N`=256 where the `int` accumulator is 1 KB and could not be a residency problem
at all. A residency reading would need it to grow with `N` (35 KB at `N`=8960 is past this
part's 32 KiB L1d, 1 KB at 256 is not); a "narrowing buys nothing, the win was the vector
max" reading would need 1x. Both are refuted by the same three cells. The narrowing is
exact rather than an approximation: |code| over an int8 surface is 0-128, which a byte holds
including the `-128` rail, and 128 >= 127 saturates as before. [HW sweep, standalone, -O2
and -O3, 2026-08-11]

The byte form ships (`ROCKET_RK3576_CALSCAN` = 0 column-outer / 1 row-major `int` / 2
row-major byte, default **2**). Three interleaved processes an arm against `CALSCAN=1`,
governor pinned, 7 of 7 rc=0, 0 redos, backstop delta 0:

| bucket, per call | `int` accumulator | byte accumulator | delta | 9 pairings | own control spread |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `calscan` | 12.41 | **1.18** | **-90.51%** | -90.50 .. -90.52 | 0.05% |
| `calibrate` | 133.36 | 120.88 | -9.36% | -11.15 .. -6.97 | 1.72% |
| `cal_nonscan` (the device half) | 120.95 | 119.70 | -1.03% | -3.09 .. +1.68 | 1.90% |
| `entry` | 60.38 | 59.43 | -1.57% | -6.21 .. +3.66 | 4.60% |
| `quant+rot` | 79.17 | 79.76 | +0.74% | -0.62 .. +3.51 | 4.09% |
| `dequant` | 9.14 | 9.09 | -0.58% | -0.69 .. -0.47 | 0.16% |
| `valloc` | 2.21 | 2.21 | +0.10% | -0.18 .. +0.35 | 0.18% |

The measured **10.5x** sits inside the standalone's own 9.4-13.4x for the same two forms
(1.32-1.74 -> 0.13-0.14 ns an element), so the device is reaching the emitted code's rate.
The scan is **1.0% of `calibrate`** against 45% two sessions ago. The wall follows on
the calibrating rep alone: rep 1 **91.28 -> 86.34 s** (1.057x, all nine pairings negative,
magnitudes spanning -1.80 .. -8.23 against a 4.0% control spread), reps 2-3 -0.87% / -0.94%.
`llama-perplexity --chunks 4` returns **PPL 12.8615 +/- 1.17731** to every printed digit
under all three forms. **`dequant`'s -0.58% is unexplained** — no mechanism in this change
reaches it, it is 0.05 ms a call, and it is visible only because `dequant` resolves 0.16%.
[HW sweep, 2026-08-11]

**Read a bucket's LEVEL from the run that produced the delta, never from a stored one.**
`calscan` resolves 0.05% across three interleaved processes in one session and its level
moved **15.8%** between two sessions (10.72 -> 12.41) on a branch in which not one statement
changed — it was recompiled inside a function that had gained a second branch. `calibrate`
(128.73 -> 133.36) and `entry` (58.39 -> 60.38) moved with it. A tight within-run spread is
a property of the binary, not of the machine.

**The sub-mechanism is L1d set conflict, not TLB reach**, and the `N` sweep separates them
with opposite signs. At `M`=2048 a column walk over `N`=1536 spans 3.1 MB = 768 pages, which
fits this part's L2 TLB, while `N`=8960 spans 18.3 MB = 4480 pages, which does not — so a
TLB reading predicts `N`=1536 is the cheaper. It is the **more expensive**, 1.32x per
element. The set-conflict reading predicts that ordering: at 64-byte lines and 128 sets,
stride 1536 gives `gcd(24, 128)`=8 and so **16 of 128 sets**, against 32 for both 8960 and
256. `N`=256 confounds set count with a span that fits L2; the 1536-vs-8960 pair does not,
and it is the cell that decides. The row-major form is flat across all three `N`, as a
sequential pass should be. [HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-11]

#### Bootstrap pass one cannot be replaced by a host predictor: its error is BOUNDED, and that is the property, not its accuracy

A calibrating W8A8 call runs two whole GEMMs where a converged one runs one, and pass one
exists only to place pass two's scale. It reads back at `cbound` = 127/(128·Σ|qB|+1), the
analytic accumulator bound, so the obvious lever is to compute its answer on the host and
delete the GEMM. **Two host predictors were scored against the device's own answer over
1.50M columns — every column of all 193 offloaded weights of Qwen2.5-1.5B at `M`=2048,
`-ub` 2048, both calibration forwards — and both are refuted.**

The readout instrument is `ROCKET_RK3576_CALMAP=<file>`, which writes one row per column
per calibration forward: the analytic bound, pass one's estimate, pass two's refined one,
the saturation flag and both operands' second moments.

| over 1.50M columns | median | p99 | max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| analytic bound's overshoot `abound/est2` | **61.8x** | 1075x | 1.08e4x |
| absolute error of `est1` (pass one, a device GEMM) | 11.4% | 747% | 8367% |
| absolute error of `z·sqrt(Kc)·rms(qA)·rms(qB[n])`, one global `z` | 23.5% | 142% | 794% |
| absolute error of `abound/D`, one global `D` | 25.5% | 1531% | 1.62e4x |

Read as absolute error the second-moment predictor looks close — 23.5% against pass one's
11.4%, and better than pass one in the tail. **That statistic is the wrong one, because the
consumer's tolerance is asymmetric.** `sc2` = 127/(est·`BOOTMARGIN`), so an estimate that
sits ABOVE the truth costs pass two readback resolution and nothing else, while one that
sits more than `BOOTMARGIN`=1.5 below it saturates pass two, and the pipeline then keeps
`est·1.5` — an underestimate of the column maximum — as the answer.

Scored that way the three separate completely:

| replacement for pass one | columns saturating pass two | worst underestimate | pass two's readback, p50 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| **pass one as shipped** | **0.000%** | **1.488x** | 84 codes |
| `z·G0`, `z` at the log-mean | 12.35% | 342.6x | 84 |
| `z·G0`, `z` set to clear 99.9% of columns | 0.043% | 60.6x | 14.9 |
| `z·G0`, `z` set to clear every column | 0.000% | 1x | **0.245** |
| `abound/D`, `D` at the log-mean | 19.72% | 28.9x | 90.3 |
| `abound/D`, `D` set to clear every column | 0.000% | 1x | 3.12 |

**Pass one never sits more than 1.488x below the truth, and `BOOTMARGIN`=1.5 is exactly the
constant that covers it.** That is not a fitted margin, it is structural: pass one reads
back a real product at a scale that is a proven upper bound, its readback maximum is a small
integer (median **2 codes**, p90 3, p99 5, and **21.5%** of columns at the one-code floor),
and its whole error is that integer's rounding. A host predictor has no such floor, so
making it safe on the worst column costs every other column its resolution — the max-safe
second-moment fit puts **99.98%** of columns under 8 readback codes, against 0.83% today.
**A cheap estimator that is better on average and unbounded in the direction that hurts is
worse than an expensive one whose error is bounded by construction.**

The same readout closes the item's other two arms:

- **A chunk cannot inherit its neighbour's estimate.** Across the four chunks of one split
  `ffn_down`, the median column's `max_c/min_c` colmax ratio is **1.79x**, p10 1.30, p90
  11.0, p99 66.3. The code's own argument — each chunk's accumulator is over its own
  contraction — is confirmed by measurement.
- **The second calibration forward raises the stored colmax on 49.9% of columns**; 2.03% by
  more than 1.5x and **0.061% by more than 3.0x**, which is past the `CALSAFE`=3.0 headroom.
  That exposure is 87x the row subsample's and it is a **column** count — the columns that
  lose coverage clip only their top accumulator rows, and the perplexity arm below finds the
  loss unresolvable. Read this readout as the bound on what `NCAL`=1 risks, not as its cost.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-11. **What it cannot see**: perplexity, another model, another
`M`, and decode.]

#### What pass one may not need is all `M` rows, and the cap on that was low by 1.37x

Pass one's estimate is a maximum over `M` accumulator rows. A subsample keeps the property
the host predictors lack — it is still a readback of a real product at a proven upper bound,
so it is still floored by the same quantum — and undershoots only by the max-of-`M'` against
max-of-`M` gap. `ROCKET_RK3576_CALROWS` caps pass one's rows; pass two always runs at full `M`.

The activations are deterministic, so the full-`M` run's stored colmax is the reference the
subsample is trying to reproduce. **Score it on the stored colmax, over ALL columns**: a
subsample makes pass two saturate exactly on the columns where pass one came up short, so
scoring only the non-saturating ones drops the evidence.

| against the full-`M` run, 750080 columns | `M'`=512 | `M'`=128 |
|---|---:|---:|
| pass two saturates, of (column, forward) pairs | 8.04% | 19.82% |
| median stored colmax, arm/reference | 0.9911 | 0.9792 |
| columns under by more than 1.5x | 0.264% | 1.384% |
| columns under by more than `CALSAFE`=3.0 | **0.0007%** (5) | **0.0060%** (45) |
| worst underestimate | 6.094x | 6.094x |
| `calibrate` bucket | **-28.2%** | **-36.3%** |

At full `M` the saturation rate is 0.260%. The median column loses 0.9-2.1% of its colmax,
which `CALSAFE`=3.0 absorbs entirely; what it does not absorb is the worst column, at 6.094x
under. **Those columns are not what costs, and the table above scores the wrong surface** —
see below.

**The cost cap was 26.5% and the measurement is 36.3%, low by 1.37x for a named reason.**
The cap counted `alloc` and `stamp` as `M`-independent because the profile separates them
from the row-scaling terms; they are not, because the SUBMIT COUNT is `M`-dependent
(31360 -> 16660 at `M'`=128), and `stamp` alone moves -48.9%. [HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-11. **One process a cell with the
`CALMAP` instrument on** — the buckets exclude the dump but not its cache effects, so read
these as a bracket, not a price. **What it cannot see**: perplexity, decode, another model.]

#### What a row subsample costs is the CALIBRATION forwards' own output, and `BOOTMARGIN` buys it back

The frozen scale is not the consumer that a subsample hurts. Cutting pass one's rows and
measuring perplexity puts **all** of the cost on windows **1 and 2** — the `NCAL`=2
calibration forwards — and leaves windows 3-8, the only ones the frozen scale governs,
inside +/-0.042 nats with both signs at every row count tried.

The mechanism is that a calibrating call hands back **pass two's** surface, at
`sc2` = 127/(`est1`·`BOOTMARGIN`). A subsampled `est1` is low, so `sc2` is high and pass two
**clips**: 8.04% of columns at `M'`=`M`/4 and 19.82% at `M`/16, against 0.26% shipped. The
code's own comment — that surface "costs resolution and nothing else" — holds only while it
does not saturate. Raising `BOOTMARGIN` with the row cut restores it, and it is free at full
rows:

| Qwen2.5-1.5B, `--chunks 8`, `AROW`=1, K-split on | `BOOTMARGIN` 1.5 | 2.25 | 3.0 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| full rows | **9.9348** | 9.8884 | — |
| `CALROWS`=128 (`M`/4) | 10.0746 (+1.41%) | **9.8804** | — |
| `CALROWS`=32 (`M`/16) | 11.8374 (+19.15%) | — | 10.0345 (+1.00%) |

Paired per-window against the 9.9348 control: `CALROWS`=128 at 2.25 is **-0.0055 +/- 0.0069
(se)** and `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 alone is **-0.0047 +/- 0.0076**, so neither is resolvable and
neither should be read as an improvement. What IS resolved is the repair — windows 1 and 2
fall from +0.126/+0.065 to +0.030/+0.013 nats at `M`/4, and from +0.519/+0.900 to
+0.060/+0.058 at `M`/16. **Both of those paired figures are 8-window numbers and both are
window-set artefacts** — see the depth block below.

##### At depth the row cut cannot reach a converged window at all, and the 8-window figures above are an artefact

A 2x2 over the two dials at 62 converged windows, plus the shipped pair on two further
models, settles the question the table above could only pose. **The shipped pair costs no
model of three anything at depth.** `CALROWS`=128 + `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 reads **+0.0009 +/-
0.0020 se** over Llama-3.2-1B's windows 3-64 (0.45 se from zero, 28 of 62 worse, sign test
p = 0.53), **-0.0092 +/- 0.0035 se** over SmolLM2-1.7B's windows 3-29 — 0.91% **better**,
2.59 se, 21 of 27 better, p = 0.0059 — and **-0.0038 +/- 0.0034 se** over Qwen2.5-1.5B's
windows 3-29 (1.11 se from zero, 9 of 27 worse, p = 0.1221, headline 8.5745 -> 8.5569 =
-0.205%).

**Compare them at a MATCHED depth**, which is the only comparison a level admits: at d=29 the
three are **+0.0026 +/- 0.0030 / -0.0092 +/- 0.0035 / -0.0038 +/- 0.0034**. They are
**heterogeneous (Q = 6.665 on 2 df)**, so quote them separately; no pooled number is
defensible, unlike `NCAL`=1's. For an **unmeasured** model the number to carry is the
random-effects mean **-0.0028** with **tau = 0.0050** — tau fell from 0.0077 when the third
model landed between the first two. Heterogeneity here is a property of the first two
models rather than a measurement of the third: Q is 6.55 at its arithmetic minimum, with the
third model exactly at the pooled mean, and 7.3-10.6 anywhere else.

**The cut ALONE, at the shipped 1.5 margin, is null on the converged windows too** —
-0.0011 +/- 0.0022, 23+/39-, sign test p = 0.0559. So the frozen-scale clipping route that
the 8.04% pass-two saturation figure suggests **does not exist at a measurable size**, and
the +1.41% in the table is an aggregate over 8 windows rather than a converged cost. The
phase split carries the whole effect: the cut alone puts **+0.0908 and +0.1297 nats** on the
two calibration windows against -0.0011 on the 62 converged ones, and the raised margin
repairs window 1 by **11x** (+0.0908 -> +0.0083) and window 2 by 2.5x. The margin alone is
+0.0002 +/- 0.0024 converged and +0.0226 on window 2. **Both dials are calibrating-window
effects and `BOOTMARGIN` does exactly the job it was added for.**

**Eight windows is not a weak instrument on this route, it is a misleading one.** All five
contrasts move **0.005-0.011 nats** between d=8 and their deepest reading and **two flip
sign**: the three Llama contrasts read -0.0065 / -0.0060 / -0.0050 at d=8 against
+0.0009 / +0.0002 / -0.0011 at d=64, SmolLM2 reads +0.0017 at d=8 against -0.0092 at d=29,
and Qwen reads **-0.0144 +/- 0.0046** at d=8 against -0.0038 at d=29. **The d=8 -> d=29 step
is ~0.010 nats on three of three models — +0.0091, -0.0109, +0.0106 — agreeing to within 10%
in magnitude and not at all in sign.** A magnitude that reproducible with an unpredictable
sign is what an artefact of the first six converged windows looks like. Qwen's recorded
-0.0055 +/- 0.0069 sits 1.07 se from its own d=8 re-read, so it is consistent and there is no
cross-build discrepancy behind the difference.

**The calibration phase's damage splits differently per model, and only its SUM is stable.**
Window 1 / window 2 are +0.0083/+0.0528 (Llama), +0.0241/+0.0521 (SmolLM2) and
+0.0296/+0.0126 (Qwen) — the third model inverts the ordering. Either window alone ranges
3.6-4.2x across the three; their sum ranges **1.8x** (0.0611 / 0.0762 / 0.0422). Gate the
calibration phase on the sum; a threshold placed on window 2 from the first two models'
1.3% agreement fails on the third while the dial has plainly taken effect.

[HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-21. Eight cells, one `.so`
and one corpus md5-gated throughout and the model md5-gated on the last two, `-ub` 512 so
`CALROWS`=128 is exactly `M`/4, governor `ondemand`. **What it cannot see**: the two dials
SEPARATELY on any model but Llama — the 2x2 ran there only, so the other two models'
readings are about the shipped PAIR and cannot attribute to one dial — the
`BOOTMARGIN`x`CALROWS` interior, another `-ub`, and decode.]

**Two things this closes about instruments.** The aggregate perplexity and the paired test
disagree (+19.15% against 1.43 se) because 2 windows of 8 carry all of it — score the phase,
not the run. And the shipped saturation report is **silent through every cell**, because the
calibrating branch passes `NULL` for `sat_elems`: a monitor scoped to the converged phase
cannot see a defect confined to calibration.

So the lever is `CALROWS`=`M`/4 **with** `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 — two dials, never one.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-12. Same-build control run at both ends of the first arm and identical to every
printed digit. **What it cannot see**: one model, 8 windows of one text, one `-ub`, and
decode — the whole term lives in the calibration forwards, so a converged prefill never pays
it.]

**The two tables above are `M`=512 cells and the clipping figures beside them are `M`=2048
cells.** The accuracy costs come from `llama-perplexity -ub 512`, where `M`/4 is
`CALROWS`=128; the 0.26 / 8.04 / 19.82% clip fractions come from `llama-bench -p 2048
-ub 2048`, where `M`/4 is `CALROWS`=**512**. Both were written as a fraction of `M`, which
made four numbers from two experiments read as one experiment.

##### `CALROWS` is an absolute row count, so its damage is a ratio and the setting is a different cut at every ubatch

The dial never scales: `ggml-rocket.cpp` clamps it (`if (rk76_calrows() > 0 &&
rk76_calrows() < M) M1 = rk76_calrows();`), so `CALROWS`=128 is `M`/4 at `-ub` 512 and
`M`/16 at `-ub` 2048. Five `llama-perplexity --chunks 8` cells at `-c/-b/-ub` **2048** on
Qwen2.5-1.5B, scored on `Dcal` — the nats added over the two `NCAL`=2 calibrating windows,
which is where all of this dial's effect lives and which is depth-independent:

| `CALROWS` | ratio at `M`=2048 | `Dcal` | 8-window | converged 3-8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 512 | `M`/4 | +0.1276 | +1.27% | -0.0045 +/- 0.0046 |
| 128 | `M`/16 | +0.6761 | +8.73% | -0.0010 +/- 0.0036 |
| 32 | `M`/64 | +1.9985 | +28.56% | +0.0019 +/- 0.0013 |
| 128 + `BOOTMARGIN` 2.25 | `M`/16 | +0.1052 | +0.98% | -0.0045 +/- 0.0040 |

**The ratio governs and the absolute row count does not.** Against the `M`=512 anchors
(`CALROWS`=128 -> 0.1906, `CALROWS`=32 -> 1.4186), matched-RATIO pairs agree to **1.49x** at
`M`/4 and **2.10x** at `M`/16 while the matched-ABSOLUTE pair at `M1`=128 disagrees by
**3.55x**. **But the ratio is not exact, and the residual has one sign**: at a matched ratio
a larger `M` is more forgiving, and the correction grows with the depth of the cut — the
signature of a logarithmically-shaped running maximum rather than a power-law one. `est1` is
a maximum over the first `M1` rows, so the undershoot is `f(M1)/f(M)`, one non-decreasing
curve divided by itself; that is a pure function of `M1/M` only where `f` is a power law.
**So re-expressing the dial as a fraction is the right shape and still owes its accuracy a
measurement at each ubatch.**

`CALROWS`=128 + `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 at `-ub` 2048 costs **+0.98% over 8 windows** and is
**-0.0045 +/- 0.0040 on the converged ones** — the margin repairs 6.4x of the cut's own
+8.73%, the same mechanism measured at 11x on window 1 at `-ub` 512. Against -0.55% for the
same pair at `-ub` 512, **the pair is not free at the larger ubatch where it was
better-than-free at the smaller**, and the whole swing is in the calibrating windows.

##### Re-expressed as a fraction the dial costs a quarter of what the absolute setting costs, and keeps 78% of the saving

`CALROWS`=**512** — `M`/4 at `-ub` 2048 — with `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 beside it costs `Dcal`
**+0.0266**, **+0.301%** over 8 windows, and **-0.00043 +/- 0.00437** over the converged
windows 3-8. Against the absolute `CALROWS`=128 at the same ubatch (`Dcal` +0.1052) that is
**0.25x the damage**, and against the bare `M`/4 cut (+0.1276) the margin repairs **4.80x**.
On price the recorded `llama-bench -ub 2048` figures are **28.2%** of `calibrate` for
`CALROWS`=512 against **36.3%** for 128, so the fraction keeps **77.7%** of the saving for a
quarter of the cost. **So the dial should be expressed as `M`/4 and not as a row count**, and
at `-ub` 2048 that setting is 512.

**The margin's repair factor is not constant in the depth of the cut**: 6.43x at `M`/16
against **4.80x** at `M`/4. A point estimate transported along that axis — the `M`/16 repair
fraction applied to the `M`/4 cut — predicted +0.0199 and missed by 1.33x, in the same
direction as the 2.1x miss the ubatch transport made.

##### The clip fraction predicts the damage along the cut axis and not across the margin axis

`BOOTMARGIN` reaches the damage twice (`ggml-rocket.cpp:2288-2295`): it sets pass two's scale
`sc2 = 127/(est1*BOOTMARGIN)`, so a larger margin saturates fewer columns, AND it sets the
recorded ceiling `est1*BOOTMARGIN` on a column that still saturates. Only the first is visible
in the clip fraction, and **the clip fraction is not a sufficient statistic for the accuracy
damage.** Raising the margin 1.5 -> 2.25 at `M`=2048:

| cut | regime | clip @1.5 | clip @2.25 | fall | damage removed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `M`/4 | no `AROW` | 8.0367% | 0.2886% | 27.9x | 79% |
| `M`/16 | no `AROW` | 19.8197% | 1.7809% | 11.1x | 84% |
| `M`/4 | `AROW`=1 | 4.3461% | **0.0836%** | **52.0x** | 79% |

**The margin removes far more clipping than damage at every point.** Fitting
`Dcal = A * clip^1.855` on the five margin-1.5 points and evaluating it at the margin-2.25
clips under-predicts the measured `Dcal` by **100.5x** (`M`/4) and **13.6x** (`M`/16); refitting
`A` through the `AROW`=1 (`M`/4, 1.5) point so that clip and `Dcal` come from the SAME
activation regime makes it worse, **318x**. So the residual damage at a raised margin is
carried by something the saturation flag cannot see, and **a 3-minute `llama-bench` CALMAP
readout cannot price this lever's accuracy** — the lever is always two dials.

`AROW` itself nearly halves the clip fraction (8.0367 -> 4.3461% at margin 1.5, 1.85x), so a
clip fraction is a statement about its activation regime; the grid runs without `AROW` and
every accuracy cell runs with it.

##### The returned surface's resolution is a real third channel, and it carries only a fifth of the residual

A calibrating call hands back pass TWO dequantized at `sc2 = 127/(est1*BOOTMARGIN)`
(`ggml-rocket.cpp:2315`), so the returned int8 quantum is `est1*BOOTMARGIN/127`. `est1` is
pass one's per-column max over `M1` rows at scale `cbound`, and `cbound` is set once per weight
from `sumabs` alone (line 2194) and never sees the margin — so the quantum **factorises**, and
**the two dials move it in OPPOSITE directions**: the margin coarsens it on every column while
a row cut shrinks `est1` and pushes it back. No clip fraction can see this.

The resolution index `R/R_ctl = (margin/1.5) * est1(cut)/est1(full)` is therefore readable
from CALMAP bytes with no run. On the uncontaminated set — blk.0's `attn_q`/`attn_k`/`attn_v`,
whose input is the embedding through an RMSNorm with no matmul upstream — `est1(512)/est1(full)`
is **0.89714** and `est1(128)/est1(full)` is **0.77888** at `M`=2048:

| cell | cut | margin | `R/R_ctl` | clip | `Dcal` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `q_ctl` | none | 1.5 | 1.00000 | 0.2600% | 0 by definition |
| `q_r512` | `M`/4 | 1.5 | 0.89714 | 8.0367% | +0.1276 |
| `q_shipfrac` | `M`/4 | 2.25 | 1.34570 | 0.2886% | +0.0266 |
| `q_ship` | `M`/16 | 2.25 | 1.16832 | 1.7809% | +0.1052 |
| `q_g2p25` | none | 2.25 | **1.50000** | **0.0000%** | **+0.0081** |

**The margin-only cell clips 0 of 1500160 columns, so it isolates resolution exactly**: a
**1.5x** coarsening of the returned surface costs **+0.0081 nats**. Interpolated to
`q_shipfrac`'s 1.34570 that is **+0.0053-0.0056** under either a linear or a quadratic-noise
form — **20-21%** of its +0.0266 — and its clipping is at the control's own level. **So
+0.021 nats, 79-80% of the residual, is not the returned quantum** — it is the clip channel,
on a curve that no linear statistic of the clipping reproduces. See the next subsection; the
clip fractions in the table above are `AROW`=0 readouts and the `Dcal` values are `AROW`=1
runs, which is why the in-regime numbers there differ.

**Damage is not monotone in resolution.** `q_ship` has a strictly FINER surface than
`q_shipfrac` (1.16832 against 1.34570) and 4x the damage, and `q_g2p25` is the coarsest cell
buildable at margin 2.25 and **3.3x less damaging** than `q_shipfrac`. An ordering argument
over the quantum alone predicts both backwards.

##### The calibrating-window damage is a SEPARABLE product of the cut and the margin

Read in the `AROW`=1 regime every `Dcal` was measured in, and with the resolution channel
subtracted as `D_res = 0.0162*(R/R_ctl - 1)`, the cells' clip damage is:

| cell | cut | margin | clip | mass `E` | `r` | `D_res` | `D_clip` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `q_ctl` | none | 1.5 | 0.0303% | 1.40e-05 | 1.00000 | 0 | 0 by definition |
| `q_g2p25` | none | 2.25 | 0.0000% | 0 | 1.00000 | +0.0081 | 0 |
| `q_r1024` | `M`/2 | 1.5 | 1.3775% | 1.27e-03 | 0.94636 | -0.0009 | **-0.0062** |
| `q_r1024g2p25` | `M`/2 | 2.25 | 0.0177% | 2.58e-05 | 0.94636 | +0.0068 | **-0.0174** |
| `q_shipfrac` | `M`/4 | 2.25 | 0.0836% | 1.24e-04 | 0.87151 | +0.0050 | +0.0216 |
| `q_g1p875r512` | `M`/4 | 1.875 | 0.4963% | 5.99e-04 | 0.87151 | +0.0014 | +0.0473 |
| `q_ship` | `M`/16 | 2.25 | 0.9604% | 1.39e-03 | 0.74417 | +0.0019 | +0.1033 |
| `q_r512` | `M`/4 | 1.5 | 4.3461% | 4.53e-03 | 0.87151 | -0.0021 | +0.1297 |
| `q_r128` | `M`/16 | 1.5 | 17.1747% | 2.41e-02 | 0.74417 | -0.0041 | +0.6803 |
| `q_r32` | `M`/64 | 1.5 | 38.3480% | 8.44e-02 | 0.62576 | -0.0061 | +2.0046 |

**`D_clip = g(cut)*h(margin)` fits the six cut cells at `M`/4 and deeper to a worst residual of
2.31%** (rms 1.86%) — three `g`'s and three `h`'s less one scale degeneracy is five parameters
through six points, so **one residual degree of freedom**. `g` = **0.1327 / 0.6649 / 2.0046**
at cuts `M`/4, `M`/16 and `M`/64; `h` = **1 / 0.3561 / 0.1590** at margins 1.5, 1.875 and 2.25.
Held out and then measured, the third margin lands within **2.1%** of the model's
power-law-in-margin extrapolation (predicted `Dcal` +0.0497, measured **+0.0487**).

`g` is **not** a power law in the cut — it rises 5.01x then 3.01x per 4x reduction. Two smooth
two-parameter forms fit those three points, `(1-r)^2.52` in the cut's own undershoot to 9.3%
and log-linear in the cut fraction to 15.6%, and **both are refuted at `M`/2 by sign**: see
below.

##### `h` is a power law in the margin and it extrapolates, and its exponent tracks the K SET

`h` was fitted only over margins [1.5, 2.25], and 1.875 is an interpolation between those
anchors — so it separates no extrapolation form, and its own verdict flips on the refuted
`D_res` index. Two cells at cut `M`/4 and margins **1.25** and **1.00** — 1.0 is the library's
floor, since `bootmargin < 1.0f` is reset to 1.5 — settle it [HW sweep, Qwen2.5-1.5B, `-ub`
2048, `--chunks 8`]:

| cut | margin | clip | `Dcal` | PPL[8] vs control | implied `h` | power law | log-linear |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `M`/4 | 1.00 | 77.171% | **+0.92760** | +12.347% | **7.3094** | 6.9407 | 3.6386 |
| `M`/4 | 1.25 | 25.672% | **+0.32464** | +4.056% | **2.5645** | 2.3897 | 1.9075 |

**The power law `h(m) = (m/1.5)^-4.778`, fitted entirely inside [1.5, 2.25], predicts a 1.5x
extrapolation to the floor within 5.3% and 7.3%** — both high, so the true `h` is slightly
steeper still. Log-linear in the margin misses 2.02x and 1.35x and is refuted. The `g`-free and
`D_res`-free readout, `Dcal`(1.0)/`Dcal`(1.25), is **2.857** against the power law's 2.912.

**The exponent is not shared across architectures.** SmolLM2-1.7B at the same cut and margin
1.0 reads `Dcal` = **+0.97094** (PPL[8] 8.3207 against its own control 7.4265, +12.041%), so
over the same [1.0, 2.25] range its margin ratio is **30.60** against Qwen's **50.57** —
exponent **4.22** against **4.84**, i.e. **1.65x shallower**. The consequence for anyone
quoting one number: at margin 2.25 SmolLM2's `M`/4 damage is **1.99x** Qwen's, and at margin
1.00 the two models are **4.7% apart**. A cross-model margin figure taken at one end of the
axis says nothing about the other. **The exponent IS shared between two models that share a K
set** — see the table below, where 4.230 and 4.219 agree to 0.26% across a tokenizer, a corpus,
8 blocks of depth and GQA-against-MHA — **but a same-family model at a different K set reads
