3.223**, so it is a property of the K set (or of what co-varies with it), not of the
architecture and not of the model.

The clip fraction at these margins is predicted, not just recorded: the off-line readout over a
CALMAP's own `est2/est1` distribution gave 78.2% and 26.0% before the cells ran, against
77.171% and 25.672% measured — **1.3%**, at clip fractions **9x** larger than the 8.7% it had
ever been checked at. **What the truncation MECHANISM cannot do is predict the damage**: taken
literally it puts `D(1.0)/D(1.5)` at 21.9-54.2 against a measured 7.31, and rescaled by its own
validation miss at 3.37-3.82. Its direction is right and no magnitude from it is usable.

##### A third model separates the two channels, and a fourth says the margin index tracks the K SET, not the architecture

Two models gave each channel two points moving opposite ways, which is compatible both with
one per-model scalar setting both and with two independent channels. Llama-3.2-1B-f16 — llama
architecture and SmolLM2's exact `n_embd` 2048 / `n_ff` 8192, at **16** blocks against 24 —
runs its own control plus three cells and separates them [HW sweep, `-ub` 2048, `--chunks 8`,
`rocket` 1.6.0, backstop delta 0]:

| model | arch | blocks | `n_embd`/`n_ff` | `head_count_kv` | ctl PPL[8] | `D_res` | R over [1.00, 2.25] | exponent |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen2.5-1.5B | qwen2 | 28 | 1536/8960 | 2 | 7.2534 | +0.00811 | 50.57 | 4.838 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | llama | 24 | 2048/8192 | 32 | 7.4265 | +0.02060 | 30.60 | 4.219 |
| Llama-3.2-1B | llama | 16 | 2048/8192 | 8 | 11.4024 | **+0.01626** | **30.87** | **4.230** |
| SmolLM2-360M | llama | 32 | **960/2560** | 5 | 9.5608 | **+0.01484** | **13.65** | **3.223** |

**The margin index is IDENTICAL on the two llama models that share a K set** — 30.87 against
30.60, exponents 4.230 against 4.219, agreeing to **0.9%** and **0.26%** across an 8-block depth
difference and across GQA-against-MHA — **while Qwen sits 1.64x away**. R is a ratio at one cut,
so both models' `g` and both models' mixed-`M` populations cancel out of it.

**But that is the K set agreeing, not the architecture, and the fourth model is what says so.**
SmolLM2-360M shares SmolLM2-1.7B's architecture, its `tokenizer.ggml.pre` smollm over an
identical 49152 vocabulary — so the same corpus segments into the same windows, true of no
other pair here — its training family and its base-checkpoint status, and differs in the K set.
**Its R is 13.65, 0.446x the anchor's.** The two pairs are the finding and neither needs a fit:

| pair | what moves | R ratio |
|---|---|---|
| SmolLM2-1.7B vs Llama-3.2-1B | tokenizer, vocabulary, corpus, depth 24 vs 16, `head_kv` 32 vs 8, base vs instruct | **1.009x** |
| SmolLM2-1.7B vs SmolLM2-360M | **the K set**, 2048/8192 vs 960/2560 (depth 24 vs 32, `head_kv` 32 vs 5) | **0.446x** |

Depth and head structure move in BOTH pairs, so both are refuted again and now by a pair that
moves them the other way; the tokenizer, the vocabulary, the corpus and the tuning status are
refuted by the first pair. **`n_embd` alone is refuted outright — R is non-monotone in it**
(960 -> 13.65, 1536 -> 50.57, 2048 -> 30.7). `n_ff` and `n_ff`/`n_embd` are both monotone over
these four points; a fifth model separates them and refutes both, below.

**What this is NOT: a measurement of the K set.** SmolLM2-360M K-splits **none** of its GEMMs —
960 and 2560 are both `rk76_k_single_ok` — where all three anchors split exactly one of the
seven per block (`ffn_down`, K >= 6176); and model scale co-varies as well. So the move is
"the K set OR the split population OR scale", jointly. `ROCKET_RK3576_FORCESPLIT=1` separates
the split. The other two are separated by a fifth model at the SAME `n_ff`/`n_embd` ratio and
0.600x the scale — SmolLM2-135M, which splits nothing either, so the split population is held
fixed there — and it refutes the ratio, `n_ff` alone and scale together, below.

[HW sweep, `-ub` 2048, `--chunks 8`, `rocket` 1.6.0, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, backstop delta 0, 2026-08-22. Scored under a gate that refuses unless nine pre-existing `Dcal` values
reproduce to 5e-4 and all three anchors' published R to 0.02.]

**The resolution channel is the model-specific one and it moves where the margin index does
not**: Llama-3.2-1B's `D_res` is **0.789x** SmolLM2's and **2.004x** Qwen's. It does move with
depth, but nowhere near proportionally — 0.789x for a 0.667x block ratio — and the reading sits
**1.5%** from the boundary between "tracks the architecture" and "scales with depth", so carry
the two ratios and not a label. **The fourth model dissociates the two channels a third time,
now on the K axis specifically**: SmolLM2-360M's `D_res` is **0.720x** its same-family anchor's
while its R is **0.446x**, and a `D_res` tracking `n_embd` through the first three points would
have read +0.00215 — a **6.9x** miss. So the resolution channel does not follow the K set
either, and stays per-model over four points spanning 2.54x (+0.00811 to +0.02060).

**One per-model scalar setting both channels is therefore not supported.** Channel A moved
0.789x while channel B moved 1.009x; an anti-correlation law fitted on the first two models
(log-log slope -0.539) demanded R = 34.76 and measured 30.87, an 11.2% residual against 0.9%
for "the index is simply the same on both". Baseline sharpness is refuted as a driver too, and
by these same three points: PPL[8] runs 7.2534 / 7.4265 / 11.4024 against `D_res` 0.00811 /
0.02060 / 0.01626, which is non-monotone.

**Two things about this model bite anyone transporting a number to it.** Its `head_count_kv` is
8 against SmolLM2's 32, so two of the seven projections a block offloads carry `N`=512 rather
than 2048 — the pair is *not* a pure depth contrast. And **24.35% of its calibrated columns sit
at `M`=1024** (309760 of 1272320) against SmolLM2's 8.57% and Qwen's 19.0%, so `CALROWS`=512 is
an `M`/2 cut on a quarter of them and the "cut `M`/4" label covers only **75.65%**. Both cancel
out of R and neither cancels out of a level, so **Dcal(cut 512, 1.00) = +0.90033 is not
comparable across models** even though Qwen's +0.92760 and SmolLM2's +0.97094 sit beside it.

The clip-fraction readout transported to a third model and held: **75.040%** measured at cut
512 / margin 1.00 against 76.7% predicted from the other two models' 77.171% and 76.14%, with
the margin-2.25 no-cut cell clipping **0 of 1272320** columns exactly as on both others — which
is what makes `D_res` a measurement rather than a response minus a model.

##### The margin index is the reciprocal of a small difference, and a fifth model puts that difference at zero

`R` is `D_clip(cut M/4, 1.00) / D_clip(cut M/4, 2.25)`, where `D_clip(m)` is `Dcal(cut, m)`
minus the no-cut `D_res(m)`. **That denominator is a difference of two same-signed quantities
of order 0.02 nats, and nothing constrains it to stay positive.** SmolLM2-135M — llama, 576/1536,
30 blocks, `head_kv` 3, smollm over the same 49152 vocabulary, the HuggingFaceTB base checkpoint,
and `n_ff`/`n_embd` = 2.666667, *exactly* SmolLM2-360M's, at 0.600x its width — puts it at
**-0.00338** [HW sweep, `-ub` 2048, `--chunks 8`, `rocket` 1.6.0, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, backstop
delta 0, 2026-08-22]:

| model | ratio | `D_res`(2.25) | `Dcal`(cut, 1.00) | `Dcal`(cut, 2.25) | `D_clip`(2.25) | `R` | raw ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen2.5-1.5B | 5.8333 | +0.00811 | +0.92760 | +0.02656 | +0.01845 | 50.57 | 34.92 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | 4.0000 | +0.02060 | +0.97094 | +0.05278 | +0.03218 | 30.60 | 18.40 |
| Llama-3.2-1B | 4.0000 | +0.01626 | +0.90033 | +0.04577 | +0.02951 | 30.87 | 19.67 |
| SmolLM2-360M | 2.6667 | +0.01484 | +0.69288 | +0.06634 | +0.05150 | 13.65 | 10.44 |
| SmolLM2-135M | **2.6667** | **+0.02332** | **+0.51870** | **+0.01994** | **-0.00338** | **-158.11** | **26.02** |

**Read the denominator column, not the `R` column.** The four anchors' 3.7055x spread in `R`
decomposes exactly into a **1.3276x** numerator move and a **2.7911x** denominator move, so
**78% of the log spread is the reciprocal of that small difference** — the ladder 50.57 / 30.87
/ 30.60 / 13.65 is mostly denominator. Fit `Dcal` directly on (cut, margin); do not derive
`D_clip` and do not quote `R` for a model whose denominator has not been read.

**On the fifth model the clip term at the shipped margin is consistent with zero, not
negative.** Its two calibrating windows disagree in sign — **-0.00709** and **+0.00371** — where
SmolLM2-360M's agree (+0.00155, +0.04995). The net is 38x the reconstruction precision, so the
arithmetic is exact; what is unresolved is the quantity, and an index dividing by it is
undefined rather than large. The mechanism is the two channels netting out: margin 2.25
coarsens the returned quantum on every column, while a row cut shrinks `est1` and pushes it
back the other way, so on a model whose resolution cost is high and whose clipping is cheap the
cut is a partial antidote to the margin.

**The margin channel itself is intact on this model** — at the same cut, margin 1.00 costs
+0.51870 against margin 2.25's +0.01994, a **26.0x** ratio in the same direction as all four
anchors. A negative `R` here means the instrument failed, not that the channel reversed.

**`n_ff`/`n_embd` is refuted as what sets the index**, on the one statistic that survives a
zero denominator: the raw `Dcal(cut, 1.00)/Dcal(cut, 2.25)` reads **26.02** against
SmolLM2-360M's **10.44** at an *identical* ratio, **2.49x** apart. The raw statistic is also
non-monotone in `n_ff` (34.9 / 18.4 / 19.7 / 10.4 / 26.0 at 8960 / 8192 / 8192 / 2560 / 1536).
**That non-monotonicity does not carry to model scale, though it was read that way once** — the
raw ratio is itself **76% denominator**, and on its numerator alone scale is monotone. See the
next section.

**The resolution channel does not scale with width either, and the fifth model is what says
so**: `D_res` is **+0.02332** on the *smallest* model, above all four anchors, **1.96x** what an
in-family power law through the two SmolLM2 checkpoints predicted (+0.01190) and in the wrong
direction. Over five models it spans 2.875x with no monotone relation to width, depth, `n_ff`
or baseline loss. Its no-cut margin-2.25 cell clips **0 of 409344** columns, so this is a
measurement and not a response minus a model.

The clip-fraction readout holds on a fourth model: **80.859%** at cut 512 / margin 1.00, against
77.171% / 75.040% / 77.351% on the other three — **nearly model-independent at a given (cut,
margin), 1.078x over four models spanning 5.8x in `n_ff`**, and read *in its own prompt regime*.

[Scored under a gate that refuses unless nine pre-existing `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4 and all four anchors' published `R` to 0.02; branch points computed before the cells.]

##### Reading the (cut, margin) surface directly: the subtraction is sound where clipping dominates, and the raw ratio inherits the index's defect

All 32 calibration cells at `-ub` 2048 read as one response `Dcal(cut, margin)` — no `D_clip`,
no index — say three things the derived form hid [HW sweep, five models, `.so` `ea40bc1b`,
corpus `3ce70e93`, `rocket` 1.6.0].

**The `D_res` subtraction is validated where the clip channel dominates, and it is the
DIVISION that fails, not the subtraction.** Separability predicts that
`D_clip(cut, 1.50)/D_clip(cut, 2.25)` does not depend on the cut, and `D_res(1.50)` is zero by
construction because the control *is* margin 1.5 with no cut. On Qwen2.5-1.5B the
*unsubtracted* margin ratio at cuts 128 and 512 reads **6.427** and **4.805** — 1.337x apart —
and subtracting the independently *measured* no-cut +0.00811 makes them **6.963** and **6.918**,
**1.0066x**, a 51x reduction bought with one constant that was never fitted to either cell. At
cut 1024 both readings are negative and 18.6x apart. So a `D_clip` is a sound quantity at
`M`/4 and deeper and an unsound one near its own zero; the defect rule 171 names is the
reciprocal, not the difference.

**The sign of `D_clip(M/4, 2.25)` is which side of a minimum the cut falls on.** Along margin
2.25, Qwen runs **+0.10521 / +0.02656 / +0.00931 / -0.01065 / +0.00335 / +0.00811** at cuts
128 / 512 / 768 / 1024 / 1536 / 2048, so it crosses its own no-cut level just above 768 and
bottoms near `M`/2. SmolLM2-1.7B is monotone over the cuts it has — +0.05278 / +0.03770 /
+0.02405 / +0.02060 at 512 / 1024 / 1536 / 2048 — so its crossing is above `M`*0.75.
SmolLM2-135M's `M`/4 cell already sits *below* its no-cut cell, so its crossing is below `M`/4.
The crossing point is model-dependent and that is what flips the denominator's sign.

**The raw ratio carries 76% of its spread in the denominator**, the same defect as `R`'s 78%.
Decomposed at the ends of the five-model ladder (Qwen over SmolLM2-360M): numerator
**1.3388x**, denominator **2.4973x**, product 3.3433x — the raw ratio exactly.

**On the numerator alone, model scale is monotone, and it is the one architectural driver left
standing.** `Dcal(cut M/4, margin 1.00)` is the largest cell in the corpus (0.52-0.97 nats
against a reconstruction precision of 8.79e-5) and subtracts no model. It is a LEVEL, so only
the three checkpoints sharing the smollm tokenizer segment the corpus identically and are
comparable:

| model | params | `n_embd` | `n_ff` | blocks | `n_ff`/`n_embd` | calibrated columns | `Dcal`(`M`/4, 1.00) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SmolLM2-135M | 0.135B | 576 | 1536 | 30 | 2.6667 | 409344 | +0.51870 |
| SmolLM2-360M | 0.360B | 960 | 2560 | 32 | 2.6667 | 651264 | +0.69288 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | 1.710B | 2048 | 8192 | 24 | 4.0000 | 1769472 | +0.97094 |

Monotone in parameters, `n_embd`, `n_ff` and the calibrated-column count; **non-monotone in
depth** (30 / 32 / 24), which refutes depth a third time; and the two checkpoints at an
*identical* `n_ff`/`n_embd` differ **1.336x**, which refutes the ratio on the numerator as well
as on the raw statistic. **Model scale is not refuted** — the statistic it was refuted on is
three-quarters denominator.

**The clip FRACTION is not the driver.** At that same cell it reads 80.859 / 77.351 / 77.171 /
75.040% on four models — **1.078x** — while the response spans **1.872x**. What is left
collinear with scale on every model here is the calibrated-column *count*; separating the two
needs a cell that moves the population at a fixed checkpoint.

**The margin axis is not a power law on the direct response.** Qwen at cut `M`/4 over five
margins reads +0.92760 / +0.32464 / +0.12764 / +0.04870 / +0.02656 at 1.00 / 1.25 / 1.50 /
1.875 / 2.25. The local log-log exponent runs **-4.705 / -5.120 / -4.318 / -3.325** — a 1.54x
spread, non-monotone — and one power law over the range misses by **1.230x** at its worst
point. What survives is the direction and the end-to-end size: **34.9x** between the two ends
at that cut on that model.

**The two channels' dissociation needs no index at all.** SmolLM2-135M carries the lowest
numerator of the five (+0.51870, 0.534x the highest) *and* the highest `D_res` (+0.02332,
2.875x the lowest), so one model refutes "one per-model scalar sets both channels" with no fit
and no derived quantity.

[`surface_cm.py`, which re-runs the same nine-value reproduction gate before it reads anything.
Scope: one corpus, one ubatch, calibrating windows only; the levels are comparable within a
tokenizer and nowhere else.]

##### The calibrated-column population is not the driver: 4.09x more columns, more clipping, a quarter of the damage

`ROCKET_RK3576_FORCESPLIT=1 ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK=512` cuts every offloaded K into 512-chunks
(960 -> 512+256+128+64, 2560 -> 5x512) and a calibrating call logs one column per output column
*per chunk*, so SmolLM2-360M's calibrated population moves **651264 -> 2666496, 4.0944x**, at a
byte-identical checkpoint, tokenizer, corpus and window segmentation. That breaks the
collinearity between model scale and column count that every model on the board carries. The
population closed form generalised to a per-GEMM chunk count predicted **both integers exactly**
before the control ran — total **2666496**, minority **443776**, share **16.643%** — which is
12 of 12 integers over six model configurations, and the first at a chunk count the shipped
path never produces.

| cell | PPL[4] | `Dcal` (windows 1-2) | clipped |
|---|---|---|---|
| `q_fsctl` (this arm's control) | 11.3676 | — | 0 of 2666496 |
| `q_fsr512g1p0` (cut 512, margin 1.00) | 11.8050 | **+0.16382** | 2250316 (**84.392%**) |
| `q_fsg2p25` (no cut, margin 2.25) | 11.3775 | **+0.00448** | 0 |

[HW sweep, 3 cells `rc=0`, `-ub` 2048 `--chunks` 4, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, corpus `3ce70e93`,
backstop 650 -> 650, 0 dmesg WARNINGs, taint 4608, `ondemand`, 2026-08-22.]

**The population is refuted as the driver, and by more than a null.** Against the same
checkpoint's unsplit **+0.69288**, the cut-512 margin-1.00 damage under 4.09x the columns is
**0.2364x** — *down* — while the clip FRACTION went *up* **1.091x** (84.392% against 77.351%).
More columns, more of them clipping, a quarter of the damage. A term that grows with the
calibrated-column count cannot produce that.

**Both channels fall at this chunk size.** `D_res` reads **+0.00448**, **0.3016x** the unsplit
+0.01484, against the clip channel's 0.2364x. A second chunk size separates them again — see
below.

**The split's own arithmetic is not what is being read.** The FORCESPLIT control reads PPL[4]
**11.3676** against the unsplit control's **11.4391**, **0.9937x**, so the chunked baseline is
if anything slightly better on this model — the "the split swamps the dial" reading is refuted
on its own covariate. [The K-split's recorded 1.60x perplexity cost is a different
configuration: it is what turning `KSPLIT` on buys for a `K >= 6176` `ffn_down` that was
otherwise declined to the CPU. Nothing on this model reaches that bound.]

Cap, declared before the cells: the arm moves the population and the contraction depth
*together*, so it bounds what a population term can contribute and does not subtract it.

[Registered and pushed before the control cell; scored under a gate that refuses unless eleven previously scored `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4.]

##### The clip damage is a law in the chunk DEPTH, not in the chunk count, and the two channels come apart again at a second chunk size

`ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK=256` on the same checkpoint takes the population to **3563520**,
**5.4717x** the shipped 651264 and 1.3364x the 512-chunk cell — a third exact forward integer
from the closed form, which is now 14 of 14. The measurements, `--chunks` 4, `-ub` 2048, three
cells 231-264 s, backstop delta 0:

| | unsplit | `KCHUNK`=512 | `KCHUNK`=256 |
|---|---|---|---|
| calibrated columns | 651264 | 2666496 | 3563520 |
| column-weighted chunks a GEMM | 1 | 4.0943 | 5.4717 |
| deepest chunk | 2560 | 512 | 256 |
| `Dcal`(cut 512, margin 1.00) | +0.69288 | +0.16382 | **+0.07245** |
| — as a fraction of unsplit | 1.0000 | 0.2364 | **0.1046** |
| clip fraction of that cell | 77.351% | 84.392% | **85.271%** |
| damage per clipped column, nats | 1.375e-06 | 7.280e-08 | **2.384e-08** |
| `D_res` = `Dcal`(no cut, 2.25) | +0.01484 | +0.00448 | **+0.01013** |

**One over the chunk count is refuted, and its apparent 3.2% fit at `KCHUNK`=512 was a law
agreeing with its own first measurement for free.** The local exponent on the count over the
segment between the two split cells is **2.8135**, against the 1.0230 the 512 point alone
gives; a reciprocal law would give 1.

**The maximum chunk depth is the variable that fits.** Halving `KCHUNK` halves the deepest
chunk exactly while raising the column-weighted count only 1.3364x — the greedy walk is
*ragged*, so count and MAX depth are not reciprocal even though count and MEAN depth are by
construction. Measured 0.4423x against 0.5000x for a halved depth (**1.13x**) and 0.7483x for
the count (**1.69x**); segment exponents 1.177 and 2.814. The residual is in the direction of
super-linearity in depth [two split points, one model].

**`D_res` reverses, so the contraction depth does not name a quantity the two channels share.**
+0.01013 is **2.2614x** its `KCHUNK`=512 value and 0.6827x the unsplit, so `D_res` is
*non-monotone* in the chunk count with a minimum at `KCHUNK`=512, while the clip channel falls
0.4423x over the same step. Two competing terms are visible and neither combination reproduces
the size: `count*quantum` gives 1.277x, `count*quantum²` 1.220x and `count²` 1.786x against a
measured 2.2614x [hypothesis — named, not isolated].

**The cross-tab by chunk depth is the durable half, and it is monotone in both columns.** Over
the control cells' own CALMAPs:

| `Kc` | mean `est1` | clip fraction of the cut cell |
|---|---|---|
| 64 | 69528 | 87.742% |
| 128 | 85110 | 85.586% |
| 256 | 94860 | 82.961% |
| 512 | 128443 | 82.346% |

A shallower chunk is quantized **finer** — the returned quantum is `est1*margin/127`, and
`est1` falls 0.5413x from `Kc`=512 to `Kc`=64 inside one run — and it **clips more**, because a
partial over fewer terms has a heavier relative tail and a 512-row max undershoots the 2048-row
max more often. That is the opposite of the "a shallower contraction clips less" story, which
is now refuted within a single run as well as between runs.

`Kc` is **not** a sufficient label for a population: the `Kc`=256 columns read `est1` 94860 at
`KCHUNK`=512 and 102305 at `KCHUNK`=256, because they sit at different offsets into K in the
two walks.

**The tail sub-population is a control the split does not reach.** K=960's walk ends 128+64 at
both chunk sizes, so those 589824+589824 columns are the identical population in both cells and
read identical (`est1` 1.0002x and 0.9997x, clip 1.0008x and 1.0004x). The two cells therefore
differ *only* in their deep chunks, and since a common additive term can only pull a ratio
toward 1, the measured 0.4423x is an upper **bound** on the deep chunks' own fall.

Cap, and it was wrong in the arm's own registration: the arm was declared unable to separate
chunk count from chunk depth "by construction". It can, because the walk is ragged.

[Registered and pushed before the control cell; scored under a gate that refuses unless thirteen previously scored `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4; cross-tab by `armae_crosstab.py`.]

##### At a fixed chunk depth the per-column clipping converges across models and the damage does not, and the numerators invert

The chunk depth is a knob, so it can be held equal across models that differ in everything
else. `ROCKET_RK3576_FORCESPLIT=1 ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK=512` gives all three checkpoints that
share the smollm tokenizer a maximum chunk depth of exactly 512 (`576 -> 512+64` and
`1536 -> 3x512`; `960 -> 512+256+128+64` and `2560 -> 5x512`; `2048 -> 4x512` and
`8192 -> 16x512`), where their shipped maxima are 1536 / 2560 / 2304. Two exact forward
population integers came out of the closed form before the cells ran — **853248** and
**6684672** columns, with `Kc` histograms {512: 478464, 64: 374784} and {512: 6684672} — and
all five landed, taking the form to 16 of 16.

| | SmolLM2-135M | SmolLM2-360M | SmolLM2-1.7B |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Dcal`(cut 512, margin 1.00), shipped walk | +0.51870 | +0.69288 | +0.97094 |
| the same at `KCHUNK`=512 | **+0.36230** | **+0.16382** | **+0.13742** |
| — as a fraction of its own shipped reading | 0.6985 | 0.2364 | **0.1415** |
| column-weighted chunk count `C` at `KCHUNK`=512 | 2.0844 | 4.0943 | 4.8571 |
| `p` in `FALL = C^-p` | 0.4886 | 1.0231 | **1.4710** |
| clip fraction of that cell | 84.481% | 84.392% | 82.727% |
| `D_res` = `Dcal`(no cut, 2.25) at `KCHUNK`=512 | +0.00758 | +0.00448 | +0.00750 |
| — as a fraction of its own shipped reading | 0.3252 | 0.3019 | 0.3640 |

[HW sweep, 6 cells `rc=0` (the 360M column is arm (ac)'s, not re-run), `-ub` 2048 `--chunks` 4,
`.so` `ea40bc1b`, corpus `3ce70e93`, 95-607 s a cell, backstop 689 -> 689, 0 dmesg WARNINGs,
taint 4608, `ondemand`, 2026-08-22. Levels are comparable here only because the three share a
tokenizer.]

**The maximum chunk depth does not set the level, and the ordering inverts.** At a fixed
maximum depth the three numerators span **2.637x** — *wider* than the 1.8719x they span on
their own shipped walks — and they run **monotone decreasing in scale** where the shipped
readings run monotone increasing. That is neither of the two outcomes the question had: not a
collapse, and not a surviving spread. A common multiplicative fall is refuted with it (it
predicts the 1.8719x is preserved) and so is `1/C` (it predicts +0.24885 and +0.25701, missing
1.46x high and 0.53x low). No single power of the count fits either: `p` is itself monotone in
scale over a 3.01x range.

**What DOES converge at a fixed depth is the per-column observable.** Read on the `Kc`=512
sub-population alone — the same chunk depth in all three walks — the cut cells clip **81.985% /
82.346% / 82.727%**, a **1.0091x** spread, at mean `est1` **117277 / 128443 / 115208**
(1.1149x). So the depth mechanism does control what it is claimed to control, at the column
level, across three checkpoints that differ 12.7x in parameter count. The damage those columns
do to the loss is 2.637x apart at the same time. **What the split does to a column is the
chunk depth's; what it does to the loss is not.**

**The two channels separate a sixth time, and here `D_res` is the well-behaved one.** The
resolution channel falls by a nearly common factor on all three — **0.3252 / 0.3019 / 0.3640**,
a 1.21x spread — against the clip channel's 0.6985 / 0.2364 / 0.1415, a **4.94x** spread. A
single multiplicative response to the split describes one channel and not the other, on the
same three cells.

Cap, declared before the cells and confirmed by them: `C` at `KCHUNK`=512 is 2.0844 / 4.0943 /
4.8571, monotone in scale over exactly these three, so the arm cannot separate a chunk-count
driver from a scale one. It did not need to — both are refuted by the inversion. What it also
does not hold equal is the depth *distribution*: 43.9% of the 135M's columns sit at `Kc`=64,
the 360M has 66.4% below 512, and the 1.7B is uniform at 512.

[Registered and pushed before either control cell; scored under a gate that refuses unless ten previously scored `Dcal` values reproduce to 5e-4.]

##### The maximum chunk depth is not the driver, and the damage is carried by the `n_embd`-K GEMMs

A five-rung `KCHUNK` ladder on one checkpoint (SmolLM2-135M, `-c/-b/-ub` 2048, `--chunks` 4,
each rung against its own control because a different chunk size is a different arithmetic):

| `KCHUNK` | `K`=576 walk | `K`=1536 walk | max depth | `C` | columns | `Dcal`(512, 1.00) | `D_res` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| off | 576 | 1536 | 1536 | 1.0000 | 409344 | **+0.51870** | +0.02332 |
| 2048 | 512+64 | 1536 | **1536** | 1.9156 | 784128 | **+0.37641** | +0.02164 |
| 1024 | 512+64 | 1024+512 | 1024 | 2.0000 | 818688 | **+0.37081** | −0.00783 |
| 512 | 512+64 | 3x512 | 512 | 2.0844 | 853248 | **+0.36230** | +0.00758 |
| 256 | 256+256+64 | 6x256 | 256 | 3.2533 | 1331712 | **+0.16241** | −0.00257 |
| 128 | 4x128+64 | 12x128 | 128 | 5.5909 | 2288640 | **+0.06283** | +0.00343 |

**`KCHUNK`=2048 and the unsplit control have the same maximum chunk depth and `Dcal` falls
27.4% between them.** The greedy walk leaves `K`=1536 whole there — 2304 and 2048 both exceed
it, so it picks 1536 — while `K`=576 still splits `512+64`. A law in the maximum chunk depth
predicts exactly zero on that pair, so it is refuted with no fitted exponent and no argument
about which tensors carry the loss. `KCHUNK`=64 refuses: `ffn_down` would need 24 chunks
against the walk's 16-chunk cap.

**`ffn_down` is nearly inert.** Its whole split — one chunk of 1536 down to `3x512`, a 3x cut
in the program's deepest chunk over 12.2% of the columns — is worth **3.7%** of `Dcal`. The
`n_embd`-K family (q/k/v/o/gate/up and the output head) carries **88%** of the 31.1% fall on
87.8% of the columns. Read the 1024-vs-512 pair under that: it moves only inside `ffn_down`'s
3.7%, so it is a real measurement of an aggregate and says nothing about the mechanism.

**A count power law with the single parameter `p`=0.4886, fitted at unsplit → 512, predicts
unsplit → 2048 (0.7258 measured against 0.7278) and unsplit → 1024 (0.7149 against 0.7127)
both to 0.3%, and misses 256 and 128 by 1.79x and 3.56x.** The boundary falls exactly where
`n_embd` stops walking `512+64`, so the exponent is a property of how finely that family is
split, not of the program's chunk count. Segment exponents on the count are 0.5616 / 1.8023 /
1.7540 over 1024 → 512 → 256 → 128; on the depth 0.0335 / 1.1576 / 1.3702, a 41x swing.

**The 512 → 256 segment RATIO transfers across models to 1.3%** — 0.44827 on the 135M against
the 360M's measured 0.44225 — where the LEVEL at a fixed depth is 2.637x apart. A ratio is
portable across these checkpoints in a way no level is.

**The two channels separate by tensor family.** `D_res` sits at the unsplit level in exactly
the two cells where `ffn_down` is one chunk and collapses to at most 0.0078 in absolute value
in the four where it is split — but with **alternating sign**, so this is an observation over
two points against four and not a mechanism [expected]. `ffn_down`'s own mean `est1` falls
monotonically **185839 / 148655 / 132503 / 108084 / 89437** at `Kc` 1536 / 1024 / 512 / 256 /
128, while the 749568 `n_embd`-side columns — frozen at `512+64` in the 2048, 1024 and 512
cells — hold `est1` to **1.0013x**. That cross-tab is exact because the CALMAP carries the
tensor name in column 0, and it is free: a ragged walk leaves a sub-population untouched.

The population closed form is **20 of 20** exact integers, four of them forward here (818688 /
1331712 / 2288640 / 784128) with their `Kc` histograms; `Kc`=64 holds the same 374784 columns
in all five split cells and reads `est1` within 1.0054x and clip within 1.0033x. The per-`Kc`
clip transfer predicted 84.506 / 84.945 / 86.083% at 1024 / 256 / 128 and measured 84.643 /
85.826 / 85.244% — good to 0.9 absolute but **not monotone**, since the 128 rung clips less
than the 256 rung.

**The chunk COUNT is refuted too, and the `n_embd` family's own maximum depth is what both
refutations leave standing.** A sixth rung, `KCHUNK`=320, holds that family at exactly two
chunks and at a mean depth of exactly 288 — the greedy walk takes `K`=576 to `320+256` where
512 took it to `512+64`, and (512+64)/2 = (320+256)/2 — while its maximum depth moves 512 →
320. `Dcal` falls **2.06x**, +0.36230 → +0.17629, against a count law's predicted 0.9626 of
the 512 rung: refuted at 1.98x, and the mean-depth reading with it. The two designed pairs
therefore bracket the answer from opposite sides — one shares a global maximum depth and
differs 27.4%, the other shares a count and a mean depth and differs 2.06x. Over seven rungs a
power law in the `n_embd` maximum depth fits to a worst factor of 1.1624x against the global
count's 1.3934x, and the three cells sharing an `n_embd` depth of 512 read within 3.89% while
their global `C` spans 8.81%. **The local exponent still runs 2.72 / 1.53 / 0.37 / 1.37 over
the segments, so what is supported is an ordering and a residual, never an exponent** — and
the regression's 1.20x edge is weak beside the two pairs, which carry the result.

**A chunk depth that is not a power of two takes a narrower rotation, and that cannot reach
`Dcal`.** 320 is the first such depth run here: `rk_hadamard_ok` is false, so the rotation is
the block-diagonal fallback at b = `Kc & -Kc` = 64 rather than a full H of the chunk's own
width. Because the rotation is orthonormal the EXACT per-chunk accumulator is unchanged, and
`Dcal` differences two cells at the same `KCHUNK`, so the width cancels **to first order**; it
survives as a second-order term, since `est1` is read off the quantized product and not the
exact one [expected]. On the control's own level, where the width does act at first order,
**both single-regressor fits are refuted**: the column-weighted depth
and rotation width correlate +0.9963 across the five split rungs and decorrelate only at 320,
where depth-only predicts 15.3112 and rotation 15.0787 against a measured 15.2165 — 3.2x and
5.3x their own worst in-sample residuals. A chunk-COUNT fit is the only one of the three that
survives there (15.2061, error +0.0104 against a residual of 0.0255), unregistered and so an
observation owing a forward band [expected].

**The per-`Kc` clip ordering inverts within a single cell.** At `KCHUNK`=320, `Kc`=256 clips
81.153% against `Kc`=320's 82.457% — the shallower chunk clipping less, the opposite of the
512 rung's 87.667 against 81.985 — and the mean `est1` inverts with it (106069 against 87610,
where the deeper chunk sums more terms). The two chunks cover different k-ranges (`n_embd`
k[0,320) against k[320,576)), and the range beats the depth: `Kc` is not a population label,
now measured inside one cell rather than across two. The whole-cell clip is 81.879%, below
every other split rung and below the transfer's own 83.540 prediction, whose per-`Kc` misses
of −2.725 and −0.812 absolute are its worst.

"The `Kc`=64 chunks carry the damage" is refuted from cells already on disk: 320 is the only
walk without them, but that population is held at exactly 374784 in all five other split rungs
while `Dcal` spans 5.99x, and its fraction reading would put a 0% cell below the 128 rung's
+0.06283 where 320 sits 2.81x above it. Whether removing them contributes to the 2.06x fall is
unbounded — no per-column statistic can bound a loss share, and this rung has no frozen
sub-population.

[Registered, each pushed before its cells; scored under a gate that refuses unless seven previously scored `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4. Twelve cells, 92-156 s each, backstop delta 0.]

**Neither family's split does anything on its own: `D_res` collapses only when BOTH are
split.** `KCHUNK`=576 is the one reachable chunk size whose `n_embd`-K family takes a single
chunk — `rk76_ksplit`'s forced branch returns the same one-element list its non-forced branch
returns, so 73.1% of the columns are bit-identical to the unsplit cell and the rung measures
`ffn_down`'s 1 → 4 chunk step alone. It completes a 2x2 whose other three corners were already
on disk:

| | `ffn_down` whole | `ffn_down` split |
|---|---|---|
| **`n_embd` whole** | +0.02332 (unsplit) | **+0.02376** (`KCHUNK`=576) |
| **`n_embd` split** | +0.02164 (`KCHUNK`=2048) | −0.00783 … +0.01187 (four rungs) |

Splitting `ffn_down` four ways with `n_embd` whole moves `D_res` by **+0.00044**, 1.9% of the
collapse the both-split rungs show; splitting `n_embd` alone moves it −0.00168. So the reading
that `D_res` collapses wherever `ffn_down` is split — which held over five cells — is refuted:
every one of those five split both families, making it a one-at-a-time reading of a condition
of two.

**The family localisation is not additive either.** `Dcal` reads +0.51361 at this rung, an
`ffn_down` step of −0.00509 against the −0.01411 the same tensor cost with `n_embd` split:
**0.360x**, and 0.98% of its own baseline against 3.75%, so a scale effect does not explain
it. A share measured for a tensor family is a property of the other family's state, not of
the tensor. What that 0.360x is *not* is an interaction of the two families — the matched-count
rung below reads the same step at **+0.00689**, the opposite sign, so the two `ffn_down` steps
differ because they are different chunk counts on a curve that is not monotone in the count.

**The control-level chunk-count fit is refuted forward.** The cell reads 15.2313 against its
predicted 15.2897 — 2.3 worst in-sample residuals — alongside the depth and rotation fits it
had beaten post hoc (7.4 and 10.6). Its miss is monotone in the extrapolation (0.0255 worst
inside the fitted range, −0.0584 at `C`=1.25328, −0.0955 at `C`=1), so it fails as an
extrapolation rather than as a fit. What predicts the cell is the bit-identity of 73.1% of the
columns, and that agreement conceals a sign inversion worth stating: `ffn_down`'s own control
step is **+0.0050** here against **−0.0060** at the 2048 → 512 pair.

**Most `KCHUNK` values are not rungs.** Of the 182 `k` that pass `rk76_k_single_ok` and give a
terminating walk on this model, greedy collapses **137** onto the `KCHUNK`=2048 rung's walk and
only **16 distinct `C`** exist; `KCHUNK`=1536 is one of the 137, a bit-identical replicate
rather than a new point. Print the whole map before choosing a value.

The forward population was exact (513024 columns; `Kc` 576:443904 256:34560 128:34560). The
whole-cell clip read 82.538% against a predicted 81.214%, and the miss belongs to the
prediction: it substituted the 2048 rung's `Kc`=1536 clip rate for the unsplit cell's.

**The bit-identity is the program's, not the columns'.** Block *L*'s `n_embd` GEMMs read
activations that passed through block *L*−1's `ffn_down`, so only block 0's columns are
identical in value once the other family is split. Five cells now share the `n_embd` walk
[512,64] — `KCHUNK` 2048, 1024, 768, 832 and 512 — and their `frozen` group's clip rate spans
**84.174% to 85.629%, 1.455 pp**, on identical arithmetic over identical 749568 columns.
Inverting a whole-cell clip rate for one chunk's contribution carries a `d/dr` lever of
**10.84** here, so that spread is **15.8 pp** on the result: no per-`Kc` rate is quotable out
of such an inversion, and any gap read off one is inside its own substitution error. The two
demonstrations that `Kc` is not a population label that compare rates measured *inside one
cell* are unaffected, as are every `Dcal` and `D_res` figure above, which are differences
between whole cells.

#### The `ffn_down` chunk-count dose-response is not monotone, and `C` does not index the control level

Two further rungs come from the one walk group that holds `n_embd` at [512,64] and moves only
`ffn_down`, so each is a matched pair against a cell already on disk.

**`KCHUNK`=832** gives `ffn_down` [832,512,128,64] — four chunks with `n_embd` split, the
matched-count complement of the 576 rung above. `Dcal` = **+0.38330**, a 1 → 4 step of
**+0.00689** against **−0.00509** for the same step with `n_embd` whole. Opposite signs. The
fixed-`n_embd` dose-response therefore reads +0.37641 / +0.37081 / +0.36230 / **+0.38330** at
chunk counts 1/2/3/4: **it reverses at the fourth chunk and lands above the one-chunk cell**,
and the three-point extrapolation that preceded it (a per-chunk step of −0.00560 then −0.00851,
apparently growing) missed by **0.0324 — 3.8x the largest measured step**. Do not fit a
`Dcal` law in the chunk count.

**`KCHUNK`=768** gives `ffn_down` [768,768] — the `KCHUNK`=1024 rung's `C` (2.00000), chunk
count (2), column population (818688) and `n_embd` walk exactly, differing only in the depth
mix (max 1024 → 768) and the rotation width, since `rk_hadamard_ok` admits 1024 and 512 at
full width and gives 768 only H_256 over three aligned blocks. `Dcal` = **+0.36823**, a move
of **−0.00258** — under a third of the smallest measured per-chunk step. **At held `C` and
held count the depth mix and rotation width are nearly inert on this channel**, which is what
makes the 832 rung's +0.021 specific to that walk rather than generic to a re-cut.

**The same pair moves the control level 0.0496 in PPL[4]** — 15.1990 against 15.2486, on two
cells identical in `C`, chunk count, population and `n_embd` walk. That column is the one the
chunk-count, maximum-depth and rotation fits were each fitted on, and their worst in-sample
residuals were 0.0255 / 0.0295 / 0.0262: the walk-specific variation at a *single* point of
`C` is **1.7-1.9x every one of them**. Perplexity here is deterministic, so the gap is the two
cells' difference and nothing else. **`C` does not index the control level**, measured
directly rather than by extrapolation — one pair at one `C`, which bounds those fits without
measuring a law. The two channels dissociate again inside the same two cells: over the two
calibrating windows the controls differ by only −0.00264 nats, 1.02x what `Dcal` moved, so the
whole 0.0496 lives in the non-calibrating windows.

`D_res` reads +0.00732 (832) and +0.01911 (768). The both-split cells now span
[−0.00783, +0.01911] against three one-or-neither cells at [+0.02164, +0.02376], so the
both-split collapse survives as a strict ordering over ten cells with its margin down from
0.0098 to 0.0025. Both forward populations were exact (887808 and 818688 columns, `Kc`
histograms exact).

`KCHUNK`=832 is the only measured cell on this model whose `ffn_down` walk goes below 128 —
it is the one carrying a 64-deep chunk — which is the standing candidate for the reversal
[hypothesis]. `KCHUNK` 960 ([960,512,64]) and 896 ([896,512,128]) join 512 ([3x512]) as a
three-cell family at `C`=2.08443, count 3 and `n_embd` [512,64], differing only in the minimum
chunk depth, and that is the pair structure the 768 rung has shown carries almost nothing on
its own.

[Registered before the six
cells; scored under a gate a gate refusing unless eight previously scored values
reproduce to 5e-4, with `armaj_frozen.py` for the frozen-group spread. Six cells, 95-105 s
each, backstop delta 0.]

Open: what sets the cross-model level, and whether the interaction is a property of the two
families or of chunk count against baseline.

[Registered, pushed before the cells;
scored under a gate the same seven-value gate. Three cells, 90-107 s each,
backstop delta 0.]

##### `BOOTMARGIN` covers a drift between the two calibrating forwards, and below 1.25 the no-cut response turns around

Four no-cut cells on Qwen2.5-1.5B span the whole margin axis at `-ub` 2048. There is no row cut
in any of them, so each one's whole reading is what the margin alone does:

| `BOOTMARGIN` | `Dcal` (windows 1-2) | clipped of 1852160 | what the cell is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.00 | **+0.04066** | 961471 (**51.911%**) | clipping-dominated; NOT a resolution reading |
| 1.25 | **-0.00761** | 4760 (0.257%) | near zero confound |
| 1.50 | 0 | — | the control, zero by construction |
| 2.25 | +0.00811 | 0 | zero confound |

[HW sweep, 2 new cells `rc=0`, `--chunks` 4, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, backstop 650 -> 650, taint 4608,
0 WARNINGs, 2026-08-22.]

**The margin exists to cover a drift between the two calibrating forwards, and that had never
been stated.** At no cut `est1` is pass one's maximum over *all* `M` rows, so the natural
reading is that pass two at margin 1.00 saturates exactly at its own maximum and barely clips.
It does not. Pass one's quantization changes every downstream activation, so pass two's
per-column maximum is a **different draw**, and at zero headroom **51.9% of columns exceed
it**. 1.25 covers all but 0.257%; 1.50 and 2.25 cover essentially everything. `BOOTMARGIN` is
headroom for that drift, not slack for nothing.

**So there is no zero-confound resolution cell below margin 1.5 by this construction** — a
no-cut cell stops being one exactly where the margin stops covering the drift, and `D_res(1.00)`
is not measurable this way.

**The no-cut response is non-monotone in the margin, and its minimum over the four measured
points is 1.25 rather than the library default 1.50.** `Dcal` = -0.00761 there, a real gain at
full rows on this model [calibrating windows only — this is not a converged-window result and
the shipped recommendation is untouched by it].

**Both forms this campaign has used for `D_res(m)` have the wrong SHAPE below the control
margin, not merely the wrong coefficient.** Linear in the quantum and quadratic in it are both
monotone through 1.5; at 1.25 they give -0.00270 and -0.00198 against a measured **-0.00761**
(2.82x and 3.84x, right sign), and at 1.00 they have the wrong sign entirely against +0.04066.
**`res_at_1p0`'s `-2/3 * D_res` — the correction inside every published `R` and every `h` — is
wrong in sign at the one margin it was written for.**

What this buys the rest of the campaign: four of the five margins at cut `M`/4 now have a
MEASURED no-cut cell, so `D_clip(cut, m) = Dcal(cut, m) - Dcal(no cut, m)` is measured minus
measured there. Over matched intervals that subtraction takes the margin exponent's local
spread from **1.322x** to **1.192x** — it tightens the axis toward a power law without
straightening it. Only 1.875, arm (y)'s own held-out point, stays modelled.

[Registered and pushed before the first cell; scored under the same gate.]

##### The row cut is not a pure cost — at `M`/2 it is a net accuracy gain, and the model does not reach there

`CALROWS`=1024 at `-ub` 2048 costs **`Dcal` = -0.00703** at margin 1.5 and **-0.01065** at
2.25 — *negative* at both, against `q_ctl`. Headline over 8 windows, **-0.382%** and
**-0.571%** (7.2257 and 7.2120 against a control 7.2534); converged windows 3-8 -0.00393 +/-
0.00268 and -0.00586 +/- 0.00340 se, 4 of 6 better in each [n=6, so the converged column
decides nothing on its own]. Both cells `rc=0`, `.so`/corpus/model md5-gated, backstop delta
**0**. The dial is read back out of the cells' own bytes rather than off the launcher (rule
146): `r` on the CLEAN set of each perplexity process's own CALMAP is **0.94335**, strictly
between the no-cut 1.0 and the `M`/4 cut's 0.87151, so 1024 rows were used.

So the damage is **non-monotone in the cut**. Along margin 1.5 as the cut deepens, `Dcal` runs
**0 -> -0.0070 -> +0.1276 -> +0.6761 -> +1.9985** at cuts 2048 / 1024 / 512 / 128 / 32, and
along 2.25 it runs **+0.0081 -> -0.0107 -> +0.0266 -> +0.1052**. There is a zero crossing
between `M`/2 and `M`/4 on both margins, and the shipped `M`/4 setting sits past an optimum
rather than at the cheap end of a rising cost.

**A row cut is an accidental percentile-clipping calibrator** [hypothesis — the sign, the
margin ordering and the crossing are measured; this mechanism is not isolated]. Pass one's
`est1` is a per-column maximum, and cutting its rows makes that maximum an under-estimate, so
`sc2 = 127/(est1*margin)` gets FINER on every column while a few columns clip. Below some cut
the finer quantum on the bulk is worth more than the clipped tail — which is why the gain is
**larger at the coarser margin** (-0.0107 at 2.25 against -0.0070 at 1.5, where the baseline
quantum is already fine and there is less to win). It is the same trade a percentile or
MSE-optimal clipping calibrator makes deliberately.

**Two consequences for the model above.** Its `g` cannot be a smooth positive function of the
cut, so it is bounded to `M`/4 and deeper and must not be extrapolated shallower. And
`D_res = 0.0081*(R-1)/0.5` with `R = (margin/1.5)*r` is refuted as a resolution index for
`R < 1.5`: two cells that clip essentially nothing read **+0.0081 at R = 1.50** and
**-0.0107 at R = 1.42**, so `D_res` is not monotone in `R` and the margin and the cut do not
reach the returned surface through one combined index. The margin rescales `est1` by a
constant on every column; a row cut rescales it by a column-dependent amount. Every `D_clip`
in the table above inherits that, so read them as `Dcal` minus a *model*, not as a measured
split.

##### The gain is a knife-edge at `M`/2, and it is narrower than a four-point interpolation can see

Bracketing that minimum at margin 2.25 puts a cell on each side of `M`/2 and both come back
**positive**. The whole cut axis at the shipped margin, `Dcal` against `q_ctl`:

| cut | 2048 | **1536** | 1024 | **768** | 512 | 128 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `M`/ | 1 | 1.33 | 2 | 2.67 | 4 | 16 |
| `d` = 1-`r` | 0 | 0.020650 | 0.053643 | 0.090150 | 0.128489 | 0.255830 |
| `Dcal` | +0.00811 | **+0.00335** | -0.01065 | **+0.00931** | +0.02656 | +0.10521 |
| vs no cut | 0 | -0.00476 | -0.01876 | +0.00120 | +0.01845 | +0.09710 |

So the minimum IS at `M`/2 and the well around it is **sharp**: one rung shallower (a 25%
cut rather than 50%) keeps only **25.4%** of the gain, and one rung deeper (62.5%) keeps
**-6.4%** of it — 768 is already worse than not cutting at all. 8-window headlines, which are
not converged costs, run 7.2534 / 7.2404 / 7.2120 / 7.2648 at 2048 / 1536 / 1024 / 768.
[HW, 2 cells, `rc=0`, `.so`/corpus/model md5-gated, backstop delta 0, 2026-08-22]

**No interpolation of the four cells that preceded these reaches either value, and all three
got the SIGN wrong at 1536.** A cubic in `d`, a two-term `0.00811 - K d + C d^k` in `d`, and a
cubic in ln(`M`/`M1`) — the same four points in three variables — predict -0.0053, -0.0193 and
-0.0091 at cut 1536 against a measured **+0.00335**. They spread 3.6x among themselves and lie
on the wrong side of zero from the measurement, because the feature they were interpolating
across is narrower than their own knot spacing. **A row-cut curve on this axis must be
measured at the cut it is quoted at**; four knots an octave apart are not a curve here.

**The mixed-`M` population is bigger than the record said and does not explain the dip.**
Under `llama-perplexity` at `-ub` 2048, **352000 of 1852160 calibrated columns (19.0%)** sit at
`M`=1024, not the "4 of 197 weights" the record carried; `llama-bench`'s prompt gives a uniform
`M`=2048, which is why `r` above is the `M`=2048 population's. Since `CALROWS` clamps only when
it is strictly below `M`, that group is uncut at both 1536 and 1024 and first cut at 768 — so
the mixture predicts a kink at 768 and nothing at all at 1024, while the minimum is at 1024.
Re-weighting `d` by the mixture (0.0167 / 0.0435 / 0.0769 / 0.1143 at 1536 / 1024 / 768 / 512)
rescales the abscissa and leaves the dip exactly as sharp. **The dip survives every
reparametrisation tried and is not an artefact of the mixture.**

**And the gain is Qwen's, not the route's.** SmolLM2-1.7B at the same cut and margin, against
its own `-c` 2048 control (a `-c` 2048 run re-segments the corpus, so no `-c` 512 level and no
other model's control is comparable), reads `Dcal` = **+0.03770** where Qwen reads **-0.01065**
— the opposite sign at **3.5x** the magnitude, headline 7.4427 against 7.4265 (**+0.218%**)
where Qwen's is -0.571%. Both cells `rc=0`, md5-gated, backstop delta 0. The next section
completes that model's cut axis and says where the difference lives.
[HW, 2 cells, 2026-08-22]

**What this does to the shipping case: `CALROWS`=1024 at `-ub` 2048 is not shippable.** It is
the only setting on this axis that beats the control at margin 2.25, its advantage does not
survive a 25% move of the `CALROWS`/`M` ratio in either direction, and on the second model ever
run at this ubatch it is a **cost** rather than a gain. `CALROWS` is an ABSOLUTE row count, so
every ubatch change moves that ratio. **Two models is the whole corpus at `-ub` 2048** — quote
the `M`/2 gain as Qwen's own number and nothing more. [The margin-1.5 axis has no 1536 or 768
cell, so the well's width there is unmeasured.]

##### The second model has no optimum at all, and the resolution channel is 2.54x model-specific

Three more SmolLM2-1.7B cells complete that model's margin-2.25 cut axis, each against its own
`-c` 2048 control (PPL[8] 7.4265). Beside Qwen's:

| cut | `M`/ | Qwen `Dcal` | Qwen ppl8 | SmolLM2 `Dcal` | SmolLM2 ppl8 | SmolLM2 clip |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2048 | 1 (none) | +0.00811 | -0.290% | **+0.02060** | -0.606% | **0.0000%** |
| 1536 | 1.33 | +0.00335 | -0.179% | **+0.02405** | +0.191% | 0.0294% |
| 1024 | 2 | -0.01065 | -0.571% | +0.03770 | +0.218% | — |
| 512 | 4 | +0.02656 | +0.301% | **+0.05278** | +0.063% | 0.3705% |

**SmolLM2's damage is monotone in the cut depth and the model has no optimum** — every cut is
worse than not cutting — where Qwen's has a minimum at `M`/2. **So the dip is Qwen's alone, and
there is no cut that is free-or-better on both models at `-ub` 2048.** Subtracting each model's
own no-cut level leaves the row cut's own term, and it is **opposite in sign** at both shallow
cuts: -0.00477 against +0.00345 at 1536, and -0.01876 against +0.01710 at `M`/2. At `M`/4 both
models pay, +0.01845 against +0.03218. [HW, 3 cells, `rc=0`, `.so`/corpus/model md5-gated,
backstop delta 0, 2026-08-22]

**The no-cut cell clips exactly zero columns on BOTH models, so it measures the resolution
channel with its confound nulled rather than merely small** — 0 of 1500160 and 0 of 1769472 —
and it costs **+0.00811 against +0.02060, a 2.54x model difference**. A third model reads
**+0.01626** at the same zero confound (0 of 1272320 columns), so the channel spans **2.00x**
across three models and does not follow the margin index, which two of the three share to
0.9%. Splitting the models' gap at the one cut both ran gives
**25.8% margin channel and 74.2% cut channel**: both differ, and the cut channel carries three
quarters.

**The mixed-`M` population differs between the models and must come out before a magnitude is
quoted.** Read off each model's own control CALMAP, the `M`=1024 minority is **19.0% on Qwen
(352000 of 1852160), 8.57% on SmolLM2-1.7B (151552 of 1769472), 24.35% on Llama-3.2-1B
(309760 of 1272320) and 16.96% on SmolLM2-360M (110464 of 651264)** — a 2.8x spread that does
not follow width, since the two models sharing `n_embd`/`n_ff` sit at the two ENDS of it — and
since `CALROWS` clamps only when it is strictly below `M`, more of Qwen's columns escape uncut
at a given cut.

###### The population is the output head plus one block's FFN, and it is exactly derivable from the GGUF header

Cross-tabbing a control CALMAP by GEMM group rather than only by `M` says what the minority
IS. It is not scattered through the model: every block's q/k/v/o sits at `M`=2048, and the
`M`=1024 rows are **the output head plus exactly ONE block's gate, up and down**. The
mechanism is upstream of this driver — `llama-perplexity` asks for logits on only half of each
2048-token window, so ggml prunes the graph to those output rows before the final block's FFN;
everything above the prune runs at the full ubatch.

One column is logged per output column of each offloaded GEMM, and a K-split `ffn_down` logs
its `N` columns once **per chunk**, so per calibrating call

    per_block = 2*n_embd + 2*head_kv*head_dim + 2*n_ff + n_embd*chunks(n_ff)
    total     = n_blocks*per_block + vocab
    minority  = vocab + 2*n_ff + n_embd*chunks(n_ff)

where `chunks(K)` is `rk76_ksplit`'s greedy walk down `RK76_KCHUNK_PREF` and is 1 for any `K`
the part takes whole. **This reproduces all ten measured integers — five minority counts and
five totals, over five models — exactly, not approximately.** Both second-order terms are
load-bearing: the head alone gives 16.41 / 5.56 / 20.16 / 15.09% against the measured 19.005 /
8.565 / 24.346 / 16.961%, and dropping the per-chunk `ffn_down` logging gives 21.50 / 9.82 /
29.04 / 16.96%.

**The fifth model was predicted from its header before its control cell ran and landed on both
integers**: SmolLM2-135M (576/1536, 30 blocks, `head_kv` 3, vocab 49152) was registered at
total **409344** and minority **105600**, share **25.797%**, and measured 409344 / 105600. That
share is a new extreme — the five now run 8.565 / 16.961 / 19.005 / 24.346 / 25.797% — and it
is the smallest model that sits highest, because the dominant term is `vocab/total` and a
smaller model divides the same vocabulary by less.

So the share is set by the **vocabulary size**, the FFN width and the `ffn_down` chunk count.
It follows neither width nor depth because its dominant term is `vocab/total`, and the
vocabulary is an axis independent of both — which is why the two models sharing `n_embd`/`n_ff`
(vocab 49152 and 128256) sit at opposite ends of the spread. **Compute it from the header
before quoting any cross-model calibration magnitude**; there is no need to spend a CALMAP pass
on it. [Derived from the four control CALMAPs and the GGUF headers, `armaa_mixedm.py`, and
confirmed as a forward prediction on a fifth, 2026-08-22. What it does not establish: which block the FFN belongs to is inferred from the
prune, not read from the `key` column; and the rule is a statement about `llama-perplexity` at
`-ub` 2048, not about `llama-bench`, where the `M` column is uniform.]
Modelling the damage as a function of the per-column ratio alone and adding across the two
groups takes the shipped fraction's model ratio from a raw **1.74x** to **1.19x** [derived, not
measured — it is a linearisation, and no independent consistency check exists inside the data].
**It cannot touch the opposite signs**, because re-weighting a population multiplies the cut
term by a positive factor and a positive factor cannot change a sign.

**`Dcal` and the 8-window headline disagree in direction across the two models**, which is what
the calibrating-window scope means in practice: at `M`/4 SmolLM2 costs **1.99x** Qwen's `Dcal`
while its headline is 5x smaller (+0.063% against +0.301%). The margin alone at full rows is a
headline **gain** on both models (-0.290% and -0.606%) while costing on the calibrating windows
— so `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 does not trade accuracy away outside the windows it repairs. The
converged column (windows 3-8) is negative in 9 of the 10 cells either model has run here, but
at n=6 per cell and with a shared control those are not independent readings and none resolves.

[**What this cannot see**: any margin but 2.25; any ubatch but 2048; a converged cost — `Dcal`
is the two calibrating windows' damage and a positive still owes a depth cell; decode; a third
model, since heterogeneity at `-ub` 512 is Q = 6.665 on 2 df; and SmolLM2's behaviour between
`M`/2 and `M`/4, where Qwen's zero crossing sits. It says WHERE the models differ — in a
cut-indexed term or not — and not why: a carrier indexed by neither, and the two models differ
in `n_embd`/`n_ff` (1536/8960 against 2048/8192), would land in the no-cut cell identically.]

**A saturating form in the clip fraction is refuted.** `D_clip = A*x/(x + x0)` with A = 0.1434
holds the four cells at `x` <= 4.35 to 12%, and its supremum is A at every `x`: `q_r128` and
`q_r32` sit **4.86x** and **14.13x** above it, and at margin 1.875 it reads **1.65x** high.
Clipping is the carrier, but no function of the clip fraction alone predicts the damage.

**Severity is not a discriminator either.** `q_r512` and `q_shipfrac` clip by the same relative
amount — mean 1.11485 against 1.11564, **0.07% apart** — at **52x** different clip counts, so
a cut cell's saturated columns always sit about a tenth over the ceiling and only their NUMBER
moves. **Nor is the mass, nor the depth the mass sits at.** Normalising `D_clip` by clip count,
by clipped mass, and by mass weighted with the blocks downstream of each clipped column
spreads **13.51x**, **6.85x** and **6.93x** across the three cut cells; the depth profile of
the clipped mass is near-identical between them (22-30% in `blk.0-3`, 36-47% in `blk.18-27`).

**The `llama-bench` prompt under-states the clip fraction 2.0-5.5x against the wiki corpus.**
Every clip fraction here comes from `llama-bench`, whose prompt is synthetic; every `Dcal`
comes from the corpus. At the one cut where both regimes now have a CALMAP, the corpus clips
**2.7975%** against the bench's 1.3775% at margin 1.5 (**2.03x**) and **0.0968%** against
0.0177% at 2.25 (**5.47x**); at `M`/4 and margin 1.875 it is 1.6602% against 0.4963%
(**3.35x**). The undershoot `r` transports far better — 0.94335 corpus against 0.94636 bench
at `M`/2 (0.32%) and 0.90584 against 0.87151 at `M`/4 (3.9%). **So quote a clip fraction with
its prompt named, and treat a `llama-bench` figure as a lower bound**; `r` may be carried
across. The fitted model above is indexed by cut and margin rather than by the clip fraction,
so the seam does not reach it.

**`AROW` does not move the clip fraction by a constant, and it does not even keep its sign.**
It moves the no-cut control **8.6x** (0.2600% -> 0.0303%), `q_r512` **1.85x**, `q_shipfrac`
**3.45x**, `q_r128` **1.15x** — and `q_r32` **0.923x**, where the flag *increases* clipping
(35.4030% -> 38.3480%). A single regime factor cannot carry a clip fraction across the cut
axis, and one fitted on the shallow cuts extrapolates through 1 before the deep ones.

**The clip fraction at any margin is computable off-line from a fixed cut's bytes.** Pass one's
`est1` is margin-free given its input, so a column clips exactly when the oracle's uncapped
colmax exceeds `est1*margin`, and both vectors are on disk for every cut already run. Two
controls bound it: `est1` is identical on the CLEAN set (**0 of 4096**) and drifts on **7.25%**
of all columns between margins 1.5 and 2.25 — concentrated on small-code columns, p50 of the
relative move 1/3 — because a calibrating call's returned surface is the next layer's input;
and re-evaluating the sweep against the same cut's other `est1` vector moves the predicted
clip by **0.9%** at margin 1.875, rising to 9.1% at 3.0 where the counts get small. Against
the device it reads **1.6%** high at margin 1.5, **1.7%** low at 2.25 and **5.7%** high at
1.875. The cells that produce it are `llama-bench`, whose prompt is synthetic, while every
`Dcal` comes from the wiki corpus — a regime seam beside `AROW` that nothing yet measures.

**`est2` is a readback, not the accumulator.** Pass two writes `est2[n] = max(1, max_m |C8|) /
sc2[n]` with `sc2 = 127/(est1*margin)`, and sets `sat[n]` at code 127
(`ggml-rocket.cpp:2013-2027`). So it is capped at the ceiling on a saturated column, quantized
to the returned surface's own code — margin/127 of `est1`, 1.77% at margin 2.25 — and floored
at one code. A no-cut cell at margin 2.25 saturates nothing, which makes its `est2` the only
uncapped per-column maximum on record and the one instrument that can price severity.

`Dcal` is reconstructible from any cell's own window line and is not carried between sessions:
a `llama-perplexity` `[k]` value is the cumulative PPL over the first k windows, so window k's
nats are `k*ln(P[k]) - (k-1)*ln(P[k-1])` and `Dcal` is the arm-minus-control sum over the two
calibrating windows. That reproduces four independently scored values to < 5e-4 nats.

[HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-22. Three CALMAP cells (172-181 s) and one perplexity cell (600 s),

WARNINGs; the off-line sweeps took no device time. The two cells that refute the saturating
form were run in an earlier arm and cost only their CALMAP. Controls, each a magnitude: the
oracle saturates 0 of 1500160 columns; where a cell did not clip, the oracle reproduces its own
`est2` to a clean-set p90 of **1.1%**, the readback quantum's own size; it reproduces each
cell's recorded `sat` flag on **99.805-100%** of clean-set columns against a 12.7% count error
at depth; and the `Dcal` reconstruction above. **What it cannot see**: any model but
Qwen2.5-1.5B, any ubatch but 2048, decode, the converged column (`Dcal` is the
calibrating-window damage), the 4 of 197 weights that calibrate at `M`/2 under
`llama-perplexity` but not under `llama-bench`, whether `h` keeps its shape outside
[1.5, 2.25] — 1.875 is an interpolation in margin though an extrapolation in `x` — and any
cut between `M` and `M`/4, where `g` is unmeasured.]

The ubatch does not set `M` uniformly under `llama-perplexity`: 4 of 197 weights —
`blk.27.ffn_{down,gate,up}` and `token_embd`, the last block's FFN and the output head, which
llama.cpp runs on a reduced token set — calibrate at `M`/2. That holds at both ubatches, so
it dilutes the contrast symmetrically. Under `llama-bench` the `M` column is uniform.

##### What the row cut saves is a FORMULA in the ubatch, and it is 18% at `-ub` 512

The saving is **linear in the rows the cut removes**, so it is a property of `M` and not of
the dial's setting alone. At `M`=512 with `NCAL` at its shipped 2 — the accuracy cells'
configuration, CALMAP off — `CALROWS`=128 + `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 removes **17.93%** of
`calibrate` per calibrating call (105.02 -> 86.19 ms; 18.02% common-mode-corrected, 18.13%
drift-corrected, 18.08% on a reversed repeat) = **7.42 s once per process** over 394
calibrating calls. **`BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 alone is free** at -0.17% once the common-mode process
shift its own `entry` bucket reports is removed, and its submit count is identical to the
control's.

| `CALROWS` | `calibrate`/calibrating call | saving | submits |
|---|---|---|---|
| full `M`=512 | 105.02 ms | — | 12828 |
| 256 | 93.21 ms | 11.24% | 10868 |
| **128** (`M`/4, shipped candidate) | **86.19 ms** | **17.93%** | **9710** |
| 64 | 82.23 ms | 21.70% | **9710** |

Fitting the three cut points through the origin gives **`s(M,M1) = (M - M1) / (1099 + 2M)`**
in rows. Fitted entirely at `M`=512 it predicts the two `M`=2048 figures at **29.6%** and
**37.0%** against measured **28.2%** (`CALROWS`=512) and **36.3%** (`CALROWS`=128) — within
1.4 points across a 4x change in `M`, a different instrument state and a different epoch. So
the lever's value scales with the ubatch: **~18% at `-ub` 512, ~37% at 2048, ~43% at 4096**
[the last two extrapolated]. The **ceiling** at `-ub` 512 as `CALROWS` -> 0 is **24.1%**, and
`M`/4 already takes **74.3%** of it — against a recorded +19.15% perplexity cost at `M`/16,
so `M`/4 is near-optimal on price as well as on accuracy.

**The submit count floors at 128 rows and the saving does not.** `r128` and `r64` emit the
identical 9710 submits, yet `calibrate` still falls 86.19 -> 82.23 ms across that floor, 13x
the repeat spread. Above 128 rows the saving is submit-proportional to 0.2% — 6.025 / 6.037 /
5.941 us per removed submit on three cells — and below it a second term carries it
[hypothesis: the row tile is quantised at 128 for SUBMIT COUNT while device time inside a
submit still scales with the actual rows]. **A row-count sweep that reads only the submit
count would call the dial dead below 128 and be wrong by 4 ms a call.**

**`NCAL` does not change this fraction.** It changes how many calibrating calls a process
makes, not what one costs: the shipped `NCAL`=2 reads -17.9% here against **-17.2%** measured
at `NCAL`=1 two epochs earlier (86.1 -> 71.4 ms a calibrating call, submits 10690 -> 9131), so
the 28.2 -> 17.2 gap between the recorded figures is `M`, not `NCAL`. The 4-window aggregates
in these cells (8.7163 / 8.7435 / 9.0511) are the calibration-window artefact above and are
**not** accuracy reads. [HW, H96 MAX M9,
seven `--chunks 4` cells, `setarch -R` armed by readback, one `.so`/corpus/model all md5-gated,
backstop delta 0, 2026-08-21. **What it cannot see**: decode, another model, the pool on, an
unpinned layout, and the accuracy of any row count but 128.]

#### `NCAL`=1 does not reach either model, and it buys a whole calibration forward

Dropping the second calibration forward changes what the **frozen** scale is built from, so
unlike the row subsample its exposure lands in the converged windows, where no margin
reaches. On Qwen2.5-1.5B at `--chunks 8`, against the same-build `NCAL`=2 control of 9.9348:

| Qwen2.5-1.5B, `AROW`=1, K-split on | PPL | paired d(NLL) | windows 3-8 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `NCAL`=1 | 9.9702 | +0.0036 +/- 0.0039 (se) | +0.0028 +/- 0.0050, 4 of 6 positive |
| `NCAL`=1 + `CALROWS`=128 / `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 | 9.9501 | +0.0015 +/- 0.0071 | -0.0055 +/- 0.0071 |

Neither is resolvable, and the readout's 0.061%-of-columns objection would have needed
roughly **18x** the measured windows-3-8 shift to show. The saturation report — which *is*
live in the converged phase, unlike the calibrating branch that feeds it `NULL` — stayed
silent in both cells, bounding the converged saturating fraction under its 0.1% threshold.

**A second model agrees, and the depth this took is the finding.** SmolLM2-1.7B — the model
whose `ffn_down` is `K`=8192 and which paid **4.2x** more than Qwen for a frozen per-tensor
scale (6.77x against 1.600x) — was run at both window counts, same `.so`, same boot:

| SmolLM2-1.7B, `AROW`=1, K-split on | windows | `ctl` | `NCAL`=1 | converged windows |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `--chunks 8` | 8 | 10.3354 | 10.4800 (**+1.40%**) | +0.0148 +/- 0.0113, 3 of 6 positive |
| every window the text has | 29 | 8.5473 | 8.5735 (**+0.31%**) | **+0.0024 +/- 0.0044**, 13 of 27 |

At depth the effect is **0.55 se** from zero and within **0.06 se** of Qwen's +0.0028, so the
two models agree **on this term** and the 4.2x factor does not appear in it. Whether that
factor survives the per-row scale on any other term is a separate question: the cell that
would answer it, `AROW` against `nosplit`, is an 8-window difference — see below.
**Eight windows is a measured-inadequate instrument on this route.** The
29-window run reproduces the 8-window arm's six per-window differences *exactly*, so nothing
changed but the sample — the +1.40% headline, and a cross-model ratio of 3.9x that matched the
rival's predicted 4.2x, were both properties of which windows the text starts with, wrong by
**4.5x**. Every 8-window number in this corpus inherits that doubt, Qwen's own +0.36%
included; a Qwen depth pair is unrun. The aggregate itself is exact rather than noisy —
`ctl2` reproduces `ctl` at **+0.0000 on every window**, on both models — so the uncertainty is
the window sample's alone.

**The row subsample transfers to the second model as well**, and to its own framing: its
entire cost there is again the one CALIBRATING window (**+0.0241** nats at window 1 against
Qwen's +0.0296, same window, same sign) and it stays independent of `NCAL` at **-0.0004 +/-
0.0040** over windows 2-8 — the best-resolved number in that arm, refuting composition at 5
se. Measured composed with `NCAL`=1; a `CALROWS`-alone cell at the shipped `NCAL`=2 on this
model is unrun.

One caution this retires rather than confirms: SmolLM2's `ctl` returned 10.3354 on a
*different* build from the one that first recorded 10.3354, where two Qwen builds spanned
0.28%. That spread is build-specific arithmetic that reached one model and not the other, not
a general between-build error bar.

**The two dials are independent, measured rather than assumed.** A second forward can repair
a column whose subsampled pass-one estimate under-read it, so the row cut's frozen-scale
damage might have composed with `NCAL`=1; it does not. The composed cell differs from plain
`NCAL`=1 over windows 2-8 by **-0.0065 +/- 0.0074 (se)**, and the row cut's calibration-window
cost reproduces exactly — window 1 is **+0.0296** nats here and +0.0296 at `NCAL`=2.

**Window 1 is byte-identical under both settings** (`calibrating` is `cal_done < ncal`, and 0
is below both 1 and 2; the freeze happens after the surface is written). Its d(NLL) is
+0.000000, which is a free control inside every `NCAL` cell — and its own wall time is that
process's noise floor on identical work, measured here at **5.4%**.

**The price, at the buckets rather than at a wall.** `NCAL`=1 replaces one calibrating call
per weight with a converged one, so the saving is `calibrate`-per-call minus `entry`-per-call,
not the `calibrate` bucket:

| per frontend call, `-ub` 512 | `NCAL`=2 | `NCAL`=1 |
|---|---:|---:|
| calibrating (`calibrate`/`cal_calls`) | 117.01 ms | 115.38 ms |
| converged (`entry`/converged calls) | 58.14 ms | 57.09 ms |

The two arms agree to 1.4% and 1.8%, and the ratio is **2.01x** — one bootstrap pass per
extra device pass, which is what the code emits. Net **58.6 ms** per replaced call over the
model's **197** offloaded GEMM sites projects **11.5 s**, against a measured offloaded-path
delta of **11.6 s** (90.2 -> 78.6 s): closure **0.994**.

That saving is **paid once per process and does not scale with prompt length** — the -12.9%
above is a share of a four-window run's offloaded path, not of a deployment. Do not quote it
as one. The run **wall** moved the other way (4.38 -> 4.62 min) and carries no signal: window
1's identical work differed 5.4% between the two processes and the totals differed 5.5%, so
the wall pair is entirely inside the process noise floor (rule 105). `quant+rot`, a bucket
this arm cannot reach, moved +5.5% in the same pair.

[HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-12. The profiled `--chunks 4` cells reproduce the unprofiled `--chunks 8` runs'
window-4 cumulative to four digits in both arms (8.7163 and 8.7238), so `ROCKET_MM_PROFILE`
does not perturb the output. **What it cannot see**: one model, 8 windows of one text, one
`-ub`, decode, and whether `CALROWS`'s own -28.2% still holds at `NCAL`=1 — that ratio was
measured at `NCAL`=2 and its composition here is [expected].]

#### The calibration regime is 2.6x the converged one, and a short bench never leaves it

`ROCKET_RK3576_NCAL` defaults to **2**, and a calibration forward is **two** device passes
(the colmax bootstrap), so the first two micro-batches each weight sees cost about twice
what every later one does. Measured per frontend call: **94.2 ms calibrating against 36.4 ms
converged at `M`=512** — 2.59x, not 2.0x, because the bootstrap's own host work is outside
the entry.

**THE `calibrate` BUCKET IS NOT THE REMOVABLE COST — SUBTRACT THE `entry` IT DISPLACES.** A
calibrating call returns before the converged path's device call, so it never pays the
`entry` bucket at all. The removable term is `calibrate - entry` = **171.3 - 47.5 = 123.8 s**
a process, against a directly observed excess of the two calibrating forwards over a
converged one of **127.8 s** — a 3.2% residual, which is the calibrating path's own
`dequant` and the scale loops. Quoting the 171.3 s bucket as a saving over-states it by
1.39x, and the same subtraction is what any bucket standing in for a branch needs.

**THIS IS A MEASUREMENT TRAP, NOT A DEFECT.** `llama-bench` runs one untimed warmup plus
`-r` timed reps, so how much of a bench is calibration is set by how many micro-batches a
pass contains — which is `-p / -ub`. At `-p 2048`:

| `-ub` | forwards per weight, `-r 1` | of which calibration |
|---|---:|---:|
| 512 | 8 | 2 (25%) |
| 1024 | 4 | 2 (50%) |
| 2048 | 2 | **2 (100%)** |

So a `-ub` sweep at `-r 1` varies the ubatch AND the calibration fraction together, and the
reported t/s falls 12.07 -> 11.49 -> 9.67 in exactly the direction that makes the larger
ubatch look worse. **A `pp512 -r 1` bench of this route is 100% calibration regime** — every
offloaded matmul runs the device twice. Read the `entry` and `calibrate` buckets, which
separate the regimes by construction, rather than the wall; or give the run enough reps that
the converged path dominates.

**The accounting closes on both paths.** Frontend `entry` against the library's own
accounted total for the same calls agrees to **2.2-5.0%** on the converged path. The
calibration path once left **27% at `M`=512 rising to 48% at `M`=2048** unbucketed; that gap
was the bootstrap's per-column maximum scan, it now has its own `calscan` bucket, and the
term itself is spent (1.0% of `calibrate`).

**One-time weight preparation is 26.7-29.7 s** (`wt_rotate` 15.6-17.1 s, `wt_quant`
11.0-12.6 s over 166 weights), paid once at first use and cached by name, so it lands in
`llama-bench`'s untimed warmup and in a real user's first prompt.

**Building `ggml-rocket` for this part needs `-DGGML_ROCKET_NATIVE_FP16=OFF`.** The option
defaults ON and adds `-march=armv8.2-a+fp16` for the RK3588's A76/A55; this part is
**ARMv8.0-A** (`/proc/cpuinfo` has neither `asimdhp` nor `asimddp`) and the resulting `.so`
would SIGILL in the convert kernels.

### The route's accuracy, measured on the part inside a model: 1.011x perplexity

`llama-perplexity` over the first 8 chunks of wikitext-2 test at `n_ctx`=512, same GGUF,
same text, same boot, CPU arm then NPU arm:

| arm | wikitext-2 PPL (8 chunks) | wall |
|---|---:|---:|
| CPU only, F16 | 9.7872 +- 0.58866 | 13.01 min |
| NPU, W8A8 | 9.8969 +- 0.59556 | 6.55 min |

**1.011x**, and the wall ratio (1.99x) corroborates the `pp512` bench's 2.01x on the same
denominator. This is the **first accuracy number for the route taken off the device inside
a model**: every 1.008x / 1.020x before it is host arithmetic over a simulator.

**It scores the block-diagonal rotation.** Every offloaded call here is `K`=1536, which is
neither a power of two nor `60*2^k`, so all of them take the block-diagonal Hadamard
fallback — the construction that lives as a static in `ggml-rocket.cpp` and that no gate in
`rocket-userspace` can link. It composes: against the unrotated route's chaos (a 2%
per-column divisor change took SmolLM2 from 10.58 to 2.6e10) a 1.1% move is nothing.

**It is NOT the same quantity as the simulator's 1.008x** and the two must not be diffed:
that one is against **fp32** with an **exactly frozen** colmax; this one is against the same
GGUF's **F16 CPU** arm with the **shipped bootstrap** at `CALSAFE`=3.0, over 8 chunks whose
own error bar is +-0.59. What it does say is that nothing on the composed device route
costs a model an order of magnitude.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, llama.cpp b10356, Qwen2.5-1.5B F16, measured 2026-08-11]

### Two things stop a GEMM frontend on this part, and both are the datapath rather than the speed

**`K` past one contraction does not merely cost more — at prefill scale it wedges the
NPU.** At `M=512 K=8192 N=2048` (plane 32x16, K slice 4608, 2 slices, N tile 256, narrow
writer, 256 submits) `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` returns `-4` after the write guard
spends its eight power cycles, and the part raises the driver's DMA-error interrupt:
`WARNING: rocket_job.c … rocket_job_irq_handler`, which is one of that handler's two
`WARN_ON`s over `PC_INTERRUPT_RAW_STATUS_DMA_{READ,WRITE}_ERROR`. The NPU is then unusable
**across processes** — shapes that ran clean minutes earlier come back `-4` — and a
`rmmod`/`modprobe` does not recover it: core 0 then fails to probe with `-22` alongside
`rk_iommu … FORCE_RESET command timed out`. **Only a reboot clears it.** Treat any
measurement taken after one of these as contaminated.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, one shape, 2026-08-07]

**`rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` now declines that `K` instead of routing onto it**,
returning `ROCKET_E_UNSUPPORTED` with nothing submitted and no BO allocated: the wedge
shape comes back in a 64 ms process (21 MB of that is generating the operands), the
`dmesg` WARNING delta is 0, and a control shape run immediately afterwards in the same
boot computes at 26.1 ms. A caller that needs a larger `K` runs it on the host.
`ROCKET_RK3576_MM_KSPLIT=1` takes the route anyway, for measurement.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-08]

**The refusal covers the whole class, and it has to, because nothing narrower is
measured.** Small cells in the class compute — `M=32 K=8192 N=128` and `M=16 K=16384
N=64` ran green in the gate list for several sessions, and `M=64 K=8192 N=32` scored
exact three times — but every axis separating them from the wedge moves together, so no
boundary can be fitted to them: submits **2 / 4 / 4 against 256**, weight cube **2304 /
1152 / 576 KiB against 4608**, per-submit output surface **16 / 4 / 8 KiB against 512**.
All four share `ks`=4608 and a 144 KiB resident slice. The route's own coverage stays in
the gate list through `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_i32()`, which is called directly and
carries the same warning in its header.
[pure, `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c cell`, 2026-08-08]

**The class is `K >= 6176`, not `K > 4608`, and the threshold does not move with `M` or
`N`.** The single-task planner shrinks the output-channel tile by halves to 32 channels
before it gives up, so the boundary is not the K-slice cap — it is the CBUF pool at a
one-group weight cube, which is a condition on `K` alone. Enumerated from the pure planner
over thirty `(M, N)` cells, `M` 64-2048 and `N` 512-8192, the smallest 32-multiple `K`
taking the int32 K split is **6176 at every one of the thirty**.
[pure enumeration, `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c map`, 2026-08-06]

**Between the two thresholds the output tile collapses in one step, and the cost is a
silent 2.7x — not the 32x its submit count suggests.** `r76_weight_slice_cap()` is stated over the *group count* while the resident
slice it is compared against, `32*K`, does not depend on the tile at all — so once `32*K`
