passes the two-group cap of 156 KiB there is no tile left but 32 channels. The ladder:

| `K` | largest output tile | why |
|---|---|---|
| `<= 4608` | up to 2048 | `32*K <= 144 KiB`, the >= 4 group cap |
| `4609-4736` | 96 | 3 groups, 148 KiB |
| `4737-4992` | 64 | 2 groups, 156 KiB |
| `4993-6144` | **32** | one group: only the CBUF pool binds |
| `>= 6176` | none | the K split |

At `M=512 N=2048` that is **32 submits at `K`=4608 and 1024 at `K`=5120**; at `N`=4096, 64
against 2048. Nothing refuses and nothing computes wrong, and a frontend that sizes a
model by "does it run" will not see it.
[pure enumeration, `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c map`, 2026-08-06]

**The band computes, and the wall says a submit count is not a cost.** At `M=512 N=2048`,
one process a cell and three processes a `K`, `taskset -c 4` on `ondemand`:

| `K` | tile | submits | wall, median of 3 processes | exact |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| 4608 | 1024 | 32 | 72.2 / 71.5 / 71.9 ms | 3 of 3 |
| 5120 | 32 | 1024 | 191.7 / 175.3 / 191.3 ms | 3 of 3 |
| 6144 | 32 | 1024 | 184.5 / 203.4 / 182.4 ms | 3 of 3 |

So the collapse costs **2.41-2.86x over the rep extremes, 2.66x on medians** — 32x the
submits buying 2.7x the wall, because the marginal cost of the 992 extra submits is
**120 us each** where a wide-tile submit costs 2.25 ms. **A per-submit cost is not a
floor: it scales with the output tile**, and the "~1.4 ms per submit whatever it carries"
figure is a wide-tile number that must not be carried into this band.

**Inside the band the wall is FLAT in `K`.** `K`=6144 carries 20% more MACs than `K`=5120
and reads the same 182-203 ms, so the collapsed band is dispatch-bound rather than
MAC-bound and the extra contraction depth is free. The band is still worth offloading —
scaling ggml Q8_0's measured 531.8 ms at `M=512 K=4096 N=4096` by MAC count puts the CPU
near 334 ms at `K`=5120 `N`=2048 **[derived, not measured]**, against the NPU's 185 — but
at roughly 1.8x rather than the 4.2x the same shape gets at `K`=4608. **Fixing the cap so
the band keeps its wide tile is capped at that 2.4-2.9x, on `4993 <= K <= 6144` alone**;
the submit count is a per-`(M, N)` quantity, so neither the ratio nor the 32x transfers to
another column.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_submit_floor`, 2026-08-08]

**The whole-board death is real and its cause is open — it is NOT attributable to the
K-split submit.** A ladder over that route at the shipped 1.5.0 module — `M=64 K=8192 N=32`
(3 submits), then `M=128 K=8192 N=256`, then `M=512 K=8192 N=256`, then the wedge itself,
one cell per process on `ondemand` with a single-threaded CPU reference between them — was
run to find which cell kills the board. The board went down during it.

Its logs survived the power cycle and say the ladder **never got past the first cell**.
`~/plan/ladder.out` and `~/plan/ladder/*.log` record `M=64 K=8192 N=32` three times, every
rep **`RC=0`, `WRONG=0 of 2048` over the whole surface**, walls 14.4-16.5 ms. The boot
carries **zero `WARNING`/`Oops`**, its last NPU message is 45 minutes before the end, and the
journal stops abruptly at 04:29:45 UTC on a routine `sudo` line with no shutdown sequence.
So the board died **after a clean repetition of the smallest cell in the ladder, with no NPU
fault of any kind recorded** — which is neither shape-selective nor announced, and does not
implicate the K-split submit that had just returned `RC=0`.

An earlier reading of this run concluded the opposite, from host unreachability alone. It
does not survive the logs, and reachability was not evidence that night in any case: the
workstation's own LAN failed within ten minutes of the board going quiet and its router
needed a factory reset. **Decide that a cell killed the part from a serial console or a
post-power-cycle log read, never from a ping.** The earlier sgemm A/B death, read at the time
as a power event because it ran pinned to `performance` with four threads busy, therefore
still stands unrefuted as a rival. [HW, H96 MAX M9, n=1 ladder, logs read 2026-08-06;
**cause unmeasured**]

**A third whole-board death, and it refutes both rivals the second one left standing — if
the three are one class.** A matmul correctness sweep (`tools/rk3576-mm-drain-sweep.sh`) was
launched on a board that had been up 1h56m, taint 4096, zero `dmesg` `WARNING`s, and that had
just completed a five-repetition device run cleanly. The board went unreachable inside the
next four minutes and did not come back. The conditions rule out the two candidates carried
forward from the previous deaths: the governor was **`ondemand`** and the workload was
**pinned to one core** with `taskset -c 4`, so the sustained-all-core-on-`performance`
reading does not apply; and **no shape in the sweep's grid takes the K split at all** — every
one is a single-task `K <= 4096` matmul — so the K-split submit does not apply either. What
this run introduced that no earlier one had is `dpu_grace_us` = **0** on the matmul path;
that is a candidate and not a finding, and it is weakened by the conv-path `rowmap` sweep
having driven grace to 0 at 10 repetitions a cell with no incident.

**This time the LAN is excluded as a rival**, which is what the previous death lacked: a
second host on the same subnet (the RK3588 board) answered SSH normally and could not ping
the RK3576, repeatedly, over ten minutes. That is the cheap discriminator — **probe the dead
board from a third machine on its own LAN before writing down that it died.**

The sweep wrote to `~/mmdrain.log`, in `$HOME` so a reboot would not clear it — and **nothing
reached it**: a filesystem-wide search after the power cycle found no `mmdrain*` at all, so the
run died before its first line was flushed and how far it got is unrecoverable.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, n=1, no serial console, 2026-08-09; **cause unmeasured**]

**A fourth death took grace 0 out of the candidate set.** The same sweep was relaunched on a
board up 7h05m, taint 4096, zero `WARNING`s, freshly rebuilt, that had just returned **58 of 58
gate rows** and a clean five-repetition matmul probe — this time with the grace list `500 4000
250 100`, so **0 was never written**, `ondemand`, one pinned core, and again no `K >= 6176`
shape. The board went unreachable anyway, and much faster: the launching `ssh` failed to
deliver an `echo` scheduled five seconds behind the detached start, which brackets the death at
**under about 5 s from the first process** against the previous four minutes. The first cell in
the safe-first order is `M=128 K=2048 N=2048` at grace 500 — a grace write that was a no-op over
the value already live, and the one grid shape nothing had run that session. `~/mmdrain.log` was
again empty. The LAN was again excluded from the RK3588.

**So each of the four deaths has had its named cause refuted as a NECESSARY condition**:
`performance` plus a sustained all-core load, the K-split submit, and now `dpu_grace_us`=0. Each
of those three was named as "the thing this run introduced", and each was excluded by the next
run setting it safely and dying anyway. Three refuted candidates in a row is the shape of
looking in the wrong place — see rule 91 — and asking what else the harness introduced puts one
candidate far ahead of the rest on cost.

**The leading candidate is one never-run SHAPE, and it is testable in a single foreground
command.** The sweep's `SHAPES` array begins with `M=128 K=2048 N=2048`, so that cell is the
first device work of **both** the third and the fourth death, and the fourth's ~5 s bracket puts
the death inside it. That shape appears **nowhere else in this part's recorded corpus**: it is
not one of the sgemm A/B's shapes, not one of the six "0 of 5" cells, and not in any gate row.
It is a single-tile matmul, the only 1-tile cell in the grid, and nothing has ever driven the
entry at one task. **So the first device action after the power cycle is
`rk3576_mm_corr 128 2048 2048 1` alone, in the foreground, before any loop and before anything
else.** If that kills the board the answer is a shape and no structural story is needed; if it
survives three times over, the shape is excluded at low cost and the structural reading below
is what is left.

**The structural reading, second in order.** What the three sweep deaths share and the
survivors do not is a **detached loop** (`setsid nohup`) running one cell per process with
per-process allocation and teardown of tens of megabytes of BOs, on a module carrying neither
the BO-lifetime fix (`0014`) nor the IOMMU-domain fix (`0013`) — both written against observed
render-group crashes on this part. It is **[hypothesis]** with no console behind it, and it is
in tension with the discipline that produced the structure (one cell per process, so a wedge
cannot contaminate the next cell). **Process churn alone is not sufficient**: the 58-row gate
list is a foreground loop of 58 device processes and it completed cleanly on this same boot,
minutes before the death. The arm is the identical grid as repetitions **inside one process**,
and under the patched module.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, n=1 per death, no serial console, 2026-08-12; **cause unmeasured**]

**A fifth death attributes the class to the SHAPE and clears the whole harness-structure
family.** `M=128 K=2048 N=2048` was run **alone**: one foreground process, one repetition,
one cell, no detached loop, no module-parameter write, no governor change, no all-core load,
no `K >= 6176` shape, on a board that had been power-cycled 49 minutes earlier and whose
whole parameter block was read rather than assumed — shipped `rocket` 1.5.0, core 0 the only
bound entry, `poll_interval_us` 50, `dpu_grace_us` 500, `dpu_blind_us` 250,
`rocket_batch_submit` 1, `ondemand`, taint 4096, zero `dmesg` `WARNING`s. The two probe files
were shipped by tar with `md5sum` matching both ends and the tree rebuilt at rc=0 first. The
command was

    timeout 120 sudo -E taskset -c 4 ./build/rk3576_mm_corr 128 2048 2048 1

It emitted the library's three initialisation lines and nothing else. **The `timeout 120`
never fired and the `ssh` never returned** — so the board left the network before its own
120 s guard could act, taking the whole system down and not just the NPU. Six probes over two
minutes from the RK3588 on the same subnet all read DOWN while that board answered SSH and
reported 1d8h of uptime, so the LAN is excluded again (rule 89).

**What that measures is the negative space.** Every structural candidate the previous four
deaths left standing is a detached loop, per-process BO churn, a module-parameter write, a
sustained load, or a governor state, and this run had **none** of them. The 58-row gate list
is 58 foreground device processes and completes cleanly on the same kind of boot. So the
harness-structure reading above is refuted as a NECESSARY condition, the same way the
governor, the K-split submit and `dpu_grace_us`=0 each were, and what is left is the cell.

**The pure plan does NOT separate it from the cell that survives.** Read off the shipped
planner with no device: at `K=2048` the CBUF feature budget is `8 * 32768 / 2048` = 128
pixels, so `r76_mm_plane` takes the widest divisor of `M` that fits and gives `iw`=128,
`ih`=1 — one row task, one N tile, one task in one submit. `M=256 K=2048 N=2048`, the
most-run cell on this route and a 10-of-10 survivor, plans the **identical per-task program**
at `iw`=128 and differs only in carrying **two** tasks instead of one. So on the axes a pure
read can see, the difference between a board-killer and a survivor is one entry in the task
array. That this points at the task-array and submit path rather than at the regcmd is
**[hypothesis]**: the two cells also differ in every runtime quantity the plan does not name,
and no serial console has ever been behind any of these deaths.

**And the handover's own basis for nominating the shape was wrong, though the nomination
held.** "Nothing has ever driven the entry at one task" is false: `32x1024x1024`,
`128x1024x1024` and `256x512x2048` in the cost table above all plan `ih`=1 and all compute,
`128x1024x1024` at 5.98 ms. A single-task submit is ordinary on this entry. Whatever the
part objects to is not the task count alone, and it is not any bound the library models —
the same is already recorded for the K-split wedge above.

**This outranks the dropped-write hazard as a port blocker.** `M=128 K=2048 N=2048` is an
unremarkable prefill shape — 128 tokens at a 2048-wide hidden state — and it is reachable by
any GEMM frontend that does not know to refuse it. A silently dropped output tail is
recoverable; a whole-board wedge needing a physical power cycle is not.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, n=1, no serial console, 2026-08-06; pure plan read from
`src/rocket_matmul_rk3576.c` and `src/rocket_hw_profile.c`; **cause still unmeasured**]

**THE DEATH DOES NOT REPRODUCE ON THE REFLASHED STACK, AND THE ATTRIBUTION TO A SHAPE DOES NOT
SURVIVE IT.** On kernel **7.1.7** carrying `rocket` **1.6.0** in-tree — the patch series built
into the image rather than loaded out of tree — `M=128 K=2048 N=2048` computes. Run first
exactly as it was when it killed the board, `timeout 120 sudo -E taskset -c 4
./build/rk3576_mm_corr 128 2048 2048 1`, as the only device action after a green control, it
returned **clean in 16.583 ms**. It then went **18 clean repetitions across 13 processes**, the
last 12 of them as a repeated one-cell-per-process loop — the same harness structure that took
the board 3 of 3 times — with the board up throughout, zero `dmesg` `WARNING`s, taint unmoved
and the backstop count unchanged.

Every other shape the class had blocked is clean on the same boot, one cell per process,
foreground, at the shipped default `dpu_grace_us`=0:

| shape | reps | result | wall |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `128x1024x1024` (the control) | 1 | clean | 4.75 ms |
| **`128x2048x2048`** (the killer) | **18** | **clean** | 16.4-17.2 ms |
| `128x2048x1024`, `128x1024x2048` (the K-vs-N discriminators) | 3 + 3 | clean | 9.5-9.7 ms |
| `256`, `384`, `512`, `1024` `x2048x2048` (the M ladder) | 5+3+3+3 | clean | 19.6-47.8 ms |
| `256x2048x4096` | 3 | clean | 38.1-38.8 ms |

**39 repetitions, 0 wrong elements, 0 deaths.** The two discriminating cells the class was
waiting on were run and are green, and so are the two never-run shapes that had been reserved
as separate device costs.

**What this does NOT establish is which change cured it.** A reimage moves the kernel
(7.1.6 -> 7.1.7), the module (1.5.0 out-of-tree -> 1.6.0 in-tree, `0025` among the series),
and whatever else the image carries, all at once — so this is a joint change and it attributes
nothing to any one patch. The honest statement is that **the class does not reproduce under
the conditions that produced it**, over 18 repetitions of the exact killer cell in both the
isolated and the looped harness. It is not proof the silicon cannot be wedged; five deaths
happened across sessions under varied conditions and 39 repetitions in one boot at one
operating point cannot close that. **Treat a never-run matmul shape as cheaper than it was,
not free** — and read the board's own parameter block before believing any of this applies to
the module in front of you.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, kernel 7.1.7 / `rocket` 1.6.0, 39 reps over 9 shapes, one cell per process, 2026-08-10]

**The wedge is not an encoder overflow.** The 143-op program the wedge's first row task
emits has no value near its word: `CNA_WEIGHT_BYTES` (0x101C) is 0x480000, which is the
weight BO's exact size; `CNA_DMA_CHANNEL` (0x107C) is `ic-1` = 4607; the CBUF data-entry
count is 2304 against a programmed feature allowance of 4096 granules. Every library bound
passes, one of them with no margin at all: the resident slice is `32 * 4608` = 147456 bytes
against a `r76_weight_slice_cap` of **exactly** 147456 at >= 4 groups, and the whole cube is
4.5 MiB against a 6 MiB bound. So whatever the part is objecting to is not in the library's
model of it. [pure, dumped from `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c -r`, 2026-08-06]

**And the entry rides the drain deadline at some prefill shapes.** At
`M=256 K=2048 N=2048` on the shipped 1.5.0 module at `dpu_grace_us`=500 the surface comes
back missing elements in **12 of 14** reps — 768 to 26112 of 524288, wandering rep to rep —
and at `dpu_grace_us`=4000 it is exact in **0 of 9**, interleaved over three alternating
passes with the failure returning every time the parameter went back. The wrong elements
sit at distance 99-100 from the reference, which is the `0xA5` output sentinel: dropped
writes, not wrong arithmetic, the drain deadline's recorded signature. The other six shapes
in the table are 0 of 5 each. **Grace is the instrument and not the fix** — the fix is the
drain-deadline patch — but the exposure is the point: a GEMM frontend cannot assume this
entry computes at every prefill shape on the shipped module.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-08-07]

**What the lost surface looks like, per repetition.** `rk3576_mm_corr` scores every
repetition of an arbitrary shape and prints each wrong repetition's bounding box, where the
earlier harness reported only a count. Five repetitions of `M=256 K=2048 N=2048` on the
shipped 1.5.0 module at `dpu_grace_us`=500, pinned, one process, were **5 of 5 wrong**, and
the five agree on structure:

| rep | wrong of 524288 | rows | cols | wrong / bounding box |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| 0 | 19840 | 128..199 | 1504..2047 | 0.506 |
| 1 | 2944 | 128..151 | 1824..2047 | 0.548 |
| 2 | 40576 | 128..239 | 1312..2047 | 0.492 |
| 3 | 17664 | 128..195 | 1536..2047 | 0.508 |
| 4 | 24688 | 128..207 | 1440..2047 | 0.508 |

- **The loss is confined to the LAST job.** The pure plan tiles this shape into two tasks of
  128 rows, and every wrong element in all five repetitions is at row >= 128 — the first row
  of the second task. Rows 0..127 are exact in 5 of 5.
- **It always reaches the last column** and never the first: the low column edge wanders
  (1312-1824) and the high edge is 2047 in all five.
- **It is not a rectangle and not a whole-row suffix.** The wrong count is **0.49-0.55 of the
  bounding box** in every repetition, so the front between correct and lost is diagonal in
  (row, column) — consistent with a write order that advances in both axes, not with a
  contiguous suffix of a channel-group-major scan, which would put the high row edge at 255.
- **Magnitude spans 14x at a fixed shape** — 2944 to 40576 elements, 0.56% to 7.7% — which is
  why a single repetition cannot price this hazard.
- The entry's host de-scatter always runs: **0 elements of 524288 retain the probe's host-side
  sentinel** in any repetition, so what is lost is inside the device buffer, not a skipped
  copy-out.
  [HW, H96 MAX M9, one shape, one process, 5 reps, 2026-08-09]

**Every lost element is a DROPPED WRITE, so the matmul entry reaches the same mechanism the
conv path's deadline was decoded as.** `rk3576_mm_corr` now classifies each wrong element by
what it holds — the library's `0xA5` device stamp (the DPU never wrote the atom), zero, an
arbitrary computed value, or the probe's host sentinel — and a second five-repetition run of
the same shape and configuration reads **`drop == wrong` exactly, ratio 1.0000, in 5 of 5**,
with `computed`, `zero` and `no-descatter` all 0:

| rep | wrong of 524288 | drop | computed | rows | cols | wrong / bounding box |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---:|
| 0 | 3968 | 3968 | 0 | 128..155 | 1792..2047 | 0.554 |
| 1 | 17664 | 17664 | 0 | 128..195 | 1536..2047 | 0.507 |
| 2 | 19840 | 19840 | 0 | 128..199 | 1504..2047 | 0.507 |
| 3 | 5760 | 5760 | 0 | 128..163 | 1760..2047 | 0.556 |
| 4 | 22272 | 22272 | 0 | 128..203 | 1472..2047 | 0.509 |

So the two entries **share the cause** — the driver retiring a job while the DPU is still
writing. Ten repetitions across two runs and two sessions agree on the confinement to the
second task and on the reach to the last column.

**The drain-deadline patch CURES this path, and grace is now the inducer rather than the
hazard.** On kernel 7.1.7 carrying `rocket` **1.6.0** in-tree, where `dpu_grace_us` **0** means
*wait for the writing block* instead of *retire on the next tick*, the same shape, probe,
process discipline and pinned core read:

| `dpu_grace_us` | wrong reps | dropped elements |
|---|---:|---:|
| 0 (wait — the shipped default) | **0 of 5** | 0 |
| 500 (a capped deadline) | **5 of 5** | 247728 |

The green half is not an instrument failure: dialling grace back to 500 on the *same boot and
same binary* brings the loss back at full strength, so the probe demonstrably retains its
discriminating power on this kernel, and the output BO stamp is reported `0xA5` in both arms so
drops are scorable throughout. Every one of the 247728 wrong elements classifies as `drop`,
with `computed`, `zero` and `no-descatter` all 0. **Grace on a 1.6.0 kernel is therefore a
research instrument that manufactures the hazard on demand** — which is what makes the shape
below cheap to map on a cured board.

**Forcing a deadline on 1.6.0 also raises the driver's own DMA-error WARNING, and the taint
word cannot show it.** One `WARNING: drivers/accel/rocket/rocket_job.c:895 at
rocket_job_irq_handler` appeared during the grace-500 arms and none during any grace-0 work —
the handler's `WARN_ON` on `DMA_READ_ERROR`/`DMA_WRITE_ERROR`, which is the early-fence
signature already recorded on the conv path. So the induced hazard is visible in `dmesg` as
well as in the surface, and a grace arm should expect it rather than read it as a new defect.
**It did not move `/proc/sys/kernel/tainted`**: this image boots with the `W` bit already set by
an unrelated `rga3` warning, so the taint word was 4608 before and after. **Count WARNINGs, not
taint** — on an image whose boot already taints, the word has no resolution left.
[HW A/B, H96 MAX M9, one shape, one process per arm, 5 + 5 reps, 2026-08-10]

**What the two entries do NOT share is the shape of what is lost, and that is undecoded.**
The conv path loses a contiguous suffix of output-channel groups; here the loss is anchored
at **both** the last task's first row (128, in 10 of 10) and the last output column (2047, in
10 of 10), filling **0.49-0.56** of its own bounding box every time. With the probe's scoring
mask now known to be **1.0** at every K — the reference is zero everywhere and the output BO
is stamped, so the wrong set IS the dropped set — that fill is the loss's own geometry and not
an artefact of the input pattern. A fill of one half anchored at a corner is what a straight
diagonal cut across a rectangle gives, so a **triangular front** was the reading off a bounding
box and a count.

**The per-row element map confirms the triangle and quantizes its front to 32 columns.**
`ROCKET_MM_CORR_ROWMAP=1` on `M=256 K=2048 N=2048`, grace 500 as the inducer on the 1.6.0
kernel, three repetitions, 308 wrong rows in total:

| | rep 0 | rep 1 | rep 2 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| wrong rows | 108 | 104 | 96 |
| rows whose wrong columns are ONE contiguous run | 108 | 104 | 93 |
| rows whose run ends at column 2047 | 108 | 104 | 96 |
| rows whose run STARTS on a multiple of 32 | 108 | 104 | 96 |
| inversions in the per-row count | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| row 128 width -> last wrong row's width | 736 -> 32 | 640 -> 32 | 640 -> 32 |

- **Each wrong row holds exactly one run, and it always ends at the last column.** The count
  equals the span in 305 of 308 rows, so the loss is a suffix of each row, never scattered.
- **The per-row width falls monotonically** from the last task's first row to the last wrong
  row — one inversion in 308 rows — narrowing **20-23x** end to end and terminating in a
  single 32-wide run. It is a triangle, not a half-full rectangle.
- **The front moves in 32-column steps at about 8 columns per row**, and every run start is
  32-aligned in 308 of 308 rows. So a channel-group *granularity* is real here; what is not
  real is a channel-group *structure* — the conv path's loss is a union of aligned groups,
  this one is a single run whose edge is merely quantized to the same 32.
- **The apex does not reach the last row.** The wrong region ends at rows 235 / 231 / 223 of a
  0..255 surface, so where the triangle terminates is a wall-clock race and varies per rep,
  exactly as the 14x magnitude spread already said.

**Read this shape in de-scattered coordinates, and do not lift a write order off it.** The
probe scores the host-de-scattered surface, and the de-scatter un-permutes the native tiled
cube — so a linear frontier in the hardware's own write order can materialise here as a
triangle. The geometry is measured; any mechanism it suggests is **[hypothesis]**.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, one shape, one process, 3 reps, 308 wrong rows, 2026-08-10]

**The extent distribution runs closer to zero than the loud reps suggest.** Over the ten
repetitions the loss spans **3968 to 40576 elements**, 0.76% to 7.7% of the surface, at one
fixed shape and one fixed grace. A bracket fitted to the first run's 12-26k range missed the
second run's floor by 2x, so **do not price this hazard from a handful of repetitions** — and
a probe that reports a small extent is not reporting a nearly-correct datapath.

**The exposure is NOT confined, and every "0 of 5" in the older tables was undersampling.**
With grace 500 as an inducer on a cured 1.6.0 kernel the whole grid can be run at depth. At
five repetitions a cell it reproduces the old confined-looking picture exactly; at
twenty-five it does not:

| shape | tasks | 5 reps | 25 reps | rate |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| `128x2048x2048` | 1 | 3 of 5 | — | 60% |
| `256x2048x2048` | 2 | 5 of 5 | — | 100% |
| `384x2048x2048` | 3 | **0 of 5** | **4 of 25** | 16% |
| `512x2048x2048` | 4 | **0 of 5** | **5 of 25** | 20% |
| `1024x2048x2048` | 8 | 0 of 5 | 0 of 25 | 0% |
| `256x2048x4096` | 2 | 5 of 5 | — | 100% |
| `128x2048x1024` | 1 | 0 of 5 | 0 of 25 | 0% |
| `128x1024x2048` | 1 | 0 of 5 | 0 of 25 | 0% |
| `128x1024x1024` | 1 | 0 of 5 | — | 0% |

- **`M`=384 and `M`=512 reach the hazard.** Both read 0 of 5 and both fail at 25, so the
  reading that the class was confined to `M`=256 was an artefact of the repetition count: five
  repetitions cannot see a 16-20% rate. **A "0 of 5" on this path bounds nothing.**
- **The RATE falls sharply with the submit count** past `M`=256 — 100%, then 16-20%, then 0 of
  25 at eight tasks — so submit count is real as a *rate* modifier while being wrong as a
  *boundary*. Both readings had to be separated by depth, not by argument.
- **Both `K`=2048 and `N`>=2048 are needed.** Halving either one takes the cell to 0 of 25,
  while doubling `N` to 4096 keeps it at 100%. What the hazard tracks is the per-submit output
  surface, not `M` and not the task count on its own.
- Why more tasks should lower the rate is **[hypothesis]** — a longer job gives the writing
  block more of its drain overlapped with later tasks' compute, leaving less residual at the
  deadline — and nothing here measures it.

**The method point outlives the numbers.** The same grid, same board, same binary, read as
"confined to one cell" at 5 repetitions and as "reaches at least four shapes" at 25. Three
sessions of planning rested on the 5-rep table.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, kernel 7.1.7 / `rocket` 1.6.0, grace 500 as inducer, one cell per
process, 2026-08-10]

**The DPU emits its 32-bit accumulator on an integer program, and that is the K split.**
`gen_conv2d_int8_rk3576_i32out()` takes `precision_int32` in DPU `0x4010`'s output-width
field and pins OUT_CVT to exact unity, and the words that come back are genuine int32
accumulators on the RK3588's int32 cube map —
`(c/4)*ow*oh_full*4 + 4*(y*ow+x) + c%4`, decoded by driving operands whose every
accumulator is distinct so that each one names exactly one word.

The writer's budget is **one 16-byte atom per (16-channel block, pixel)** whatever the
OUTPUT element width is, so at four bytes an element it delivers **the first eight output
channels of every thirty-two** and touches nothing else. The way round it is the weight
cube rather than a register: program four times the output channels, put real channel `n`
at programmed channel `32*(n/8) + n%8`, and the surface that comes back is the plain
int32 cube with every channel present.

**What the budget is a function of is the DPU's own operand width**, `PROC_PRECISION`,
not the output element's — and widening `0x4010[2:0]` from int8 to int32 makes it two
atoms. The delivered set becomes the first eight of every SIXTEEN, so a real channel
costs two programmed ones instead of four, the arithmetic is bit-identical, and the CNA
and CORE programs are byte-unchanged. Two atoms is the ceiling: int16, fp16 and bfloat16
reach it too, the float ones destroying the arithmetic on the way, and int4 and int8 do
not. `gen_conv2d_int8_rk3576_i32out_wide()` emits it and
`rocket_rk3576_i32_wide_word()` addresses it — the map is a stream `s = 2*p + j` cut into
runs of `ow*oh_full` atoms inside 32-channel super-groups, decoded across `oc` 8 to 192
and pixel counts 4 to 33. It carries two bounds that fail silently: `oc` must be a
multiple of 32, and the surface must be short. The height rule the library enforces —
`oh_full * oc < 4096` — is a SAFE CAP and not the boundary: swept with the cap lifted, the
map is exact well past it at `oc` 32, 64, 96 and 256, and wrong INSIDE it at `oc` 128. The
plane width is not in it, and the failures are neither monotone in height nor repairable
by the writer's own redo. See `chips/rk3576-regcmd.md` and
`tests/rk3576_i32_height_bound.c`.

`rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_i32()` drives the narrow writer by default and the wide one
under `ROCKET_RK3576_I32_OC_MULT=2`. Both are bit-exact against a CPU int32 model at K up
to 16384, and `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` routes a K past one contraction through it.
What the multiplier costs is the N tile, and so submits: measured 4 submits down to 2 at
M=64 K=1024 N=2048, and 8 down to 4 at M=32 K=1024 N=4096; below the tile cap the two
cost the same. The wide one is opt-in because it **intermittently emits zeros**: on
about one run in ten of the shape that shows it most, a contiguous block of one
super-group's stream comes back zero while the rest of the surface is exact. It does not
drop writes — stamp the surface with a sentinel first and the bad atoms hold zero rather
than the sentinel, so the writer reaches them and the data is wrong; on a fresh BO, which
arrives zeroed, the two readings are indistinguishable. It is not the poisoning either,
being indifferent to the idle ahead of it. The corruption is contiguous in the writer's
**emission** order and in no other, sits inside a single super-group, and always ends two
emissions short of that group's last, so it is detected and the row task is redone —
0 failures over 40 runs of the worst shape, 3 of them redoing a task, with no idle needed
in front of the redo.

Two traps come with it. **An i32out job poisons the next submit** — of any kind, across
processes, because the state lives in the NPU and not in the fd — which then completes
normally in the usual per-submit time and writes nothing. This is not a property of the int32
writer: it is the part's one wide-output hazard, and every fp16 program carries it too.
The scope, the two negatives, and the law the guard is sized by are settled above under
"The poisoning is one hazard". Check that a job WROTE per task and not per tile: one
poisoned submit among a row-split tile's leaves its own rows empty while its siblings are
full. And **an output buffer must not be zeroed from the CPU before a submit**: the dirty
cache lines race the DPU's DMA and the writeback lands on top of the result. The int32
sweep also wedged the IOMMU once, reboot-only; the mechanism is **not** established, and
the measured extent stays inside the allocated BO, so an overrun is not the explanation
the data supports. Details in [rk3576-regcmd.md](rk3576-regcmd.md).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-27]

Of the machine parameters, the CBUF is swept — the budget, the data/weight split, and the
field that trades between them — and the matmul tile cap and weight N-group are measured
against the matmul above. Only `kgroup_4b` remains carried over, and nothing reads it.

**The op library reaches this part.** Convolution is behind
`rocket_conv2d_int8_rk3576()`, `rocket_conv2d_dw_int8_rk3576()` and
`rocket_conv2d_fp16_rk3576()`, which take row-major tensors and own the cubes, the row
plan, the output-channel split, the de-scatter and the submit-loop retries. Where the
public entry's semantics are the ones the part computes it dispatches there and a caller
writes nothing chip-specific: `rocket_conv2d_dw_int8()` and `rocket_conv2d_fp16()` both
do. `rocket_conv2d_int8()` does not — it writes a raw int32 accumulator where this part's
direct int8 datapath requantizes on chip — so it keeps refusing and names the entry that
matches, exactly as `rocket_matmul_int8()` does. `tests/rk3576_conv_lib_gate.c` drives
the emitter gate's own shape table through the library: 111 pass bit-exact, 1 refuses.

Zero points are folded exactly and gated for the first time. The input's correction is
`-in_zp*sum(w)` and needs no border special case, because the emitter programs the pad
constant to the input zero point and a pad tap's true value is therefore zero; the
weight's rides in the coefficient group's `B`, which the DPU ADDS, so it is programmed
negated; the output's becomes the `OUT_CVT` offset. Depthwise takes the input and output
ones and refuses a weight zero point, whose group has no `B` field.

**A layer's weights are packed once through a handle**, which is where most of a graph's
wall went — see the MobileNet section for the 2.94x. `rocket_conv2d_int8_pack_rk3576()`
takes the weights, the bias and the quant contract and returns one;
`rocket_conv2d_int8_prepacked_rk3576()` runs it per inference. One handle covers all three
per-call entries: `desc.depthwise` picks direct or depthwise, and a non-NULL `w_scale_oc`
picks the per-axis requant. **Three things it must freeze, and none is bookkeeping.** The
`fd`, because a BO belongs to the file that created it and an IOVA is per-fd — a handle run
on another fd would hand the NPU addresses that mean nothing there and the submit would
still succeed, so a foreign fd is refused. The output-channel TILE, because the weight
cube's group count follows the tile rather than the layer, and on the per-axis path that
tile is an accuracy choice made from the weights and the scales. And the quant contract,
because both zero points and all three scales are folded into the coefficient group at pack
time. The handle also holds the scratch whose size follows the frozen geometry — the feature
cube, the regcmd buffer, each tile's output surface — so an inference allocates no BO at
all, which turned out to matter more than the arithmetic did.

Reusing an output surface across inferences is safe on a part where "did this task write
anything" is a real check, because the sentinel is re-stamped before every submit; with the
sentinel off there is no check to mislead. Reusing the feature cube skips its zeroing memset
from the second call on, which is sound because the channels past `ic` are the cube's zero
padding and nothing writes them again.

The gate is not wall time: `ROCKET_LG_RESIDENT=1` runs 173 shapes of the library table
through pack plus **two** prepacked calls and compares to the transient output element for
element — two, because the second call is the first one to reuse a held cube and a held
surface, and one would not notice a stale byte. 0 differ.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_conv_lib_gate.c`]

**The library's envelope is wider than the emitter's, and by a decoded amount.** The
weight-slice cap is a bound on one PROGRAM, keyed on the output-channel groups that
program drives; splitting the output channels lowers the group count and so raises the
slice the part accepts. Fourteen shapes the emitter refuses compute through the library,
including `ic=576 k=3` and `ic=5184 k=1` at four tiles of 32 channels. The rule
`ic*kh*kw <= 4608` is the single-program one; through the library it is the CBUF pool at
one group, about `ic*kh*kw <= 5600`. One refusal survives — `ic=256 k=5`, at 200 KiB — and
it is the one whose only recourse is an ic split the on-chip requant forecloses.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-27]

**The per-submit floor is the driver's completion poll, and it is about 340 us.** The
`INTERRUPT_MASK` `PC_DONE` pair (bits 28-29) rejects writes, so `patches/rk3576/npu/0006`
polls `PC_DONE` on an hrtimer and `0009` makes the period `rocket.poll_interval_us`.
Measured through `rk3576_submit_floor` (an 8x64x32 int8 matmul, n=300, `taskset` to one
A72), the period at 50 us gives a median of **329-331 us through 0020, 344-348 us through
0021 and 0022**, against 1654 at the stock 1 ms. Eight runs across three modules,
reproducing to within 20 us; a figure of 439 us taken earlier in the series does not
reproduce on any module now on the board. That is every submit-bound workload, which on
this part is most of them. Do not measure the floor with a gate's total wall time; the
conv gate is dominated by host packing and its CPU model and reads flat at every period.

**The DPU completion interrupt reaches the GIC, so the poll is a driver choice, not a
silicon bound.** [HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-13] The pre-`0008` premise "not routable to the
GIC" was measured about `PC_DONE` and never covered the DPU pair. A diagnostic module
(a diagnostic patch on the 1.6.0 OOT reconstruction) masked `DPU_0`/`DPU_1`
(bits 8-9) in at submit: the mask HOLDS them (wrote `0x3300`, read `0x3300`), and the NPU's
shared line (GIC 279, with `27702000.iommu`) counted **1 -> 311 across a 300-submit floor
run** — one interrupt per submit, `raw=0x30000155` at each arrival — with retirement left
to the poll so nothing else moved. Taking the interrupt as the completion is therefore
open to the series (it is what gahingwoo's upstream v7 does); what an interrupt-only path
cannot retire is the two classes that raise no DPU completion — pooling, whose
`PC_OPERATION_ENABLE` bitmap never enables the DPU, and any output element wider than one
byte — so the poll (or a grace) has to survive for them. **Under the armed module the
floor itself read 279.4 us median against the same-base control's 351.2** — with
retirement still the poll's; the mechanism is unmeasured, plausibly the interrupt holding
the waiting CPU out of deep idle [hypothesis] — so the interrupt may be worth more than
removing the poll's latency alone.

**What made the period safe to shorten is retiring on the DPU's own completion.**
`PC_DONE` means the program counter finished issuing, not that the DPU's write DMA has
drained, and the driver both tears the IOMMU mapping down and signals the fence as soon as
it sees it. The IOMMU half is fixed by `patches/rk3576/npu/0011` — the per-job detach
faults writes still in flight, leaving a write page fault (`RK_MMU_STATUS 0x2b`) that
blocks the next `enable_stall` — which takes stall timeouts and NPU DMA errors to zero at
every period. The visibility half is fixed by `0012`: `RAW_STATUS` bits `DPU_0`/`DPU_1`,
what the RK3588 takes as its completion interrupt, set **82-343 us after `PC_DONE`** on
this part, scaling with the bytes in flight, and requiring them retires the job when the
writes have landed. On `PC_DONE` the int8 matmul gate is 42/43 at a 250 us period and
36/43 at 50 us; on the DPU bit it is 43/43 at both, and the whole gate set is clean.

**A task whose DPU output element is wider than one byte raises no DPU completion at
all** — the int32 writer and fp16 output stop at `RAW_STATUS = 0x30000055`, CNA and CORE
done and nothing further for milliseconds — so the wait is bounded by
`rocket.dpu_grace_us` and those retire on `PC_DONE` as before. The grace is load-bearing:
they fail at 100 us and pass from 250 up, the same threshold a blind settle needed. The
default is 500 us. This is the same set of programs that carries the poisoning, but the
two are separate hazards — the poison probe's `scope` map is unchanged under `0012`.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-28]

**Every wide-output submit pays that grace in full, so it is the fp16 path's largest
single cost.** An fp16 convolution is `ic/16` such submits and takes no poisoning retries
at all, so a slice is the poll floor plus the whole blind settle: `ic=128` at 28x28 k3
runs a median 15.89 ms over 8 slices at the 500 us default and 13.29 at 250, five runs
each with disjoint ranges — 300 us of a 250 us cut, landing once per slice. **Three
consecutive full gate suites pass at 250** (each 102 conv, 156+7 conv-library, 43 matmul
on each int32 writer, 15 refusals, the host regcmd gate), which covers the wide-output
programs the grace exists for. The default has NOT been moved, because 250 is the
measured cliff edge rather than a margin above it — the value to ship is between 250 and
500, and picking it wants the int32 sweeps run at that value rather than at this one.
[HW sweep, measured 2026-07-28, `tests/rk3576_fp16_split_cost.c`]

**That sweep's premise is gone: `0017` split the two waits and the fp16 path now pays
`dpu_blind_us`, not `dpu_grace_us`.** A job that raises no DPU completion names itself with
`ROCKET_JOB_NO_DPU_DONE` and waits `dpu_blind_us`, default 250; both fp16 entries in
`rocket_conv2d_rk3576.c` pass that flag. Driving each dial with the other held, five
interleaved pairs a dial, governor pinned, `ic=128` at 28x28 k3 on the shipped 1.5.0 module:

| dial | held | low | high | effect |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| `dpu_blind_us` | grace 500 | 250: **9.85 ms** (9.25-12.17) | 500: **12.90 ms** (12.09-14.63) | **3.05 ms, ranges disjoint once the cold first pair is dropped** |
| `dpu_grace_us` | blind 250 | 250: 9.83 ms (9.23-10.39) | 500: 9.95 ms (9.25-11.62) | **0.12 ms, ranges fully overlapping** |

So the 2.6 ms the old sweep wanted to buy by lowering `dpu_grace_us` is already collected at the
shipped `dpu_blind_us`, and moving the grace buys this path nothing. The measured step is
**1.5-2.1x the 8 x 250 us = 2.0 ms** one blind interval a slice would predict, which is
[hypothesis] more than one blind wait per slice and is not decoded. **Do not read these absolutes
against the 15.89 / 13.29 above** — those were taken on the shipped governor and these with it
pinned, and one arm's spread here (9.25-12.17) is wider than the 2.6 ms the comparison is about.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-06, `tests/rk3576_fp16_split_cost.c`]

**A per-job `rocket_core_reset()` does not clear the int32 poisoning; it takes the IOMMU
down** (`MMU_DTE_ADDR is not functioning`), drops the matmul gate from 43 shapes to 2, and
leaves the part wedged. The driver's own timeout path resets inside a scheduler stop and an
IOMMU detach/re-attach, and it is that surrounding re-init a bare call is missing; the
vendor `rknpu` driver never resets per job either.

**A half-started job pins the NPU `active`, which then breaks the int32 path and nothing
else** — fixed by `patches/rk3576/npu/0010`, and present in mainline `rocket` on the
RK3588 as well. `rocket_job_run()`'s two early returns leave both a runtime-PM reference
nothing will drop and a fence nothing will signal, and each pins the device on its own:
the usage counter never returns to zero, and the scheduler never takes back the job's
credit, which `rocket_device_runtime_suspend()` waits on. The usage-counter half lives on
the platform device, so `rmmod`/`insmod` does not clear it. The part then comes back
computing convolutions bit-exactly — 102/102 and 111/1 — while every i32 matmul shape
writes nothing, because the poisoning's clear is the power domain cycling and the domain
no longer cycles. It reads as a bug in the int32 writer and it is a machine state. Without
0010 it is also self-sustaining: one failed attach times out, the timeout path's core reset
takes the IOMMU down, and the next attach fails for real — one injected failure produced
seven and leaked seven references. Check `power/runtime_status` before believing any int32
result.
[HW, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-27]

**The kernel build loop is not blocked.** The board's `linux-headers` package is a full
kbuild tree with `Makefile`, `Module.symvers`, `arch`, `include` and `scripts`, so the
module builds ON the board: take pristine v7.1 `drivers/accel/rocket/`, apply 0006 and
0008 (the only two in the series that touch that directory — the rest patch pmdomain, the
IOMMU and the DT, which are built in), give it an `obj-m` Makefile and `make -C
/lib/modules/$(uname -r)/build M=$PWD modules`. The compiler differs from the kernel's and
warns; the module loads and both conv gates and the matmul gate pass on it unchanged.

### The third thing that stops a GEMM frontend is what the entry WRITES, and the fix is two changes

The speed question is answered and the datapath ones above are refusals a caller can see.
This one is silent, and it is measured on real models rather than on synthetic operands:
**one per-tensor output scale writing int8 costs a real LLM's prefill between 1.11x and 2.26x
in perplexity depending on the model**, and the pair of changes that takes both to 1.00x is
known.

The arm is the entry's own expression, `sat8(round_even((A*B^T + bias) * scale))`, simulated
on the host from a real model's own prefill activations — SmolLM2-1.7B (`d`=2048, ffn=8192,
24 layers, 144 offloadable projections) and Qwen2.5-1.5B (`d`=1536, ffn=8960, 28 layers, 168),
512 real wikitext-2 tokens, every scale an ORACLE taken from the tensor it quantizes, so these
are the entry's BEST case. A projection is treated as
offloadable exactly where the entry accepts it (`K`%32, `N`%32, `K` < 6176), which puts every
`d_model`-input projection on the NPU and leaves the FFN down-projection on the CPU.

Per GEMM, relative Frobenius error against a float64 reference, over 36 offloadable cells:

| what quantizes how | SmolLM2-1.7B | Qwen2.5-1.5B |
|---|---:|---:|
| fp16 — the floor the CPU path already delivers | **0.0003** | 0.0003 |
| per-tensor A, per-tensor B, per-tensor int8 out | 0.0965 | 0.0712 |
| per-ROW A, per-CHANNEL B, per-tensor int8 out | 0.0439 | 0.0408 |
| Hadamard-rotated A and B, per-tensor, int8 out | 0.0202 | — (`K`=1536 is not 2^k) |

**Read the per-GEMM table and you will pick the wrong one.** End to end — every offloadable
projection replaced in every layer, wikitext-2 perplexity over 8 windows of 512 tokens, top-1
agreement against the fp32 run. **Two models, because one of these rows differs between them
by a factor of eighty:**

| arm | SmolLM2 ppl | x fp32 | top-1 | Qwen ppl | x fp32 | top-1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| fp32 / fp16 | 11.1937 / 11.1924 | 1.000 | 99.73% | 12.8403 / 12.8385 | 1.000 | 99.63% |
| the entry as specified (per-tensor everywhere) | 25.2618 | **2.26x** | 61.99% | 14.2294 | **1.11x** | 83.18% |
| per-ROW A + per-CHANNEL B through it | 964.3787 | **86x** | 60.69% | 13.2236 | **1.03x** | 91.19% |
| per-channel OUTPUT requant, no rotation | 17.2697 | 1.54x | 72.36% | 13.6248 | 1.06x | 85.86% |
| Hadamard rotation, per-tensor output requant | 14.6771 | 1.31x | 76.78% | 13.1144 | 1.02x | 90.48% |
| per-axis inputs, NO output requant at all | 15.1168 | 1.35x | 77.22% | 13.1369 | 1.02x | 93.51% |
| **Hadamard + per-channel output requant** | **11.2366** | **1.004x** | **97.29%** | **12.8645** | **1.002x** | **96.85%** |

Four things follow, and the first is what the second model bought.

- **The COST is model-dependent by more than an order of magnitude, and per-axis input scales
  are a coin flip.** `scale` is the OUTPUT's alone — A and B are quantized by the caller — so
  per-row activation and per-channel weight scales are free at this interface, and they lower
  the per-GEMM error on both models. End to end they take **Qwen from 1.11x to 1.03x** and
  **SmolLM2 from 2.26x to 86x**. **So a port must not assume they help, and must measure a
  candidate model end to end rather than reading a norm.** Rule 95.
- **The obvious mechanism for that is refuted, and it is worth saying so.** A per-tensor
  requant's error is uniform in ACCUMULATOR units, so per-axis input scales make it rank-1
  in real ones, and on SmolLM2 the arm does concentrate: up to **20.6%** of a call's error
  energy in one row of 512, against 0.62% for per-tensor inputs. But Qwen's numbers are the
  same shape — **14.84%** against 0.64%, per-row scale spread 4.4x median / 24x max against
  SmolLM2's 3.7x / 22x — and Qwen does not blow up. **The concentration statistic does not
  separate the two models**, so it is not the explanation. What SmolLM2's arm does show is
  that its damage is CALIBRATION rather than ranking: top-1 agreement is 60.69% against the
  per-tensor arm's 61.99%, essentially unchanged, while perplexity goes 25 -> 964. Mechanism
  **open**.
- **The route is a rotation AND a per-output-channel requant, it holds on BOTH models, and
  neither half is enough alone** (1.31x / 1.02x and 1.54x / 1.06x separately; 1.004x / 1.002x
  together at 97% top-1). The rotation is host-side and needs no library change: an
  orthonormal Hadamard along `K` before quantization, which
  `ggml-rocket/test-hadamard-quant.cpp` already builds for non-power-of-two `K` — Qwen's
  `K`=1536 took the weaker block-diagonal form over 512-element blocks and still landed. The
  per-channel requant is a library change whose machinery is already on the part: this DPU's
  epilogue is `(acc + A[oc]) * C[oc]` then one `(v*MUL)>>SHIFT` per task, and
  `rocket_conv2d_rk3576.c` programs that C ramp today for per-axis convolution. The matmul
  entry pins `in_scale`/`w_scale` to 1 and puts the caller's factor in `out_scale`, and emits
  one row task per N tile — the same situation the conv path solves by sorting channels by
  scale. It needs that planner, not a new capability.
- **1.004x is a CEILING, not the route's number.** The arm gives every output column an exact
  per-column scale; the hardware gives an integer C ramp at a resolution of `0.5/C[oc]` with
  one shift per task, which is why the per-axis ResNet-18 scores 89 of 100 against a
  per-tensor control's 93. What the ramp itself costs a GEMM is unmeasured.

**What this does NOT show** is that the on-NPU path agrees with this host model of it. Every
number here is host arithmetic; the tiled, requantized device path is a second gate.
[host arithmetic over two real models, `M`=512, 2026-08-10]

**And the port's cap survives the host work it adds, because the down-projection dominates.**
A W8A8 path pays, per call, an activation quantization and a rotation the sgemm A/B never ran.
Measured on the board's own A72s at `M=512 K=N=2048` (`~/r76_w8a8_hostterm.c`, best of 5 after
a discarded first, `taskset -c 4-7`): quantize **1.08 ms**, FWHT **2.06 ms**, dequantize
**0.57 ms** — **3.70 ms** on four cores, 12.22 on one. That is 13% of the entry's 28.2 ms at
that shape and takes it from 4.72x to 4.18x, but re-weighting the A/B's shapes by a
Llama-3.2-1B layer's MAC shares moves the model-weighted ratio only **2.44x -> 2.37x**,
because the refused down-projection is 65% of the NPU path's wall and pays none of it. The
rotation is also per INPUT, not per call: q/k/v share one and gate/up share one, so a real
port rotates twice a layer rather than six times. [HW, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-10; the
down-projection's CPU wall is taken as gate/up's at equal MACs, **[expected]**]

### Where the per-column output scale comes from, and what supplying it costs

Every perplexity figure above uses an ORACLE per-column scale, taken from the accumulator it
is about to quantize. `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_perc()` takes `scale_n` from its caller, and
a caller holds A and B and no accumulator — so the oracle figures are the route's ceiling and
not a frontend's. What a caller can actually supply, on the same eight windows of 512
wikitext-2 tokens and the same 168 offloaded projections of Qwen2.5-1.5B:

| per-column scale source | ppl | x fp32 | top-1 vs fp32 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| oracle, exact per-column (`hadamard_chanout`) | 12.8645 | 1.0019 | 96.85% |
| oracle, through the shipped ramp | 12.8724 | 1.0025 | 96.63% |
| **frozen over 2 disjoint calibration windows** | **13.4451** | **1.047** | **87.28%** |
| the analytic bound `128*sum_k|Bq[n][k]|` | 389.5999 | 30.342 | 19.63% |
| the frozen scale's perturbations, dealt to the WRONG columns | 12.9315 | 1.007 | 92.70% |

**The scale source is the largest single term on this route.** Its excess over the exact-scale
ceiling is 0.045 of ratio, against the integer ramp's 0.0006 on this same model — a factor of
seventy-five — so a port that budgets for the ramp and not for the scale has mis-scoped the
accuracy by an order of magnitude.

**The calibrated arm's damage is a TAIL, not an average.** The frozen scale is on median
**1.083x looser** than the call's own column maximum, and the surface saturates **less** than
the oracle's (0.180% against 0.212%), so "the frozen scale clips" is the wrong reading. Over
1344 forwards the tightest single column sits at **0.228x** — 4.4x too tight, saturating hard.
A max over the calibration set is therefore the wrong estimator on the tail; a percentile or a
per-column safety factor is the untested lever, as is a larger calibration set.

**And the last row is the control that earns the reading.** It applies exactly the calibrated
arm's multiset of per-column perturbations to a fixed random permutation of the columns — same
magnitude, same distribution, nothing about which column needed the headroom — on a profile
that is if anything harsher (median 1.078x, tightest column 0.209x, saturating 0.326% against
the calibrated arm's 0.180%). It costs **1.007x against 1.047x**. So the metric is stable under
a same-size information-free perturbation and the calibrated arm's cost is attributable; and
**scrambling which columns get the bad scale makes the model six times better**, so the harm is
the CORRELATION rather than the magnitude. A column the calibration set under-estimated is a
column whose activations grew, and clipping that one costs where the same clip on a random
column is nearly free.

**The analytic bound is not a substitute, and its failure is pure aim.** It overshoots a real
column's accumulator by **57.7x median** (28-266x), spending about six bits of the int8 output
on headroom nothing reaches, and it saturates **0.000%** of the surface. Its C ramp is
meanwhile nearly exact — `worst_rel_err` 0.15% against the oracle's 0.59% — because under this
source `cs/cmax` is the same constant in every unclipped column, so `C` lands exactly on its
cap and only the integer rounding of `C` is left. **The ramp's resolution and the scale's aim
are independent failure modes, and on this route the aim is the one that costs.**

`rocket-userspace/tools/rk3576-w8a8-ppl.py` suffixes `_cal`, `_bnd` and `_calshuf`. [host arithmetic, Qwen2.5-1.5B, 2026-08-11]

**The second model costs nearly three times as much, and its control splits differently.**
SmolLM2-1.7B on its own eight windows and 144 offloaded projections:

| per-column scale source | ppl | x fp32 | top-1 vs fp32 | tightest column | saturating |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| oracle, exact per-column | 11.2366 | 1.0038 | 97.29% | 1.000x | 0.221% |
| oracle, through the shipped ramp | 11.2855 | 1.008 | 96.14% | 1.000x | 0.213% |
| **frozen over 2 calibration windows** | **12.6872** | **1.133** | 84.57% | **0.161x** | 0.176% |
| the analytic bound | 1.043e9 | 9.3e7 | 3.22% | 2.352x | 0.000% |
| the same perturbations, wrong columns | 12.0254 | 1.074 | 90.06% | 0.127x | 0.360% |

Its excess over its own exact-scale ceiling is **0.129 of ratio against Qwen's 0.045** — so the
scale source is the largest term on both models but is **2.9x** worse on one of them, and a
port that budgets from one model budgets low. Its tail is deeper too (**0.161x**, 6.2x too
tight, against 0.228x). **And the control's share differs by model**: the information-free
perturbation costs 0.070 of the 0.129 here — **54%** of the damage — against **12%** of Qwen's
0.045. So "the harm is the correlation, not the magnitude" holds in direction on both and in
magnitude on neither: on SmolLM2 about half of it is what any perturbation of that size costs.
The analytic bound is unusable on both, and its SmolLM2 number (9.3e7x, 65.9x median overshoot)
is a destroyed model rather than a measurement of a lever.

### A global safety factor on the frozen scale recovers 87% of that, on both models

`CALSAFE` multiplies the frozen per-column colmax before it is inverted, trading output
resolution everywhere for clipping headroom in the tail. Same windows, same calibration set,
`NCAL`=2 throughout; the ratio and saturation columns are over 1344 forwards on Qwen and 1152
on SmolLM2. Run one process per point.

| CALSAFE | Qwen ppl | x fp32 | ratio median | tightest | saturating | SmolLM2 ppl | x fp32 |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1.0 | 13.4451 | 1.047 | 1.083x | 0.228x | 0.180% | 12.6872 | 1.133 |
| 1.25 | 13.0510 | 1.016 | 1.349x | 0.273x | 0.021% | 11.8534 | 1.059 |
| **1.5** | **12.9395** | **1.008** | 1.614x | 0.308x | 0.004% | - | - |
| 2.0 | 12.9510 | 1.009 | 2.156x | 0.410x | 0.000% | **11.4165** | **1.020** |
| 3.0 | 12.9452 | 1.008 | 3.233x | 0.588x | 0.000% | - | - |
| 4.5 | 13.0487 | 1.016 | 4.846x | 0.889x | 0.000% | - | - |

**It is a U with a broad flat floor, and one scalar takes most of the term.** Qwen's best point
leaves **0.0058 of ratio** over the exact-scale ceiling against 0.045 unfactored — **87%
recovered** — and SmolLM2's `CALSAFE`=2.0 leaves 0.016 against 0.129, **87.5%**. The two models
recover the same FRACTION while differing 2.9x in the term's size, which is the strongest
evidence available that the lever acts on the mechanism rather than on a model's quirk.

**The floor is flat from 1.5 to 3.0 and the walls are not steep**, so this is not a dial that
needs tuning per model: 1.008, 1.009 and 1.008 at 1.5, 2.0 and 3.0 on Qwen, and 4.5 is worse
than the floor by 0.0085 of ratio. Below the floor the cost is clipping the tail; above it, the
cost is resolution spent on headroom nothing reaches — the same failure the analytic bound
takes to its limit at 57.7x median overshoot. **Only Qwen's floor was mapped**: SmolLM2 ran
1.25 and 2.0 alone, so that 2 sits inside a floor there too is **[expected]**. What is measured
on it is that 1.25 recovers 58% against 2.0's 87.5%, so err high rather than low.

**The residual after the factor is roughly what the information-free control costs.** Qwen's
0.0058 sits just above `_calshuf`'s 0.0052, so on that model the safety factor removes
essentially all of the correlated clipping damage and leaves the size-of-perturbation term
underneath it. That residual is what a per-column or per-layer estimator would have to attack,
and neither is built.

### More calibration windows buy the same thing more slowly, and the two levers are one curve

`NCAL` is the other dial: how many disjoint windows the colmax is frozen over. At `CALSAFE`=1.0:

| NCAL | Qwen ppl | x fp32 | ratio median | tightest | saturating | SmolLM2 ppl | x fp32 |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2 | 13.4451 | 1.047 | 1.083x | 0.228x | 0.180% | 12.6872 | 1.133 |
| 4 | 13.1409 | 1.023 | 1.156x | 0.250x | 0.088% | - | - |
| 8 | 13.0340 | 1.015 | 1.224x | 0.281x | 0.043% | 11.7297 | 1.048 |

Four times the calibration tokens recovers **71%** of Qwen's gap and **66%** of SmolLM2's,
against the free scalar's 87%. **So the colmax has NOT converged at two windows — it is still
climbing at eight — but sampling is the expensive way to buy what a multiplier gives.**

**Both dials act through the same quantity, and on Qwen the points fall on one curve in it.**
Order every point by the median ratio of supplied colmax to the call's own and the perplexity
is monotone down to a floor and back up, whichever dial produced the point: 1.083x/1.047,
1.156x/1.023, 1.224x/1.015, 1.349x/1.016, 1.614x/1.008, 2.156x/1.009, 3.233x/1.008,
4.846x/1.016. `NCAL`=8 (median 1.224x, 1.015) and `CALSAFE`=1.25 (median 1.349x, 1.016) are
indistinguishable in outcome. The aimed dial does buy a better tail per unit of median
looseness — 0.281x at median 1.224x against 0.273x at 1.349x — but the difference does not
reach the model's score, so **treat the two as interchangeable at equal looseness and pick the
one that costs nothing.** Their COMPOSITION was not run and is not in any number here.

**What this cannot see**: `CALSAFE` is one scalar for every column of every layer, so nothing
here says whether a per-layer or per-column factor beats it; both models are ~1.5B on one
activation regime; a percentile over calibration rows — which moves the scale TIGHTER, the
wrong way for this tail — has never been run; and the `NCAL` axis stops at eight windows, so
where the colmax converges is unmeasured. [host arithmetic, two models, 2026-08-10]

### Where the frozen colmax comes from: the part's own int8 output, over two passes

Every arm above freezes the exact per-column max of the calibration accumulator, and no caller
of `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_perc()` can see it — the entry writes int8 and the accumulator
never leaves the part. Computing it on the host means a host int32 GEMM per calibration window
per projection, which is the cost the offload exists to avoid.

**Two device calls give it instead.** Pass one runs the entry at the analytic accumulator bound
`127/(128*sum_k|B[n][k]|)`, which overshoots a real column by ~60x median and therefore cannot
saturate, and reads each column's largest `|C8|` back as a lower bound on that column's
magnitude. Pass two runs at that readback times a margin (1.5), so the codes are actually
spent, and reads it again. A column that saturates in pass two keeps pass one's margined value.
Both passes go through the shipped integer ramp and are de-quantized by the scale asked for
rather than the gain delivered, so the ramp's own error is inside the estimate.

**The estimator is accurate and its cost is not the estimator's size.** `|est/true - 1|`
median-over-columns is **0.30%** on Qwen2.5-1.5B and **0.31%** on SmolLM2-1.7B, flat across every
shape, worst single column 2.4% / 8.3%, with 0.14% / 0.37% of columns falling back from pass
two. What that costs the model at `CALSAFE`=2.0, both arms in one process against one `fp32`:

| model | `_cal` (exact frozen colmax) | `_calboot` (two device passes) | delta |
|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen2.5-1.5B | 12.9510, **1.0086x**, 95.41% top-1 | 12.9517, **1.0087x**, 96.02% | **0.000055** |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | 11.4165, **1.0199x**, 94.80% top-1 | 11.6192, **1.0380x**, 95.12% | **0.0181** |

**A 0.3% estimator error is free on one model and 0.018 of ratio on the other**, taking the
recovered fraction from 90.5% to 76.0% there. That is the same split these two models have now
shown three times — `_calshuf` costs SmolLM2 54% of its damage against Qwen's 12%, the
scale-source term itself is 2.9x larger there, and a sub-percent perturbation is 330x more
expensive there. **The size of a perturbation does not price its cost on this route**; how
correlated it is with the columns that matter does, and SmolLM2 is where that correlation is
expensive.

**A DIAL THAT IS FLAT FOR AN EXACTLY-FROZEN SCALE IS NOT FLAT FOR AN ESTIMATED ONE, and that is
where the shipped constant comes from.** Raising `CALSAFE` from 2.0 to 3.0 on SmolLM2 moves the
exact arm **0.0017** and the bootstrapped arm **0.016**:

| SmolLM2, `CALSAFE` | `_cal` | `_calboot` | gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.0 | 11.4165, 1.0199x | 11.6192, 1.0380x | **0.0181** |
| 3.0 | 11.4356, 1.0216x | 11.4591, **1.0237x** | **0.0021** |

**An 88% reduction, taking the bootstrapped arm's recovery of that model's 0.129 term from 76.0%
to 87.4%** — the same fraction the exact freeze gets on both models. So the estimator's 0.3%
tightening lands on columns that were already near their clip, and margin applied to all of them
absorbs it; `ROCKET_RK3576_CALSAFE` therefore defaults to **3.0**, the top of the flat floor,
rather than to its middle. Qwen's exact arm is 1.008x at both 2.0 and 3.0 and its bootstrapped
gap at 2.0 is 0.000055, so 3.0 being safe there too is **[expected]**, not measured.

**Pass two is load-bearing, and the readout that says so is the ABSOLUTE error.** Pass one's
signed median ratio is **1.499x / 1.523x** against a 1.5 margin — a perfect-looking estimator —
while its own `|est/true - 1|` is **49.9% / 52.3%** median and **563% / 230%** at worst. At the
analytic bound a column reads back a couple of codes, so pass one is unbiased in the median and
enormous per column. **A signed median ratio that matches the margin exactly is the statistic
that hides this.** [host arithmetic, two models, 2026-08-11]

### The two passes composed on the part, and what M does to them

The route above runs on the device end to end in `tests/rk3576_w8a8_route` — pseudorandom
operands with a deliberate ~30x spread of `sum|w|` across the columns of one tile, no rotation
(the Hadamard's job is to make a real activation Gaussian and these already are), and the exact
int32 accumulator on the host as the reference no frontend has.

| `M` | `K` | `N` | pass-1 `SHIFT` | pass-1 median &#124;err&#124; | est median | est worst | composed RMS rel | `worst_rel_err` | saturating |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1536 | 2048 | 39 | 53.1% | **0.455%** | 22268% | **15.3%** | 33.1% | 0.0000% |
| 512 | 1536 | 2048 | 39 | 6.9% | **0.283%** | 0.98% | **2.23%** | 0.417% | 0.0000% |
| 1 | 2048 | 2048 | 39 | 61.8% | **0.480%** | 611% | **18.1%** | 39.3% | 0.0000% |
| 256 | 2048 | 1024 | 39 | 12.2% | **0.296%** | 1.11% | **2.11%** | 0.655% | 0.0000% |

**The estimator survives the part.** Its median per-column error is **0.28-0.48%** against the
host simulation's 0.30% / 0.31%, at every `M` — so the C ramp's integer gain, which the host arm
does not model at all, does not dominate it. Pass one's signed median again lands on the margin
(0.97-1.63) while its absolute error is 6.9-61.8%.

**There is no SHIFT bound to reach on this route, and there cannot be.** Pass one's scale is
~6e-8 and the concern was that it drives the OUT_CVT shift out of DPU 0x40B4's six-bit field.
`rocket_rk3576_plan_perchannel()` divides the scale it is handed by a `cmax` carrying the SAME
accumulator bound `128*sum|w|+1`, so with a NULL bias the two cancel exactly and `best_base` is
the constant `127/INT32_MAX` whatever the weights are: the device programs `SHIFT`=**39** at
every shape, 24 values inside the field, and the per-column `C` comes out AT its `cmax` ceiling.
The frozen call programs 27-34. A standalone readout prints the same 39 flat over
`sum|w|` from 128 to 1.7e7. Note there is nothing to refuse even if it did overflow —
`rocket_rk3576_requant_params()` carries no bound check, and the RK3576 emitter writes
`out_cvt_shift` UNMASKED where the RK3588 path masks `& 0x3F`.

**`M` carries no shape constraint here and a large ACCURACY one.** `M`=1 computes (`rc`=0, no
row rule — the RK3588's height<4 is the opposite part), and its composed error is **6.9-8.6x**
the prefill figure. The mechanism is the tile, not the estimator: a column's frozen colmax is a
max over `M` accumulator rows, tight at a prefill `M` and one Gaussian draw at `M`=1, and the
entry plans one `(MUL, SHIFT)` per output-channel tile, so the spread of `scale_n` inside a tile
sets every column's `C` resolution. `ggml-rocket` does not route `M` below `rocket_min_m()`
(128), so this is the library entry's envelope rather than a frontend path.

**AND FREEZING FROM THE EXACT COLUMN MAXIMUM IS 13x WORSE THAN THE BOOTSTRAP AT `M`=1.** The
crossed arm, exact colmax against bootstrapped colmax and the `N` tile:

| `M` | `K` | colmax source | `NT` 2048 | `NT` 256 | `NT` 32 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1536 | bootstrap | 15.3% | 15.0% | **10.7%** |
| 1 | 1536 | exact | 201% | 148% | **70.7%** |
| 1 | 2048 | bootstrap | 18.1% | 18.1% | **14.5%** |
| 1 | 2048 | exact | 181% | 121% | **63.4%** |

The bootstrap's readback floors at one code — a column reading back zero is credited one — which
bounds its colmax from BELOW and so bounds `scale_n` from above. The exact maximum does not, so
at `M`=1 a column whose single accumulator draw is near zero is handed an enormous scale, and
`best_base = max_j(cs_j/cmax_j)` is taken over the WHOLE tile: one such column costs every other
column in that tile its `C`. **The over-estimate that reads as the estimator's worst per-column
error (22268%) is the same thing that keeps the shared ramp usable** — a floor whose stated
reason in the code is that a too-tight scale costs a model, and whose larger effect at low `M` is
this one. A smaller tile moves it monotonically and does not close it, which prices the
sort-then-tile lever on this route at ~1.43x of the `M`=1 composed error at 64 tiles.
[HW gate, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_w8a8_route`, 2026-08-11]

### The RK3576 entry's rotation gate refuses one of the two models the numbers came from

`ggml-rocket`'s `rk_hadamard_ok()` admits a power-of-two `K` and `K = 60*2^k` and nothing else.
**Qwen2.5-1.5B contracts every projection over `K`=1536** — `3*2^9`, not a power of two, and
`1536 % 60 = 36` — so that gate refuses the whole model, including the one whose 1.047x set the
scale-source term's size. What the accuracy simulation actually used wherever `K` was not a
power of two is the **block-diagonal** H over the largest power of two dividing `K`: still
orthonormal, so the product reconstructs exactly, and it smears an outlier over its block
rather than over the whole contraction, which makes it the weaker of the constructions and the
right fallback rather than a replacement. **A shape gate written from one construction's
availability is not the same object as the arithmetic a number was measured in**, and here the
two disagreed on a whole model.

### The matmul entry's requant is the model, and the per-column fix runs on the device

Every accuracy statement above is host arithmetic, and until it was measured nothing anywhere
compared the on-NPU requant against a host model of it. `tests/rk3576_mm_requant` is that
comparison, at the entry.

**The device computes the shared model exactly.** `sat8(round_half_to_even((acc + bias) *
MUL >> SHIFT))`, with `MUL`/`SHIFT` from the emitter's own QNNPACK derivation, matches the
part on **0 wrong of 1,396,736 elements** — five cells from `32x256x64` to `512x2048x1024`,
each at a scale that fills the int8 range and at one 4x larger that clips a quarter to a
third of the surface, four distinct `(MUL, SHIFT)` pairs, nothing unwritten. So
`tests/requant_model.h` is the matmul path's arithmetic as well as the convolution's.

**What the exact-float model costs as an instrument is two to three orders of magnitude below
what it was used to decide.** The integer requant and the exact-float-with-round-to-even
requant the accuracy simulations used disagree on **0.014% to 0.21%** of elements, **every
disagreement exactly 1 LSB**. The perplexity numbers above stand as written.

**A PER-OUTPUT-COLUMN output scale works on this datapath, expressed on the coefficient
group's C ramp**, and it is `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_perc()`. The epilogue the convolution
path's per-axis planner assumes — `(acc + bias[n]) * C[n]` in saturating int32, then ONE
shared `(v*MUL)>>SHIFT` per task — is what the part does on the matmul plane too: **0 wrong
of 698,368 elements** over twelve arms, six cells, two column-scale spreads each, including
one run forced to eight N tiles so the per-tile `(MUL, SHIFT)` re-plan is exercised. The
planner is the convolution's, not a copy.

**What binds the ramp at GEMM depths is the int32 product, not C's int16 field**, and this is
the number a caller has to read rather than assume. C is capped at
`INT32_MAX / (128*sum_k|B[n][k]|)`, so it is set by the OPERANDS and not by the shape alone.
The delivered worst-case relative gain error is roughly `0.5 * spread / C_max` and grows
linearly in BOTH the contraction depth and the spread of the column scales.

**`C_max` differs by an order of magnitude between the probe and a real model, and the probe
is the pessimistic end.** The gate's probe draws B from a full-range generator, so
`sum_k|B[n][k]|` ~ 64*K and `C_max` ~ `262144/K` = **128 at K=2048**. A real per-channel
quantized weight row has mean `|Bq|` near **5.5** — the channel's scale is set by a few
outlier weights, so most weights land on small integers — and the same K=2048 projection
gets `C_max` **424 to 1496**. Measured over SmolLM2-1.7B's 144 offloadable projections:
median 1496 unrotated, 424 with a Hadamard rotation along K, which raises `sum|Bq|` by
spreading each row's mass and so **costs 3.5x of the ramp's resolution** — an interaction
between the two levers a port would compose. The same models' within-tile column-scale
spread is **7.6-9.5x median** (range 3.5-160.9), giving a delivered `worst_rel_err` of
**0.69-0.89% median and 3.4-7.3% max** rather than the probe table's 3.0%. Qwen2.5-1.5B's 168
projections agree on every input and reproduce the rotation's cost: spread **10.1x unrotated /
6.3x rotated**, `C_max` median **1848 -> 555** (3.3x), `worst_rel_err` median **0.45-0.56%**.
[host arithmetic over the shipped planner, two models, 2026-08-10 and 2026-08-11]

The probe table below is therefore the instrument's own figure, and the host model of the
planner reproduces it within 0.92-1.19x on the discriminating (spread) arms:

| K | flat columns | 8x column spread |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 256 | 0.042% | 0.341% |
| 512 | 0.080% | 0.611% |
| 1024 | 0.162% | 1.533% |
| 2048 | 0.220% | 2.96-3.01% |

Against an EXACT per-column scale — which is what the 1.004x / 1.002x simulation used — that
ramp moves 2.2% of elements by 1 LSB at K=2048 flat and **12.7% at K=2048 with an 8x spread**,
with a few at 2. **So 1.004x is the route's ceiling and not this entry's number**: the term
the simulation did not include is this one.

**What the ramp costs a model end to end is small.** The perplexity arm re-run with the ramp
modelled instead of an exact per-column scale — `chanout_ramp` and `hadamard_chanout_ramp` in
`tools/rk3576-w8a8-ppl.py` — on SmolLM2-1.7B over 8 windows of 512 wikitext-2 tokens, 144
offloadable projections:

| route | exact per-column scale | the shipped integer ramp |
|---|---:|---:|
| per-column output requant alone, SmolLM2 | 1.543x | **1.505x** |
| per-column output requant alone, Qwen2.5 | 1.061x | **1.080x** |
| Hadamard + per-column requant, SmolLM2 | 1.004x (97.29% top-1) | **1.008x (96.14%)** |
| **Hadamard + per-column requant, Qwen2.5** | 1.002x (96.85%) | **1.0025x (96.63%)** |

So on the composed route the ramp costs **0.4% of ratio on SmolLM2-1.7B and 0.06% on
Qwen2.5-1.5B** — an eightfold difference between two models, both negligible, and two orders
of magnitude away from the per-tensor output scale it replaces (1.11-2.26x). The unrotated
route is where the models disagree: 1.543x against 1.061x for the same exact-scale arm.
[host arithmetic, 8 windows x 512 wikitext-2 tokens, 2026-08-11]

**A model's UNROTATED int8 route can be chaotic, and SmolLM2-1.7B's is.** De-quantizing by the
gain the ramp ACHIEVED (`C[n] * MUL/2^SHIFT`, at most 2% from the requested scale) instead of
the one requested reads **22.209x there against the requested scale's 1.505x** — and the same
substitution on Qwen2.5-1.5B reads **1.083x against 1.080x**, a non-event. The blow-up is not
a property of the reconstruction:

- a per-element map on the model's own layers, on either arm's own trajectory, shows the
  achieved-gain reconstruction **equal or smaller in error in every band of every mapped
  projection**, with no `C` at either clip and no floored column;
- a per-layer magnitude trace localises the divergence entirely to the LAST decoder layer,
  and that localisation is **false** — restoring the requested divisor in that one layer
  leaves 2.24e10, while the achieved divisor in that layer ALONE costs nothing (10.578
  against a 10.577 baseline);
- the depth sweep is **non-monotone**: the achieved divisor in the first L decoder layers
  gives 3.6e10 at L=1, **10.68 at L=2**, 1.3e10 at L=3;
- and the control settles it — an **arbitrary +-0.5% per-column divisor jitter carrying no
  information, in layer 0 alone, gives 2.3e10**, the same catastrophe, while the same jitter
  over layers 0-1 gives 10.33.

**The Hadamard rotation buys stability as well as accuracy.** Under the identical perturbations
the rotated route moves between **8.24 and 8.35** over four configurations including the
achieved divisor everywhere — so the route the port would ship is not on the knife edge, and
the numbers quoted for the unrotated route on SmolLM2 are samples from an unstable regime
rather than measurements of a lever. **Read the achieved-gain question as settled and closed
by a wash**: 1.0828 against 1.0800 unrotated and 0.9989 against 1.0025 rotated on Qwen, so a
signature change returning the achieved gains is neither dangerous nor worth building.
[host arithmetic, 2026-08-11]

**Two levers follow, and neither is built.** The N tile sets the ramp's resolution as well as
throughput — forcing eight tiles at K=1024 took an 8x-spread cell from 1.53% to **0.265%**,
because each tile then spans only `8^(1/8)` = 1.30x. But that measurement is the SORTED case:
the probe's column scales are monotone in the column index, so consecutive tiles are narrow
bands by construction, and a real model's unsorted scales would not see it. The convolution
path already sorts channels by scale (`r76_sort_by_scale`) and passes a permutation; the
matmul entry passes NULL. The second lever is the accumulator bound itself — `128*sum|w|` is
the int8 worst case and a real GEMM's accumulator is ~90x below it at K=2048, which is about
six bits of C resolution given away. Tightening it needs a statistical bound and therefore
evidence about whether `(acc + A)*C` saturates or wraps, which is unmeasured.
[HW gate, H96 MAX M9, `rocket` 1.6.0, 2026-08-10]

## Quantization

The RK3576 vendor path uses **per-output-channel** weight scales; Mesa's single global
scale saturates most channels. [source] This is the same per-channel requant story as
the RK3588, so the on-chip requant work (per-OC `OUT_CVT` MUL with a single global
SHIFT) transfers; see [../encodings/out-cvt-converter.md](../encodings/out-cvt-converter.md).

## The DPU LUT: the input map, and the register that hides it

A nonlinear activation on this part is two programs — a DPU-only one that loads two
513-entry tables through an auto-incrementing window, and an ordinary convolution with
the LUT enabled. `gen_lut_load_rk3576()` emits the first; the second is the direct int8
convolution plus a short register set. The load and the use want to be ONE job, because
the table lives in the LUT RAM and a separate submit can take a runtime-PM cycle.

**The input map is `index = (value - LE_START) / 2^sel`**, the `0x00020000` table
covering `[LE_START, 0]` and the `0x00030000` one `[0, LO_END)` at 512 intervals each,
with all three of `LE_START` (`0x4110`-`0x411C`, four lanes), `LO_END`
(`0x4140`-`0x414C`) and the index step (`0x410C`) live and programmable. The hardware
interpolates linearly between entries at the full resolution of the step, and the domain
is half-open at the top: `value == LO_END` takes the overflow clamp. Bit-explained
against a CPU model at four spans, 8192 samples each, max one output count
(`tests/rk3576_lut_probe.c`). [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**The value the LUT sees is the BS stage's output** — `(acc + A[oc]) * C[oc]` — so a
caller places each output channel's range inside the window with the same per-channel
`C` a per-axis quantization already uses. That is what the vendor does, which is why it
never moves the window, and why at fp16 — where that scale rides the coefficient buffer
