rather than a register — sigmoid and tanh emit register-identical programs with tables
spanning 6.25 and 3.02 input units.

**BN_CFG `0x4060` is the trap.** Every vendor activation carries `0x20` there against
`0x903` in its own bare-conv control, and transcribing it pins the LUT at the table join
for every input across twenty-two binades — while the readout still tracks the table
faithfully as the table is changed, so it reads as a working LUT with an input map that
is nowhere in the registers. The BN stage is then multiplying by an operand register no
vendor program writes, on a part whose register file is not cleared between jobs. Left
at the generator's own `0x903` the stage is bypassed and the map appears. The LUT itself
is the **EW** stage: `EW_LUT_BYPASS` is bit 7 of `0x407C`, the RK3588's field layout at
a moved offset.

### The op: an int8 activation that is exact at every input

`rocket_act_int8_rk3576()` is the entry point over that map — sigmoid, tanh, swish,
hardswish, hardsigmoid and elu over a row-major `[C][H][W]` int8 tensor with a per-tensor
quantization on both sides. `exp`, `gelu` and `sqrt` are deliberately absent: no vendor
capture emits a 513-entry table for any of the three, so they lower some other way and a
table for them would be an invention.

**It is exact for every int8 input rather than an interpolation of one.** The op lowers
onto a depthwise 1x1 identity convolution whose weight is the LUT's own index step,
`2^sel`, which puts input value `q` at table index `q - in_zp + 512` EXACTLY — so each of
the 256 possible inputs reads its own entry and the hardware's interpolation never runs.
An identity weight of 1 would have put all 256 inputs inside eight index intervals. The
per-output-channel `C` could express the same scaling and is not needed for this op: the
coefficient `A` fold already carries `-in_zp*sum_w`, so the input zero point comes out
right with no special case, and `C` stays free for a fused conv->activation where the
accumulator range differs per channel.

**Depthwise, and it has to be.** A 1x1 DIRECT identity needs an `oc x ic` weight matrix,
which is quadratic in the channel count and past the emitter's `ic*kh*kw <= 4608` weight
slice rule at any real width. Depthwise binds output channel `c` to input channel `c`, so
the identity is one weight per channel and the layer is one task whatever the width.

**The table's two scales.** A datapath value `v` means the real number
`in_scale * v / 2^sel`, and an entry is read in units of `out_scale * gain` where `gain`
is the OUT_CVT pair the emitter will actually program — read back from
`rocket_rk3576_requant_params()` rather than re-derived, because a table built against a
slightly different gain is a curve that is quietly off everywhere. The conv scale is held
at 1/128, the finest gain whose int16 entry still spans the whole int8 output range a
zero point can ask for (128 * 255 = 32640).

The two programs go out as ONE job, which needed a third submit layout in the conv driver
beside "one submit per row task" and "one chained kick": a multi-descriptor job, because
the chain rewrite links programs inside one BO and the table load is a separate one.
Chaining is declined for a LUT call and says so; it costs the single-kick saving, not
correctness.

6 kinds x 5 quantizations x 6 shapes, dense over all 256 byte values: **180 of 180
bit-exact against a CPU model of the chip's own arithmetic**, and at most **one count**
from an exact `f()` anywhere (`tests/rk3576_act_gate.c`). [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

### The activation fuses into a convolution's epilogue

The standalone op invents a convolution to reach the LUT. A real one already has one and
the LUT sits in the EW stage of the same program, so `rocket_conv2d_int8_act_rk3576()`
costs only the table-load task: the activation is free.

**It is not the same arithmetic as conv-then-activation, and it is the better one.** The
standalone op reads the convolution's REQUANTIZED int8 output, so its input alphabet is
256 values; the fused form reads the ACCUMULATOR, before any rounding. What it gives up is
exactness — the standalone form puts each of its 256 inputs on its own table entry, and a
real accumulator lands between entries, so the hardware's linear interpolation runs.
**Measured over 28 cases (six kinds, direct and depthwise, three quantizations): at most
ONE count from an exact `f()`**, with the pre-activation inside the curved region rather
than on a saturating tail. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_act_gate.c fused`]

**Nothing about the conv entries' contract has to change**, which is the opposite of what
pinning the standalone op's scale at 1/128 suggested. Three quantities follow from the
three scales a caller already passes:

- **The value unit.** With a per-tensor requant the BS multiplier `C` is unity, so the
  value the LUT sees is the accumulator itself — `A` included, which is where the bias and
  the zero-point fold already are — and one count of it means `in_scale*w_scale`.
- **The entry unit.** OUT_CVT scales the table's output by the gain the emitter derives
  from `in_scale*w_scale/out_scale`, so an entry is read in units of `out_scale*gain`. The
  standalone op has to PIN its conv scale to make that well defined; a real conv has the
  model's own three.
- **The step.** The window is the symmetric `[-512*2^sel, 512*2^sel)`, so `sel` is the
  smallest whose real half-span covers the domain the activation still varies over. Past
  it the LUT returns its endpoint clamp — the right answer for a SATURATING kind, and for
  one that keeps growing the bound is where the int8 OUTPUT saturates instead, since
  `f(x)` is `x` by then.

**Two bounds are refused rather than computed wrong**, and both name `in_scale*w_scale` as
the quantity to change: a value unit too fine for the table's peak to fit an int16 (an
unbounded kind over a fine unit — swish and elu at `in_scale*w_scale = 1.6e-4` need 49151
entries and the table saturates at 32767, which would give a flat-topped curve), and a
domain no `sel` reaches.

**Per-output-channel `C` is the lever this leaves open.** It divides the value unit per
channel, which is how a fused conv whose channels have very different accumulator ranges
puts each inside `[LE_START, LO_END)` — and `(acc + A)*C` saturates at int32, a bound to
plan inside. The per-tensor form above needs none of it.

TWO REFERENCE TRAPS, both of which read as hardware faults. **A liveness check against the
same conv WITHOUT the LUT has to allow a rounding**: the two surfaces differing by one
count is the requant's tie-to-even against a reference that rounds half away from zero,
not the activation doing something. And **ELU under an output quantization with no
negative range IS the identity clamped at zero**, which the DPU's int8 saturation already
computes — so on that quantization the fused and plain surfaces are legitimately
identical and a differs-from-plain assertion fires on a working LUT.

## The precision fork: what the op library should be founded on here

The RK3588's composed op library — the norms, softmax, the gated FFN, attention, the
ViT/Whisper encoder blocks — is built on the **fp16 matmul**. That foundation does not
port to this part, and the choice of what to put in its place is a decision rather than a
port.

**fp16 is not a matmul precision here.** One int8 task contracts `ic*kh*kw <= 4608` and one
fp16 task contracts exactly sixteen input channels, so the same K costs one submit against
288, and the measured ratio runs 30x to 299x over ordinary shapes. Anything that lowers to
an fp16 GEMM inherits that. This is the RK3588's answer inverted, and it is a machine
parameter, not arithmetic.

**int8 carries the GEMMs but not the reductions.** `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` is fast
and bit-exact, but its output goes through the DPU's requant and is **int8**. A reduction
expressed as a matmul by a ones vector — which is how the RK3588 builds RMSNorm, softmax's
row sums and every ReduceMean — needs an accumulator wider than its operands, and an int8
output saturates on the first long row. The 32-bit writer exists
(`rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_i32()`) but it is the wrong instrument to put inside a
per-layer norm: it carries the wide-output poisoning, so it pays a power cycle per submit;
it drops atoms at a rate the memory system sets, so it needs a per-atom sentinel and an
arithmetic re-check; and its surface is bounded at 8 KiB per row task. That is a decoded,
gated research path, not a foundation for a hot inner op.

**So the call is: on this part the NPU runs convolution, int8 GEMM, pooling and LUT
activations, and the transformer composition stays on the CPU.** The three pieces that
argue for it are all on the convolution/DPU side rather than the matmul side — the direct
and depthwise int8 convolutions and the int8 first conv are bit-exact and 10-76x a scalar
CPU reference, the PPU gives pooling as an independent 31-write program in the cube the
conv path already packs, and the DPU LUT gives the nonlinear activations. That is a
CNN/detection/classification stack, and it is the one this silicon is shaped for.

All four pieces that decision named are now built and gated: per-output-channel weight
scales on the direct and depthwise paths and through a graph, the PPU pooling emitter, the
DPU LUT and its activation entry, and a whole network. Revisit the transformer question
only if a use for it appears — and if it does, the thing to settle first is whether an
int32 accumulation path can be made cheap enough to sit inside a norm, not whether the
fp16 one can be made faster.

## A whole network

MobileNetV1-224 at int8 runs end to end on this part — 27 convolutions, a PPU pooling
layer and a classifier — and returns TFLite's own label. Every one of the 29 compute
layers is **bit-exact against a CPU model of the part's own arithmetic**, and the whole
graph is **5.1 ms** per inference with resident weights, the tensors kept in cube layout
between layers, each run of cube-linked layers going out as ONE hardware kick, and the
pooling layer retiring on the PPU's own completion — 6.2 ms before that last one, 10.4-10.5
ms with one submit per layer, 21 ms with the transposes at every layer boundary, 115 ms
without resident weights at all, and the sections below are how the difference was found and
what each part of it was.
`tests/rk3576_net_gate.c` is the gate; `tests/data/rk3576-net/mknet.py` flattens a
per-tensor-quantized `.tflite` into the blob it reads, carrying TFLite's output for
**every intermediate tensor** so a wrong layer is named by its own index rather than by a
wrong label 27 layers later.

**THE PLANNER IS NOT THE GATE'S, AND IT IS THE PRODUCT.** The placement and linking passes
— one buffer per add and per concatenation with a slice per operand, the declared tails, the
shared surfaces, the adjacent-pair join with its refusal buckets, and the caller of the run
finder — live in `graph/rocket_graph_rk3576.c` over a frontend-neutral layer description:
kind, geometry, the operand graph BY PRODUCER, the resident handles, and one flag for the
thing no rule there can derive, whether the CALLER prepares that layer's input on the host.
The gate is its first caller and owns what a caller owns: the weights, the tensors, the
ping-pong, the skip lifetimes and the de-scatter points. It is a separate target the
frontends link and **not** part of `librocketnpu`, which holds no graph-execution object —
the run FINDER (`rocket_chain_plan_rk3576()`) is in the library because it is pure and is
the one thing a frontend cannot own. The reason it is shared rather than copied: twelve
refusal classes have been closed so far and **every one was a host-buffer rule rather than
the part's**, so a second copy would fork where being wrong is silent. What a frontend gains
by using it is the whole ladder — per-op entries with transient weights ~115 ms on
MobileNetV1-224, resident ~21, cube layout 10.4-10.5, one kick 5.0 — so calling only the op
entries is about a twentieth of the demonstrated throughput. The move is behavior-preserving
and was checked as such: every join, refusal bucket, run boundary, submit and task count and
TFLite distance is identical on four graphs, and Inception V3 differs only in two pooling
layers' submit counts, which vary run to run on ONE binary because they are the divisor
lag's redos. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-04]

**AND A SECOND CALLER RUNS IT: the TFLite delegate, on real `.tflite` files.**
`tflite-rocket` lowers a delegated partition onto the same neutral description and runs the
plan, so the part is reachable from an unmodified `.tflite` through the ordinary
external-delegate API rather than only from a hand-built blob. Warm on the H96 MAX M9
(kernel 7.1.6, 786 MHz), against the same interpreter with no delegate, real image, median
of 19 — with the nodes claimed, the adjacent-pair joins and the hardware kicks the plan
produced:

| model | delegate | CPU | claimed | joins | kicks |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1-224 | 5.2 ms | 71.7 | 29/31 | 28/28 | 1 |
| MobileNetV2-224 | 6.8 ms | 45.3 | 64/66 | 63/63 | 1 |
| ResNet-18-224 | 8.8 ms | 167.1 | 36/38 | 27/27 | 1 |
| Inception V1-224 | 7.4 ms | 180.5 | 81/83 | 53/53 | 1 |
| Inception V3-299 | 34.5 ms | 669.3 | 130/132 | 90/94 | 16 |
| SSD-MobileNetV2-COCO | 17.0 ms | 109.0 | 87/111 | 70/82 | 6 |
| EfficientDet-Lite0-320 | 120.3 ms | 122.7 | 231/267 | 85/89 | 10 |

Every classifier returns the interpreter's own top-1, and **the plans are identical to the
blob's, join for join and run for run** — which is the check that two independent lowerings
of the same five networks are the same lowering. The delegate's wall runs 0.3-0.7 ms above
`rk3576_net_gate`'s on the four graphs where both take the same stem; that difference holds
the interpreter's own invoke, the two boundary transposes and the harness difference, and
**none of the three is measured apart, so no cap is quoted for it**. ResNet-18 is 8.8 against
the gate's 6.6 because the gate takes the packed-image stem there and the delegate does not
(it attempts that encoding only at a zero input zero point, where the surface stays a cube).

**Two node kinds no hand-built graph could produce, and the detectors found both.** A
`DEPTHWISE_CONV_2D` of four or fewer channels **has no form on this part** — the
packed-image first conv folds the kernel's columns into the channel axis, so there is
nothing left to be depthwise over, and `direct_datapath` is refused on a depthwise
descriptor rather than ignored. And a `QUANTIZE` on a narrow tensor hits the same wall
through its lowering, since the requantization edge's natural form IS a depthwise 1x1
identity; on four or fewer channels it takes the DIRECT identity instead, which is the same
arithmetic and sixteen weights. An SSD box-encoding branch is exactly both shapes. **Both
have to be answered at CLAIM time**: a layer the library will not pack is a Prepare failure
and a Prepare failure fails the whole model, where an unclaimed node is one the framework
runs itself. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-04]

**AND A CLAIM-TIME REFUSAL IS CHEAPER THAN A LATE ONE BY MORE THAN A PREPARE.** Every bound
the library owns that is decidable from the SHAPE is now asked before a node is claimed —
the pooling plan, the per-axis gain ramp, and (through `rocket_conv2d_int8_plan_rk3576()`)
the convolution geometry, the resident weight slice and the row window. **The row window is
the one that says why it matters**: it is not reached by the PACK at all, only by the run's
per-task plan, so a model that lands past it builds cleanly, passes `AllocateTensors`, and
throws at every `Invoke` — later than the failure the check was written for and past any
load-time validation a caller could do. A probe model of three layers whose middle
convolution is ic=256 oc=32 8x8 k5 (a 200 KiB resident slice, past the CBUF pool at a single
output-channel group) demonstrates both halves: before, `rocket_conv2d_int8_prepacked_rk3576`
returns -5 from inside the partition and the whole model dies; after, 2 of 3 nodes are
claimed and the refused convolution runs on the CPU between two delegated partitions.
Nothing in the corpus reaches any of these bounds, so **the arm had to be written rather than
found** (`tflite-rocket/tools/mk_claim_probe.py`), and the anti-drift control is
`rk3576_conv_lib_gate claimplan`: over all 160 shapes of the envelope table it asserts the
pure plan's verdict against `lib_refuse`, the run's own — 0 accept-a-refusal, 0 over-refusal,
and it needs no device. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-04]

**BOTH DETECTORS SCORE AT CPU PARITY, AND GETTING THERE TOOK TWO PER-AXIS FIXES.**
mAP@[.5:.95] over 500 COCO val2017 images, the same interpreter with and without the
delegate (`tflite-rocket/tools/coco_map.py`): SSD-MobileNetV2 **0.2617 against 0.2631**,
EfficientDet-Lite0 **0.2826 against 0.2823**, delta **-0.0014** and **+0.0003**. That is the comparison a
detector admits: its output is an NMS-ordered list, so one count of arithmetic drift
reorders or drops a box and an element-wise diff against the CPU says nothing (SSD disagreed
on 4 of 20 class ids and 75 of 80 box coordinates while 19 of 20 scores agreed to 0.02).

EfficientDet-Lite0 got there from **0.0000**. It was the first model with **per-axis** weights
to reach the part through a frontend, and it emitted exactly 25 boxes an image, almost all
class 1, with scores pinned in [0.3125, 0.5] — a degenerate head, not drift. The
discriminator for what a per-axis defect costs is a per-axis CLASSIFIER, where the label is
an assertion a detector cannot carry: `mkresnet18.py --per-axis` builds one from the same
weights and the same calibration set as the per-tensor ResNet-18, so the quantization
granularity is the only variable, and `rk3576_net_ab.py --images DIR` scores top-1 agreement
against the CPU as a RATE over 100 real photographs rather than on one image. It read
**0 of 100 against the per-tensor build's 93 of 100**. Two independent defects were under
that, and neither is the arithmetic `rk3576_perchannel_gate` asserts.

**THE PER-CHANNEL C RAMP HAS A FLOOR, AND BELOW IT A CHANNEL COMPUTES AT THE TILE'S GAIN
INSTEAD OF ITS OWN.** A tile is one task and a task carries one `OUT_CVT` shift, so a channel
reaches its own scale through the int16 `C` in its coefficient group and nothing else. The
base gain is already the smallest the tile admits — every channel's `C` must fit that
channel's own accumulator bound — so a channel whose scale sits below HALF the base wants a
`C` under one, and the field's floor is one. It is then wrong by a FACTOR: the packer's own
log read **311% and 8150%** on two per-axis ResNet-18 layers and past 100% on 7 of
EfficientDet's 182 per-channel layers, on surfaces that are still full, correctly sized and
entirely plausible. The clamp is the boundary and it is the ramp's own rather than a chosen
threshold — above it `C` is an integer and the resolution is `0.5/C`, which is what the tile
search spends submits to improve; below it there is no `C` to choose. Equivalently, a clamped
channel is exactly one whose relative gain error exceeds 100%. So the layer is REFUSED:
`rocket_conv2d_int8_perchannel_plan_rk3576()` is that question, PURE, and the delegate asks it
at CLAIM time where a refusal costs one node the framework runs itself. The median EfficientDet
layer is 0.021%. **DEPTHWISE is where it bites**: one task whatever the channel count means no
tile lever at all, and 6 of the 7 clamped layers are depthwise (1152, 1152, 1152, 672, 240, 32)
against 1 direct one, the stem, already at the emitter's floor of 32.
`ROCKET_RK3576_PC_ALLOW_CLAMP=1` computes anyway, and costs **0.0428 mAP** on EfficientDet —
0.2395 against the CPU's 0.2823, where refusing scores 0.2809.

**BUT WHAT CLAMPS IS A PRUNED CHANNEL, AND A CLAMP IS ONLY A DEFECT WHERE IT REACHES THE
SURFACE.** Every one of the 111 clamped channels across those six depthwise layers, and the
stem's four, has an ALL-ZERO filter: an all-zero tensor is quantized against the quantizer's
own floor (TFLite's 1e-6), so its scale sits four to five orders below its neighbours' while
its int32 bias still carries the layer's magnitude — which is exactly the shape that puts a
channel below half the tile's base gain. A channel with no live weight reaches exactly ONE
accumulator, the zero-point fold `A`, at every output position, because the MAC term is zero
whatever the feature cube holds. So for it the question is not how wrong the gain is but
whether the BYTE differs, and that is decidable exactly, with no bound and no sampling.

It usually does not: a large negative fold saturates to -128 under either gain, which is **105
of those 111**. The rule the library asks is written as SATURATION rather than as equality —
both gains must drive the fold past the same rail by more than a count — so the answer cannot
depend on the DPU's rounding, which is modelled here rather than run. A LIVE channel is
refused without asking, since its accumulator ranges over `+-128*sum|w|` around `A` and a gain
wrong by a factor is wrong somewhere in it.

That would take EfficientDet-Lite0 from **7 refusals to 4** — its stem, its first depthwise
layer and the 240-channel one — and every per-op instrument says those three are exact:
`rk3576_perchannel_gate`'s `pruned` and `dw-pruned` cells measure **0.0 counts** of distance
from an exact per-axis requant on the pruned channels ON THE PART, `pruned-live` (differing
only in the fold those channels carry) refuses, and the three layers' NON-clamped channels are
all within **0.077-0.097%** of their own gain, so the ramp's resolution is not in question
either.

**AND THE MODEL LOST 0.043 mAP — WHICH WAS THE CBUF RUNG, NOT THE PER-AXIS ARITHMETIC AND
NOT THE GRAPH.** Accepting the three scored **0.2380** against the refusal's 0.2809, and
accepting only the two DEPTHWISE ones scored **0.2076**. One of that pair is `blocks_0` —
160x160, ic = oc = 32, k3 s1 — whose three row windows all selected F=512, a rung the
depthwise path does not honour, and the layer came back wrong on 28 of its 32 LIVE channels
in exactly the output rows past each window's 51st staged input row. Forcing the window small
closed the whole gap and then some, which is what localised it:

| arm | mAP | vs CPU's 0.2823 |
|---|---:|---:|
| the refusal | 0.2809 | -0.0014 |
| `PC_INERT=dw`, before the rung fix | 0.2076 | -0.0747 |
| `PC_INERT=dw` + `MAX_ROWS=48`, before the fix | 0.2832 | +0.0009 |
| `PC_INERT=1` + `MAX_ROWS=48`, before the fix | 0.2826 | +0.0003 |
| `MAX_ROWS=48` alone | 0.2809 | -0.0014 |
| **`PC_INERT=dw`, after the rung fix, NO forced cap** | **0.2832** | **+0.0009** |

So the per-axis acceptance is a small WIN and the clamped-channel arithmetic owes none of the
loss. Two arms separate the causes: `MAX_ROWS=48` ALONE reproduces the refusal's 0.2809, so the
cap moves nothing by itself, and once the rung is fixed the acceptance reaches the same 0.2832
with no cap forced at all. See "On the DEPTHWISE path the two low CBUF rungs do not deliver".
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 500 val2017 images per arm, 2026-08-04]

**IT SHIPS ON, AND THE WALL IS WHAT DECIDES IT.** `ROCKET_RK3576_PC_INERT` defaults to full
acceptance; `0` refuses every clamped channel and `dw` restricts the acceptance to the
depthwise path. The mAP is the smaller half of the case — the larger one is that **a refused
layer SPLITS the partition it sits in**, so its cost is a graph property and not a node's.
Refusing leaves EfficientDet-Lite0's head as a 12-layer and a 28-layer partition carrying
5394 KiB of cube joins between them; accepting makes it ONE 43-layer partition carrying 7594.
The claimed-node counts barely move (231, 233, 234 of 267) and the wall moves 17%:

| arm | claimed | head partition | wall | vs CPU TFLite |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|
| `PC_INERT=0` | 231 | 12 + 28 layers, 5394 KiB joined | 99.3 ms | 1.23x |
| `PC_INERT=dw` | 233 | 42 layers, 6794 KiB | 90.3 ms | 1.35x |
| **default (full)** | **234** | **43 layers, 7594 KiB** | **82.8 ms** | **1.47x** |

Five interleaved rounds of 20 iterations, governor pinned to `performance`, first process
discarded; each arm's four rounds sit inside 0.6 ms and the CPU arm is constant at 121.9. On
`ondemand` the same ordering reads 120.2 / 110.9 / ~99 with more spread. **The wall is what
separates the two acceptance modes, where the mAP cannot**: 7.5 ms is twelve times the
run-to-run spread, where their 0.0006 mAP difference is inside what one count of drift does to
an NMS-ordered detection list. Under the shipped default the model scores **0.2826 against the
CPU's 0.2823**, and SSD — which carries no clamped channel and is the control — is unmoved at
0.2617 against 0.2631 and 17.0 ms. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-04]

**A CHANNEL-RANGE SPLIT IS NOT THE LEVER FOR THE REST.** A depthwise conv is one task because
output channel `c` is bound to INPUT channel `c`, and the obvious recourse — give a channel
RANGE its own task and its own `OUT_CVT` shift — was read out over every reachable tile (a
multiple of the surface's 16-channel atom) on all six layers, asking the shipped planner with
the range sliced as a layer of its own. It clears **3 of the 6**: op#15 at a uniform tile of 96
and ops #44 and #52 at 32 and 16, or at **3, 6 and 3** programs with a non-uniform partition
(greedy-longest from the left, which a planner could emit). The other three carry an
IRREDUCIBLE 16-channel atom — one whose base-setting channel and whose smallest-scale channel
sit inside the finest slice the surface admits — and no 16-aligned partition clears them at
all; one of them (op#48) gets WORSE as the tile shrinks, 1 clamped range at 1152 against 4 at
32. The mechanism is that a depthwise layer cannot SORT: a consecutive range only narrows the
scale spread where the model's channel order happens to be locally scale-ordered, and that is
a property of the model rather than of the tiling. [host readout, 2026-08-04]

**AND A PER-AXIS HANDLE'S SURFACE WAS NOT IN THE MODEL'S CHANNEL ORDER.** The direct
per-axis path sorts its output channels by scale so that each tile spans as little of the
layer's range as the tiling allows, and a tile's channels occupy CONSECUTIVE slots of the
surface — so the surface comes out sorted. The de-scatter puts a materialised tensor back in
model order and hides it completely, which is why every per-op gate is exact and why the
graph gate's per-axis pass, which runs each layer as a materialising per-op call, is green.
But a surface is what the cube path hands the next layer as its FEATURE CUBE, and there the
channel order IS the layout: the consumer reads sorted channels as model order and computes a
full, correctly sized, entirely plausible surface from the wrong data. Fixed where it costs
nothing: with ONE output-channel tile the sort buys nothing either, because that tile spans
every channel whatever their order — the base gain and each channel's `C` are the same values
in different slots — so it is dropped and the surface comes out in model order. Multi-tile
handles keep the sort, where it is the whole accuracy lever, and their cube is refused anyway
because each tile owns its own surface. The invariant now sits in `r76_cube_shape_ok()` as
well, at the place that depends on it.

With both: per-axis ResNet-18 **89 of 100** against the per-tensor control's **93 of 100** —
the remaining gap being the per-axis requant's own accuracy on near-ties, which is the
expected regime — and EfficientDet at CPU parity. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-04]

**Against TFLite the graph differs on 0.095% of elements, every one of them by a single
count**, and the pooling layer and the classifier match exactly. That residue is the
requant and not a defect: this DPU's `OUT_CVT` carries a 15-bit multiplier and rounds
ties to even, where TFLite carries 31 bits and rounds half away from zero, so two
fixed-point approximations of one real-valued scale disagree on the elements near a
boundary. Fed its own previous output rather than TFLite's, a layer's disagreements
compound down the graph — by the classifier only 76% of the logits are bit-identical —
and the **top-1 and the whole top-5 still agree**. Which is the useful statement about a
quantized classifier: the ranking survives what the last bit does not.

**The fused RELU6 is free.** Every intermediate tensor is quantized at scale 6/255 with
its zero point at the bottom of the range, so the activation's clamp is exactly the int8
saturation the DPU already applies. The gate asserts that rather than assuming it.

**Three things a per-op gate structurally could not see**, all found by running the graph:

- **The two dead CBUF rungs** above. Every shape in the correctness envelope sat at F=0;
  the first plane large enough to need a rung was this network's second depthwise block.
  (The axis that measurement read off — the kernel — is not the one on the DIRECT path,
  where it is the resident weight footprint and a square-kernel sweep cannot separate the
  two. On the DEPTHWISE path no footprint threshold fits at all and the rungs are declined.
  See the symmetry audit below, and "On the DEPTHWISE path the two low CBUF rungs do not
  deliver".)
- **TFLite's SAME padding is ASYMMETRIC, and it is an OUTPUT EXTENT rather than a
  materialised border.** A stride-2 SAME convolution over an even plane pads one row and
  one column at the END only. The CNA takes no configured trailing pad — it DERIVES the
  pad its last window consumes from the output extent and the leading pad — so that layer
  is a leading pad of zero against an extent one larger than the symmetric formula gives,
  and nothing has to be copied. Written up below; the first reading of this was that the
  border had to be materialised, and that buffer is what broke the cube chain.
- **The int8 first conv's bounds are jointly the ONNX symmetric-SAME convention**, and a
  TFLite SAME stem has `pad_left = 0`, so it does not satisfy them directly. The network
  runs its stem on the DIRECT path — and at the model's own three channels, because the int8
  direct cube is a 32-channel MAC group at every count (see below). The widening to eight
  channels that came first is exact too: the added channels hold the input zero point against
  zero weights, which contributes nothing to either half of the coefficient `A` fold and is
  exact for any zero point. That is not
  the last word — shifting the INPUT by one pixel against a pad of one moves the sample
  grid by a whole stride and reproduces TFLite's grid bit-exactly in the interior. That
  lowering is wired and gated but is a LOSS against a resident widened stem; it is
  written up above.

**Every layer of it runs on paths with no wide-output hazard.** The graph is int8
throughout, so it pays no poison guard and no power cycle, and the atom drop the int32
writers show did not appear — which is what makes this wall a wall and not a floor
with retries in it. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_net_gate.c`]

**THE GRAPH'S WALL IS HOST ARITHMETIC OVER THE WEIGHTS, NOT DISPATCH — at this operating
point, which the levers below then leave behind.** The whole inference is **40 submits
carrying 40 tasks**, and the three layers that cost most — the stem and the two
1024-channel 1x1 convolutions — are 5, 1 and 1 of them. So output-channel tiling, the
obvious suspect when a layer is slow on a submit-floored part, is not what those layers
are spending: they were already one submit each. `ROCKET_RK3576_INT8_PROF=1` splits a call
into sums / feature pack / weight pack / coefficients / plan+gen / submit / de-scatter, and
across the graph the submit is **1-16% of a layer and 3% of the two most expensive**,
against a host share of 84-99%. Measure the submit COUNT before believing a submit-floor
story, and then measure the submit TIME before believing the count: the ~340 us floor is a
floor on a round trip that waits for a full surface to drain, and these jobs are well under
it (0.13-0.25 ms each).

**That ratio is a measurement of THIS graph, not a property of the part**, and it does not
survive the levers in the sections below: residency removes the weight arithmetic, the cube
layout removes the transposes, and the kick removes most of the submits. On the graph that
runs today the host share is 6-12% and the two live terms are the submit and the write
guard — see "What the wall is made of, once the graph work is collected".

What the host is doing is two loops over the weight tensor, and **neither depends on the
input**: the per-output-channel filter sums fold the input zero point, and the cube scatter
is a pure re-layout. Straightened in place they took the graph from 146 ms to 115 ms.

**HOISTED OUT OF THE INFERENCE ENTIRELY, THEY AND THE BO CHURN THEY CARRIED ARE 65% OF THE
WALL: 98.8 ms -> 33.6 ms, a 2.94x speedup on the same 40 submits.** That is
`rocket_conv2d_int8_pack_rk3576()` — a handle a caller packs once and passes per inference
— and its answer is bit-identical to the per-call entry's: the graph's TFLite distance is
the same 4792 of 5044713 elements, every one of the 29 compute layers still bit-exact
against a CPU model of the part's own arithmetic, and the whole top-5 unchanged. It is one
code path, not a second implementation: a transient call builds the same handle and packs
each output-channel tile as its tile loop reaches it, so there is no second arithmetic to
keep in agreement. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_net_gate.c`,
`ROCKET_RK3576_NET_RESIDENT=1`]

**Only 29 ms of the 65 is the weight arithmetic the profile predicted.** Closing the
accounting is what found the rest — the profiler stopped at the last bucket and the
teardown happens after it, so 16 ms per inference had been attributed to nothing at all.
Per inference over 20 iterations:

| bucket | per-call | resident | removed |
|---|---|---|---|
| filter sums | 4.0 | 0.0 | 4.0 |
| feature pack | 27.8 | 11.6 | **16.2** |
| weight cube | 24.0 | 0.0 | 24.0 |
| coefficients | 0.6 | 0.0 | 0.6 |
| plan + gen | 6.7 | 2.2 | 4.5 |
| submit | 7.6 | 7.6 | 0.0 |
| de-scatter | 6.6 | 6.8 | 0.0 |
| **BO teardown** | **16.1** | 0.0 | **16.1** |
| library total | 93.3 | 28.3 | 65.0 |

Three things in that table are not what the earlier reading of this path expected.
**Unmapping the BOs a layer touched is 16 ms of a 99 ms graph** — a megabyte-sized dirty
mapping per tile, once per layer per inference — and it is invisible to any profile that
ends at the de-scatter. **The feature bucket more than halved without its scatter loop
changing at all**: 16 of its 28 ms were the allocation, the zeroing memset and the two
cache-maintenance ioctls, so the answer to "is the stem's feature pack the scatter?" is no,
most of it was the buffer. And **`plan+gen` was mostly an allocation too** — the regcmd BO.
The scatter that remains, 11.6 ms, is the transpose itself — vectorized below.

After it the graph is 33.6 ms and the submit is **22% of the library time rather than 8%**,
so this path has moved much closer to the dispatch story it was wrongly assumed to be in.
What is left on the host is the feature scatter (11.6), the de-scatter (6.8) and the
2.2 ms the table calls `plan + gen`.

**And that 2.2 ms is not the register program.** A resident handle's DMA addresses do not
move between calls, so the whole per-row-task stream is a pure function of frozen state
and can be generated once at pack time — built, gated bit-identical over the 173-shape
resident A/B and the whole network, and **worth 0.13 ms of 33.6**. What the bucket
actually holds is the OUTPUT SURFACE'S SENTINEL STAMP: the same bucket is 2.06 ms per
inference with the stamp on and **0.02 ms with it off**, so neither the row plan nor the
emitter is measurable at all. Cutting the stamp's `memset` from the whole surface to 64
bytes moves it 1.53 -> 1.45. The stamp itself is load-bearing: it is what makes "this task
never wrote" a property of the surface, and a conv inherits the wide-output poisoning from
whatever ran before it — so the lever is WHERE it is written, not whether.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-07-29]

**The next call's stamp now rides the de-scatter's own bracket** — the surface is already
inside a `PREP_BO`/`FINI_BO` pair there, so a resident tile pays one round trip per call
instead of two. Interleaved bench-100 runs of two binaries over five pairs: **30.2 ms
against 29.7**, and four sequential blocks of 100 each way put 29.9-30.6 against 29.8 and
below with no overlap. About **0.5 ms of 30**, never negative.

**And that corrects the attribution above.** "Two cache ioctls" predicts that removing one
of the two brackets removes half the cost, and it does not: with the sentinel off entirely
the same bench is **28.5 ms**, so the whole stamp costs 1.7 ms in the old arrangement and
1.2 in the new one. The fill's OWN cache maintenance follows it into whichever bracket it
lands in — `dma_sync_sgtable_for_device` walks the buffer whatever is dirty in it, which is
also why the 64-byte memset bought almost nothing. What the fold removes is one ioctl PAIR
per tile per call, and that is the 0.5 ms.

**The bucket profile is the wrong instrument for a lever this size.** Under
`ROCKET_RK3576_INT8_PROF=1` the two buckets are not additive with the wall: the
sentinel-off run reads WORSE in `plan+gen` plus `de-scatter` than either stamped
arrangement while being 1.2 ms FASTER in the wall, because the stamp also warms the output
surface for the de-scatter that follows it and that saving lands in a different bucket than
its cost. Long interleaved runs of the whole graph are what separated the two.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-07-29]

### The CHW <-> NC1HWC2 transpose is a 16x16 block, and eight live vectors is the point

The cube interleaves sixteen channels into every sixteen-byte atom, so a row-major tensor
and a cube are a **transpose** and not a copy. Written an element at a time that is one
useful byte per destination cache line, and the sixteen channels of a group each walk the
same lines again — four line touches per byte moved. A **16x16 block** does the same work
in whole vectors: sixteen 16-byte loads, four levels of `TRN1`/`TRN2`, four full cache
lines stored, every line touched once.

**Eight live vectors, not sixteen.** A 16x16 written as one array of sixteen `uint8x16_t`
needs thirty-two registers with its scratch, so the compiler puts the array on the STACK
and the whole point is lost — measured on the part at **3x slower** than the same
transpose done as two 8x8 halves plus a swap of the off-diagonal quadrants, which never
has more than eight live. Against the scalar loop, at `ic=128 px=3136`: pack 0.42 -> 0.23
as an array of sixteen and -> **0.078** as quadrants; unpack 0.35 -> 0.21 -> **0.095**.

The full-group predicate is a **constant argument to one `always_inline` helper**, so the
compiler emits the unpredicated sixteen-channel loop and the partial one from one source
rather than from two hand-written kernels. A per-lane runtime predicate costs 10-25%.
Dead lanes are still WRITTEN on the way in: they carry the cube's channel padding, which
must be zero, and a whole atom is stored either way.

**On the graph it is 1.41x**, bit-identical — the same 4792-element TFLite distance, the
same top-5, the same 173-shape resident A/B:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| feature scatter | 10.91 | 4.94 |
| de-scatter (readback) | 7.90 | 5.30 |
| submit | 7.17 | 7.22 |
| library total | 26.26 | 17.70 |
| **inference** | **29.5-30.3** | **20.3-21.3** |

**The submit is now the largest single bucket** at 41% of the library time, for the first
time on this path.

**What is left in those two buckets is the bytes, not the loop.** The de-scatter's
`PREP_BO` — the DPU write drain — is only **0.53 ms** of the 5.3, measured rather than
assumed, so the transpose really is the rest. At ~5 MB each way the two buckets run at
2.1-2.4 GB/s against this part's single-thread `memcpy` ceiling of **4.4 GB/s**
(`build/membench`, 256 MB): within about 2x of the cost of moving the bytes at all, where
before they were at 6-9x. Further vectorization is worth little.

### A tensor stays in CUBE LAYOUT between consecutive layers

The transposes above do not have to be paid at all between two NPU layers. A direct
conv's output surface stride is `ow*oh` exactly — `rocket_rk3576_out_surf_elems` pads only
the depthwise case — and a feature cube's channel-group stride is `ih*iw`, so **layer n's
output surface IS layer n+1's feature cube, byte for byte**, whenever the plane and the
channel rounding agree.

It is an API question and not a hardware one. Both ends are properties of the resident
handle, which already owns the input and output BOs and already freezes the fd:
`rocket_conv2d_int8_cube_of_rk3576()` describes a handle's output surface,
`_cube_in_rk3576()` makes another handle read from it instead of scattering, and
`_cube_out_rk3576()` leaves the output where the DPU wrote it. `in` and `out` may be NULL
for the end a handle no longer uses. A cube-in call does no cache maintenance either —
nothing on the CPU touches those lines, so the scatter's `PREP_BO`/`FINI_BO` pair goes
with it.

**The channel-group STRIDE is not one of the bounds.** A direct convolution writes `ow*oh`
elements per group and a depthwise one `round4(ow*oh)` — 52 against a 7x7 plane's 49 — but
the consumer's DDR group jump is a REGISTER the emitter fills (`R76_CNA_SURF_FULL`,
`0x1094`, in 16-byte atoms) rather than a constant, and **the part honours any value at or
above the plane**: bit-exact at +3, +16 and +64 elements over five geometries, at one row
task and at ten, with the task-window stride (`0x1098`) left derived, and the control that
lays the same padded buffer out without telling the register differs every time
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_surf_stride.c`]. So `rocket_rk3576_cube` carries its
producer's stride and `_cube_in_rk3576()` passes it to the program; a depthwise producer at
a plane whose element count is not a multiple of four joins like any other. A stride SHORTER
than the plane is refused — it would make the groups overlap, and nothing here produces one.

**Three bounds are refusals rather than fix-ups**, each naming a shape where the two
buffers are not the same object:

- **More than one output-channel tile.** Each tile owns its own surface BO and several BOs
  are not one cube. Nothing in a MobileNet reaches it: a 1x1 layer's weight slice is 32 KiB
  against the 144 KiB that leaves the tile at the whole layer.
- **An input channel count that is not a multiple of 32, from a producer that does not say
  what its tail holds.** A handle's own cube fills the channels past `ic` with its border
  constant and a producer's surface holds whatever the DPU wrote into its own padded output
  channels. Their weights are zero, so nothing reaches the MAC either way — what reads them
  is the coefficient group's B term, which sums every tap of the whole programmed group. Two
  things are past it: a symmetric weight quantization has no B term at all, and a DIRECT
  producer's partial output group carries its output zero point, which at a graph join is
  the consumer's own border constant. `rocket_rk3576_cube` carries `pad_from`/`pad_value` so
  that is stated rather than assumed, and the check is against the consumer's `in_zp`. A
  DEPTHWISE producer's surface carries the deeper tail the host fills at pack time — see
  "What a producer leaves past its live output channels" below.
- A mismatched fd or plane.

A cube may also be a SLICE of a larger buffer, which is what a residual topology needs; see
"A cube's base is a plain address" below.

#### What a producer leaves past its live output channels

A consumer whose `ic` is not a multiple of 32 walks the round-32 count, so the join turns on
what is in the channels between the producer's `oc` and that boundary. It is not unknown.

**A direct producer's partial output group carries its OUTPUT ZERO POINT** — the channels
past `oc` are packed with zero weights and a zero A term, so their accumulator is zero and
the OUT_CVT sends them there. At a graph join that IS the consumer's input zero point,
because the two are one tensor's quantization, and so exactly the border constant the
consumer's own cube would have filled. Measured 12544 of 12544 padding elements at `oc` 16,
24 and 144 and at `k` 1 and 3 (`tests/rk3576_pad_channels.c`).

**Only once the tail is given NO B TERM, and that is the trap.** `B` is per output channel
and the obvious packer loop gives every channel of the PROGRAMMED count the same one — and
then a padding channel carries `requant(B*sum(x))`, which is data-dependent. Measured 5776
of 12544 padding elements away from the output zero point at `oc=16 w_zp=12`. Those channels
are not a live output, so the correction they lose is one nobody applies; the live ones are
unchanged. **A gate whose producers are all symmetric cannot see this axis** — the one that
missed it accepted MobileNetV2's layer 2 -> 3 join and took that layer to 0.5% agreement
with the row-major path, while every isolated per-op case stayed byte-identical.

**A depthwise producer is programmed with the RAW output count and writes nothing past it**
— 12544 of 12544 padding elements still holding the sentinel — so its surface is a group
SHORT of what a consumer reads: 9 groups at `oc=144` against the 10 a direct consumer of 144
channels walks.

**That tail is a host fact, not a hardware one.** Such a tile allocates the deeper surface
and fills the group past its own with the OUTPUT ZERO POINT once, at pack time. Nothing
overwrites it: the program is told the raw count and does not address those bytes, and the
sentinel covers the written groups alone. Read back after an inference the fill is still
standing (12544 of 12544 at the output zero point, `oc` 16/48/144), so a depthwise producer
declares the same tail a direct one does and the join is available.

Only when the raw count fills its last written group. Below that — `oc=24`, whose two groups
hold 32 channels — the tail lies INSIDE a group the program does write, whether the DPU
leaves those channels alone has not been measured, and the surface stays short and the join
refused.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_pad_channels.c`, 2026-07-31]

**The linking has to be done as a PRE-PASS over already-packed handles**, not decided as
the graph runs: the producer's "leave it in the cube" and the consumer's "read it from
there" are one decision, because a producer that skipped its de-scatter for a consumer
that then refused the cube would have left no row-major tensor at all.

On MobileNetV1-224 that is **24 joins of 30 layers** and takes the graph
**20.9-21.4 -> 11.9-12.3 ms, 1.73x**, on the same 40 submits and 40 tasks — with the
asymmetric-pad lowering below, which is what closes four of the joins. The two compose
superadditively; measured interleaved at `bench 100`:

| asymmetric pad | cube chain | ms per inference |
|---|---|---|
| materialised | off | 20.9-21.4 |
| extent | off | 20.1-20.5 |
| materialised | on | 15.7-16.4 |
| **extent** | **on** | **11.9-12.3** |

| bucket | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| submit | 8.4 | 7.5 |
| feature scatter | 6.5 | 3.0 |
| de-scatter | 5.5 | 0.8 |
| plan+gen (carries the stamp) | 0.8 | 2.6 |

**Gate it against the ARITHMETIC.** A layout change owes byte-identity: run the graph
row-major, snapshot every layer, link the cubes, run it again, and compare every layer the
second run still materialises plus the final logits. A wrong byte inside a join reaches the
next materialisation point, so nothing hides in the middle of a chain. Measured: every
materialised layer identical, the logits byte-identical, the top-5 unchanged, the
4792-element TFLite distance unmoved.

**The SUBMIT was 62% of the library time** — 40 submits of about 190 us each, well under
the ~440 us floor, so the lever there is the submit COUNT across layers and not the floor.
**A submit removed is worth about 110 us on this graph**, measured rather than inferred:
chaining a tile's row tasks into one hardware kick takes the graph from 40 submits to 29 and
from 11.8-12.2 ms to 10.5-11.1 — the same 40 tasks, the same assertions. That is 9-11% here
against about 3% over the per-op conv gate, because a graph's layers are now cheap enough for
the submit to dominate, and it is why that lever is DEFAULT-ON
(`ROCKET_RK3576_BATCH_TASKS=0` turns it off). Chaining ACROSS layers is the same lever taken
further and is worth much more; see below.
The sentinel stamp is 0.9-1.9 ms of the 12 whichever path it rides: on the row-major path it
is the fill inside the de-scatter's bracket, and on the cube path its own `PREP_BO`/`FINI_BO`
pair at the start of the next call, because the surface is read as an input before that
handle runs again. The two cost the same, so the cube chain does not make the guard more
expensive. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-07-29]

### The sentinel guard costs a bracket per surface per call, and the bracket count is the lever

Once a run of layers is one hardware kick, the guard is the largest single bucket in the
whole graph: `ROCKET_RK3576_I32_SENTINEL=0` takes MobileNetV2 from **10.0 to 5.9 ms** and
MobileNetV1 from 6.4 to 3.5. Most of it is not the fill: on the graphs as they run today the
memsets are **15-18%** of the guard and the CACHE MAINTENANCE is the rest (see the two
sections below, where both terms are measured directly rather than inferred from an A/B).

`PREP_BO` syncs a whole BO for the CPU and `FINI_BO` syncs the whole BO back for the device,
neither taking a range, so a bracket costs two passes over the surface however few bytes are
touched inside it. The chained path opened two brackets on every layer's surface per call —
one to ask whether its tasks wrote, one to put the next call's sentinel back — for a single
CPU visit. Folding the re-stamp into the verify bracket is **10.0 -> 8.8 ms on V2 and
6.4 -> 5.4 on V1**, byte-identical, on the same submits and tasks. The order is safe because
the redo path re-stamps every surface anyway, so a layer stamped before a later one is found
bad loses nothing.

The last layer keeps its own handling under the de-scatter, which has to read the surface
before anything may overwrite it, and a surface shared with something outside the chain still
takes its stamp at the start of the next call — those two are read after the kick by
something the kick does not contain.

What is left is one bracket per BO per call, and it is BYTES-proportional rather than
per-ioctl.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_net_gate.c`, 2026-07-31]

### One bracket per BO, not per layer, and this one is free

A bracket syncs the WHOLE object, and several layers of one run write SLICES of one buffer
whenever a concatenation is placed — every operand of one does. The pass therefore re-synced
that buffer once per operand while checking a different slice of it each time. Naming each BO
once checks exactly the same bytes, so unlike the two levers below there is no coverage in it.

Key on the KERNEL HANDLE. A placed slice carries its own descriptor of the same underlying
object, so keyed on the `rocket_bo` pointer the redundancy reads as **zero on every graph**,
including one with fourteen placed concatenations — a probe whose arm that must show a
positive shows none.

Byte-identical per layer, TFLite's own top-1, the same submits and the same tasks
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate bench 100`, ondemand, 2026-08-03]:

| | brackets | bytes | guard | wall | -> brackets | bytes | guard | wall |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | 30.3 | 4987 KiB | 2.03 | 5.0 | 30.3 | 4987 | 2.07 | 5.0 |
| MobileNetV2 | 85.4 | 8665 | 3.36 | 6.9 | **65.4** | **7721** | **3.19** | **6.6** |
| ResNet-18 | 51.1 | 8243 | 2.87 | 7.4 | **35.1** | **5303** | **2.10** | **6.5-6.6** |
| Inception V1 | 122.2 | 14465 | 4.75 | 8.8 | **68.2** | **7207** | **2.83** | **7.0-7.1** |
| Inception V3 | 122.4 | 19713 | 6.35 | 42.3 | **102.4** | **17272** | **5.80** | **40.7-41.9** |

**MobileNetV1 is the zero and it is the control**: it has no placed concatenation, shares no
BO, and does not move. The guard-bucket saving equals the wall saving to **0.05 ms on every
graph**, which is what says the lever is the bracket bytes and nothing else.

(Inception V3's row is that lever's own A/B and is left at the numbers it was measured on.
That graph's wall is **38.4-38.8 ms** since its fifteenth concatenation became placement; the
other four rows are current.)

Ranged brackets are left per layer: two slices' ranges are disjoint, so there is nothing
redundant to remove and a pooled bracket would have to carry their union.

What it ADDS is a linear scan of the open set per layer, bounded by a run's distinct BO count,
and one pointer array per chain.

### A bracket is a 2.24 us floor plus 0.194 us/KiB, so the bytes are the whole of it

Measured directly over a 4 KiB - 4 MiB ladder, 400 iterations a rung, taking the median
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_prep_floor.c`, 2026-08-02]:

| | floor | slope | rate |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `PREP_BO` | 1.33 us | 0.0969 us/KiB | 10.57 GB/s |
| `FINI_BO` | 1.11 us | 0.0970 us/KiB | 10.56 GB/s |
| one bracket | 2.24 us | 0.1939 us/KiB | 5.28 GB/s |

An ioctl the kernel refuses at handle lookup — no fence wait and no cache walk — is **1.17
us**, which is the syscall, the drm dispatch and the GEM lookup. So past a few KiB the ioctl
is entirely the walk. The memset inside a bracket adds a further **0.113 us/KiB**, and it is
charged once per SURFACE per call rather than once per bracket, because each surface is
stamped either in the verify bracket or in the next call's stamp loop and never both.

The chain counts its own guard (`ROCKET_RK3576_INT8_PROF=1` prints brackets and bytes), which
turns the guard's milliseconds into arithmetic: **brackets x the floor is 0.07-0.27 ms, 4-7%
of each graph's guard.** The floor is not what needs removing.

### The guard is a fill and a bracket, and the fill is a fifth of it

The two halves have separate levers, so the chain counts them separately
(`ROCKET_RK3576_INT8_PROF=1` prints `write guard = fill ... + brackets and scan ...`). The
FILL is the memset that puts the sentinel back, paid per LAYER because every slice of a
shared buffer still has to be filled; the remainder is the two cache-maintenance ioctls, the
scan and the loop. Naming each BO once reached the second only, which is what leaves the
first worth measuring rather than inferring as a residual — inferred from the bracket cost
model it comes out 2.1x too large.

Per inference, default guard [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate bench 100`,
`INT8_PROF=1`, ondemand, 2026-08-03]:

| | guard | fill | fill bytes | rate | brackets + scan |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | 2.07 | 0.56 (27%) | 4933 KiB | 0.113 us/KiB | 1.52 |
| MobileNetV2 | 3.19 | 0.81 (25%) | 7116 | 0.114 | 2.38 |
| ResNet-18 | 2.12 | 0.40 (19%) | 3470 | 0.114 | 1.73 |
| Inception V1 | 2.79 | 0.53 (19%) | 4581 | 0.116 | 2.26 |
| Inception V3 | 5.80 | 1.00 (17%) | 8629 | 0.117 | 4.82 |

The rate is flat at **0.113-0.117 us/KiB on all five graphs**, independently reproducing the
0.113 `tests/rk3576_prep_floor.c` measures on a synthetic ladder — so the fill is a memset
running at the part's own speed and there is nothing in the loop to collect.

**NO COVERAGE-FREE LEVER EXISTS ON IT**, and that is a property of the CHECK rather than of
the loop: the check reads the whole extent on a task that wrote nothing, so the stamp has to
cover whatever the check may read. Narrowing the fill IS narrowing the check. The free lever
really was spent when each BO was named once.

### What narrowing collects, in those two halves

`GUARD_NARROW=1` against the deduplicated default, same run structure, all five graphs green
and byte-identical with TFLite's own top-1 [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate bench 100`,
`INT8_PROF=1`, ondemand, 2026-08-03]:

| | guard | narrowed | lever | of which fill | of which brackets + scan |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | 2.07 | 0.67 | 1.40 | 0.53 (38%) | 0.88 |
| MobileNetV2 | 3.19 | 1.20 | 1.99 | 0.75 (38%) | 1.23 |
| ResNet-18 | 2.12 | 0.71 | 1.41 | 0.38 (27%) | 1.04 |
| Inception V1 | 2.79 | 1.50 | 1.29 | 0.49 (38%) | 0.80 |
| Inception V3 | 5.80 | 1.60 | 4.20 | 0.97 (23%) | 3.27 |

The fill is **23-38% of what narrowing buys**; the rest is the bracket bytes, which need the
ranged ioctl. The two were recorded as conjunctive from their rates — this is the measured
split of that conjunction.

The lever is **1.3-4.2 ms, not the 1.2-1.3 ms** a headline once stated: that figure was
Inception V1's alone, and the table it sat above already showed 1.2-3.4.

The bracket COUNT rises as the footprint narrows (30 / 65 / 35 / 68 / 102 -> 30 / 85 / 51 /
122 / 122), because ranged brackets are not pooled — two slices' ranges are disjoint.
Inception V3's narrowed guard is 1.60 ms where brackets x the floor predicts 0.30, so about
1.3 ms of it is per-RANGE kernel work, which is the same residual recorded elsewhere on this
page at a few hundred ns per 64-byte range.

### The guard's bytes come off with a narrowed footprint and a ranged ioctl, together

The check asks whether a task wrote ANYTHING, so it reads only until the first byte that
differs from the sentinel — but the stamp has to cover whatever the check MAY read, and on a
task that wrote nothing that is the whole extent. Narrowed to one cache line per (task,
channel group), both ends touch tens of bytes instead of the surface
(`ROCKET_RK3576_GUARD_NARROW=1`).

That alone collects only the FILL, because `PREP_BO` and `FINI_BO` sync the whole BO however
few bytes sit inside them. `DRM_ROCKET_PREP_BO_RANGES` / `_FINI_BO_RANGES`
(`patches/rk3576/npu/0023`, interface **1.5**) take an array of `{offset, size}` and sync only
those. The two are CONJUNCTIVE by construction: a ranged ioctl can only name a small footprint
if the guard has one, and the un-narrowed extents' union is the whole surface.

Together, on the same submits and tasks, byte-identical per layer and each graph still
returning TFLite's own top-1 [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate bench 100`, 2026-08-02]:

The three forms priced against each other and against the guard-off floor, at the current
default (which already names each BO once) [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate bench 100`,
ondemand, 2026-08-03]:

| | default | guard-off floor | per-kick | narrowed |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | 4.9 | 3.0 | **3.0** | 3.7 |
| MobileNetV2 | 6.5 | 3.5 | **3.5** | 4.7 |
| ResNet-18 | 6.6 | 4.5 | **4.5** | 5.2 |
| Inception V1 | 7.0 | 4.2 | **4.1** | 5.7 |
| Inception V3 | 38.5-38.8 | 31.9-32.9 | 36.1-36.5 | 34.9-35.5 |

(Inception V3's row is re-measured, two runs an arm, since its fifteenth concatenation became
placement; the other four are unmoved by that and are single runs as before. **That row's
BASELINE has since moved again** — the divisor-lag check now scores the tail, so the graph's
default wall is 33.4-33.7 rather than 38.5-38.8. The guard deltas are a different term and are
not expected to move with it, but the row is a stored `ondemand` table and has not been
re-taken.)

**`GUARD_PER_KICK` lands on the guard-off floor on every graph that is ONE kick, and collects
only 2.3-2.4 of Inception V3's 5.9-6.6** — that graph runs its convolution calls outside any
kick in the twenties, and a per-kick guard reaches none of them. Its coverage is a property of
the run structure.

**Pinned to `performance`, where the arms' differing host work cannot move the clock, the two
cheap forms are worth LESS than that `ondemand` table reads** — three interleaved repeats an
arm on Inception V3, at a within-arm spread of 0.3 [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-04]:

| arm | ms | vs default |
|---|---:|---:|
| default | 29.53 | — |
| `GUARD_PER_KICK` | 27.53 | −2.00 |
| `GUARD_NARROW` | 27.20 | −2.33 |
| `POOL_LAG_TAIL` | 26.00 | −3.53 |
| per-kick + tail | 24.07 | −5.46 |
| conv-guard-off floor | 24.50 | −5.03 |

Two readings. **The guard and the lag check are additive to 0.07 ms** (−2.00 + −3.53 against
−5.46 measured), so they are separate terms and not two views of one. And **per-kick + tail
sits BELOW the conv-guard-off floor while still guarding every kick**, because that floor arm
removes only the conv write guard and the pair also collects the lag check — a floor is a
property of the term it was measured on, not of the wall. On `ondemand` the pair is not
separable from the tail alone (33.50 against 33.43, at a 4.7 ms spread), which is what the
"pin the governor for an A/B whose arms differ in host work" rule is about.

The narrowed form's advantage over the default is now **1.2-1.3 ms rather than 3.1** on
Inception V1: naming each BO once collects, with no coverage trade, more than half of what
narrowing was collecting.

Earlier rows for Inception V1 in this section were measured with its stem on the DIRECT
lowering and with a bracket per LAYER; that graph's stem now runs the packed-image encoding.

The residual guard is well above brackets-times-floor (0.59 against 0.07 on V1) because a
RANGE costs per-range kernel work of its own — `memdup_user`, the validation pass, and a
scatterlist walk plus an `iommu_iova_to_phys` per range. At 12-20 us a bracket against a 2.24
us floor and tens of 64-byte ranges each, that is a few hundred nanoseconds a range. It is
what the change ADDS, and it is a fifth to a third of what a floor-only model predicts.

**The narrowing is a coverage trade, which is why it is off by default.** A line per group per
task still witnesses the hazard the guard exists for — the wide-output poisoning kills a whole
submit, so every line of every extent still holds the sentinel. What it stops seeing is a
PARTIAL write that spares exactly those lines, the same class `GUARD_PER_KICK` trades away. It
also raises the false-alarm rate: a task whose first 64 bytes per group genuinely equal the
sentinel byte reads as unwritten and costs a redo, where the wide check needs the whole extent
to.

**Spending FEWER BRACKETS is the other lever, and it is nearly all of the guard.** Asking one
surface per kick rather than every layer's lands on the guard-off floor on all four one-kick
graphs (table above), on the same submits and tasks, with logits and every materialised layer
byte-identical. `ROCKET_RK3576_GUARD_PER_KICK=1`; the default asks every layer.

The reason one surface can be enough is that the hazard is per SUBMIT: a job whose DPU output
element is wider than one byte leaves the next submit of any kind writing nothing, and a
cross-layer kick is one submit, so every surface in it witnesses a poisoning equally.
**Measured rather than argued** — a wide-output matmul injected before each inference, two
alternating inputs, each inference scored byte for byte against its own clean answer:
**6 of 150 inferences wrong with the guard off, 0 of 150 with either guard**, at
indistinguishable repair rates (90.3 against 88.8 tasks per inference where an unrepaired one
is 78) [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `ROCKET_RK3576_NET_POISON` / `_VARY`, 2026-08-01].

Two things that measurement does NOT settle. It induces the whole-submit poisoning, so it says
nothing about a program dying in the MIDDLE of a stream while the last one still writes —
which is the failure the full guard is shaped for and the one a single check cannot see by
construction. And at a 4% event rate over 150 inferences it would not have surfaced a class
occurring an order of magnitude more rarely.

**A BENCH THAT FEEDS THE SAME INPUT EVERY ITERATION IS BLIND TO ALL OF THIS**, and that is not
a property of this test. A layer whose program did not run leaves its surface holding the
previous inference's contents, which on a repeated input are exactly the contents it would
have written — so the graph returns the right answer for the wrong reason and every check
downstream passes. The staleness question only has an answer once the input varies.

### A run of cube-linked layers goes out as ONE hardware kick

Once the transposes are gone the submit is the largest single bucket, and the count is what
is left to spend: 29 jobs for 29 layers, each ~190 us against 130-250 us of compute. A run of
consecutive **cube-linked** layers can be one job instead.

**A chained stream HONOURS READ-AFTER-WRITE between its programs** [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9,
`tests/rk3576_chain_raw.c`]. That was the question chaining row tasks never had to ask — row
tasks write disjoint rows — and `PC_DONE` firing 82-343 us before the DPU's writes become
visible was a reason to expect the answer to be no. The probe runs two 1x1 int8 convolutions
where the second's feature cube IS the first's output surface, byte for byte, and compares the
batched job against the same pair as two submits and against the second program alone on a
surface nothing wrote: **40 of 40 iterations over five planes from 2 KB to 387 KB** gave the
two-submit answer. The plane is swept because the write visibility scales with the bytes in
flight, so a small surface would look ordered whether or not the part orders it.

The cube link is the eligibility test and it is one the caller has already made: between two
linked layers there is no host work at all — no de-scatter, no scatter, no cache maintenance —
so there is nothing that has to happen between the two programs.
`rocket_conv2d_int8_chain_new_rk3576()` asserts the links against the BO ADDRESSES rather than
the flags, plans and generates every program once (the stream is a function of the frozen
geometry, so it is not regenerated per call), and lays it out contiguously in one BO with each
trailer linked to the next.

**MobileNetV1-224: 10.4-10.5 ms -> 6.7 ms, 29 submits -> 6, on the same 40 tasks**, with
byte-identical logits and every materialised layer identical. 25 of 30 layers ride in two
kicks. The gate A/Bs the kicked graph against the PER-LAYER cube-chained one rather than
against the row-major one, because comparing it to row-major would prove both levers at once
and a wrong byte inside a kick would read as a cube-chain defect.

Per-layer afterwards the 23-layer kick is 65% of the wall and nothing outside it reaches
0.31 ms. The **PPU pooling layer was the other 20%**, and is now 2%; see "The pooling layer"
below.

**WHAT THE KICK IS MADE OF IS READABLE, AND IT IS FRONT-LOADED ON THE STEM.** There is no
per-program wall inside one hardware kick, but the kick's own bucket is timed and
`ROCKET_RK3576_NET_KICK_MAX=<m>` caps how many layers a run may cover — so the bucket at cap
`m` is the cost of the run's first `m` programs, the layers the cap excludes leave the kick
entirely and become their own buckets, and the FINITE DIFFERENCE between consecutive caps is
what one program costs inside the kick:

| cap | 2 | 5 | 10 | 15 | 20 | 23 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| kick bucket, ms | 1.78 | 2.53 | 3.25 | 3.52 | 3.77 | 3.98 |
| graph, ms | 8.4 | 7.0 | 6.7 | 6.0 | 5.3 | 4.9 |
| submits | 27 | 24 | 19 | 14 | 9 | 6 |

**The first two programs are 1.78 ms of the 3.98 — 45% of the kick — and the other 21 cost
0.105 ms each.** Those two are the 224x224 stem and the depthwise that follows it, by far
the largest planes in the network, so the kick's cost is the stem's compute plus a flat
per-program floor. That floor is worth stating on its own: **a program costs ~105 us inside
a chained stream against ~270 us as its own submit** (the graph falls 0.167 ms for each
layer the cap moves into the kick), and both are well under the ~440 us driver poll floor,
which bounds a round trip that waits for a full surface to drain rather than a program.

The consequence for optimizing further is that 21 of 23 programs are already at a flat floor
chaining cannot lower, and the remaining lever inside the kick is the stem — which is
separately settled and off by default. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-07-31, `bench 50`]

**BUT A KICK'S BUCKET IS NOT ALL PROGRAM EXECUTION, AND ON MOBILENETV2 MOST OF IT WAS NOT.**
The same instrument over V2's three large runs reads 4.49 / 2.59 / 1.32 ms — 84% of a 10.0 ms
wall — with a finite difference that puts most of the first run in its first four layers, the
biggest planes in the network. That reads as compute and it is a trap: turning the sentinel
off takes the same three buckets to 2.57 / 1.02 / 0.91, so **41% of the wall attributed to
three hardware kicks was the guard's cache maintenance on the surfaces inside them**, charged
to the run's first layer along with everything else. A kick's bucket is the whole `PREP` /
submit / verify / re-stamp / de-scatter round trip, so decompose it against a
guard-off control before reading a finite difference as a program cost.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-07-31, `bench 30`/`bench 100`]

Two traps, both of which write a full and entirely plausible wrong surface:

- **Both trailer fields describe the NEXT segment, and a uniform-length chain cannot tell.**
  The driver programs `PC_BASE_ADDRESS` and `PC_REGISTER_AMOUNTS` from task 0 alone, so every
  later program's address AND its length come from its predecessor's trailer. A tile's row
  tasks are all the same size, so passing "this program's count" is indistinguishable there;
  across layers a depthwise program and a direct one differ, and the mis-sized fetch left the
  affected program writing nothing at all.
- **Re-stamping the last layer's surface is only safe when the chain rewrites it before
  anyone reads it.** Every interior layer is read by the next program in the same kick, so the
  sentinel can ride the epilogue's bracket — one `PREP_BO`/`FINI_BO` pair per surface per
  call. A last layer that LEAVES A CUBE is read by a layer outside the chain later in the same
  inference, and stamping it there replaces the layer's output with the sentinel: the consumer
  then computes on 0xA5 and nothing before the next materialised layer can see it.

**HOW LONG A CHAINED STREAM MAY BE IS WHERE THE KERNEL STARTS ITS COMPLETION WAIT, NOT THE
PART.** `patches/rk3576/npu/0012` retires a job when the writing block raises its own
completion in `INTERRUPT_RAW_STATUS`, bounded by `dpu_grace_us` (500 us) so a job that
raises none cannot stall behind one — and that bound is measured on a job of ONE task.
Neither of the two signals it starts that wait on says what it was taken to say:
**`PC_DONE` is raised per TASK**, so on a `0016` kick of n tasks the two `PC_DONE` bits
alternate as the program counter retires each program; and **`PC_OPERATION_ENABLE` is a
self-clearing GO bit** that reads back 0 at every poll of a running kick, so the
`OPERATION_ENABLE == 0` half of the condition is vacuously true from the first poll onward.
The wait therefore started at the first poll tick, and `dpu_grace_us` was being asked to
cover the whole remaining stream rather than a drain. Past it the driver signals the fence
while the core is still writing: the caller reads a surface that never landed, and the next
job is programmed into a core that has not finished the last one.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9; the GO bit also source-confirmed — the vendor driver writes 1 then 0
to it back to back. See [rk3576-regcmd.md](rk3576-regcmd.md).]

The deadline each stream needs before its answer comes back bit-exact, measured by moving
`dpu_grace_us` under a cross-layer kick [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]:

| programs (layers) | deadline needed |
|---|---|
| 34 (23) | under 200 us |
| 30 (23) | 200 - 500 us |
| 35 (24) | 650 - 700 us |
| 220 (44) | under 150 us |

It is **not monotone in the program count and not predictable from the stream**, and the 500
us default sits inside the range — so the streams on the wrong side of it failed and the
ones on the right side passed by luck. That table is a race, not a property: what the
deadline has to beat is the host reaching the surface, so a stream of cheap programs (the
220-program mix, at 32 channels over a 32x32 plane) can retire early and still read
correctly while a shorter stream of expensive ones cannot. The same 35-program stream that
failed 3 of 3 is bit-exact 3 of 3 at a longer deadline, and equally at a coarser
`poll_interval_us` (250 us and up), which delays the observation and so the deadline with
it. The taint word does not move at either.

**Scaling the deadline with the tasks one kick covers retires the bound in practice**: a
uniform 3x3 stream at 128 channels then chains **40 deep where it first failed at 4**, a 5x5
one at 64 channels 24 deep where it first failed at 6, a 3x3 one at 96 channels 24 deep where
it first failed at 9, and a mixed direct/depthwise stream 220 programs deep. **It is still a
fitted number, and a stream of slower programs outruns it**: ONE 224x224 k3 convolution at 256
channels row-splits into 75 chained tasks, and the scaled deadline — 75 x 500 us — expires
37.5 ms in with the task counter reading 67 tasks started of 75. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**The part exposes the signal the deadline was standing in for.** `PC_TASK_STATUS` — at
`0x48` here, the vendor `rknpu` config's `pc_task_status_offset` — is a live pair of
counters, tasks started in the low half and tasks completed in the high half, both modulo
the programmed `TASK_NUMBER`. Mid-stream at least one half is non-zero, so `PC_DONE` set
together with a zero register means the whole kick has retired, and nothing else does.
`patches/rk3576/npu/0022` anchors the wait there: `dpu_grace_us` becomes the drain it was
measured as, applied to the program that wrote last, and a fixed backstop covers the case
where a job raises no `PC_DONE` at all. The interface version goes to **1.4**, and
`rocket_batch_completion_tracked()` is how the library tells — below it, it caps a stream at
34 programs.

Measured [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]: the 75-task convolution is **bit-exact, 150 programs in one
kick**, where a deadline scaled by the task count retires it at **t=37554 us** — 75 x 500 us
to the microsecond, counter at 67 started of 75 — and the layer computes wrong. **29 gates
pass with the taint word unmoved and ZERO backstop hits**, which also settles that every job
class raises `PC_DONE` (the pool, whose `PC_OPERATION_ENABLE` is `0x60` with no DPU stage, and
the LUT-load program at `0x18`, included). **Neither the graph nor the per-submit floor
moves** across the change — 6.4-6.5 ms and a median of 348 us on each side of it, measured
2026-07-31, before the levers above the driver took the graph further — because the DPU's
own completion is when the wait ends either way and this changes only when it begins.

**A chained stream of slow programs on a kernel without the gate hangs the board hard.** Twice,
on the same 75-task convolution: once against the scaled deadline and once against plain 0021.
Each needed a **physical power cycle** — the board does not come back on its own, answers no
ping, and a full LAN sweep finds no host accepting SSH. That is what a job programmed into a
core that has not finished the last one costs from outside. The "before" measurement is already
recorded above and does not need repeating as a control. [HW, H96 MAX M9]

**THE DMA WRITE ERROR AND THE WEDGED IOMMU WERE THE SAME BUG.** Past the old bound the NPU
raised a DMA write error, which `rocket_job_irq_handler()` takes as `WARN_ON` (taint +512),
leaving the IOMMU reporting `Enable stall request timed out` / `FORCE_RESET command timed
out` after which every job on every path failed. That is what programming a job into a core
that is still writing the previous one does, and no length on the fixed driver has reached
it. It is not an independent client-reachable defect.

**Nothing measured after the taint moves is a hardware answer about the chain.** A sweep on
the old driver reported "the per-layer reference failed at layer 0" at three lengths in a
row, at a geometry that had passed minutes earlier — purely because the IOMMU was already
wedged. Read `/proc/sys/kernel/tainted` after every length and reboot when it changes; on a
kernel without `0022`, `sweep` therefore leaves `wide` out and stops a mix at its first
non-bit-exact length.

**The cost is the fence no longer being early, and it is not recoverable in the driver.**
MobileNetV1-224 goes **5.0 -> 6.3 ms** per inference in one fewer submit (the graph's runs
cover 26 layers in two kicks rather than 25). That 1.3 ms is not a late fence being brought
forward — it is the old fence being signalled while most of the stream had **not yet run**,
which the trace shows directly: on the 35-program kick the first `PC_DONE` lands at 12 us
and the kick ends at 1921 us. The DPU's own completion sets **10 us after the last program
starts**, so it is already the earliest safe instant and the counter gate retires on it at
the same microsecond a deadline does. Anything the host is to overlap with NPU work has to be scheduled
by the caller between submit and wait; there is no driver signal that legitimately arrives
sooner.

**`tests/rk3576_chain_len.c` is the instrument**, and every mode takes the footprint knobs
`ROCKET_CHAINLEN_C` / `_P` / `_K` — channels, plane, and the direct leg's kernel — so the
length, the mix and the footprint move independently. Its defaults are per-mode and are the
shapes the modes were characterised at: 32 channels over a 32x32 plane with a 1x1 direct leg
for `uniform`/`mixed`/`geom`, 128 over 28x28 with a 3x3 leg for `wide`. It asserts the
kicked answer against the same handles run one submit each.

### The pooling layer: a POOL RAISES NO DPU COMPLETION

At 6.2 ms the graph's PPU pooling layer was 1.24-1.48 ms, 20% of the wall, for a
`1024x7x7 -> 1024x1x1` average. It is now **0.12 ms** and the graph is **5.1 ms**,
byte-identical. Three findings, and the first is what made the other two worth building.

Its cost was legible as soon as it was split
(`ROCKET_RK3576_POOL_PROF=1`, per call, microseconds):

| bucket | us | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| the completion wait | 640 | `PREP_BO` on the output |
| the host cube scatter | 320 | row-major CHW into NC1HWC2 |
| three BO allocations | 300 | feature cube, surface, regcmd |
| the BO teardown | 150 | |
| stamp / submit ioctl / de-scatter | 8 / 13 / 13 | |

**A POOL RAISES NO DPU COMPLETION, BECAUSE IT HAS NO DPU STAGE.**
`PC_OPERATION_ENABLE` is a per-block bitmap: a convolution enables CNA/CORE/DPU/DPU_RDMA
with `0x1D`, a pool enables PPU and PPU_RDMA with `0x60`, and the two bit sets are
disjoint. `patches/rk3576/npu/0012` retires a job when the DPU raises its own completion
in `INTERRUPT_RAW_STATUS`, so for a pooling job that bit can never set and the job retires
on the `dpu_grace_us` deadline instead — paid in full, on every submit. The wait tracks
the parameter count for count, which is what says it is not retiring on a completion at
all:

| `dpu_grace_us` | 500 | 250 | 100 |
|---|---|---|---|
| the pool's wait, us | 641 | 389 | 213 |

The PPU raises its own bits two positions over (`PPU_0` at `0x400`, `PPU_1` at `0x800`,
against the DPU's `0x100`/`0x200`). `patches/rk3576/npu/0021` adds
`DRM_ROCKET_JOB_PPU_DONE`, which selects those bits in place of the DPU's, latched per
submit — and it is a per-JOB flag for the same reason `0017`'s is: which class a job is in
is invisible to the driver at `PC_DONE` time and obvious to the userspace that wrote
`PC_OPERATION_ENABLE`. **641 -> 71 us**, which is the poll floor. Interface version 1.3.

**That flag must be GATED on the kernel version, not sent blind.** The submit ioctl
REJECTS a flag word it does not recognise, so an older kernel fails the submit rather than
ignoring the bit — the opposite of `DRM_ROCKET_JOB_NO_DPU_DONE`, which a kernel that knows
the field simply ignores. `rocket_ppu_done_supported()` asks the interface version.

**A POOLING SUBMIT THAT NAMES NO CLASS PAYS THE FALLBACK WAIT ON EVERY KERNEL, AND THE
SHIPPED ONE IS NOT A FREE BASELINE.** Every library entry names its class, so no shipping
