path is exposed; a raw submit is. Measured on one binary with the flag word as the only
variable, over the two gates whose harness helpers submitted with `flags = 0`
(`rk3576_row_pitch`, `rk3576_chain_pool`), in ms:

| gate, ms | module | `flags = 0` | `PPU_DONE` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `chain_pool` / `row_pitch` | 1.5.0 shipped, `dpu_grace_us` 500 | 149 / 229 | 110 / 186 |
| | 1.6.0 candidate, wait untimed | 10300 / 4804 | 104 / 185 |
| `pool_gate` | 1.5.0 shipped | 2461 | 2452 |
| | 1.6.0 candidate | 4494 | 2452 |

Three facts fall out. The **untimed** wait is what makes an unnamed class catastrophic rather
than merely slow — 0022's 125 ms backstop times the submit count, which is why removing the
deadline cannot land until every caller names a class. The **shipped** module was already
charging these submits the full 500 us grace apiece, so the 149/229 that reads like a
baseline is itself the cost; flagged, the two modules agree to within the run-to-run spread.

And **the cost is invisible in exactly the places it is small**, which is why enumerating
the call sites beats reading the walls. `pool_gate` moves 8 ms on the shipped module — ~16
submits at 500 us, against a 2452 ms row — and 2033 ms on the candidate one, so no
shipped-module measurement could have pointed at it. The population is every caller of
`gen_pool_rk3576`: the library entry, which has always named its class, plus four raw test
submits in three files (`rk3576_row_pitch.c`, `rk3576_chain_pool.c`, and
`rk3576_pool_probe.c`'s `run_pool` and `dst_arm`). With all four named, a full-list backstop
count is module-independent (28 shipped against 29 candidate) where it differed by ~10
before. **The wide-output class (`NO_DPU_DONE`) is a separate population and was not
enumerated here.**
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-05]

**THE PPU HONOURS A SOURCE SURFACE STRIDE THAT IS NOT A MULTIPLE OF FOUR**, and that is
what makes a cube-in pool available at all. Every vendor pooling program carries
`round4(iw*ih)` in `PPU_RDMA`'s source-surface register, while a direct convolution's
output surface stride is `ow*oh` exactly — at a 7x7 plane those are 52 and 49, so the join
is either a register the part takes verbatim or it is nothing. It takes it: bit-exact at
49, 25 and 9 elements per channel group (`rk3576_pool_probe lib`, both strides,
`ROCKET_RK3576_POOL_PACK_SRC=1` forcing the packed one). **A wrong stride there computes a
full and plausible surface rather than faulting**, so this is gated rather than assumed —
the join was written before the question was asked.

**The pool is resident.** `rocket_pool_int8_pack_rk3576()` / `_prepacked_rk3576()` /
`_free_rk3576()` hold the three BOs and the register program across calls, and
`rocket_pool_int8_cube_in_rk3576()` points the handle at a producer's output surface so
neither the scatter nor its cache maintenance runs. The transient
`rocket_pool_int8_rk3576()` IS pack-run-free, so a prepacked call is bit-identical to a
transient one by construction rather than by two implementations agreeing.

What is left of the layer at 0.12 ms is the completion wait, 71 us of it.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-07-31]

**THE POOL'S WRITE GUARD IS WHOLE-SURFACE, SO A PARTIAL WRITE WOULD PASS IT — AND THE RATE
IS UNDER 0.025% OF CELLS.** The guard asks whether ANY byte of the output differs from the
sentinel, where the convolution path asks it of each row task's own extent. One observation
said that might matter: `rk3576_pool_probe bound` reported **1 wrong cell of 40 once inside a
full gate run** — 128 elements at `c=16` on a 16x16 plane, exactly one output row across all
sixteen channels, which is the shape a dropped block of atoms has.

Measured as a RATE, it does not reproduce. `bound` run **150 times quiet and 150 times under
four streaming-memcpy threads** — 6000 cells an arm, max pooling k3 s2 with a leading pad over
planes 16-112 and channels 16-128 — is **0 wrong cells in 12000**. By the rule of three that
bounds the rate at **0.025% of cells (95%)**, which is two orders below the cost of a per-row
check. The load arm has its POSITIVE CONTROL from the same session and the same load: the
int32 wide writer drops atoms on **30.8% of its row tasks** there, so the memory system was
under the pressure the hazard tracks and the pool did not follow it. That is consistent with
every other measurement of the drop — it has only ever been seen on a writer whose output
element is wider than one byte, and the conv entries and the narrow int8 matmul were clean at
this same load.

**THE TRIGGER IS THE CONTEXT, AND IT IS A PRECEDING UNIT-KERNEL POOL.** The event reproduced
in the very next full gate run — and then on demand. Three arms of 30 runs each, differing
only in what runs BEFORE each `bound` sweep:

| what precedes each sweep | runs with a wrong cell |
|---|---:|
| `rk3576_pool_probe pad` (pooling with a UNIT kernel on one axis) | **2 of 30** |
| a wide-output int32 matmul (the poisoning source) | 0 of 30 |
| nothing (the control) | 0 of 30, plus the 300 above |

Both failures are byte-identical to each other and to what the gate run showed: the FIRST TWO
cells of the sweep, at `c=16`, **128 wrong elements on the 16x16 plane — exactly one output
row across all sixteen channels — and 2302 of 2304 on the 24x24 one**. That 128-element
signature is also exactly what the original single observation was. So this is not a partial
write slipping past a whole-surface check and it is not the atom drop: **a pooling program
with a unit kernel leaves the part in a state where the next process's first pooling submits
compute wrong**, and it clears itself after two.

It also joins the other open pooling thread to this one. `rk3576_pool_probe pad`'s own
unit-kernel cells are unstable, which is why that mode is not an asserting gate; the same
unit-kernel program is what poisons the next process here. Treat them as one question.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-02]

**A POOL IS A CUBE PRODUCER TOO** — `rocket_pool_int8_cube_out_rk3576()`, `_cube_of_` and
`_cube_out_at_`, which is what lets a pool sit between two convolutions with no host
transpose on either side, or write its output into a slice of a residual add's buffer beside
another producer's. The PPU's own output surface stride is `round4(ow*oh)` rather than the
plane; that is no bound on the consumer, since the CNA's DDR channel-group jump is a register
the emitter fills and the part honours it at any value at or above the plane, so the cube
carries it. **The DESTINATION stride is refused rather than worked around**: the pooling
program derives it from the output plane and no register to move it is decoded, so a slice at
any other channel-group stride is rejected — a plane whose element count is already a
multiple of four needs nothing. Both entries also refuse a channel count that is not a
multiple of 16, because pooling reduces WITHIN a channel and a partial group's tail is then
whatever the input cube's padding held rather than a value the handle can declare.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate`, 2026-08-02]

### Finding the runs is the library's, not the caller's

`rocket_chain_new_rk3576()` takes ONE run of already-linked handles and asserts it, which is
right for the primitive and wrong for a frontend: a frontend has a graph and no reason to
know about runs. `rocket_chain_plan_rk3576()` groups an array of nodes into maximal runs and
splits them at the program-count bound — pure, no hardware, nothing written on the handles.
An UNSET node breaks a run, which is how a caller says "this layer is not a resident
convolution or pool" or "a run may not START here" (a chain scatters the tensor it is handed,
so a layer whose input the caller prepares on the host must not begin one). The
convolution-only `rocket_conv2d_int8_chain_plan_rk3576()` / `_chain_new_rk3576()` are the
same functions with every node a convolution — one implementation, because two would be a
second set of rules to keep in agreement.

**THE RUN'S RULE IS WEAKER THAN THE PAIR'S, AND THE PART IS WHAT SETS IT.** The finder used
to grow a run while `cube_linked(h[i], h[i+1])` held — layer j reads layer j-1's surface —
which is a sufficient condition and not the necessary one. A chained stream honours
read-after-write between its programs, so what a stream needs is that **every layer but the
last leaves a cube and every layer but the first reads a cube some EARLIER member wrote**,
not necessarily the one immediately before it. Sound because the chain emits its programs in
array order, so "an earlier layer" is "an earlier program"; and it is a strict superset of
the pairwise test, so nothing that chained before stops chaining.

A ResNet block that changes width is exactly the difference: its 1x1 shortcut convolution
reads the block input three layers back and writes a slice of the add's buffer, so a pairwise
rule strands each of the three between two kicks even though no host work happens on either
side of it. Widening the rule takes **ResNet-18 from four runs covering 24 layers to ONE of
27 covering 29 programs, 11 submits -> 5 and 10.5 -> 9.9 ms**, byte-identical, and moves
neither MobileNet, which was already one run.

Requiring an IN-RUN producer rather than merely a stable buffer is the conservative half: an
operand no member of the stream writes is a BO the job never names, and the layout's
BO dedup rests on every intermediate surface being some member's output.

### A POOLING layer sits inside the stream

A classifier puts a pooling layer in the middle of a run, and a chain of convolution handles
had to break at one — which on ResNet-18 isolated the stem, the maxpool after it, the final
average pool and the classifier. The reason to doubt that a pool could sit in the stream is
`PC_OPERATION_ENABLE`, a per-block BITMAP rather than a constant: a convolution enables
CNA/CORE/DPU with `0x1D` and a pool enables PPU/PPU_RDMA with `0x60`, and the two sets are
disjoint.

**It does not partition a stream.** `conv -> pool -> conv` as one batched job, against the
same three as three submits and against each consumer alone on an input nothing wrote:
**20 of 20 iterations over five geometries** — planes 16 to 110, channels 32 and 64 — gave
the same pool output AND the same output from the convolution after it. Both intermediates
are read, so a failure would have named the boundary it happened at, and the sentinel is what
separates "never ran" from "ran on stale input" (a fresh BO's zeros could not).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_chain_pool.c`, 2026-08-02]

So a run is described by NODES — `rocket_chain_node_rk3576`, exactly one of a convolution
handle and a pooling handle — rather than by convolution handles. A pool may only be an
**INTERIOR** node: one that began a run would need the chain to scatter into its cube and one
that ended a run would need its output de-scattered, and both are the host work the stream
exists to remove, so the finder trims a trailing pool off a run and never opens one on a pool.
Two bookkeeping consequences: a pool names only its surface in the job's BO lists (it has no
weight cube and no coefficient group), and its surface is **never re-stamped inside the verify
bracket** — the pooling handle keeps no "already stamped" flag for the chain to clear, so its
sentinel goes on at the start of the next call, one extra bracket for at most one layer.

**All three networks then go out as ONE hardware kick in ONE submit** — ResNet-18
9.9 -> 9.2 ms and 5 submits -> 1, MobileNetV1 5.1 -> 4.9 and 3 -> 1, MobileNetV2 6.8 -> 6.7
and 3 -> 1 — byte-identical on all three, same task counts, TFLite's own top-1.

A marginal submit prices differently here than the earlier kick A/B measured: **0.13 ms on
ResNet-18** (10 submits removed for 1.3 ms) and **0.05-0.10 ms on the MobileNets** (2 removed
for 0.1-0.2), against the 0.145-0.25 measured when a graph still had tens of them. What a
submit is worth is a function of what else is in flight, so re-measure it rather than scaling
the old figure.

Applying the program bound in the finder replaces a build-and-shorten loop in the caller,
which is the better place for it: a layer is one or several programs, so the longest legal
run is not a function of the layer count, and a run too long is now SPLIT rather than
refused. `rocket_conv2d_int8_programs_rk3576()` and
`rocket_conv2d_int8_chain_max_programs_rk3576()` are the two queries it rests on.

**No graph-execution object goes inside the library.** The frontends already own
scheduling; the one thing they cannot own is which streams the part will run.

### A narrow input-channel count runs on the DIRECT datapath

The int8 direct cube is a **32-channel MAC group at every count** — `pad_ic(3)` and
`pad_ic(32)` are both 32 — so `ic` 3 and `ic` 32 are the same register program, the same
weight-cube size and the same feature-cube size, and the channels past `ic` are the cube's own
padding. Routing every `ic <= 4` to the CNA's packed-image sub-encoding was therefore hiding a
path that needs no host widening and carries none of that encoding's three geometry bounds
(a non-zero left pad, an output width of exactly `iw/stride`, and that width also a multiple
of 16).

`rocket_conv2d_desc.direct_datapath` asks for it. The int8 entries honour it; the RK3576 fp16
entry refuses it, because the float cube groups sixteen input channels rather than
thirty-two and no `ic` below five has been run through it; the RK3588 generator satisfies it
by construction, having only one convolution encoding. 24 shapes bit-exact including a TFLite
224 stem at its own asymmetric extent, and on the graph the stem goes **2.21-2.24 ms to
1.97-1.99**, byte-identical over all 401408 elements against both the widened lowering and the
packed-image one.

**The saving is the host BUFFER, not the scatter**, which corrects the estimate that motivated
it. The NEON packer stores whole 16-byte atoms whatever the live channel count is, so the
scatter writes the same 802 KB either way and only its READS shrink (150 KB against 401 KB);
what actually goes away is the widened image's own allocation, fill and copy. The stem's
feature bucket moves 0.71-0.91 ms to 0.60, and the rest of its wall is the submit.

**AND IT FOUND A DEFECT THAT PRE-DATES IT.** The CNA's border substitution covers the whole
**programmed** channel group, so at a pad tap the cube's padding channels read the border
constant while at an interior tap they read whatever was memset into them. The `B` term sums
over every tap, so the disagreement lands in the accumulator scaled by that output position's
pad-tap count — which varies per position and cannot be folded into `A` at all. Every `ic`
from 1 to 31 with a non-zero input zero point AND a non-zero weight zero point computed a
wrong BORDER with an exact interior; `ic` 8, 16 and 24 reach it with no flag involved. No gate
had seen it because every existing shape has `ic` a multiple of 32 or `w_zp` zero.

The fix is two halves and both are required — either alone is wrong everywhere instead of only
on the border. The cube's padding channels hold the **border constant itself**, the input zero
point (the allocation's fill, and the dead lanes of a partial C2 atom, which the NEON packer
was zeroing), and the zero-point fold counts the **programmed** taps: `A = bias -
in_zp*sum_w + in_zp*w_zp*icreg*kh*kw`. It is inert wherever the old form was already right —
at `ic` a multiple of 32 the two counts are equal, and at `w_zp == 0` the term is zero.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_conv_lib_gate.c` `nic`, 2026-07-30]

### An asymmetric pad is an OUTPUT EXTENT, not a materialised border

**The CNA has no configured trailing pad.** It takes the pad its LAST WINDOW CONSUMES,
derived from the output extent and the leading pad — `r76_trail_pad()` in the emitter is
that derivation, and `CNA_PAD_CON0`'s bottom and right fields carry the result. So TFLite's
SAME at an even input and stride two, `pad_before = 0` and `pad_after = 1`, is a leading pad
of **zero** against an output extent one larger than the symmetric formula gives. Nothing
has to be copied.

That geometry was already inside the int8 correctness envelope: six shapes of
`tests/rk3576_conv_shapes.h` carry a zero leading pad against a consumed trailing one,
direct and depthwise, and all are bit-exact. What kept it out of a graph was the
DESCRIPTOR, which could only say "derive the output from a symmetric pad" — so a graph
runner materialised the border into a row-major buffer, and that buffer is what broke the
cube chain at every stride-2 depthwise layer.

`rocket_conv2d_desc.oh/.ow` now name the extent, zero meaning "whatever
`rocket_conv2d_oh/ow` derive" — every caller that memsets the struct, so nothing existing
changes — and `rocket_conv2d_trail_y/_x` are the pad it implies. **The bound is the
KERNEL**: a trailing pad of `kh` or more is an output row whose whole window is pad, and it
is refused. The fp16 RK3576 path, both packed-image paths and `rocket_conv2d_plan` (the
RK3588 fp16 generator) refuse an extent they did not derive themselves, because that
geometry is claimed at int8 and measured nowhere else.

On MobileNetV1-224 five layers pad asymmetrically — the stem and the four stride-2
depthwise — the derived trailing pad matches the model on 30 of 30 compute layers, and every
layer stays bit-exact against a CPU model of the part's own arithmetic.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-07-29]

**TRAP: a per-layer reference has to be told the same lowering.** The gate's per-axis pass
builds its own descriptor; left without the extent, its entry computed 55 rows while its
reference computed 56, and the five asymmetric layers reported maxdiff 255 at 5-29% exact —
a lowering disagreement that reads exactly like a hardware fault.

**A depthwise weight zero point rides in the WEIGHT CUBE**, which is what let the graph's
thirteen depthwise layers run at all: every per-tensor TFLite depthwise filter carries
one, and the 48-byte depthwise coefficient group has no `B` field to fold it into. It does
not need one. The int8 depthwise cube gives every (channel, tap) **two live bytes and the
datapath adds them**, so the effective weight is a nine-bit value and `w - w_zp` is
carried exactly across the pair, bounded only by the pair's own [-256, 254]. The
coefficient `A` already computes `-in_zp*(sum_w - w_zp*taps)`, which is the fold a
pre-centred cube wants, so nothing downstream changes. Re-centring in int8 instead is NOT
available — 12 of this model's 13 depthwise layers overflow — and re-quantizing to a
symmetric scale would have been an accuracy loss of up to 1.65x coarsening rather than an
exact answer. Bit-exact at both signs, at both channel-group boundaries, at k=5 and at
stride 2. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_conv_lib_gate.c` group `zp`]

**Per-axis weight quantization reaches the depthwise path, and depthwise is the cheaper
half of it.** The 48-byte depthwise coefficient group carries the same per-output-channel
int16 `C` the 64-byte one does; the planner was wired to the direct path's group and
nothing else stood in the way. What makes depthwise the easy case is the accumulator
bound: `128*sum|w|` over `kh*kw` taps rather than `ic*kh*kw`, one to two orders of
magnitude smaller, so the `C` ramp reaches the int16 field's own 32767 ceiling and the
gain resolution a single `OUT_CVT` shift leaves is finer than the direct path's at the
same channel count. It is also ONE task whatever the channel count — output channel `c`
is bound to input channel `c`, so there is no output-channel window to program and no
scale sort to make, and the accuracy comes without the submits the direct path spends on
it. Measured over eight shapes (kernels 1/3/5, both strides, 32-256 channels): the worst
distance to an exact per-axis requant is **0.5 counts at spreads to 100x and 0.8 at
1000x**, against 0.5-1.9 on the direct path. A MobileNet is mostly depthwise, which is
what a per-axis model needs from this path.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_perchannel_gate.c`]

**AND IT RUNS AS A GRAPH, WHERE THE ERROR DOES NOT COMPOUND.** A per-axis requant's error
is a per-layer quantity, so what one layer gives up to the single `OUT_CVT` shift is not
what twenty-nine of them give up, and only a network answers that. `rk3576_net_gate
peraxis` runs MobileNetV1-224 with every convolution and depthwise convolution on the
per-axis entry, chained.

The per-axis model comes from re-quantizing this model's OWN weights per output channel —
what a TFLite int8 converter does to the same float tensor: each channel moves from the
shared grid onto `max|w_c - w_zp| * w_scale / 127`, with its weights and bias rescaled and
the activations keeping the model's per-tensor scales and zero points. The spread that
produces is real and wide: **150x at the stem, 145x and 129x at the early depthwise
layers, 105x at layer 25**.

Every one of the 29 compute layers is within **ONE COUNT** of an exact per-axis requant —
87.7% to 99.9% of elements identical to it — and the chained network returns TFLite's own
top-1 AND its whole top-5. The per-op gate's worst-case reading of 26.6 counts stands
where it was measured (a synthetic 100x spread at ic=128 with the whole layer in one
tile); a real model's spread does not land there, because the planner sizes `C` against
the ACTUAL accumulator bound and this model's wide-scale channels are also its
small-accumulator ones. The output-channel tile lever is **never taken**: the predicted
worst-case gain error is 0.003%-0.82% per layer, under the 1% target, so the planner keeps
each layer in one task and pays no extra submit.

What that pass asserts is what a graph is for — no layer refuses, no layer is far enough
from an exact per-axis requant to be a defect rather than the single shift, and the
compounded chain still returns the framework's label. The bit-exact assertion against the
chip's own arithmetic stays in `rk3576_perchannel_gate`, where the planner's `C`
multipliers and `OUT_CVT` shift are visible to a model; they are not visible to a graph.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_net_gate.c peraxis`]

**WHAT NEITHER OF THOSE TWO GATES CAN SEE IS THE CHANNEL ORDER, and both miss it the same
way.** Every cell of the per-op gate and every layer of that per-axis pass runs a
MATERIALISING call, and the de-scatter walks the sort permutation — so a surface written in
scale-sorted channel order is read back in model order and scores exact. The order is only
observable where a surface is consumed as a cube, which no per-axis shape here ever was: the
corpus's per-axis coverage is a synthetic re-quantization of one graph run one layer at a
time. It took a real per-axis model through a frontend to reach it, and the symptom was a
whole network's label rather than any layer's numbers. **A gate that materialises every
intermediate cannot see a layout defect**, and the same is true of any accuracy pass whose
reference is fed the same tensor the part was.

## The residual add: not the elementwise stage, the convolution datapath

A skip connection is the one primitive between this part's op library and a second
network. MobileNetV2 puts one on ten of its seventeen inverted residuals and every ResNet
block ends in one; MobileNetV1, the graph gated here, is a straight chain and has none.

**The DPU's elementwise stage is not where it lands.** That stage computes an affine
requantization of exactly ONE cube, and the search for a second operand is now exhaustive
rather than a lead not yet chased — six sweeps over the whole register interface, set out
in [rk3576-regcmd.md](rk3576-regcmd.md). The two that close it are the ones that cross
axes: every register the program leaves at zero **crossed with the full 32-rung output
gain ladder**, because a placement sweep at one gain and a gain sweep at one placement are
each blind to an operand that both sits somewhere unexpected and enters the accumulator
unscaled; and a delta probe that walks a single non-zero atom over an operand buffer eight
cubes long, which says the program reads **exactly the addressed cube** and nothing within
two cubes either side of it.

**So the add is lowered onto a convolution.** Concatenate the two operands along the
channel axis and convolve with a 1x1 kernel of two diagonal blocks — `W[o][o] = w1`,
`W[o][C+o] = w2`, zero elsewhere:

```
out[o] = requant( w1 * (a[o] - a_zp) + w2 * (b[o] - b_zp) )
```

Bit-exact against a CPU model of the part's own arithmetic over nine MobileNetV2 and
ResNet-18 residual shapes — channels 24 to 512, planes 7x7 to 56x56 — and within **one
count** of an exact float residual add at every one, at unequal operand scales and unequal
zero points both (`tests/rk3576_residual_add.c`, 12 of 12).

Two of its properties are better than the vendor's own elementwise program, not merely
equal to it:

- **The operands may carry different scales.** The ratio rides in the weights as `w2/w1`,
  so the pair only has to be representable as two int8s — about one part in 127. The
  vendor's program has one operand converter, and its compiler works around that by
  quantizing both operands to a COMMON scale: two graph inputs calibrated 64x apart still
  compile to a single converter, with the OUT_CVT gain coming out as the ratio of one
  shared input scale to the output's.
- **The two zero points ride in the bias, exactly.** The datapath has one input zero
  point, but `w2 * (a_zp - b_zp)` is a per-output-channel constant, which is what the bias
  is. Nothing is approximated.

**And the DATAPATH fuses it, measured rather than argued** — though a real block's
quantization then refuses the weight it needs, which is the paragraph after this one. The
concatenation is along channels; a
feature cube's channel-group stride is the plane and a direct conv's output surface stride
is `ow*oh`, so two convolutions writing adjacent halves of one buffer already ARE the
concatenated operand, byte for byte, with no host work — the same identity the cube chain
rests on. So a block's last convolution absorbs its own skip by taking `C` more input
channels and an identity block at the **centre tap** of its kernel, and the add costs **no
program at all** against the one program the vendor pays. It is the hardware's own idiom:
the vendor compiler folds `Add(Conv(x), x)` into exactly this shape, which is why that
graph is useless as a capture of an add. Bit-exact at MobileNetV2's project convolution
for every residual width (1x1, `ic` 168 to 1120) and at ResNet-18's second convolution
(3x3, `ic` 128 to 512).

**What stops the fusion is the IDENTITY WEIGHT, and the weight-slice rule never binds
first.** The slice rule `ic*kh*kw <= 4608` is real — a 3x3 reaches it exactly at `C = 256`
(`512*9 = 4608`) and is refused at `C = 512` — but a real block is refused long before it,
by its own quantization.

A standalone add gets to CHOOSE its weight scale, because its weights are two diagonal
blocks rather than a quantized tensor: `w_scale = 1/w1` is a free parameter, which is how
the operand ratio fits in a pair of int8s. A FUSED one inherits the convolution's, and the
convolution's is fixed by its own filter. So the skip has to reach the output through the
accumulator unit the main term already uses:

    w_skip = skip_scale / (in_scale * w_scale) = (skip_scale / out_scale) / gain

with `gain` the convolution's own requant gain. A residual's two operands have comparable
ranges, so `skip_scale/out_scale` is order one (0.64 to 0.98 on this model) and the fusion
needs `gain > ~1/127` — a SHALLOW contraction. A project convolution over 576 to 960
channels runs at 1/242 to 1/893, and **all ten of MobileNetV2's residual blocks want an
identity weight of 173 to 865. Not one fits int8.** Nothing else in the program can move it:
the coefficient group's `C` multiplies the whole accumulator, main term and skip together,
so a per-channel gain cannot separate them, and a per-axis quantization makes it strictly
worse — the per-tensor `w_scale` is the largest of the per-channel ones.

`rocket_residual_fuse_weight_rk3576()` is that arithmetic and that refusal, pure; where the
weight does fit it also carries the bias correction `w_skip*(in_zp - skip_zp)`, and a block
inside the envelope lands **one count** from an exact float main+skip on the part. So the
standalone add is the form a real network uses, and the same slice rule caps it at
`C = 2304`, well past any real residual width. The standalone weight cube is `2C*C` bytes,
512 KiB at ResNet-18's widest stage, a resident-weights cost paid once rather than per
inference; measured transiently (no resident handle, so mostly host packing) a standalone
add is 0.6-3.3 ms across the shape table.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_residual_add.c`, 2026-07-31]

## A second network: MobileNetV2, and what a skip costs a chain

MobileNetV2-224 at int8 runs end to end on this part. **65 layers — 36 convolutions, 17
depthwise convolutions, 10 residual adds, a PPU pooling layer and a classifier — and every
one of the 64 compute layers is bit-exact against a CPU model of the part's own
arithmetic**, returning TFLite's own top-1. It is the same gate and the same blob format as
MobileNetV1: `rk3576_net_gate --net v2`, built by the same `mknet.py`.

Nothing in the datapath had to change for it. What the add needed was the lowering above,
and what the graph needed was two pieces of bookkeeping — a layer that names the LAYERS its
operands come from rather than assuming "the one before", and a buffer that outlives the
ping-pong for the ten tensors a skip reads three to five layers later.

**Against TFLite the graph differs on 0.0573% of elements, every one of them by a single
count** — 3951 of 6896777, a lower rate than MobileNetV1's 0.095% over a graph twice as
deep. **The ten adds are inside that one count**, which is the non-obvious part: the add is
not TFLite's arithmetic at all. TFLite scales both operands onto a common intermediate; this
lowering carries their scale ratio in a pair of int8 weights and requantizes once. The ratio
is what could cost accuracy, and the answer is to search **every** denominator rather than
anchor the larger term at 127: on these ten skips the best rational approximation under 128
is 1e-5 to 5e-4 relative where the anchored choice is 1e-3 to 4e-3, and at 1e-4 the second
operand's full int8 range moves an output by a tenth of a count. That is why no add exceeds
the single count a requant difference already owes.

Fed its own previous output the disagreements compound as they do on V1, and the top-1
survives. The tail of the top-5 reorders, and on this image that is not a disagreement worth
reading: **TFLite's own second through fifth classes all sit at probability 2/255**, so their
order is a tie-break between equal values.

### The residual topology, not the channel alignment, is what fragments a chain

The interesting measurement is the cube chain, because a residual network is where the join
rules first have something to refuse. MobileNetV1 links **25 of its 28 adjacent pairs** into
2 runs covering 26 layers. With every producer owning its own output buffer MobileNetV2
links **32 of 63** into **16 runs of two or three layers**, and the gate counts the refusals
by reason rather than reporting the total:

| refused | why |
|---:|---|
| 10 | the consumer is an ADD, whose input is a host-built concatenation |
| 10 | the producer feeds a SKIP and so must leave a row-major tensor |
| 6 | the consumer's input channel count is not a multiple of 32 |
| 4 | the producer's output surface is not a feature cube (padded stride, or tiled) |
| 1 | the consumer is not a resident convolution |

**Twenty of the thirty-one are the skip topology and six are the alignment**, which is the
opposite of what the channel counts suggest. MobileNetV2's widths 16, 24 and 144 are the
ones that are not multiples of 32 and they do cost joins — but three times as many are lost
because an add reads a concatenation the host builds, and because the tensor that add reads
has to still exist in row-major form when it gets there. A part with no alignment rule at
all would still fragment this graph into runs of three.

**Both of those twenty want one thing, and it is an address**: a producer writing its output
surface at a channel-group OFFSET inside a larger buffer. That is the section below, and
with it, the padding-channel rule and the producer's own group stride the same graph links
**62 of 63** into **one run covering 62 layers**.

### A cube's base is a plain address, so a concatenation costs nothing

A convolution's feature base and its output base are both plain addresses on this part. A
program whose base moves by G channel groups inside a bigger buffer computes exactly what it
computes at offset 0, writes only its own slice, leaves every byte outside it untouched, and
composes with the row-task offset already added to the same register — measured over 34
placements at three geometries, one of them row-split, with an unaligned group index
(`tests/rk3576_offset_cube.c`).

So the two twenty-refusal buckets are not a hardware bound at all. Allocate **one buffer per
add**, give operand A the low slice and operand B the high one, point the two producers at
them with `rocket_conv2d_int8_cube_out_at_rk3576()`, and the add reads the whole buffer as
its feature cube. No concatenation is built, and the skip source no longer has to
materialise a row-major tensor for it — its output IS the second operand, in place, and the
layer after it reads the same slice back as its own cube.

What changes in the library is OWNERSHIP, not encoding: a handle can be told to write into a
caller's buffer instead of allocating its own. `rocket_rk3576_cube_alloc()` /
`_cube_slice()` are that buffer and its views, and `rocket_rk3576_cube` gained a byte offset
that `_cube_in_` and the pooling entry honour on the read side — the PPU is a separate
program with its own DMA, and a pool over a producer's high slice is bit-identical to the
same pool over that producer's row-major output.

Four things it needs, three of which are bookkeeping and one a real bound:

- **A slice starts every SIXTEEN channels.** Sixteen channels share every atom, so channel
  24 is not an address. A concatenation of two 24-channel operands therefore puts operand B
  at channel 32, with the gap carrying zero weights — which contributes nothing at any
  content, so the same weight matrix, descriptor and CPU reference serve both layouts.
- **The wiring is a PAIR, refused as one.** Operand B's producer is also the layer before
  the next block's expand convolution, so placing it means that layer has to read the same
  slice as its cube. A producer that leaves no row-major tensor for a consumer that cannot
  take a cube would leave the graph with nothing at all.
- **A BO is named once per job.** The driver locks each through `drm_exec` and locking one
  twice is `EALREADY`, which rejects the submit — and a layer reading one slice of a
  concatenation buffer while writing another is exactly one BO in both roles. It is named as
  the output, and a chained stream deduplicates its whole BO list the same way.
- **A shared surface is never re-stamped after a kick.** The sentinel normally goes back on
  a surface at the end of the call that read it; a slice is read later in the same inference
  by a program outside the run that wrote it, so its stamp goes on at the START of the next
  call instead. Stamping it in place would replace the layer's output with 0xA5, invisibly.

**A PLACED PRODUCER'S SLICE IS ASKED FOR ITS OWN CUBE, not for the slice the link pass
built.** The two name the same bytes, but only the producer can say what its tail holds, and
the consumer that reads operand B's slice back may be one whose channel count is not a
multiple of 32. Taking the slice from `rocket_conv2d_int8_cube_of_rk3576()` after placing
the producer is what makes that statement travel, and it is the difference between nine of
the ten adds wiring up and **all ten**.

**And the alignment refusal is the WEIGHT ZERO POINT'S, not the channel count's.** A handle
whose `ic` is not a multiple of 32 fills the channels past `ic` with its border constant,
where a producer's surface carries whatever the DPU wrote into its own padded output
channels — but those channels' weights are the zeros the cube was memset with, so their MAC
contribution is zero whatever they hold. What is not zero at a non-zero weight zero point is
the coefficient group's B term: the DPU adds `B*sum(x)` over every tap of the whole
programmed group, padding channels included. So a symmetric weight quantization — every
TFLite int8 filter, and every per-axis one by construction — joins at any channel count. And
so does an ASYMMETRIC one from a DIRECT producer, because that producer's own partial output
group lands on its output zero point, which is the constant the consumer's fold assumes; see
"What a producer leaves past its live output channels" above; and so does one from a
DEPTHWISE producer, once its surface carries the deeper tail the host fills at pack time.
**No channel count refuses a join on this part**, and neither does a producer's padded
group stride. What remains, on 63 adjacent pairs:

| refused | why |
|---:|---|
| 0 | every adjacent pair joins |

### What it costs

Measured at `bench 100`, warm, one hardware kick per run of cube-linked layers:

| | ms/inference | submits |
|---|---:|---:|
| transient weights | 112.9 | 64 |
| resident weights | 26.1 | 64 |
| + tensors kept in cube layout | 18.2-18.5 | 64 |
| + cross-layer kick | 15.7-16.3 | 40 |
| + concatenation buffers | 13.8 | 37 |
| + the padding-channel join | 10.0-10.4 | 9 |
| + one bracket for the sentinel | 8.8-8.9 | 9 |
| + the depthwise producer's tail | 7.5-7.7 | 7 |
| + the depthwise producer's group stride | 6.9-7.1 | 3 |
| + a pooling layer inside the stream | 6.7 | 1 |

Byte-identical at every rung: the same logits, the same top-5, the same 77 tasks. What the
concatenation buffers, the two padding-channel rungs and the group stride buy is not the
memcpys they remove — it is that an add, an unaligned channel count and a 7x7 depthwise all
stop being walls between two chained runs, and the submit count falls with the walls. At the
last rung the whole graph is a SINGLE run: the pool rides in the kick and the classifier
behind it, which is worth 0.1 ms here — the submit count is what moves, not the wall, because
by this point a marginal submit prices about 0.05-0.10 ms on this graph. The same ladder on
MobileNetV1 ends at **4.9-5.1 ms in 1 submit**; V1 does not move under the concatenation or
padding-channel levers at all, because its widths are all multiples of 32 and it has no
skips, but the sentinel bracket reaches it (6.4 -> 5.4) and so does the group stride, which
closes its two 7x7 depthwise joins (5.4 -> 5.1, 5 submits -> 3). Residency is still the
largest single lever (4.3x), and no single layer dominates.

Per-output-channel weight scales run here too, with the same result as V1: every one of the
53 weighted layers lands within **one count** of an exact per-axis requant, at real scale
spreads to 200x, and the output-channel tile lever is never taken.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_net_gate.c`, 2026-07-31]

## A third network: ResNet-18, and the three facts it found

ResNet-18-224 at int8 runs on this part. **32 layers — 21 convolutions, 8 residual adds, a
max pool, an average pool and a classifier** — and it is a different question from the two
MobileNets rather than a bigger instance of the same one: it is **3x3-heavy** where both of
those are 1x1-heavy, it has a **7x7 stem**, a **max pool**, a **fused ReLU on every add**,
and **explicit torchvision padding**, which puts a LEAD pad of 3 against a trailing 2 on the
stem — an asymmetry in the direction TFLite's SAME never produces.

**No such model is published.** MobileNetV1 and V2 arrive from google-coral/test_data
already quantized per tensor; every quantized ResNet-18 in circulation is per-axis or float.
`tests/data/rk3576-net/mkresnet18.py` therefore assembles one: the timm `resnet18.a1_in1k`
safetensors checkpoint (torchvision's parameter layout under torchvision's names, read with
a 30-line parser rather than a framework), batch norm folded into each convolution, the
ImageNet normalization folded into conv1 so the network's input is the raw pixel and the
quantized input tensor is the identity on bytes, and TFLite post-training quantization with
per-channel disabled. It asserts both of those properties, because a quantizer flag that
silently stopped working would produce a model the gate reads WRONG rather than one it
refuses. The tail is `AveragePooling2D 7x7 -> a 1x1 CONV_2D classifier -> softmax`, which is
MobileNetV1's own tail and keeps `FULLY_CONNECTED` and `MEAN` out of the op set.

The one deviation from torchvision: conv1's explicit 3-pixel border is padded in the RAW
pixel domain, so it is black where torchvision's is the channel mean. It is a 3-pixel frame
on a 224-pixel plane and everything is scored against this model's own TFLite output.

**Against TFLite the graph differs on 0.0386% of elements, all by a single count** — 1327 of
3438568, the lowest rate of the three networks. Every add, every 3x3 convolution, both pools
and the classifier are bit-exact against a CPU model of the part's own arithmetic, and the
chained graph returns TFLite's own top-1.

| | ms/inference | submits |
|---|---:|---:|
| transient weights | 144.9 | 31 |
| resident weights | 35.4 | 31 |
| + tensors kept in cube layout | 32.8 | 31 |
| + cross-layer kick | 18.3 | 25 |
| + every refusal closed | **10.5** | 11 |

Byte-identical at every rung: the same logits, the same top-5, the same 38 tasks. The chain
fragmented far more than either MobileNet's at first — 18 joins of 30 adjacent pairs in 5
runs covering 11 layers, against V2's 62 of 63 — and **none of the three refusal classes
turned out to be a property of the part**; see below. Nothing about the channel counts was
ever involved: every width here is a multiple of 64.

**WHAT EACH LEVER IS WORTH HERE, AND WHY A COUNT IS THE WRONG CURRENCY.** Four flags move
independently, so the rungs above decompose (`bench 100`, 2 repeats, spread under 0.3 ms):

| arm | joins | submits | ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| no cube layout | 0 | 31 | 34.5-34.6 |
| + adjacent-pair joins | 11 | 31 | 22.8-23.1 |
| + cross-layer kick | 11 | 26 | 20.7-20.8 |
| + concatenation-wired adds | 24 + 8 far | 17 | 13.7-13.9 |
| + shared surfaces | 27 + 11 far | 11 | **10.6** |
| + the run rule, not the pair rule | 27 + 11 far | 5 | 9.9 |
| + a pooling layer inside the stream | 27 + 11 far | **1** | **9.2** |

So **eleven adjacent-pair joins are worth about 11.6 ms at a CONSTANT submit count** — the
whole of it host transposes and the buffer machinery around them, none of it dispatch —
against 2.2 ms for the kick's five submits there. **The transposes are the larger term by
about 5x, and a cap taken over submits alone misses that**: a submit prices 0.15-0.45 ms
depending on which A/B is asked, a join 0.10-6.80 ms depending on the bytes it carries.

The last two rungs are dispatch alone — no join changes, no task changes, the same 38
programs — and they take the graph from 11 submits to 1 for **1.4 ms, about 0.13 ms per
submit**. That is at the bottom of the earlier 0.145-0.25 band and the MobileNets price it
lower still (0.05-0.10), which is the general point: **what a marginal submit is worth falls
as a graph runs out of them**, so re-measure it rather than scaling a figure taken when the
graph had thirty.

**FIX THE ITERATION COUNT ACROSS THE ARMS, and 10 is not enough.** The row-major arm reads
**43.3-45.1 ms at `bench 10` and 34.5-34.6 at `bench 100`**; every other arm in the table is
identical at both. It is the arm that does the most host allocation — a `calloc`/`free` of a
concatenation buffer per add per inference on top of the ping-pong — and the first few
inferences pay something the rest do not [glibc's dynamic mmap threshold rising once those
blocks are freed would explain it — hypothesis, not measured]. Read across arms at different
counts and this one lever reads 1.8x too large, which is exactly what happened to the first
version of this table.

**The refusals are therefore counted in BYTES as well as in reasons**, since what a join
removes is two transposes over the producer's whole output surface and a 56x56 pair moves
64x what a 7x7 one does:

| refused | KiB | why |
|---:|---:|---|
| 6 | 514 | the producer feeds a SKIP and must leave a row-major tensor |
| 4 | 368 | the consumer is an ADD reading a host-built concatenation |
| 2 | 196 | the consumer is not a resident convolution |

Against 2278 KiB carried by the 18 joins that existed then. **That ratio is an ORDERING and not a
cap** — 0.47 of 23.0 ms is still most of a wall that contains a 2.94 ms stem, and the per-join
value is not uniform: the two classes already closed differ by 8x. What prices a pair is a
finite difference, and `ROCKET_RK3576_NET_NOJOIN=<producer>` is the instrument — it refuses
exactly the join that producer owns and leaves every other one intact.

**A JOIN'S PRICE IS ITS BYTES, ACROSS A 32x RANGE.** Each of the 18 existing joins forced off
in turn, 100 iterations, 3 repeats, against a base of 18.40 ms (spread under 0.2 ms):

| producer's tensor | joins removed | ms |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 784 KiB (the stem) | 1 | 6.80 |
| 196 KiB | 1 | 0.57 - 1.10 |
| 98 KiB | 1 | 0.53 - 0.63 |
| 49 KiB | 1 | 0.20 - 0.33 |
| 24.5 KiB | 1 | 0.10 - 0.17 |

The 18 arms sum to **15.0 ms over 2278 KiB, about 6.6 us/KiB**, and with the kick turned off
— submits pinned at 31, so the difference is pure host — they sum to 17.25 ms over the same
bytes. So a join is paid in bytes moved and the stem's is the single most valuable one in the
graph. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate bench 100`]

**The profile, which no ResNet-18 run had ever taken.** 18.4 ms in 25 submits and 38 tasks;
the single largest layer is the **7x7 stem at 2.94 ms, 19.4%**, and the next nine run
0.53-1.02 ms each. Every convolution is one output-channel tile and one task, so `oc` tiling
is not what any of them spend — the same reading the two MobileNets gave.

### None of the three refusal classes was a property of the part

All twelve close, and **ResNet-18 goes 18.4 -> 10.5 ms and 25 -> 11 submits on the same 38
tasks, byte-identical** — the same logits, the same top-5, the same TFLite distance. Each
class needed a different thing and none of them needed a register.

**A SKIP SOURCE MAY WRITE A CUBE, when every layer that reads it can read one.** "A producer
some later layer reads must materialise" is a statement about HOST BUFFERS, not about the
part: it held because the reader three layers on wanted a row-major tensor. A resident handle
OWNS its output surface and nothing writes there until that handle runs again, so the surface
already outlives the whole inference — which is exactly the lifetime a distant reader needs,
and two readers of one surface is the same cube described twice rather than a second copy.
ResNet-18's identity blocks end in an add read TWICE, by the next block's 3x3 convolution and
three layers later by that block's 1x1 downsample, and both are ordinary convolutions. Three
such producers, 343 KiB.

That one has a trap the chain owns. **A cross-layer kick re-stamps every interior layer's
surface inside its verify bracket**, which is sound only because the next kick rewrites that
surface before anything reads it — and a shared surface is read FIRST. Stamping it replaces
the layer's output with 0xA5 and the outside reader computes a full and plausible surface
from the sentinel, invisible until the next materialised layer.
`rocket_conv2d_int8_cube_shared_rk3576()` is the declaration that moves the stamp to the
start of the producer's next call, where cube-out already puts it; a handle writing into a
caller's buffer was already that case and needs no declaration.

**NEITHER OPERAND OF AN ADD IS "THE LAYER BEFORE", AND THAT IS NOT THE PLACEMENT RULE
EITHER.** The concatenation buffers were keyed on operand A's producer being the layer
immediately before the add, which is MobileNetV2's shape and the exact reverse of a ResNet
block that changes width — there operand B is the 1x1 downsample sitting immediately before
the add and operand A is the 3x3 leg past it. The buffer is a dedicated allocation per add
and each slice has exactly one writer, so the ORDER the two producers run in never mattered;
what has to hold is that both precede the add, which they do by construction, and that every
OTHER reader of either producer can read the slice back. Stated that way it wires both
families: ResNet-18 goes from 4 concatenation-wired adds to 8, MobileNetV2 stays at 10.

**A POOL IS A CUBE PRODUCER.** The last two refusals were both the max pool, which feeds the
next convolution AND a residual add three layers on, so nothing could be closed until the PPU
could leave its output in place. `rocket_pool_int8_cube_out_rk3576()` / `_cube_of_` /
`_cube_out_at_` are that side of the join. The PPU's own surface stride is `round4(ow*oh)`
rather than the plane, which is no bound on the consumer — the CNA's DDR channel-group jump
is a register the emitter fills and the part honours it at any value at or above the plane —
so the cube carries it and a conv consumer joins at any plane. **The DESTINATION stride is a
different matter and is refused rather than worked around**: the pooling program derives it
from the output plane and there is no decoded register to move it, so a slice at any other
stride would be written at the wrong offsets. A plane whose element count is already a
multiple of four (56x56 is) needs nothing.

With those three, every adjacent pair of all three networks joins: **ResNet-18 27 of 30**
(the other three index-adjacent pairs are not dataflow edges at all — its downsample reads
across them), MobileNetV1 **28 of 28**, MobileNetV2 **63 of 63**. The two MobileNets' walls do
not move (5.0 and 6.9 ms), which confirms rather than contradicts the earlier reading that
their last unjoined pair — the pool's output into the classifier — is worth nothing.

**A JOIN AND A RUN ARE DIFFERENT QUESTIONS, and closing every join did not make a graph one
stream.** ResNet-18 still ran in 11 submits with all 27 joins in place, because two rules in
the RUN FINDER, neither of them the part's, split the joined layers up: the finder grew a run
only while consecutive pairs were linked, which strands a shortcut convolution reading three
layers back; and it took a pooling layer as a break, which it need not be. Both are above —
"Finding the runs is the library's" and "A POOLING layer sits inside the stream" — and with
them the graph is **one kick in one submit, 10.5 -> 9.2 ms**. Count the RUNS as well as the
joins: a pair can be joined and still sit at a run boundary.

Each class priced on its own, all three flags otherwise on (`bench 100`):

| off | joins | submits | ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| shared surfaces (`ROCKET_RK3576_NET_MULTI=0`) | 24 + 8 far | 17 | 13.7-13.9 |
| concatenation buffers (`ROCKET_RK3576_NET_CAT=0`) | 14 + 3 far | 26 | 18.9-19.0 |
| cross-layer kick (`ROCKET_RK3576_NET_KICK=0`) | 27 + 11 far | 31 | 13.4-13.8 |
| — (everything on) | 27 + 11 far | 11 | **10.6** |

**THE CAP WAS TOO LOW BY 1.8x, AND THE MISSING TERMS WERE BOTH IN THE ACCOUNTING.** Priced
per refused pair at its own size, the twelve came to about 4.4 ms of 18.4; the measurement is
7.9. Two terms sat outside the refused-pair table: the closures also removed **11
NON-ADJACENT links carrying 1078 KiB** — a distant reader's scatter, which no adjacent-pair
row could hold — and they merged runs, taking 25 submits to 11. The refusal table counts what
a pair-shaped instrument can see, so **read it as an ordering over the classes and not as a
bound on the win**. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_net_gate`, 2026-08-02]

Three facts came out of it. Two are registers and neither was reachable from a MobileNet;
the third is about how a harness reads a graph.

### The PPU's pad nibbles: the LEADING pads are the LOW half

`0x6040` packs four four-bit pads, and **bits [3:0] are the LEFT pad and bits [7:4] the
TOP** — the leading edges, not the trailing ones.

Every pool in every vendor capture is padded symmetrically, and **a symmetric pad cannot
distinguish this map from its transpose**. The consequence is not a refusal: with the pairs
swapped, an asymmetric pool computes a full, correctly sized, entirely plausible surface
that is wrong nearly everywhere. A classifier's first max pool is exactly that shape —
TFLite's SAME on an even plane at stride 2, or torchvision's explicit pad whose trailing row
no window reaches — so it is the first thing a real graph hits and nothing in a per-op gate
had ever asked.

What pins it is a ONE-DIMENSIONAL sweep (`rk3576_pool_probe pad`): a single row, a unit
kernel on the other axis, and the part's line scored against the window grid each candidate
leading pad implies. The grid follows the nibble at [3:0] on x and [7:4] on y, at pads 0, 1
and 2, on both axes. With the map corrected, `rk3576_pool_probe bound` — max k3 s2 with one
leading pad and none trailing, over planes 16 to 112 and channel counts 16 to 128 — is
**exact in all 40 cells**, where every one of them was wrong before.

The trailing pads are the high pair and nothing the emitter produces depends on them: the
window count is programmed explicitly in `PPU_OUT_WIDTH`/`_HEIGHT` and the span in
`PPU_IN_WIDTH`/`_HEIGHT`.

**TRAP: give the pool the pad its last window CONSUMES.** The descriptor's trailing pad can
legitimately be smaller than the pad the model declares — torchvision's stride-2 3x3 pads one
row at each end of an even plane and the last window never reaches the trailing one — and it
is the consumed value the geometry has to agree with.

### There is no row-allowance law on the direct path: the wall is the driver's grace

**A single convolution program whose DPU drain outlasts `dpu_grace_us` loses the tail of its
output surface, silently, and nothing faults.** The driver signals the job's fence while the
core is still writing; what the DPU has not written yet is the last rows of the last
output-channel group, so the caller reads a full, correctly sized surface whose corner is
wrong. This is the whole of what had been recorded here as a CBUF "row allowance" that the
planner was over on.

**The arm is one module parameter and it is decisive.** At `dpu_grace_us = 500` (the
shipping default) a `112x112 k7 ic32` convolution at four output-channel groups is wrong at
every plane height from 45 rows up; at **750** every one of them is bit-exact, at the same
shape, the same program and the same CBUF `F`; restoring 500 brings the failure back at the
same heights with the same signature. The threshold sits between 500 and 750 us at four
groups and at six alike — a fixed TIME, not a function of the shape, which is what a
deadline is and what no capacity bound can be. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-05]

**Every measured wall is the same amount of WORK, and no CBUF sum can be written down for
them.** One VALID-padded plane per probe, laid out as exactly one task, walked row by row
with `rk3576_conv_lib_gate rowmap` and three runs per height (618 heights):

| groups | oc | tallest exact plane | feature granules | MMACs |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 32 | never fails to 109, the reachable top | 6104 | 548 |
| 2 | 64 | 78 | 4368 | 766 |
| 3 | 96 | 56 | 3136 | 798 |
| 4 | 128 | 44 | 2464 | 808 |
| 5 | 160 | 36 | 2016 | 798 |
| 6 | 192 | 32 | 1792 | 830 |

The walls span 766-830 MMACs, an 8% band, while the feature footprint they sit at falls 2.4x
across the same rows. **One output-channel group never fails at any reachable height** — its
work tops out at 548 MMACs, under the band — which is why every earlier k7 cell at one or two
groups read as agreeing. And `feature + weights <= pool` cannot be fitted at all: one group
computes at a feature-plus-cube total of 6888 granules where two groups FAIL at 5992.

#### What the deadline is spent on is the program's execution, not the surface it writes

The six rows above cannot tell those apart. They hold `ic` and `k` fixed, and there the MACs
are exactly proportional to the output elements, so a WORK law and an output-BYTES law fit the
same table. Five further cells break that proportionality — through the kernel, through the
plane width and through `ic` — and they separate the two by factors, not by margins. Same
instrument, one VALID task per height, three runs each. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-05]

| cell | ic | oc | iw | k | first wrong height | output there | work there |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `k7g2-112` | 32 | 64 | 112 | 7 | 79 | 495 KB | 777 MMAC |
| `k7g3-112` | 32 | 96 | 112 | 7 | 57 | 519 KB | 814 MMAC |
| `k7g4-112` | 32 | 128 | 112 | 7 | 45 | 529 KB | 830 MMAC |
| `k7g5-112` | 32 | 160 | 112 | 7 | 37 | 526 KB | 824 MMAC |
| `k7g6-112` | 32 | 192 | 112 | 7 | 33 | 550 KB | 862 MMAC |
| `k5g4-112` | 32 | 128 | 112 | 5 | 77 | 1009 KB | 807 MMAC |
| `k7g4-224` | 32 | 128 | 224 | 7 | 25 | 530 KB | 831 MMAC |
| `k7g4-56` | 32 | 128 | 56 | 7 | 60, dense from 89 | 346 KB, 531 KB | 542, 833 MMAC |
| `k7ic64-g2` | 64 | 64 | 112 | 7 | 34 | 190 KB | 596 MMAC |
| `k3g4-112` | 32 | 128 | 112 | 3 | none to 109 | 1507 KB exact | 434 MMAC |
| `k7g1-112` | 32 | 32 | 112 | 7 | none to 109 | 349 KB exact | 548 MMAC |

**The output surface is not the quantity.** Across the first failures it spans 190 KB to
1009 KB, a factor of 5.3, where the work spans 542-862 MMACs, a factor of 1.6. The
contradiction is direct rather than statistical: `k3g4-112` writes **1507 KB** with no error at
any reachable height while `k7ic64-g2` is wrong at **190 KB**, eight times less. At three taps
the MACs per output element fall 5.4x and the surface grows, and the wall goes away.

**Nor is it the output ROWS.** `k7g4-224` is wrong 3 of 3 at **19** output rows; `k7g4-56` is
exact past **78**. A row count fitted on the `112`-wide family — 38 output rows — is wrong in
both directions by about a factor of two, so the width moves the wall by the total and not by
the row count.

**Work is a proxy for the program's execution time, and it is a good one only where the weight
traffic per output is fixed.** The five `112`-wide k7 cells and `k7g4-224` land in 777-862
MMACs; `k7g4-56`'s dense region starts at 833. `k7ic64-g2` fails 27% below that band at
596 MMACs, and it carries twice the resident weight slice per output-channel group (1568
granules against 784), so each output element costs more DMA than a MAC count sees.
**[hypothesis]** for the residual spread; what is measured is the ordering.

At 786 MHz — the NPU clock here is fixed, see "The clock does not park" — 800 MMACs in 500 us
is about 2050 MACs per cycle, and two cores at that rate is the part's marketed 6 TOPS. So a
one-task program's whole compute sits inside the grace: `PC_DONE` is raised when the program
counter retires the descriptor, not when the pipeline behind it has finished.

**TRAP: THE HEIGHTS ABOVE A REFUSAL READ AS EXACT.** `k7ic64-g2` is wrong from 34 to 45 and
then reports `0/3 wrong` from 46 up, which looks like a BAND with a clean upper edge — the
depthwise rung signature exactly. It is not one: those rows carry `(3 refused or skipped)` and
the entry never emitted a program. **An untouched output BO is not evidence about anything**;
read the annotation, not the count.

**Its failure signature changes shape at 37, and that is TWO mechanisms, not one.** Below 37
the wrong elements are a row tail of the last output-channel group, which is what a marginal
overrun looks like; from 37 up they are the whole surface of that group (rows 0-30 of 31), and
the planner's CBUF `F` moves from 0 to 1024 at the same height. **Removing the deadline
separates them**: on a kernel that waits for the writing block the low heights go to 0 of 3
where they were 1 and 3 of 3, and **37-45 stay wrong at exactly the same element counts**
(104936, 108489, 111936 at 37, 38, 39 under both), which is what says the second one is
deterministic and owes nothing to timing. The heights from 37 now refuse at the library, since
the slice cap's `F`-aware form does not cover them.

#### The deadline's residue below the refusal is the deadline, and both its rate and its shape are wider than 3 reps can see

The heights the planner still honours at `F=0` — 33 to 36 on `k7ic64-g2`, where the plane needs
3696 to 4032 of 4096 granules and the 98 KiB slice sits inside the 156 KiB `F=0` cap — are wrong
on the shipped module and **bit-exact under either of two independent removals of the deadline**.
`rowmap` with `MAP_REPS=10`, one task per cell, the planner choosing `F`
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-05]:

| arm | ih 33 | ih 34 | ih 35 | ih 36 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| shipped 1.5.0, `dpu_grace_us` 500 | 2 of 30 | 14 of 60 | 26 of 60 | 56 of 60 |
| shipped 1.5.0, `dpu_grace_us` 1000 | — | 0 of 30 | 0 of 30 | 0 of 30 |
| the waiting kernel (1.6.0), its own default | 0 of 30 | 0 of 30 | 0 of 30 | 0 of 30 |

The 500-vs-1000 comparison is **interleaved**, three alternating passes in one session: 40 of 90
wrong at 500 against 0 of 90 at 1000, with the failure returning every time the grace does. That
matters because **the rate at a fixed height wanders across identical walks** — ih 35 reads 0, 1,
1, 6, 8 and 10 of 10 over six runs of the same command at the same grace — so a single walk's
zero is not evidence, and neither is a single walk's localisation. **The inherited "34-36" was an
artefact of 3 reps**: ih 33 fails too, and ih 35 read 0 of 10 in the first walk of this session
and 10 of 10 in the fourth.

**The deadline's severe shape is the same drop with the cut landing earlier — ONE mechanism, on a
continuum, and the extent is a readout of the cut time rather than a property of the failure.**
Alongside the row tail of one output-channel group (hundreds to a few thousand wrong elements,
`chans 32-47`), the same cells sometimes return a surface wrong across every row and all 64
channels, 150000 to 170000 of 203520 elements. Sweeping `dpu_grace_us` as an instrument moves the
extent smoothly from one to the other — `k7ic64-g2`, ih 33-36, `rowmap` with `MAP_REPS=10` and
`MAP_SEQ=1`, every rep printed, 131 wrong reps [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-05]:

| `dpu_grace_us` | wrong reps | wrong elements per rep | channel span | row span |
|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| 500 | 11 of 40 | 1184 - 3808 | `c 32-47` | a 1-3 row suffix |
| 250 | 40 of 40 | 27798 - 68768 | `c 32-63` | from row 5-16 down |
| 100 | 40 of 40 | 84638 - 131008 | `c 0-63` | every row |
| 0 | 40 of 40 | 116364 - 155008 | `c 0-63` | every row |

Grace 0 at ih 36 reaches **155008 elements**, inside the severe shape's own band, so the severe
shape at grace 500 is the tail of one distribution and not a second event. **107 of the 131 wrong
reps land between the two shapes**, in a range a two-mechanism reading requires to be empty. The
severe shape did not occur once in **240 reps** at grace 500 this session, which is why three
sessions of 3-to-10-rep walks read the two ends as two things.

**Every wrong element is a DROPPED write, never a wrong value.** The library stamps each output
surface with `0xA5` before the submit (`rocket_rk3576_sentinel_on`, on by default), and in **131
of 131 wrong reps every wrong element still holds the stamp** — zero elements carrying data, at
every grace value including 0, where the driver retires each job as early as it can. So a lost
drain costs a corner of the job's *own* surface; it does not leave the core writing into an
unrelated job's window. Scoped to what that arm reaches: one process, sequential conv submits
~10 ms apart, one geometry. It says nothing about a victim in another process, of another job
type, or in a graph submitting back to back.

**The order the drop grows in is `c 32-47` first, then `c 48-63`, then `c 0-31`** [HW sweep] —
never the natural channel order, at any grace value. Reading that as the part's DDR write order,
and so as "the last channel group this geometry writes is 32-47 rather than the highest-numbered
one", is **[expected]**: nothing in this arm separates write order from another cause of the same
ordering.

**The shape is not a safe discriminator in the direction it was used.** What separates the slice
cap from the deadline is that the slice loses an integral number of trailing output-channel
groups *completely* — every row of `chans 32-63`, exactly 50% here — where the deadline's loss is
a suffix in write order at whatever depth the cut fell. A "whole surface" reading alone does not
tell them apart; the stamp does, since the slice cap's loss is also unwritten but lands on group
boundaries.

### The weight slice is charged against what the feature allowance leaves, and `F` is the other half of that pool

The second mechanism at 37 is **not** a dead `F=1024` rung. It is the resident weight slice
overrunning the CBUF pool once the feature allowance grows, and the graded output-channel-group
loss the slice cap already models is the same bound met from the other end of one shared budget.

**The emitter programs `4096+F` granules for the feature side whatever the plane's actual
height is**, so every granule of `F` is a granule the weight path does not get. The cap table
above was measured at `F=0`, where the weight path has 3072 granules; at `F` it has `3072-F`,
and the usable part falls granule for granule with it.

Measured on two shapes, four `F` values and two group counts — one task per cell, `MAP_REPS=3`,
the plane held well inside the `F=0` budget so that a rung the part declined could not matter:

| shape | groups | slice | `F` | usable | the part |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `112x112 k7 ic 64 oc 64` | 2 | 98 KiB | 0 | 156 KiB | exact |
| | | | 1024 | **92 KiB** | WRONG, `chans 32-63` — 1 of 2 groups |
| | | | 2048 | 28 KiB | **no `PC_DONE` at all** |
| `112x112 k7 ic 32 oc 128` | 4 | 49 KiB | 0 | 144 KiB | exact |
| | | | 1024 | 80 KiB | exact |
| | | | 2048 | **16 KiB** | WRONG, `chans 32-127` — 3 of 4 groups |

**Both wrong cells are shapes the pool check accepts** (6688 and 6928 granules against 7168),
and the wrong ones are wrong at *every* row of the groups they lose — exactly the slice cap's
recorded signature, and not the dead rung's, which is a full surface with a wrong row TAIL past
`4096/entries`.

**What refutes the rung reading.** Forcing `F=1024` on the two-group cell is 3 of 3 wrong at
**all 30 plane heights from 7 to 36**, including heights needing 784 granules of a 4096-granule
budget, where a rung delivering only `4096` would be harmless; `worst` is exactly half the
surface at every one. The same forced rung beside a **16-granule** slice is exact at **42 of 42**
cells over 1, 2 and 3 output-channel groups, which also rules out "programming an allowance
larger than the plane is itself invalid". And `F=2048`, which the rung table records as
delivering at every slice tried, does not deliver here at all: the job raises no `PC_DONE`, the
surface guard spends eight power cycles and the entry gives up with `-4`. **The governing
quantity is the slice against what `F` leaves, not the rung.**

**Past the pool the failure stops being quiet.** That `-4` is the useful boundary: inside the
pool a slice that is over computes a plausible wrong surface, and past it the program does not
execute. So the pool check guards a real cliff and this bound sits inside it.

#### The slope is 1:1 to the granule, and the reserve is what the group count buys

Read directly rather than bracketed: a 4x2 plane where nothing drains, `k=1` so the slice is
`32*ic` and moves in 1 KiB steps, `F` forced so it is the only thing that changes, and the walk
reported as a map. The last exact slice, one task per cell:

| groups (`oc`) | `F=1024` | `F=2048` | the weight path holds | usable = hold - reserve |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 (64) | **96 KiB** (1536 gran) | **32 KiB** (512 gran) | 2048 / 1024 granules | reserve **512** granules |
| 3 (96) | **85 KiB** (1360 gran) | **21 KiB** (336 gran) | | reserve **688** |
| 4 (128) | **80 KiB** (1280 gran) | **16 KiB** (256 gran) | | reserve **768** |

The weight path holds `3072-F` granules, and **the reserve is constant in `F` at every group
count** — 512, 688 and 768 granules, identical at both allowances. So the slope is 1:1 to the
granule at three group counts, and what the group count costs is a fixed reserve rather than a
scaling. Each boundary is deterministic: 3 of 3 wrong at the first KiB over and 0 of 3 at the
last KiB under. **[HW sweep, `rk3576_conv_lib_gate slicemap`]**

Those reserves put the `F=0` intercepts at **160 / 149 / 144 KiB**, against the cap table's
156 / 148 / 144 — consistent with it, because that sweep's samples were 144, 145, 148, 152, 156
and 162 and it took the last measured-good one rather than the boundary. **The shipped numbers
over-charge by 4 / 1 / 0 KiB and never under-charge**, at six directly measured cells. They are
not raised to the implied intercepts: `F=0` is the one point this instrument cannot reach,
because the output-channel tile splits a conv above 156 KiB and it never runs as two groups
there, and raising a cap on an extrapolation is the direction that computes a wrong surface.

**A slice that is an exact multiple of 512 granules (32 KiB) computes anyway**, far above the
cap. At `F=1024` both 96 KiB and 128 KiB are exact at all three group counts while every
neighbouring KiB is wrong; at `F=2048` so are 64 KiB (2 groups) and 32 KiB (3 groups). A
multiple of 16 KiB that is not one of 32 does not escape — 112 KiB is wrong. This also rules out
the two readings the first sighting suggested: 96 KiB is neither the pool edge (128 KiB is at
`F=1024`) nor a power-of-two `ic` (3072). **The bound therefore has an alignment term nothing
models**, and the library deliberately does not model it, since the only thing it could do is
accept more. **[HW sweep]**, mechanism **[hypothesis]**: the weight path stages the slice in
32 KiB units and a partial trailing unit is what costs the trailing output-channel group.

**The escape is a property of the slice's BYTE COUNT, not of the kernel or the plane the sweep
was taken at.** Both conditions every recorded cell shared have been moved, at `F=1024` and
`oc=128`, where the cap is 80 KiB and 96 and 128 KiB are both well above it:

| walk | cap | 81-95 KiB | **96 KiB** | 97-127 KiB | **128 KiB** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `k=1`, 4x2 plane (the record) | 80 KiB | 1 group lost to 85, 2 from 86 | **0 of 3** | 3 groups | **0 of 3** |
| `k=2`, 4x2 plane | 80 KiB | 1 at 84, 2 at 88 and 92 | **0 of 20** | 3 (20 of 20 each) | **0 of 20** |
| `k=1`, 4x19 plane | 80 KiB | 1 to 85, 2 from 86 | **0 of 10** | 3 to 107; **108-127 do not COMPLETE** | **0 of 10** |

`ic` is 3072 at `k=1` and 768 at `k=2` for the same 96 KiB, so nothing about the cube survives
as an explanation; the graded group loss steps at the same slice sizes on both lattices too. The
4x19 plane consumes 3040-4864 granules of the forced 5120 allowance against 4x2's 160-256, and
moves neither the cap nor the escape. **[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_conv_lib_gate slicemap`
with `ROCKET_LG_SLICE_K`]** **What this did not vary**: one group count and one allowance, so the
invariance is measured where the slope table has three group counts and two — and `k=4`, the only
other kernel that reaches the lattice below the pool, was not tried.

**What the tall plane DOES move is the quiet-to-cliff boundary**: at 4x2 every cell to 128 KiB
computes a wrong surface, and at 4x19 the entry returns `-4` with no completion at all from 108
to 127 KiB. So the non-execution cliff is a function of the plane and the slice together, while
the graded loss and the escape are functions of the slice alone. **128 KiB then runs, and is
exact** — the escape jumps clean over a band where the program does not execute, which no
reading of it as "a little more headroom" survives.

**Which kernels can ask this question at all is fixed by arithmetic.** The slice is `32*ic*k*k`
and the direct path requires `ic` to be a multiple of 32, so the reachable slices are exactly
the multiples of `k*k` KiB. A 32 KiB multiple therefore needs `k` in {1, 2, 4}; `k=3` reaches
one first at 288 KiB and `k=5` at 800 KiB, both far past the 128 KiB the pool leaves at
`F=1024`. An arm framed as "repeat the walk at `k=3`" cannot be built.

The library charges it in both places that read the cap: the plan refuses, and
`rocket_rk3576_max_task_rows()` skips a rung whose F-aware cap no longer covers the slice.
Without the second one it returned **45** input rows for `112x112 k7 ic 64 oc 64` — `F=1024`'s
window, and the whole failing band — so every task the row planner cut was a full surface with
its trailing group wrong. It now returns 36, and heights 37-45 REFUSE where they used to
compute. `claimplan` still reports 160 shapes, 0 accept-a-refusal and 0 over-refusal.

**It is invisible in the corpus.** No model reaches the geometry — measured, in that the five net
gates keep their structure across the change — and until the deadline was removed it sat inside the
deadline's own failure region. The REASON is **[expected]** rather than enumerated layer by layer:
reaching the bound needs a slice near the cap *and* a plane large enough to need a rung, a 92 KiB
slice is `ic*kh*kw >= 2944`, and a layer carrying that much `ic` tends to have a plane small enough
to sit at `F=0` — so the two requirements look anti-correlated. `rk3576_conv_lib_gate rowmap`'s `ROCKET_LG_MAP_F` is the
instrument, and the cells are `k7ic64-g2` and `k7g4-112`.

**The shipped planner is not over.** `rk3576_conv_lib_gate rowbound` puts it in exact
agreement with the part on **23 of 23 geometries** at `dpu_grace_us = 750`, including
`56x56 k7 ic 64 oc 64` — the one geometry that had been recorded as over and deliberately
left so, which goes from 58 rows back to the planner's own 91. At 500 that single
disagreement is the only one, and it is this.

**What the corpus is protected by is an accident.** The row planner windows a task to fit the
CBUF allowance, and on the shapes real models carry that window keeps every task's drain under
500 us. Nothing in the five classifiers, the two detectors or the per-axis ResNet-18 reaches
the deadline; the probe knob `ROCKET_RK3576_ROW_CAP_PROBE` is what emits windows past it. So
the class is unreachable in the corpus and entirely reachable by a caller with a wider plane
or a deeper output-channel count.

**TRAP: THE EDGE MOVES BETWEEN RUNS, so a bisection over it reports a bound and not a law.**
At two groups the boundary height is wrong on every run inside a long sequential walk and
clean in a short one, same binary and same shape. Reading it as a capacity bound is what
produced the earlier "ceiling that falls with the group count", and the earlier record of it
moving 1-4 rows between runs was this. **Read it as a RATE** — `ROCKET_LG_MAP_REPS` — because
below the height where the failure is 3 of 3 there is a region where it is 1 of 3: `k7g4-56`
is wrong once at 60 and once at 68 and then clean to 84, and `k7g4-224` once at 19 and then
clean to 24.

**What moves it is the HOST's timing, not the part's.** The NPU clock is fixed at 786 MHz and
does not park, and there is no NPU devfreq node — only the GPU has one — so nothing on the
part's side of this varies. What does vary is when a 50 us poll tick lands relative to
`PC_DONE`, and how late the hrtimer callback runs under load, both of which move the start of
the grace window by a poll period on a 500 us budget. **[hypothesis]**: it is stated here
because the earlier reading, that a slower clock lengthens the drain, contradicts the fixed
clock recorded in this same file and would send the next person to the wrong register.

**TRAP: `dpu_grace_us` is a fitted number in a shipping path, and 0022 already removed the
other one.** That patch made a job's wait depend on the hardware's own account of the kick
rather than on a deadline, but only for a kick of more than one task: a job of one task
programs `TASK_NUMBER = 1`, where both `PC_TASK_STATUS` counters read zero always, so
`PC_DONE` alone signals it and the drain after it is bounded by the grace. The same is true of
the LAST program of any kick. **Raising the grace is a workaround, not the fix** — how long
one program may take is not a property of the driver's timing.

#### The write-guard reading of it, which is the older half

A task past the deadline by a wide enough margin writes NOTHING the guard can see, and the
whole-surface write guard cannot tell that from a poisoned submit — both look empty, and it
spends eight power cycles on each before the entry returns `ROCKET_E_DEVICE` and the caller
falls back to the host. **A deterministic failure at the same row task every time is the
plan or the deadline, not the poisoning hazard.**

Past a smaller margin the surface is written in full and is wrong: **0 dropped-to-zero
elements in 12 of 12 failing cells**, every wrong element carrying a plausible value. Both
readings are the same mechanism at different distances past the same fence.

#### The output-channel group loss at a large weight slice is a separate, real bound

`r76_weight_slice_cap()` is measured on a **4x2 plane**, where the drain is nowhere near the
grace, and it is about the SLICE rather than the window: at four output-channel groups a
resident slice of 144 KiB leaves every group exact, 145 KiB loses one, 152 KiB loses two, and
162 KiB leaves one. That table stands. What does not stand is reading a large-window failure
at a SMALL slice — 49 KiB against that 144 KiB cap — as the same rule met from the other end.

#### On the DEPTHWISE path the two low CBUF rungs do not deliver, and the failure is a BAND

The allowance is not one budget, it is a LADDER: F takes one of five measured values and the
planner programs the smallest rung whose `4096+F` granules cover the task's window. A rung
the part does not honour delivers the F=0 budget instead — so the surface is exact below the
rung's reach, **wrong across the band of windows that select it, and exact again above**. The
failure is not monotone in the window height, which is why a probe that bisects the window
cannot see it and reports whichever edge it walks into.

Walked one task per height at 160x160 ic = oc = 32 k3 depthwise, 80 granules a row, with the
planner choosing F (`rk3576_conv_lib_gate rowmap`):

| plane rows | granules needed | planner F | surface |
|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 45-51 | 3600-4080 | 0 | exact |
| **52-54** | **4160-4320** | **256** | **WRONG** |
| **55-57** | **4400-4560** | **512** | **WRONG** |
| 58-64 | 4640-5120 | 1024 | exact |
| 65-76 | 5200-6080 | 2048 | exact |

The same walk on the DIRECT path at the same plane, the same channels and the same entries
per row is exact at every height, 49 through 60. In the band the wrong elements are exactly
the output rows past `4096/entries` — one row at a 52-row plane, the F=0 budget's 51 rows
staged under a programmed 4352.

**Forcing each rung under one fixed window says no threshold on the depthwise weight
footprint fits.** One window that F=0 does not buy, `ROCKET_RK3576_CBUF_F` forcing each rung
beneath it:

| path | kernel | oc | resident footprint | F=256 / F=512 |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| direct | 1x1 | 32 | 1024 B = 16 granules | deliver |
| direct | 1x1 | 64 | 2048 B = 32 granules | fall back to 4096 |
| depthwise | 1x1 | 32 | 64 B = 1 granule | deliver |
| depthwise | 1x1 | 256 | 512 B = 8 granules | deliver |
| depthwise | 1x1 | 1024 | 2048 B = 32 granules | deliver |
| depthwise | 2x2 | 32 | 256 B = 4 granules | fall back to 4096 |
| depthwise | 3x3 | 32 | 576 B = 9 granules | fall back to 4096 |
| depthwise | 5x5 | 32 | 1600 B = 25 granules | fall back to 4096 |
