Two localisations were inherited and both were wrong. "It enters where the global pool reads
the final concatenation as its cube" came from a single knob; "it is the kick, not the joins"
came from a single `NET_KICK=0` arm that happened not to fire a ~30% hazard. The
`NET_KICK_MAX` table that read as a race — 8 passes, 20 and 30 fail, 40 passes, 41 fails — is a
one-run-per-cell sample of that same rate. **A non-monotone table is a race OR an intermittent
hazard sampled once per cell**, and nothing distinguishes them without repeats.

With the check extended, `rk3576_net_gate cube --net iv3` is green and is in the gate list.

### Why Inception V3 fragments, counted by reason

A join is a property of a PAIR and a run is broken by ONE layer, so the join-refusal table
cannot answer this. `kick_build()` reports it per layer instead: **40 layers sit outside any
run and each one is a submit.**

| count | why |
|---:|---|
| 9 | it feeds a SKIP that reads a row-major tensor, so it must materialise one |
| 9 | it is a padded average pool whose divisor can lag |
| 5 | it leaves no cube, so the layer after it would need a host de-scatter |
| 5 | it reads no cube any earlier member of the run wrote |
| 12 | it is linked at both ends but is the only program between two breaks |

The other four graphs are ONE run covering every layer, so the count is zero on all of them.
**Only the second reason is the part's** — the rest are the gate's own lowering and this
graph's topology, which is where ResNet-18's and Inception V1's refusal classes also turned
out to live — and the 12 in the last row are consequential rather than causal: they are what
the other 28 cost.

The pool row has to be marked where the candidate is built rather than read off the plan,
or the table reports the layers stranded AROUND each pool and never the pool itself, which
reads as fragmentation the topology caused. **A refusal count does not say where a run
breaks, and a bucket that names a symptom is worse than one that names nothing.**

## The symmetry audit, second round: the CNA

The pooling block has produced one transposed FIELD (the pad nibbles) and one BOUND fitted on
the same symmetry (the output-width allowance, keyed on the kernel width where the axis is the
height). Both compute a full, correctly sized, entirely plausible surface when they are wrong,
and neither could be seen by a cell holding `kh == kw` or `sy == sx`. Every cell of
`rk3576_conv_lib_gate` sets both pairs square, so the convolution block's two remaining axes had
never been driven. `tests/rk3576_conv_sym.c` drives them, as PAIRS at one rectangular plane —
a transpose maps each cell of a pair onto the other, so a transposed field fails BOTH halves and
a formula keyed on the wrong axis fails one.

**NON-SQUARE STRIDES ARE CLEAN: 12 of 12 cells bit-exact.** `s2x1` against `s1x2` at a 48x32
plane over square kernels (k3, k5), non-square ones in both orientations (k3x1, k1x3), the
DEPTHWISE datapath, and a stride larger than the kernel on one axis only (`s3x1` / `s1x3`).
Nothing in the corpus has a non-square stride, so this was unverified rather than suspected, and
it stays a negative rather than a finding. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]

**AND THE AUDIT'S REAL YIELD WAS A FORMULA AGAIN — the CBUF `F` rung liveness.** The planner
offered the 256 and 512 rungs wherever `kh == 1`, from a measurement that held `ic` at 32 and
moved the kernel. On the DIRECT path the axis is the **resident weight footprint**, not the
kernel: at one granule total and one kernel, the same shape is exact at `ic` 32 and wrong at
`ic` 64 and 128. Written up in [rk3576-regcmd.md](rk3576-regcmd.md); the planner is keyed on the
footprint and every cell of the map is bit-exact after it, with `regcmd_rk3576_gate`'s sixteen
planner cells and `rk3576_conv_lib_gate`'s 244 unmoved. **That footprint does not carry to the
DEPTHWISE path**, where 4 granules is dead and 32 is live and the low rungs are declined
outright — see "On the DEPTHWISE path the two low CBUF rungs do not deliver".

**The probe's own control is what made that readable.** `k1x1` is the arm that must SUCCEED —
it is where the rungs were measured to deliver — and it FAILED, which says the finding is about
the control and not about the `k1xN` cells the probe was written for. A probe whose
must-succeed arm fails has to become a MAP before it becomes a verdict: the F=0 boundary walked
row by row, the same granule total reached through different (row size, row count)
factorizations, and the same planes forced under the boundary as the arm that must be exact.

## What the wall is made of, once the graph work is collected

Every lever above is in, so the shares have all moved and none of the earlier readings
describes the graph that runs today. Measured at `bench 100`, resident weights, cube layout,
cross-layer kicks, `ROCKET_RK3576_INT8_PROF=1` — which now covers the CHAIN as well as the
per-call path, so a kick is no longer a single opaque number.

Every graph is ONE chain call, so the profile is the whole wall rather than a sample of it
and the accounting is exact rather than approximate:

| | ms | submits | tasks | submit + wait | verify | stamp | feature scatter | de-scatter |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | 5.0 | 1 | 40 | 2.36 (47%) | 2.04 (40%) | 0.03 | 0.61 (12%) | 0.01 |
| MobileNetV2 | 6.6 | 1 | 77 | 2.88 (43%) | 2.93 (44%) | 0.26 (4%) | 0.61 (9%) | 0.01 |
| ResNet-18 | 6.6 | 1 | 33 | 4.38 (66%) | 1.32 (20%) | 0.78 (12%) | 0.17 (3%) | 0.02 |
| Inception V1 | 7.1 | 1 | 72 | 4.08 (58%) | 1.71 (24%) | 1.12 (16%) | 0.09 (1%) | 0.01 |

The **write guard is `verify` + `stamp`** and is confirmed independently by the
`ROCKET_RK3576_I32_SENTINEL=0` A/B, which gives 1.9 / 3.0 / 2.1 / 2.8 ms against the columns'
2.07 / 3.19 / 2.10 / 2.83. `plan+gen` no longer appears: a chain generates its whole stream
once at construction, so on a graph that is one kick there is no per-inference regcmd work at
all. **Inception V3 is not in this table** because it is not one chain call — 18 kicks and 21
per-layer convolution calls beside 16 pooling ones; its own accounting is under "The pooling
path's read interval is three things".

**THESE GRAPHS ARE NO LONGER HOST-BOUND, and that reverses the reading the earlier profile
here supports.** "The submit is 1-16% of a layer against a host share of 84-99%" was measured
on a 40-submit graph that transposed at every layer boundary and repacked its weights every
inference; residency, the cube layout and the kick have since removed all three of those
terms, and what is left is dominated by the two that were never host arithmetic. **The host
operand packing that was 84-99% of the wall is now 6-12%** — one feature scatter per graph,
at the stem, because every other layer reads a cube.

**THE WRITE GUARD IS THE SECOND TERM EVERYWHERE AND ON MobileNetV2 IT IS THE FIRST**, at 3.0
ms of 6.7 against the submit's 3.0. It is a `PREP_BO`/`FINI_BO` pair per surface per call and
both halves sync the whole BO, so it is bytes-proportional and independent of how few bytes
the check reads.

**Its cheaper form reaches exactly as much of it as the graph keeps inside kicks, and that
is a property of the run structure rather than of the mechanism.**
`ROCKET_RK3576_GUARD_PER_KICK=1` asks one surface per kick rather than every layer's. When
ResNet-18 ran in eleven submits it collected the whole guard on both MobileNets (each already
one kick) and only **65%** on ResNet-18 — 10.6 -> 8.9 against a guard-off floor of 8.0 —
because seven of its submits sat outside any kick and each paid its own per-call bracket.

Once every graph is ONE kick the shortfall is gone. All four now land on the guard-off floor
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `bench 100`, 2026-08-02]:

| | default | `GUARD_PER_KICK=1` | `I32_SENTINEL=0` floor |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | 4.8 | **3.0** | 3.0 |
| MobileNetV2 | 6.8 | **3.5** | 3.5 |
| ResNet-18 | 9.2 | **6.5** | 6.4 |
| Inception V1 | 10.8 | **6.2** | 6.2 |

So the guard is now the **largest single term on three of the four** — 1.8 ms of 4.8 (38%),
3.3 of 6.8 (49%), 2.8 of 9.2 (30%), 4.6 of 10.8 (43%) — and the cheaper form's coverage is no
longer network-dependent.
What the two forms cover is unchanged and is a correctness question, not a performance one:
the reduced guard catches the whole-submit poisoning (6 of 150 inferences wrong unguarded, 0
of 150 under either form) and cannot by construction see a single program dying mid-stream.
Which one ships is the maintainer's call.

This is also the shape a stale ratio takes: "the flag collects 65% on ResNet-18" was a
correct measurement of a graph that no longer exists, and nothing about the flag changed.

**What the STAMP column says.** In the steady state a surface carries its sentinel from the
previous call's verify bracket and costs nothing — but a surface the chain does not rewrite
before someone reads it cannot be re-stamped there, so it pays a second bracket at the start
of the next call. Three kinds do: a PLACED slice of a concatenation buffer, a DECLARED shared
surface, and a POOL (whose handle keeps no flag for the chain to clear). **That term
therefore scales with the residual topology, the placed slices and the pooling layers, not
with the layer count**: 0.01 ms on MobileNetV1, which has no adds at all, against 0.39 on
V2's ten, 1.16 on ResNet-18's eight adds plus three shared skip sources plus two pools, and
**2.07 on Inception V1's 37 placed producers plus thirteen pools** — the largest stamp of the
four, and the reason wiring its concatenations gave 3.26 ms of the lever back to the guard.

**The submit column is a round trip AND the execution inside it**, since the fence is waited
on through the last layer's surface. A marginal submit, from the A/Bs that add or remove one,
prices between **0.145 and 0.25 ms** depending on the surface it drains — ResNet-18's stem is
six row tasks in one submit at 2.13 ms and `ROCKET_RK3576_BATCH_TASKS=0` makes it six submits
at 3.39 ms, 0.25 ms each **on a task writing a 112x112x64 surface**, against 0.145 for twenty
small layers — **and LESS as a graph runs out of submits**: taking ResNet-18 from 11 to 1 is
0.13 ms each and the MobileNets' last two are 0.05-0.10. Re-measure it against the graph in
front of you rather than scaling one of these figures. What that column is MADE of is the
next section.

### The submit bucket is the part running programs, and the rest of it is 1-4%

At one kick per graph the submit column is 61-81% of every wall, which makes it the only term
above 20% and the one any further performance claim has to be quoted against. It is a single
interval covering the ioctl, the kick's execution and the completion wait, and from userspace
those cannot be separated — so a share of it is not a cap until something says which of the
three it is.

Two instruments say. The chain's own profile splits its interval into the **submit ioctl**,
the **fence wait** (a `PREP_BO` with a timeout on the last layer's surface) and the
**`FINI_BO`** that hands it back. And `rocket-userspace/tools/rk3576-job-timing-debug.patch`
— a debug module, not a series patch — adds five microsecond accumulators to the driver's
polled completion path, exposed as writable module parameters, splitting the wait at the
events only the driver can see: the **scheduler wakeup** (push to `.run_job` entry), the
**prologue** (`.run_job` to `OPERATION_ENABLE`, carrying `pm_runtime_get_sync`, the IOMMU
attach test and the first task's register programming), the **execution** (the kick to the
poll that sees every task retired), the **drain** (that to the writing block's own
completion), and the **signal** (that to `dma_fence_signal`).

Per inference, `bench 100` with the guard narrowed, resident weights and cube layout, on the
0023 module plus the instrument. The driver's terms are `bench(200) - bench(100)` over 100, so
the gate's fixed setup jobs cancel [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-02]:

| | submit bucket | ioctl | wakeup | prologue | **execution** | drain | signal | `FINI_BO` |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | 2.34 ms | 0.057 | 0.012 | 0.003 | **2.252** | 0.001 | 0.008 | 0.004 |
| MobileNetV2 | 2.86 | 0.134 | 0.017 | 0.002 | **2.698** | 0.000 | 0.007 | 0.004 |
| ResNet-18 | 5.84 | 0.055 | 0.014 | 0.002 | **5.752** | 0.000 | 0.008 | 0.004 |
| Inception V1 | 5.56 | 0.107 | 0.018 | 0.003 | **5.450** | 0.001 | 0.008 | 0.004 |

**THE ACCOUNTING CLOSES TO UNDER 1%** — the terms sum to 2.34 / 2.86 / 5.84 / 5.59 ms against
measured buckets of 2.34 / 2.86 / 5.84 / 5.56 — and the driver's independent `push -> fence
signal` total (2.21 / 2.73 / 5.78 / 5.48) reproduces the caller's own fence wait (2.28 / 2.72
/ 5.78 / 5.45) to within tens of microseconds. So this is a decomposition rather than a
residual: nothing is attributed to what was not measured.

**EVERYTHING THAT IS NOT THE PART RUNNING PROGRAMS IS 0.08-0.17 ms — 1.4-5.8% of the bucket
and 1.1-3.7% of the wall.** The submit ioctl is the largest of them and scales with the BO
count rather than the work (~0.7 us per BO: 83 BOs on ResNet-18 for 0.055 ms, 182 on
MobileNetV2 for 0.134). The scheduler wakeup is 12-18 us, the prologue 2-3, the fence signal
about 8, the `FINI_BO` 4. **There is no dispatch lever left in this bucket**, and a proposal
against it has to make the part do less work or do it faster.

**The ~340 us per-submit floor does NOT appear as a fixed cost inside a one-kick graph.** That
figure is a one-task job's, where the execution is nil and the number is the drain plus the
poll's quantisation; a kick pays 0.08-0.17 ms instead. The reason is visible in the `drain`
column: `drain0` — jobs whose completion bit was already up on the tick that first saw the
kick over — fires on **every** job of all four graphs, so an interior program's drain overlaps
the next program's execution and the last one's finishes inside the poll interval that detects
the kick being over. **The drain is a term of a per-layer graph and not of a chained one**:
run the same networks with `ROCKET_RK3576_NET_KICK=0` and it is 0.20-3.17 ms per inference
across 29-72 submits.

**The poll period is worth at most one period per inference and is not a lever.** Moving
`poll_interval_us` 50 -> 10 takes MobileNetV1's execution 1764 -> 1740 us — 24 us, half a
period, exactly the mean quantisation — and 50 -> 200 costs 145; neither moves a wall outside
run-to-run spread. What this also means is that the execution column is an **upper bound** on
the part's own time by up to one poll period, which at 25 us against 2.2-5.8 ms changes
nothing.

The NPU clock is **786 MHz** and does not park: `aclk_rknn0`, `aclk_rknn1`, `aclk_rknn_cbuf`
and `clk_rknn_dsu0` all read 786431998 Hz idle and under load alike, with no devfreq node —
so there is no RK3576 analogue of the RK3588's 200 MHz boot park to undo.

#### And the execution is FRONT-LOADED: the stem is a third of it

Which program the execution is spent on comes from the per-layer arm
(`ROCKET_RK3576_NET_KICK=0`), where each layer prints its own profile and its feature scatter
is a separate column from its submit. Subtract the ~0.05 ms of per-job non-execution the
driver's counters give for that arm and a layer's submit column is its execution plus its own
drain [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `bench 20`]:

| | stem | its submit | its execution | next layers | graph's execution | stem's share |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| MobileNetV1 | `ic=3 oc=32 224x224 k3 s2` | 0.50 ms | ~0.44 | 0.26-0.47 | 2.25 | **20%** |
| ResNet-18 | `ic=3 oc=64 224x224 k7 s2` | 2.11 | ~2.05 | 0.32-0.39 | 5.75 | **36%** |

Past the stem the layers are flat — 0.15-0.35 ms of execution each across 28-30 of them, which
sums with the stem to the kick arm's total (30 x ~0.12 + 2.05 = 5.65 against 5.75 measured), so
the accounting closes here too. **One program of 29 to 81 is a third of ResNet-18's and
Inception V1's whole execution**, and it is a THREE-channel image on a datapath whose int8
direct MAC group is 32 channels wide: the part contracts 32 where 3 carry data. Whether that
is reducible is a question about the ENCODING — is there a narrower int8 program — and not
about the host or the driver.

The one narrower encoding that exists is the packed-image path. It DOES reach both 7x7 stem
geometries, it computes bit-exactly there, and it costs 7.2x less — the next section is that
measurement, and the sentence this one used to carry ("it refuses a non-zero left pad") had
the bound backwards: that path REQUIRES a non-zero left pad, which is why TFLite's SAME stems
are the ones it cannot take.

**A chained kick does not make the part COMPUTE faster; it takes the per-job drain and poll
quantisation off the critical path.** Comparing `execution + drain` — the only quantity the
two arms measure the same way, since a one-task job's `exec` ends at `PC_DONE` and its drain
is separate, while a kick absorbs interior drains into `exec` — ResNet-18 is 6.62 ms per
inference per-layer against 5.75 chained, and the 0.87 ms difference over 30 extra submits is
29 us each, which is the poll quantisation. The per-layer arm's drain is 101 us a job on
ResNet-18 and 18 on MobileNetV1, tracking the bytes each program writes, as the DPU
completion's own 82-343 us range says it should.

**The stem was the largest single layer of any of the three at 29% of ResNet-18** when that
graph ran in eleven submits — 3.08 ms, of which 2.13 was submit and wait, 0.62 the image
scatter and 0.34 plan+gen on a resident handle. It is now inside the kick and has no bucket
of its own, but its arithmetic is unchanged and it is still the dominant program in that
stream. It runs on the direct datapath at the model's own three input channels, and the int8
direct cube is a 32-channel MAC group at every count, so the part contracts 32 channels where
3 carry data: the program is doing about **10.6x the MACs the layer needs**.

### The execution is the programmed MAC count, and the feature DMA is 8% of it

A convolution's execution has two candidate terms and a stem is where they are hardest to
tell apart: the direct int8 datapath contracts a 32-channel MAC group at any input-channel
count, and it also streams a 32-channel cube through the CBUF, so a narrower encoding would
shrink both. Crossing the two axes separates them, where sweeping either alone cannot
(`tests/rk3576_stem_cost.c`, resident weights, driver `stat_exec_us` via the job-timing debug
module, `bench(2N) - bench(N)`) [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-02]:

| arm | tasks | programmed MMAC | feature KiB read | execution |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **A** `ic=3 oc=64 224x224 s2`, k=1 | 5 | 25.7 | 1533 | 0.301 ms |
| k=3 | 5 | 231.2 | 1596 | 0.452 |
| k=5 | 5 | 642.3 | 1652 | 1.003 |
| k=7 | 6 | 1258.8 | 1743 | 1.802 |
| **B** same MACs, 4.2x fewer bytes: `112x112 k7 s1` | 2 | 1258.8 | 413 | **1.651** |
| **C** k7 `oc=16` (programs a 32-channel group) | 5 | 629.4 | 1708 | 0.953 |
| k7 `oc=32` | 5 | 629.4 | 1708 | 0.952 |
| **D** `k3 oc=32 ic=32` | 5 | 115.6 | 1596 | 0.302 |
| `ic=64` | 9 | 231.2 | 3248 | 0.602 |
| `ic=128` | 19 | 462.4 | 6776 | 1.249 |

Every arm is bit-exact against a CPU model of the part's own arithmetic. Group A holds the
feature cube constant and moves the MAC count 49x; a pure feature-DMA bound predicts a flat
column and a pure MAC bound 49x, and it is **6.0x** — a two-term sum. Least squares on A:

> **execution = 0.218 ms + 1.246e-3 ms per programmed MMAC** — 1.246 ps/MAC, **0.803 TMAC/s**

which reproduces the independent output-channel sweep to **0.05 ms** and the two low
input-channel arms to 0.06 and 0.10. **Group B prices a byte directly**, holding the MAC
count identical and cutting the feature read 4.2x: **1.802 -> 1.651 ms**, so the feature DMA
is **0.151 ms of the stem's execution, 8.4%**. The one arm the model misses is `ic=128`, by
+0.46 ms, and it is the arm whose row allowance collapses to 19 tasks re-reading 6776 KiB —
**the byte term is small at a stem and grows with the task count**, not with the plane.

Group C is worth reading on its own: `oc=16` and `oc=32` cost the same to three decimal
places, because the direct cube groups output channels by 32 and both program one group. The
MAC count that predicts execution is the PROGRAMMED one throughout — `round32(oc)`,
`round32(ic)`, every tap of the kernel — which is exactly why a 3-channel stem is expensive.

#### So the packed-image encoding is 7.2x, and it is 8x fewer MACs

The consequence is not an extrapolation. The packed-image first conv interleaves four lanes
per pixel instead of contracting a 32-lane group, so at the same geometry it programs 8.0x
fewer MACs — and it runs at both stem geometries, bit-exactly [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9]:

| `ic=3 224x224 s2`, in_zp 0 | programmed MMAC | exec | drain | exec+drain | push -> fence signal |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| k7 `oc=64` direct | 1258.8 | 1.803 ms | 0.000 | 1.803 | 1.856 ms |
| k7 `oc=64` **packed** | 157.4 | 0.053 | 0.198 | **0.251** | **0.290** |
| k3 `oc=32` direct | 115.6 | 0.302 | 0.000 | 0.302 | 0.344 |
| k3 `oc=32` **packed** | 14.5 | 0.053 | 0.049 | **0.102** | **0.138** |

Compare `exec + drain`, not `exec`: a short program's drain is exposed where a long one's
finishes inside the poll interval that detects the job over. On that quantity the packed form
is **7.2x** at the 7x7 stem and **3.0x** at the 3x3 — a lever of **1.566 ms** of driver-measured
job time per inference at the 7x7 geometry. And the two encodings run at the SAME rate,
1.59e-3 against 1.43e-3 ms per programmed MMAC: **the packed path is not a faster datapath, it
is fewer MACs**, which is the cost model closing on itself.

#### What blocks it is different on each graph, and only one is the part's

Read out of the four `.rnet` blobs, against the packed encoding's four decoded bounds (a
non-zero left pad, an output width of `iw/stride`, that width a multiple of 16, and — for an
exact border — a zero input zero point):

| graph | stem | `pad_left` | `in_zp` | packed encoding |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| MobileNetV1 | `ic=3 oc=32 k3 s2` | 0 | 0 | refused: TFLite SAME has no leading pad |
| MobileNetV2 | `ic=3 oc=32 k3 s2` | 0 | 0 | refused: same |
| ResNet-18 | `ic=3 oc=64 k7 s2` | 3 | **-128** | refused: the zero point, on the pad columns |
| Inception V1 | `ic=3 oc=64 k7 s2` | **2** | **0** | **runs** |

So the encoding requires a leading pad rather than refusing one, and the two graphs where the
stem is 36% of the execution fail its bounds for two different reasons. ResNet-18's is a
measured hardware bound — the pad COLUMNS feed 0 at both ends whatever `CNA_PAD_CON1` holds
— and repairing it on the host costs 9.8 ms against a ~2 ms saving. But **the columns are
materialisable**: extending the image at the zero point and cropping the surplus output column
is bit-exact against the direct lowering on that exact stem (above), so the bound is on the
naive descriptor rather than on the encoding. **Inception V1's was not a hardware bound at
all** and is closed. The three refusals are the LIBRARY's own messages read out of a graph run
rather than an arithmetic done here: `rk3576_net_gate` attempts the packed pack for every
`ic <= 4` convolution and prints what came back, so the count is a measurement.

#### The asymmetric output extent is HONOURED on the packed encoding

The CNA takes no configured trailing pad — it derives the pad its last window consumes from
the output extent and the leading pad — and the emitter writes that derivation into
`CNA_PAD_CON0`'s high bytes for BOTH sub-encodings out of one shared
`r76_trail_pad(OW, stride, KW, pad_left, IW)`. The packed converter reads those bytes exactly
as the direct path does [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_argb_extent.c`]: **eight
asymmetric cells bit-exact** over kernels 5 and 7, strides 1 and 2, planes 64/128/224,
trailing pads 2/3/4, and each axis asymmetric while the other stays derived.

The score is **by pad region** — every output position classified by which pads its window
reaches, interior / leading / trailing / both — because that is what separates the two ways
the answer could have been no: a derived pad the sub-encoding does not read shows as an exact
interior against a wrong trailing edge, where a mis-programmed geometry is wrong everywhere.
Each cell carries two controls, the DIRECT path at the same extent (the arm that must
succeed, since it is what makes the reference trustworthy) and the packed path at the
symmetric lead.

So the refusal was the LIBRARY's. What bounds an extent on this path is the shared rule every
int8 entry already applies: a trailing pad of `kh` or more is a whole window of pad.

#### A packed program may HEAD a chained stream

The other half of reachability, since all four graphs are one hardware kick in one submit: a
stem that cannot chain splits the run and pays a 784 KiB join the per-join sweep priced at
6.80 ms, which would make the cheaper program a large net loss. It chains — **20 of 20 over
five geometries, both boundaries scored** [HW sweep, `tests/rk3576_chain_argb.c`], with the
consumer's stale form distinguishable throughout. The reason to ask was that the packed
sub-encoding is the one place a convolution program configures the CNA front end differently
(a folded channel count that disagrees with the DMA's, a live CVT stage, a packed row stride
instead of a cube's), so if any of that crossed a chained boundary the program after it is
where it would show. A packed handle can only BEGIN a run — its feature buffer is an
interleaved image and no producer's surface is one — which is the only position a stem
occupies anyway; its output side is an ordinary NC1HWC2 cube and joins normally.

#### The lever lands once the encoding is RESIDENT and its pack is VECTORISED

Neither is encoding work and both were worth more than the register question. Measured as the
whole prepacked entry, resident on both arms, governor pinned [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9,
`rk3576_stem_cost` E and F]:

| `ic=3 224x224 s2`, resident | tasks | programmed MMAC | wall | direct | packed saves |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Inception V1 stem, k7 `oc=64` pad 2, extent 112 | 1 | 157.4 | **1.24 ms** | 3.09 | **1.85 ms (60%)** |
| k7 `oc=64` pad 3 | 1 | 157.4 | **1.23** | 3.12 | **1.89 (61%)** |
| k3 `oc=32` pad 1 | 1 | 14.5 | **0.63** | 1.13 | **0.50 (44%)** |

The packed program plans into ONE row task where the direct lowering takes five or six, and
every arm is bit-exact against the CPU model.

Two library facts had to change first, and each was worth about a millisecond:

- **RESIDENCY.** The packed encoding had no handle, so it ran the transient entry and paid a
  per-call BO teardown the direct arms did not. Profiled transiently at the Inception V1
  geometry the call was 4.79 ms of which teardown was 1.82 and `plan+gen` 0.85. It is one
  handle now — `rocket_conv2d_int8_pack_rk3576()` packs the packed cube as well as the direct
  one, and `rocket_conv2d_int8_rk3576()` builds the same handle for a transient call — so
  there is no second arithmetic to keep in agreement. The per-axis requant and the fused LUT
  activation are refused on it: both are the direct path's levers and neither is measured on
  this encoding.
- **THE HOST IMAGE PACK.** A CHW tensor to an interleaved image is a 2/3/4-way interleave of
  whole planes, which is one `vst2`/`vst3`/`vst4` — not the 16x16 block transpose the cube
  path uses. Written an element at a time it gave most of the lever back: at the Inception V1
  stem the wall was **2.80 ms scalar against 1.64 vectorised** (same run conditions), and the
  profiled split says the encoding saves **1.70 ms of submit** and was spending **1.88 ms** of
  it on that loop. `rocket_rk3576_argb_pack()` is the shared entry.

**TRAP — the RK3576 board runs the `ondemand` governor and a host-bound arm is sensitive to
it.** The direct arm's wall read 3.82-3.86 ms before the interleave landed and 4.53-4.61
after, with nothing about it changed: the packed arm ahead of it in the same process got
1.2 ms faster, so the governor had ramped less by the time the direct arm ran. The same arm
run alone through the name filter reads 4.6-4.8 and reads 3.86 as the last row of a full
sweep. **Pin the governor for any A/B whose arms differ in host work, and discard the first
PROCESS as well as the first iteration** — with `performance` set, runs 2 and 3 agree to
0.01 ms while run 1 is 25% high.

#### What the change adds, enumerated

Against the direct lowering the packed form removes the MAC count and five row tasks, and
adds: a host interleave (now vectorised), a weight cube that is a different object, four
geometry bounds that are silent if violated and are therefore refused, a zero-input-zero-point
bound, no per-axis requant, no fused activation, and no cube IN. Reachability across the
corpus is unchanged by any of it — **one graph of four**, Inception V1, because the bounds
that block the other three are the part's.

#### And it lands on the GRAPH, at 19% of Inception V1's wall

Wired into `rk3576_net_gate` — one descriptor field, the rest of the run structure untouched
— the whole graph is **10.7-10.8 -> 8.7 ms**, byte-identical, in the SAME one submit and one
kick over 81 layers, with the task count 77 -> 72 [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `bench 100`, three
runs an arm, `ondemand`]. The saving is the same absolute 2.1 ms in every guard arm, since
the guard does not move (122 brackets over 14445 KiB either way):

| Inception V1, `bench 100` | direct stem | packed stem | saves |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| default guard | 10.7-10.8 ms | **8.7** | 2.0-2.1 (19%) |
| `GUARD_NARROW=1` | 7.7 | **5.6** | 2.1 (27%) |
| `GUARD_PER_KICK=1` | 6.2 | **4.1** | 2.1 (34%) |

The other three graphs do not move — 4.8-4.9 / 6.7 / 9.2-9.3 — and each prints the library's
own refusal for its stem, which is what makes "one graph of four" a measurement.

Two terms move and the profile names both [`ROCKET_RK3576_INT8_PROF=1`, ratios from the
profiled run and walls from an unprofiled one]:

| term | direct | packed | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| submit (the part running programs) | 5.59 ms | 4.05-4.15 | **-1.48** |
| feature (the host operand pack) | 0.50-0.55 | 0.07-0.09 | **-0.44** |
| stamp + verify (the write guard) | 4.28-4.45 | 4.36-4.63 | 0 |
| de-scatter | 0.01 | 0.01 | 0 |

The feature term is the second half and was not in the cap: the direct lowering scatters the
image into a 32-channel cube — `round32(3)` — where the packed one interleaves three bytes
per pixel, about 10x fewer bytes through a loop that is already vectorised on both paths.
**CAP 1.85 ms (17%), measured 2.0-2.1 (19%)** — a hit, low by the feature term, and the
"per-op A/B is an upper bound" reasoning was wrong in sign: a per-op A/B compares two whole
calls that each pay `plan+gen` and a readback the chain does not, so it is not an upper bound
on anything, it is a different quantity.

**The packed handle's surface IS the next layer's cube**, which was tagged expected and is now
measured: the graph keeps all 53 joins of 80 adjacent pairs and its single 81-layer run, so
the stem is an ordinary interior-adjacent producer.

**And the encoding is bit-exact at a non-zero WEIGHT zero point** — Inception V1's stem
carries `w_zp = 13` and every layer of the graph scores `hw exact` against the CPU model. The
per-op probes all ran at `w_zp = 0`, so that axis first varied here; the packed path's tap
count is the LIVE `ic*kh*kw` and its feature buffer has no padded MAC group, so the `B` term
folds over exactly the taps the program walks.

It is DEFAULT-ON in the gate (`ROCKET_RK3576_NET_ARGB=0` is the control, and is a gate: the
two lowerings must agree byte for byte and return the same label).

### What is open

**A CONVOLUTION PROGRAM WHOSE DPU DRAIN OUTLASTS `dpu_grace_us` LOSES ITS OUTPUT TAIL, AND
THE FIX IS THE DRIVER'S.** See above: raising the parameter from 500 to 750 us takes the
shipped planner to exact agreement with the part on 23 of 23 geometries, and every measured
wall is the same 766-830 MMACs of work rather than any CBUF footprint. Raising it is a
workaround — one program's duration is not a property of the driver's timing, and 0022
already removed the same magic number for a kick of more than one task. What is open is
whether a job of ONE task can be given the same treatment: it programs `TASK_NUMBER = 1`,
where both `PC_TASK_STATUS` counters read zero always, so the register that closed the
multi-task case says nothing here. Unreachable in this corpus, where the row window keeps
every task's drain under the deadline by accident.

**WHETHER THE PLANNER'S WHOLE-CUBE CHARGE CAN GO once the driver stops retiring early.**
`rocket_rk3576_max_task_rows()` charges every output-channel group's slice where a rung still
fits, which was fitted to the ResNet stem's 48-row measurement — a measurement that is now
known to be the deadline. It costs the stem a fifth row task. The one geometry that keeps the
one-slice model, `56x56 k7 ic 64 oc 64`, is honoured in full at a grace of 750, so the charge
is [expected] to be unnecessary as well; that is a prediction, and relaxing a bound whose
safety would then rest on a module parameter's value is not a trade to take before the driver
side is settled.

**A UNIT-KERNEL POOLING PROGRAM POISONS THE NEXT PROCESS'S FIRST POOLING SUBMITS.** 2 of 30
runs with `rk3576_pool_probe pad` in front, 0 of 30 with a wide-output int32 program in
front, 0 of 30 alone and 0 of 300 alone earlier — and the failure is byte-identical every
time (the first two cells at `c=16`: one whole output row wrong on the 16x16 plane, 2302 of
2304 on the 24x24). What is NOT known: which register or state carries it, whether a
non-pooling submit in between clears it, and whether any geometry a real graph runs can
produce it. It is the same question as `pool_probe pad`'s own unstable unit-kernel cells.

**Whether a POOL may START or END a chained run is unmeasured, and refused.** One that began
a run would need the chain to scatter a row-major tensor into its cube and one that ended a
run would need its output de-scattered, which is host work the stream exists to remove — so
the question is a library one rather than a hardware one, and no graph here asks it (both
MobileNets and ResNet-18 pool in the middle). Worth nothing on this corpus.
